```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Perun adalah dewa tertinggi dalam mitologi Slavia, penguasa langit, guruh, dan petir, yang juga dikaitkan dengan perang, hukum, dan pohon ek.",
  "en": "Perun is the highest god of Slavic mythology, lord of the sky, thunder and lightning, also linked with war, law and oak trees.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "perun-c01",
   "perun-c19"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, Perun menempati puncak jajaran dewa. Ia memerintah langit, guruh, petir, badai, dan hujan, serta berkaitan dengan hukum, perang, kesuburan, dan pohon ek. Lambang lain yang melekat padanya antara lain api, gunung, angin, bunga iris, elang, kuda dan kereta, serta senjata berupa palu, kapak, dan anak panah. Para peneliti juga menggambarkannya sebagai dewa badai tertinggi yang dihubungkan dengan batu, guruh, kilat, hujan, perang, dan keadilan.",
   "en": "In Slavic mythology Perun stands at the top of the pantheon. He rules the sky, thunder, lightning, storms and rain, and is tied to law, war, fertility and oak trees. Other emblems attached to him include fire, mountains, wind, the iris, the eagle, horses and carts, and weapons such as the hammer, the axe and the arrow. Scholars also describe him as a supreme storm god connected with rock, thunder, lightning, rain, war and justice.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perun-c01",
    "perun-c02",
    "perun-c24"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Di Rus Kiev pada abad ke-9 sampai ke-10 ia adalah dewa tertinggi, mula-mula dikaitkan dengan senjata batu lalu dengan senjata logam. Ia sudah disebut pada abad ke-6 oleh sejarawan Romawi Timur Procopius dalam De Bello Gothico. Pada tahun 980 Vladimir mendirikan arca lima dewa pagan di depan istananya, dan Perun menjadi yang utama, dengan kepala perak dan kumis emas. Hari rayanya dirayakan dengan persembahan pada bulan Juli.",
   "en": "In Kievan Rus during the 9th and 10th centuries he was the supreme god, associated first with stone weapons and later with metal ones. He was already mentioned in the 6th century by the Eastern Roman historian Procopius in De Bello Gothico. In 980 Vladimir set up statues of five pagan gods before his palace, with Perun foremost among them, bearing a silver head and a golden moustache. His feast was celebrated with sacrifices in July.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perun-c03",
    "perun-c05",
    "perun-c06",
    "perun-c20"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut kepercayaan rakyat, kilat Perun berupa batu dan anak panah batu, sehingga fulgurit, belemnit, dan perkakas batu prasejarah yang ditemukan di tanah dianggap sisa senjatanya. Senjata itu dipercaya melindungi dari nasib buruk, sihir jahat, penyakit, dan petir. Satu sumber menggambarkannya sebagai lelaki paruh baya berjanggut yang terbang di atas batu gilingan sambil melepaskan kilat dan guruh.",
   "en": "In folk belief Perun's lightning bolts were stones and stone arrows, so fulgurites, belemnites and prehistoric stone tools found in the ground were taken as remains of his weapons. These weapons were thought to guard against bad luck, evil magic, disease and lightning. One source pictures him as a bearded middle-aged man flying on a millstone while hurling lightning and thunder.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perun-c09",
    "perun-c10",
    "perun-c29"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Perun dikatakan menikahi Matahari, tetapi berbagi istri dengan musuhnya Veles, karena Matahari setiap malam turun ke dunia bawah yang dikuasai Veles. Tempat pemujaannya berada di puncak gunung atau bukit, atau di hutan keramat di bawah pohon ek tua, dengan persembahan banteng, sapi, domba jantan, dan telur. Ia juga dihubungkan dengan hari Kamis dan dengan logam timah.",
   "en": "Perun was said to be married to the Sun, but he shared his wife with his enemy Veles, since the Sun sank each night into the underworld ruled by Veles. His shrines stood on mountain tops or hills, or in sacred groves under ancient oaks, with offerings of a bull, an ox, a ram and eggs. He was also linked with Thursday and with tin.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perun-c11",
    "perun-c12",
    "perun-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Setelah masuk Kristen, Vladimir memerintahkan arca-arca Perun dirobohkan, diseret, dipukuli, lalu dibuang ke sungai; menurut satu sumber arcanya dilempar ke Sungai Dnipro dan di tempatnya dibangun Gereja Santo Basil. Gereja menggantikan pemujaan Perun dengan pemujaan Santo Elia, yang mewarisi semua sifat Perun, tetapi orang Lemko masih menganggap ungkapan \"Semoga Perun menyambarmu mati\" sebagai kutukan paling mengerikan. Kini Perun termasuk dewa yang paling dihormati dalam neopaganisme Slavia (rodnovery), sebagai dewa pejuang dan saingan Veles.",
   "en": "After his conversion Vladimir had the statues of Perun torn down, dragged, beaten and thrown into rivers; one source says the statue was cast into the Dnipro River and Saint Basil's Church was built on its site. The Church replaced the cult of Perun with that of Saint Elijah, who inherited all his qualities, yet the Lemkos still regard \"May Perun strike you dead\" as the most terrible curse. Today Perun is among the most revered gods of Slavic neopaganism (rodnovery), as a god of warriors and a rival of Veles.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perun-c14",
    "perun-c15",
    "perun-c21",
    "perun-c22",
    "perun-c23"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "perun-c01",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "In Slavic mythology, Perun (Cyrillic: Перун ) is the highest god of the pantheon and the god of sky, thunder, lightning, storms, rain, law, war, fertility and oak trees.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, Perun (Перун) adalah dewa tertinggi dalam jajaran dewa, dewa langit, guruh, petir, badai, hujan, hukum, perang, kesuburan, dan pohon ek.",
    "en": "In Slavic mythology, Perun (Перун) is the highest god of the pantheon and god of sky, thunder, lightning, storms, rain, law, war, fertility and oak trees."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c02",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "His other attributes were fire, mountains, wind, iris, eagle, firmament ... horses and carts, and weapons (hammer, axe (Axe of Perun), and arrow).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Atribut lain Perun adalah api, gunung, angin, bunga iris, elang, cakrawala, kuda dan kereta, serta senjata berupa palu, kapak (Kapak Perun), dan anak panah.",
    "en": "His other attributes were fire, mountains, wind, iris, eagle, firmament, horses and carts, and weapons: hammer, axe (Axe of Perun) and arrow."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c03",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "The supreme god in the Kievan Rus' during the 9th-10th centuries, Perun was first associated with weapons made of stone and later with those of metal.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagai dewa tertinggi di Rus Kiev pada abad ke-9 sampai ke-10, Perun mula-mula dikaitkan dengan senjata dari batu, kemudian dengan senjata logam.",
    "en": "The supreme god in Kievan Rus in the 9th-10th centuries, Perun was first associated with stone weapons and later with metal ones."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c04",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "To this day, the word perun in a number of Slavic languages means \"thunder,\" or \"lightning bolt\".",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hingga kini, dalam sejumlah bahasa Slavia kata perun berarti \"guruh\" atau \"sambaran petir\".",
    "en": "To this day, in a number of Slavic languages the word perun means \"thunder\" or \"lightning bolt\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c05",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "As early as the 6th century, he was mentioned in De Bello Gothico, a historical source written by the Eastern Roman historian Procopius.",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sejak abad ke-6 ia sudah disebut dalam De Bello Gothico, sumber sejarah karya sejarawan Romawi Timur Procopius.",
    "en": "As early as the 6th century he was mentioned in De Bello Gothico, a historical source by the Eastern Roman historian Procopius."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c06",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "In 980, when prince Vladimir the Great came to the throne of Kiev, he erected statues of five pagan gods in front of his palace which he soon thereafter discarded after his Christianization in 988. Perun was chief among these, represented with a silver head and a golden moustache.",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada tahun 980, ketika pangeran Vladimir Agung naik takhta di Kiev, ia mendirikan arca lima dewa pagan di depan istananya, yang kemudian ia singkirkan setelah masuk Kristen pada 988; Perun adalah yang utama, digambarkan berkepala perak dan berkumis emas.",
    "en": "In 980, when Prince Vladimir the Great took the throne of Kiev, he erected statues of five pagan gods before his palace, discarding them after his Christianization in 988; Perun was chief among them, with a silver head and a golden moustache."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c07",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "Perun is strongly correlated with the near-identical Perkūnas/Pērkons from Baltic mythology, suggesting either a common derivative of the Proto-Indo European thunder god",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perun sangat berkaitan dengan Perkūnas/Pērkons yang hampir identik dari mitologi Baltik, yang menunjukkan asal bersama dari dewa guruh Proto-Indo-Eropa atau peminjaman.",
    "en": "Perun is strongly correlated with the near-identical Perkūnas/Pērkons of Baltic mythology, suggesting a common Proto-Indo-European thunder god origin or borrowing."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c08",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "The root *perkwu originally probably meant oak, but in Proto-Slavic this evolved into *per- meaning \"to strike, to slay\".",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Akar *perkwu semula mungkin berarti pohon ek, tetapi dalam bahasa Proto-Slavia berkembang menjadi *per- yang berarti \"memukul, membunuh\".",
    "en": "The root *perkwu probably first meant oak, but in Proto-Slavic it became *per-, meaning \"to strike, to slay\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c09",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "Perun's lightning bolts were believed to be stones and stone arrows. According to folk beliefs, fulgurites, belemnites, and sometimes even the remains of prehistoric stone tools found in the ground are remains of these weapons.",
   "locator": "Weapons",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kilat Perun dipercaya berupa batu dan anak panah batu; menurut kepercayaan rakyat, fulgurit, belemnit, dan kadang perkakas batu prasejarah yang ditemukan di tanah adalah sisa senjata itu.",
    "en": "Perun's lightning bolts were believed to be stones and stone arrows; folk belief held fulgurites, belemnites and sometimes prehistoric stone tools found in the ground to be remains of these weapons."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c10",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "The weapons of Perun protected against bad luck, evil magic, disease, and – naturally enough – lightning itself.",
   "locator": "Weapons",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Senjata Perun melindungi dari nasib buruk, sihir jahat, penyakit, dan juga dari petir itu sendiri.",
    "en": "The weapons of Perun protected against bad luck, evil magic, disease and lightning itself."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c11",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "it can also be shown that Perun was married to the Sun. He, however, shared his wife with his enemy Veles, as each night the Sun was thought of as diving behind the horizon and into the underworld, the realm of the dead over which Veles ruled.",
   "locator": "Characteristics",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perun dapat ditunjukkan menikahi Matahari, tetapi ia berbagi istri dengan musuhnya Veles, karena setiap malam Matahari dianggap terbenam ke dunia bawah, alam orang mati yang dikuasai Veles.",
    "en": "Perun can be shown to have been married to the Sun, but he shared his wife with his enemy Veles, as each night the Sun was thought to dive into the underworld, the realm of the dead ruled by Veles."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c12",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "Shrines of Perun were located either on top of mountains or hills, or in sacred groves underneath ancient oaks. These were general places of worship and sacrifices (with a bull, an ox, a ram, and eggs).",
   "locator": "Characteristics",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tempat pemujaan Perun berada di puncak gunung atau bukit, atau di hutan keramat di bawah pohon ek tua; di sana dilakukan ibadah dan persembahan berupa banteng, sapi, domba jantan, dan telur.",
    "en": "Perun's shrines stood on mountain tops or hills, or in sacred groves under ancient oaks, as places of worship and sacrifice (a bull, an ox, a ram and eggs)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c13",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "In addition to the tree association, Perun had a day association (Thursday) as well as the material association (tin).",
   "locator": "Characteristics",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Selain pohon, Perun juga dikaitkan dengan satu hari, yaitu Kamis (Thursday), dan dengan satu bahan, yaitu timah.",
    "en": "Besides the tree, Perun was associated with a day (Thursday) and a material (tin)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c14",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "He ordered that the statues of Perun which he himself had erected formerly, be dethroned, torn down with great dishonor and dragged through the streets as they were beaten with sticks. The idols were then cast into rivers and not permitted to land on the shore.",
   "locator": "Post-Christian Perun",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia memerintahkan agar arca-arca Perun yang dulu didirikannya sendiri dirobohkan dengan hina dan diseret di jalan sambil dipukuli tongkat, lalu dibuang ke sungai dan tidak dibiarkan terdampar di tepi.",
    "en": "He ordered the statues of Perun he had once erected to be torn down in disgrace and dragged through the streets while beaten with sticks, then cast into rivers and not allowed to land on the shore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c15",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "Perun is one of the most revered gods in Slavic neopaganism (rodnovery). He is considered a thunderer, the god of warriors, and a rival of Veles, also embodying spring thunderstorms that fertilize the earth.",
   "locator": "In neopaganism",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perun adalah salah satu dewa yang paling dihormati dalam neopaganisme Slavia (rodnovery); ia dianggap dewa guruh, dewa para pejuang, dan saingan Veles, serta perwujudan badai musim semi yang menyuburkan bumi.",
    "en": "Perun is one of the most revered gods of Slavic neopaganism (rodnovery), seen as a thunderer, god of warriors and rival of Veles, embodying spring storms that fertilize the earth."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c16",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "The Baltic tribes had a widespread cult of the thunderer Perkunas, one of the main deities of the Baltic pantheon. With Perun, this deity also shares common attributes (amulets in the form of an axe, a fiery four-pointed symbol, oak as the main tree)",
   "locator": "The cult of Perun among neighboring tribes",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Suku-suku Baltik memuja dewa guruh Perkunas, salah satu dewa utama mereka, yang memiliki atribut sama dengan Perun: jimat berbentuk kapak, simbol api berujung empat, dan pohon ek sebagai pohon utama.",
    "en": "The Baltic tribes widely worshipped the thunderer Perkunas, a main Baltic deity sharing attributes with Perun: axe amulets, a fiery four-pointed symbol and the oak as chief tree."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c17",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "Among South Slavs, a mountain plant Iris germanica is known in folklore as perunika (\"Perun's plant\") and sometimes also as bogisha (\"god's plant\"), and was believed to grow from ground that had been struck by lightning.",
   "locator": "Toponyms",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di kalangan Slavia Selatan, tanaman gunung Iris germanica dikenal dalam cerita rakyat sebagai perunika (\"tanaman Perun\") dan kadang bogisha (\"tanaman dewa\"), dan dipercaya tumbuh dari tanah yang tersambar petir.",
    "en": "Among South Slavs the mountain plant Iris germanica is known in folklore as perunika (\"Perun's plant\") and sometimes bogisha (\"god's plant\"), believed to grow from ground struck by lightning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c18",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "The Bulgarian and Macedonian people believe that the name of the Bulgarian mountain Pirin, one of the highest mountains of the Balkan Peninsula, was named after Perun.",
   "locator": "Toponyms",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Bulgaria dan Makedonia percaya bahwa Pirin, salah satu gunung tertinggi di Semenanjung Balkan, dinamai menurut Perun.",
    "en": "Bulgarians and Macedonians believe the Bulgarian mountain Pirin, one of the highest in the Balkan Peninsula, was named after Perun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c19",
   "source_id": "perun-s2",
   "quote": "Perun. One of the principal deities in eastern and northern Slavic mythology. In pre-Christian Kyivan Rus’ this was the chief god—the god of thunder, lightning, and rain, the ruler of the heavens, and, later, the god of war.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perun adalah salah satu dewa utama dalam mitologi Slavia timur dan utara; di Rus Kyiv sebelum Kristen ia dewa tertinggi, dewa guruh, kilat, dan hujan, penguasa langit, dan kemudian dewa perang.",
    "en": "Perun is one of the principal deities of eastern and northern Slavic mythology; in pre-Christian Kyivan Rus he was the chief god, god of thunder, lightning and rain, ruler of the heavens and later god of war."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c20",
   "source_id": "perun-s2",
   "quote": "In 980 a wooden statue of Perun with a silver head and golden moustache stood on Perun Hill in Kyiv. Perun's feast, celebrated with sacrifices, took place in July.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada tahun 980 arca kayu Perun berkepala perak dan berkumis emas berdiri di Bukit Perun di Kyiv; hari raya Perun dirayakan dengan persembahan pada bulan Juli.",
    "en": "In 980 a wooden statue of Perun with a silver head and golden moustache stood on Perun Hill in Kyiv; his feast, with sacrifices, took place in July."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c21",
   "source_id": "perun-s2",
   "quote": "After adopting Christianity Prince Volodymyr the Great ordered Perun's statue to be cast into the Dnipro River and on its site built Saint Basil's Church.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah memeluk Kristen, Pangeran Volodymyr (Vladimir) Agung memerintahkan arca Perun dilempar ke Sungai Dnipro dan membangun Gereja Santo Basil di tempatnya.",
    "en": "After adopting Christianity, Prince Volodymyr the Great had Perun's statue cast into the Dnipro River and built Saint Basil's Church on its site."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c22",
   "source_id": "perun-s2",
   "quote": "The Christian church replaced the cult of Perun with the cult of Saint Elijah (feast day: 2 August, or 20 July OS), to whom all of Perun's qualities were transferred.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gereja Kristen menggantikan pemujaan Perun dengan pemujaan Santo Elia, yang menerima semua sifat Perun.",
    "en": "The Christian church replaced the cult of Perun with that of Saint Elijah, to whom all of Perun's qualities were transferred."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c23",
   "source_id": "perun-s2",
   "quote": "The Lemkos still consider the expression ‘May Perun strike you dead’ as the most terrible curse.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Lemko masih menganggap ungkapan \"Semoga Perun menyambarmu mati\" sebagai kutukan paling mengerikan.",
    "en": "The Lemkos still regard the expression \"May Perun strike you dead\" as the most terrible curse."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c24",
   "source_id": "perun-s3",
   "quote": "The author analyses Perun, a supreme Storm-God in Old-Slavic pagan religion and mythology, which is correlated to rock, thunder, lightning and rain, war and justice.",
   "locator": "abstract",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Penulis menganalisis Perun sebagai dewa badai tertinggi dalam agama dan mitologi pagan Slavia Kuno, yang berkaitan dengan batu, guruh, kilat, hujan, perang, dan keadilan.",
    "en": "The author analyses Perun as a supreme storm god of Old Slavic pagan religion and mythology, correlated with rock, thunder, lightning, rain, war and justice."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c25",
   "source_id": "perun-s4",
   "quote": "For more than a century, scholars of Slavic mythology have adopted the assumption connecting the German iris (Iris germanica) plant and the cult of the god Perun.",
   "locator": "abstract",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Selama lebih dari seabad, para ahli mitologi Slavia menerima anggapan yang menghubungkan tanaman iris Jerman (Iris germanica) dengan pemujaan dewa Perun.",
    "en": "For more than a century, scholars of Slavic mythology have assumed a connection between the German iris (Iris germanica) and the cult of Perun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c26",
   "source_id": "perun-s4",
   "quote": "the author concludes that there are no grounds for linking the plant to the cult of Perun, the god of thunder. The motivation for the name “perunika” comes from its dominant feather-like (perasti) leaves",
   "locator": "abstract",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Penulis menyimpulkan tidak ada dasar untuk mengaitkan tanaman itu dengan pemujaan Perun, dewa guruh; nama \"perunika\" berasal dari daunnya yang mirip bulu (perasti).",
    "en": "The author concludes there are no grounds for linking the plant to the cult of Perun, the thunder god; the name \"perunika\" comes from its feather-like (perasti) leaves."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c27",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "Perun was worshipped by the Varangian (Scandinavian) warriors hired by Oleg and Igor during the campaigns against Byzantium ... It is likely that the purely Slavic god Perun replaced for them the Scandinavian Thor, also the thunderer.",
   "locator": "The cult of Perun among neighboring tribes",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perun dipuja para prajurit Varangia (Skandinavia) yang disewa Oleg dan Igor dalam kampanye melawan Bizantium; kemungkinan besar bagi mereka Perun menggantikan Thor dari Skandinavia, yang juga dewa guruh.",
    "en": "Perun was worshipped by the Varangian (Scandinavian) warriors hired by Oleg and Igor against Byzantium; for them the purely Slavic Perun likely replaced the Scandinavian Thor, also a thunderer."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c28",
   "source_id": "perun-s5",
   "quote": "ペルーン（ウクライナ語 / キリル表記：Перун、ラテン表記：Perun）またはペルンは、スラヴ神話の主神であり、雷神。ロシア語ではほかにピィエルン (Pyerun)、またポーランド語ではピョルンまたはピオルン (Piorun)",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perun (Перун) adalah dewa utama mitologi Slavia dan dewa guruh; dalam bahasa Rusia ia juga disebut Pyerun, dan dalam bahasa Polandia Piorun.",
    "en": "Perun (Перун) is the chief god of Slavic mythology and a thunder god; in Russian he is also called Pyerun, and in Polish Piorun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c29",
   "source_id": "perun-s5",
   "quote": "類似する多くの雷神と同じように、髭を生やした中年男性の姿をしている。 石臼に乗って空を飛びながら稲妻を放ち雷鳴を轟かせる。",
   "locator": "神話",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Seperti banyak dewa guruh lain, ia berwujud lelaki paruh baya berjanggut; ia terbang di atas batu gilingan sambil melepaskan kilat dan guruh.",
    "en": "Like many thunder gods he has the form of a bearded middle-aged man; he flies on a millstone, hurling lightning and thunder."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perun-c30",
   "source_id": "perun-s1",
   "quote": "The Finnic peoples had a deity Ukko, which had similar functions and attributes with the Slavic and Baltic deities.",
   "locator": "The cult of Perun among neighboring tribes",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bangsa-bangsa Finnik memiliki dewa Ukko dengan fungsi dan atribut yang mirip dengan dewa Slavia dan Baltik.",
    "en": "The Finnic peoples had a god Ukko with functions and attributes similar to the Slavic and Baltic deities."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
  {
   "topic": {
    "id": "Hubungan bunga perunika dengan Perun",
    "en": "Link between the perunika iris and Perun"
   },
   "positions": [
    {
     "summary": {
      "id": "Satu sumber: bunga iris disebut perunika (\"tanaman Perun\") dan dipercaya tumbuh di tanah bekas sambaran petir; para ahli lama mengaitkannya dengan pemujaan Perun.",
      "en": "One source: the iris is called perunika (\"Perun's plant\") and was believed to grow where lightning struck; scholars long linked it with Perun's cult."
     },
     "claim_ids": [

     ]
    },
    {
     "summary": {

      "en": "Another source: there are no grounds for linking the plant to Perun; the name comes from its feather-like leaves."
     },
     "claim_ids": [
      "perun-c26"
     ]
    }
   ]
  }
 ],

 ]
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Rusalka adalah makhluk perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Slavia yang erat dengan air dan sering memusuhi manusia, biasanya arwah perempuan muda yang mati tenggelam.",
  "en": "The rusalka is a female being of Slavic folklore, closely tied to water and often hostile to people, usually the spirit of a young woman who drowned.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "rusalka-c01",
   "rusalka-c06"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Slavia, rusalka adalah sosok perempuan yang sering berbuat jahat kepada manusia dan biasanya dikaitkan dengan air. Padanannya di Eropa lain antara lain Melusine dari Prancis dan Nixie dari dunia Jermanik. Di Rusia utara ia punya banyak sebutan seperti vodyanitsa, sedangkan di Ukraina ia disebut mavka.",
   "en": "In Slavic folklore the rusalka is a female figure, frequently harmful to people and usually associated with water. Her counterparts elsewhere in Europe include the French Melusine and the Germanic Nixie. In northern Russia she had many names, such as vodyanitsa, while in Ukraine she was called a mavka.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "rusalka-c01",
    "rusalka-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut kebanyakan cerita, arwah perempuan muda yang mati di atau dekat sungai atau danau kembali menghantui perairan itu. Ia tidak selalu jahat dan dapat beristirahat dengan tenang bila kematiannya dibalas, tetapi tujuan utamanya memikat pemuda dengan rupa atau suaranya, lalu menjerat kaki mereka dengan rambut panjangnya dan menenggelamkan mereka. Dalam sebagian cerita, korban dibunuh dengan digelitik sampai mati atau dipaksa ikut tarian yang liar. Rambutnya selalu terurai dan bisa pirang, hitam, kehijauan, atau hijau sama sekali.",
   "en": "In most stories the soul of a young woman who died in or near a river or lake returns to haunt those waters. She is not always malevolent and may rest in peace once her death is avenged, but her chief aim is to lure young men with her looks or voice, bind their feet with her long hair and drag them under. In some tales victims were tickled to death or forced to join a frenzied dance. Her hair is always loose and may be fair, black, greenish or entirely green.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "rusalka-c06",
    "rusalka-c07",
    "rusalka-c08",
    "rusalka-c16"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Gambaran rusalka berbeda menurut daerah. Di Ukraina ia lebih sering dikaitkan dengan air, di Belarus dengan hutan dan ladang; biasanya ia digambarkan sebagai gadis cantik tanpa busana, tetapi di beberapa tempat sebagai makhluk buruk rupa dan berbulu. Rusalka dipercaya paling berbahaya pada Pekan Rusalka di awal Juni, ketika mereka keluar dari air untuk berayun di dahan pohon birch dan willow pada malam hari, sehingga berenang saat itu dilarang keras.",
   "en": "Images of the rusalka varied by region. In Ukraine she was more often tied to water, in Belarus to forest and field; she was usually a beautiful naked maiden, but in some areas a hideous, hairy creature. Rusalki were thought most dangerous during Rusalka Week in early June, when they left the water to swing on birch and willow branches at night, so swimming then was strictly forbidden.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "rusalka-c09",
    "rusalka-c10",
    "rusalka-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya berasal dari rusalija, yang masuk ke bahasa Slavia lewat bahasa Yunani Bizantium dari kata Latin Rosālia, nama untuk Pentakosta dan hari-hari di sekitarnya. Menurut Vladimir Propp, bangsa Slavia pagan semula mengaitkan rusalka dengan kesuburan: mereka keluar dari air pada musim semi untuk membawa kelembapan ke ladang dan tidak dianggap jahat sebelum abad ke-19. Dalam versi abad ke-19, rusalka menjadi makhluk mati yang gelisah dan berbahaya.",
   "en": "Her name comes from rusalija, which reached the Slavic languages through Byzantine Greek from the Latin Rosālia, a name for Pentecost and the days around it. According to Vladimir Propp, pagan Slavs first linked rusalki with fertility: they rose from the water in spring to bring moisture to the fields and were not thought evil before the 19th century. In 19th-century versions the rusalka became a restless, dangerous dead being.",

   ]

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c01",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "In Slavic folklore, the rusalka (plural: rusalki; Cyrillic: русалка, Russian pronunciation ... is a female entity, often malicious toward mankind and frequently associated with water. It has counterparts in other parts of Europe, such as the French Melusine and the Germanic Nixie.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Slavia, rusalka (русалка) adalah sosok perempuan yang sering memusuhi manusia dan sering dikaitkan dengan air; padanannya di Eropa lain antara lain Melusine dari Prancis dan Nixie dari dunia Jermanik.",
    "en": "In Slavic folklore the rusalka (русалка) is a female entity, often malicious toward people and frequently associated with water; counterparts elsewhere in Europe include the French Melusine and the Germanic Nixie."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c02",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "In northern Russia, the rusalka was also known by various names such as the vodyanitsa ... In Ukraine, the rusalka was called a mavka.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Rusia utara rusalka juga dikenal dengan berbagai nama seperti vodyanitsa; di Ukraina ia disebut mavka.",
    "en": "In northern Russia the rusalka was also known by names such as vodyanitsa; in Ukraine she was called a mavka."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c03",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "The term \"Rusalka\" derives from \"rusalija\" ... which entered Slavic languages, via Byzantine Greek \"rousália\" ... from the Latin \"Rosālia\" as a name for Pentecost and the days adjacent to it.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Istilah \"rusalka\" berasal dari \"rusalija\", yang masuk ke bahasa Slavia melalui bahasa Yunani Bizantium \"rousália\" dari kata Latin \"Rosālia\", nama untuk Pentakosta dan hari-hari di sekitarnya.",
    "en": "The term \"rusalka\" derives from \"rusalija\", which entered Slavic via Byzantine Greek \"rousália\" from the Latin \"Rosālia\", a name for Pentecost and the adjacent days."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c04",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "According to Vladimir Propp, the original \"rusalka\" was an appellation used by pagan Slavic peoples, who linked them with fertility and did not consider rusalki evil before the 19th century. They came out of the water in the spring to transfer life-giving moisture to the fields",
   "locator": "Origin and appearance",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Vladimir Propp, bangsa Slavia pagan mengaitkan rusalka dengan kesuburan dan tidak menganggapnya jahat sebelum abad ke-19; rusalka keluar dari air pada musim semi untuk membawa kelembapan pemberi kehidupan ke ladang.",
    "en": "According to Vladimir Propp, pagan Slavs linked rusalki with fertility and did not consider them evil before the 19th century; they came out of the water in spring to bring life-giving moisture to the fields."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c05",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "In 19th-century versions, a rusalka is an unquiet, dangerous being who is no longer alive, associated with the unclean spirit.",
   "locator": "Origin and appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam versi abad ke-19, rusalka adalah makhluk yang sudah mati, gelisah, dan berbahaya, yang dikaitkan dengan roh najis.",
    "en": "In 19th-century versions the rusalka is a restless, dangerous being no longer alive, associated with the unclean spirit."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c06",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "It is accounted by most stories that the soul of a young woman who had died in or near a river or a lake would come back to haunt that waterway. This undead rusalka is not invariably malevolent, and would be allowed to die in peace if her death is avenged.",
   "locator": "Origin and appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kebanyakan cerita, arwah perempuan muda yang mati di atau dekat sungai atau danau kembali menghantui perairan itu; rusalka mayat hidup ini tidak selalu jahat dan dapat mati dengan tenang bila kematiannya dibalas.",
    "en": "Most stories say the soul of a young woman who died in or near a river or lake returns to haunt it; this undead rusalka is not always malevolent and may die in peace if her death is avenged."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c07",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "Her main purpose is, however, to lure young men, seduced by either her looks or her voice, into the depths of said waterways where she would entangle their feet with her long hair and submerge them.",
   "locator": "Origin and appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tujuan utamanya adalah memikat pemuda dengan rupa atau suaranya ke kedalaman perairan, lalu menjerat kaki mereka dengan rambut panjangnya dan menenggelamkan mereka.",
    "en": "Her main purpose is to lure young men with her looks or voice into the depths, entangle their feet in her long hair and drown them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c08",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "They killed their victims by tickling them to death or forcing them to participate in a frenzied dance.",
   "locator": "Region-specific",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka membunuh korban dengan menggelitikinya sampai mati atau memaksanya ikut serta dalam tarian yang liar.",
    "en": "They killed their victims by tickling them to death or forcing them into a frenzied dance."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c09",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "In stories from Ukraine, they were often linked with water. In Belarus they were linked with the forest and field. They were usually pictured as beautiful naked maidens, but in some areas they were imagined as hideous and hairy.",
   "locator": "Region-specific",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita dari Ukraina rusalka sering dikaitkan dengan air, di Belarus dengan hutan dan ladang; biasanya mereka digambarkan sebagai gadis cantik tanpa busana, tetapi di beberapa daerah sebagai makhluk buruk rupa dan berbulu.",
    "en": "In Ukrainian stories rusalki were often linked with water, in Belarus with forest and field; usually pictured as beautiful naked maidens, in some areas they were hideous and hairy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c10",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "The rusalki were believed to be at their most dangerous during the 'Rusalka week' ... in early June. At this time, they were supposed to have left their watery depths in order to swing on branches of birch and willow trees by night.",
   "locator": "Rusalka week",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Rusalka dipercaya paling berbahaya pada Pekan Rusalka di awal Juni, ketika mereka keluar dari air untuk berayun di dahan pohon birch dan willow pada malam hari.",
    "en": "Rusalki were believed most dangerous during Rusalka Week in early June, when they left the water to swing on birch and willow branches at night."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c11",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "Swimming during this week was strictly forbidden, lest mermaids drag a swimmer down to the river bed.",
   "locator": "Rusalka week",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Selama pekan itu berenang dilarang keras, agar perenang tidak diseret ke dasar sungai.",
    "en": "Swimming that week was strictly forbidden, lest a swimmer be dragged to the river bed."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c12",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "the currently dominant presents her as something like a mermaid, though she is pictured as having legs rather than a fish tail ... In the past, her image was more complex and she more closely resembled a nature spirit, found not only near water but in fields, forests, and mountains, rather like the vila",
   "locator": "Modern depictions of rusalki",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut seorang ahli folklor, gambaran yang kini dominan menyerupai putri duyung tetapi berkaki, bukan berekor ikan; dulu gambarannya lebih rumit dan lebih mirip roh alam yang ditemukan di dekat air, ladang, hutan, dan gunung.",
    "en": "According to a folklorist, the dominant image today is mermaid-like but with legs rather than a fish tail; formerly she was more complex, closer to a nature spirit found by water, in fields, forests and mountains."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c13",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "1901 – Rusalka, an opera by Antonín Dvořák.",
   "locator": "List of notable works featuring rusalki",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada 1901 Antonín Dvořák menggubah opera Rusalka.",
    "en": "In 1901 Antonín Dvořák composed the opera Rusalka."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c14",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s2",
   "quote": "Menurut sebagian besar tradisi, rusalka merupakan perempuan ikan, yang tinggal di dasar sungai. Rusalka berasal dari para perempuan yang meninggal di sungai.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut sebagian besar tradisi, rusalka adalah perempuan ikan yang tinggal di dasar sungai dan berasal dari perempuan yang meninggal di sungai.",
    "en": "In most traditions the rusalka is a fish-woman living on the river bed, arising from women who died in the river."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c15",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s2",
   "quote": "Rusalka tidak bisa tinggal lama di atas permukaan, tetapi sisir mereka bisa mengeluarkan air untuk membasahi rambut mereka, jika rambutnya kering maka rusalka akan mati.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Rusalka tidak bisa lama di atas permukaan air, tetapi sisirnya dapat mengeluarkan air untuk membasahi rambutnya; jika rambutnya kering, rusalka akan mati.",
    "en": "The rusalka cannot stay long above water, but her comb can produce water to wet her hair; if her hair dries, she dies."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "rusalka-c16",
   "source_id": "rusalka-s1",
   "quote": "In most beliefs rusalki always have loose hair ... The hair of the rusalka can be fair, black, greenish or completely green.",
   "locator": "Origin and appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kebanyakan kepercayaan rambut rusalka selalu terurai, dan warnanya bisa pirang, hitam, kehijauan, atau hijau sepenuhnya.",
    "en": "In most beliefs rusalki always have loose hair, which can be fair, black, greenish or completely green."
   }
  }
 ],
```

```json
 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Veles, juga disebut Volos, adalah dewa dunia bawah dalam mitologi Slavia, dikenal dalam sumber lama sebagai dewa ternak.",
  "en": "Veles, also called Volos, is a chthonic god of the underworld in Slavic mythology, known in early sources as the cattle god.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "veles-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Veles atau Volos adalah dewa dunia bawah dalam mitologi Slavia. Sumber sezaman menyebutnya dewa ternak, tetapi berbagai hipotesis memberinya wilayah kuasa yang lebih luas: kekayaan, alam orang mati yang dibayangkan sebagai padang rumput tempat jiwa-jiwa merumput, serta sihir, pengetahuan, ramalan, dan puisi. Sebagai penjaga batas antara dunia orang hidup dan orang mati, ia juga dewa alam liar, perburuan, dan hewan.",
   "en": "Veles or Volos is a chthonic god of the underworld in Slavic mythology. Contemporary sources call him the cattle god, but various hypotheses give him a wider domain: wealth, the realm of the dead imagined as a pasture where souls graze, and magic, knowledge, divination and poetry. Because he watched over the line separating living and dead, he also ruled the wild, the hunt and animals.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "veles-c01",
    "veles-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia menjamin sumpah dan menghukum pelanggarnya dengan membuat mereka \"menguning\". Dalam perjanjian dengan Bizantium, orang Rus bersumpah demi Perun dan demi Volos, dewa ternak, dan rela menjadi kuning seperti emas bila melanggar. Setelah dibaptis, Vladimir memerintahkan berhala Volos dilempar ke Sungai Pochaiv. Satu sumber menambahkan bahwa sesudah Kristenisasi Veles ditafsirkan ulang sebagai Iblis, dan peran pelindung ternaknya diambil alih Santo Blasius.",
   "en": "He guaranteed oaths and punished those who broke them by \"turning them yellow\". In treaties with Byzantium the Rus swore by Perun and by Volos, the cattle god, accepting to become yellow as gold if they failed. After his baptism Vladimir ordered the idol of Volos thrown into the Pochaiv River. One source adds that after Christianization Veles was reinterpreted as the Devil, and Saint Blasius took over his role as protector of cattle.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "veles-c01",
    "veles-c03",
    "veles-c04",
    "veles-c11",
    "veles-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ivanov dan Toporov merekonstruksi mitos tentang permusuhan Perun dan Veles: Veles sebagai ular merayap naik pohon dunia, diserang petir Perun, bersembunyi dengan berubah wujud, lalu dibunuh sehingga apa yang dicurinya jatuh sebagai hujan. Teori ini kemudian ditolak bahkan oleh Toporov sendiri, dan anggapan bahwa Veles adalah naga atau ular tidak lagi menjadi kesepakatan para ahli.",
   "en": "Ivanov and Toporov reconstructed a myth of enmity between Perun and Veles: Veles as a serpent climbs the world tree, is struck by Perun's lightning, hides by changing form, and is finally killed so that what he stole falls as rain. The theory was later rejected even by Toporov himself, and the view of Veles as a dragon or serpent is no longer scholarly consensus.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "veles-c05",
    "veles-c06",
    "veles-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "learning_questions": [
  {
   "id": "Mengapa dewa ternak juga bisa menjadi penguasa dunia orang mati?",

 ],
 "images": [],
 "sources": [
  {
   "id": "veles-s1",
   "url": "https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veles_(god)",
   "title": "Veles (god)",
   "author": null,
   "publisher": "Wikipedia (en)",
   "published": null,

   "type": "wikipedia",

  },
  {
   "id": "veles-s2",
   "url": "https://de.wikipedia.org/wiki/Veles_(Gott)",
   "title": "Veles (Gott)",
   "author": null,
   "publisher": "Wikipedia (de)",
   "published": null,
   "language": "de",
   "type": "wikipedia",
   "accessed": "2026-10-03"
  }
 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "veles-c01",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "Veles, also known as Volos, is a chthonic god of the underworld in Slavic mythology, described in contemporary sources as a cattle god (skotij bogъ), but according to various hypotheses, he had a much broader sphere of influence.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Veles, juga dikenal sebagai Volos, adalah dewa dunia bawah dalam mitologi Slavia; sumber sezaman menyebutnya dewa ternak (skotij bogъ), tetapi menurut berbagai hipotesis wilayah kuasanya jauh lebih luas.",
    "en": "Veles, also known as Volos, is a chthonic underworld god of Slavic mythology, called a cattle god (skotij bogъ) in contemporary sources, though various hypotheses give him a much broader sphere."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c02",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "His function as protector of cattle may be connected to both wealth and rule of the underworld, which was imagined as a pasture where human souls grazed. Through comparative methods, he can be interpreted as a deity of magic, knowledge, divination, and poetry.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Fungsinya sebagai pelindung ternak mungkin berkaitan dengan kekayaan dan kekuasaan atas dunia bawah, yang dibayangkan sebagai padang rumput tempat jiwa manusia merumput; lewat perbandingan, ia dapat ditafsirkan sebagai dewa sihir, pengetahuan, ramalan, dan puisi.",
    "en": "His role as protector of cattle may link to wealth and rule of the underworld, imagined as a pasture where souls grazed; comparatively he can be read as a god of magic, knowledge, divination and poetry."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c03",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "As the guardian of the boundary between the world of the living and the dead, he was also a god of the wilderness, hunting and animals. He was also a guarantor of oaths, punishing their violation by \"turning them yellow\"",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagai penjaga batas antara dunia orang hidup dan orang mati, ia juga dewa alam liar, perburuan, dan hewan; ia menjamin sumpah dan menghukum pelanggarnya dengan \"membuat mereka menguning\".",
    "en": "As guardian of the boundary between the living and the dead, he was also god of the wilderness, hunting and animals; he guaranteed oaths, punishing breakers by \"turning them yellow\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c04",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "He commanded that the idol of Volos, whom they called the cattle god, be thrown into the Pochaiv River",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Riwayat Vladimir, ia memerintahkan agar berhala Volos, yang disebut dewa ternak, dilempar ke Sungai Pochaiv.",
    "en": "According to the Life of Vladimir, he ordered the idol of Volos, called the cattle god, thrown into the Pochaiv River."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c05",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "Veles is sometimes incorrectly identified with a dragon or a serpent. This hypothesis, based on the reconstruction by Ivanov and Toporov and published in 1974",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Veles kadang keliru disamakan dengan naga atau ular; hipotesis ini berasal dari rekonstruksi Ivanov dan Toporov yang terbit pada 1974.",
    "en": "Veles is sometimes wrongly identified with a dragon or serpent, a hypothesis from Ivanov and Toporov's reconstruction published in 1974."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c06",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "They present Veles, in the form of a serpent, who slithers from the caves of the underworld and coils up the Slavic world tree towards Perun's heavenly domain. Perun attacks Veles with his lightning bolts. Veles flees, hiding or transforming himself into trees, animals or people. In the end, he is killed by Perun",
   "locator": "Basic myth theory",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam rekonstruksi itu, Veles berwujud ular merayap dari gua dunia bawah dan melilit naik pohon dunia Slavia menuju wilayah Perun; Perun menyerangnya dengan petir, Veles lari dengan bersembunyi atau berubah menjadi pohon, hewan, atau manusia, dan akhirnya dibunuh Perun.",
    "en": "In that reconstruction Veles as a serpent slithers from underworld caves up the world tree toward Perun's domain; Perun strikes with lightning, Veles flees by hiding or turning into trees, animals or people, and is finally killed by Perun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c07",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "It was later rejected even by its own author Toporov. In terms of Slavic mythology, it conjectures the conflict between the two gods, Perun and Veles.",
   "locator": "Basic myth theory",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Teori ini kemudian ditolak bahkan oleh pencetusnya sendiri, Toporov; dalam mitologi Slavia teori itu menduga adanya konflik antara Perun dan Veles.",
    "en": "The theory was later rejected even by its author Toporov; for Slavic mythology it conjectures a conflict between Perun and Veles."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c08",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "The constellation of the Pleiades is most likely connected with Veles in some Slavic languages, such as Old Russian Volosyni or Bulgarian Vlascite",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gugus bintang Pleiades dalam beberapa bahasa Slavia kemungkinan besar berkaitan dengan Veles, misalnya Volosyni dalam bahasa Rusia Kuno atau Vlascite dalam bahasa Bulgaria.",
    "en": "The Pleiades are most likely connected with Veles in some Slavic languages, e.g. Old Russian Volosyni or Bulgarian Vlascite."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c09",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "His mythological nature and powers are similar, though not entirely identical, to those of Varuna, Velnias, Odin (marginally), Hermes, Afsati and Hades.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sifat dan kekuatan mitologisnya mirip, meski tidak sepenuhnya identik, dengan Varuna, Velnias, Odin (sedikit), Hermes, Afsati, dan Hades.",
    "en": "His mythological nature and powers resemble, though not identically, those of Varuna, Velnias, Odin (marginally), Hermes, Afsati and Hades."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c10",
   "source_id": "veles-s2",
   "quote": "Veles wird als gehörnte Schlange mit einem langen Bart dargestellt.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Veles digambarkan sebagai ular bertanduk dengan janggut panjang.",
    "en": "Veles is depicted as a horned serpent with a long beard."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c11",
   "source_id": "veles-s2",
   "quote": "Nach der Christianisierung wurde Veles zum Teufel umgedeutet, sein Name taucht in dieser Funktion in Sprichwörtern auf. Die Rolle des Viehbeschützers übernahm, vermutlich aufgrund der Namensähnlichkeit, der Heilige Blasius.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah Kristenisasi, Veles ditafsirkan ulang sebagai Iblis (Teufel) dan namanya muncul dalam peribahasa; peran pelindung ternak diambil alih Santo Blasius, mungkin karena kemiripan nama.",
    "en": "After Christianization Veles was reinterpreted as the Devil, appearing so in proverbs; Saint Blasius took over his role as cattle protector, probably due to the similar name."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "veles-c12",
   "source_id": "veles-s1",
   "quote": "may we be cursed by our god in whom we believe, by Perun and by Volos, the cattle god (skotij bogъ), and may we become yellow as gold",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam perjanjian dengan Bizantium, orang Rus bersumpah: semoga kami dikutuk oleh dewa kami, oleh Perun dan oleh Volos, dewa ternak, dan menjadi kuning seperti emas bila melanggar.",
    "en": "In the treaty with Byzantium the Rus swore to be cursed by Perun and by Volos, the cattle god, and become yellow as gold if they broke it."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Svarog adalah dewa Slavia yang dikaitkan dengan api dan pandai besi, disamakan dengan Hephaestus dalam satu-satunya sumber yang menyebutnya.",
  "en": "Svarog is a Slavic god linked with fire and blacksmithing, equated with Hephaestus in the only source that names him.",
  "claim_ids": [

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Svarog adalah dewa Slavia yang mungkin berhubungan dengan api dan pekerjaan pandai besi. Ia hanya disebut dalam satu sumber, yaitu Primary Chronicle, yang sulit ditafsirkan; di sana ia muncul sebagai padanan Slavia bagi dewa Yunani Hephaestus dan sebagai ayah Dazhbog serta Svarozhits. Dulu ia ditafsirkan sebagai dewa langit berdasarkan etimologi yang kini ditolak para ahli, dan sebagian peneliti bahkan menduga ia tidak pernah ada dalam kepercayaan Slavia.",
   "en": "Svarog is a Slavic god perhaps connected with fire and smithing. Only one source, the Primary Chronicle, names him, and it is hard to interpret; there he appears as the Slavic match for the Greek god Hephaestus and as father of Dazhbog and Svarozhits. He was once read as a sky god on the strength of an etymology modern scholars reject, and some even suggest he never existed in Slavic belief.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svarog-c01"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sumber itu adalah terjemahan Slavia Kronik John Malalas, yang dalam glosnya menerjemahkan Hephaestus sebagai Svarog dan putranya, dewa matahari Helios, sebagai Dazhbog. Menurut kisah itu, pada masa pemerintahannya sebuah penjepit jatuh dari langit dan ia mulai menempa senjata, lalu ia menetapkan hukum bahwa seorang lelaki hanya boleh beristri satu.",
   "en": "That source is the Slavic translation of the Chronicle of John Malalas, whose glosses render Hephaestus as Svarog and his son, the sun god Helios, as Dazhbog. In that account, tongs fell from the sky during his reign and he began forging weapons, and he then decreed that a man should have only one wife.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svarog-c04",
    "svarog-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya dikaitkan dengan api: menurut satu penjelasan berasal dari bentuk Proto-Slavia *sъvarъ, gabungan awalan \"baik, milik sendiri\" dan akar \"api, panas\", yang juga menurunkan kata-kata tentang menempa. Setelah Kristenisasi, namanya bertahan dalam nama tempat dan kosakata, misalnya kata dialek svarog di Novgorod yang berarti \"api\" dan \"pandai besi\". Aleksander Gieysztor menafsirkan Svarog sebagai api langit (matahari), Perun sebagai api di udara (petir), dan Svarozhits-Dazhbog sebagai api bumi.",
   "en": "His name is tied to fire: one explanation derives it from Proto-Slavic *sъvarъ, joining a prefix meaning \"good, own\" to a stem meaning \"fire, heat\", the same stem behind words for forging. After Christianization his name survived in place names and vocabulary, such as the Novgorod dialect word svarog, \"fire\" and \"blacksmith\". Aleksander Gieysztor read Svarog as celestial fire (the sun), Perun as atmospheric fire (lightning) and Svarozhits-Dazhbog as earthly fire.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svarog-c02",

    "svarog-c06",
    "svarog-c07",
    "svarog-c11"
   ]

   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "In this translation, in glosses, the Greek god of fire and smithing Hephaestus is translated as Svarog, and his son, the sun god Helios, is translated as Dazhbog",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam terjemahan Slavia Kronik John Malalas, glos menerjemahkan Hephaestus, dewa api dan pandai besi Yunani, sebagai Svarog, dan putranya, dewa matahari Helios, sebagai Dazhbog.",
    "en": "In the Slavic translation of the Chronicle of John Malalas, glosses render Hephaestus, Greek god of fire and smithing, as Svarog, and his son the sun god Helios as Dazhbog."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c05",
   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "During the reign of this Hephaestus in Egypt, at the time of his reign, tongs fell from the sky and he began to forge weapons, as before that they beat each other with sticks and stones.",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada masa pemerintahan Hephaestus (Svarog) di Mesir, sebuah penjepit jatuh dari langit dan ia mulai menempa senjata, karena sebelumnya orang saling memukul dengan tongkat dan batu.",
    "en": "During the reign of this Hephaestus (Svarog) in Egypt, tongs fell from the sky and he began to forge weapons, since before then people beat each other with sticks and stones."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c06",
   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "After Christianization, Svarog was preserved in toponymy and vocabulary.",
   "locator": "Legacy",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah Kristenisasi, nama Svarog bertahan dalam nama tempat dan kosakata.",
    "en": "After Christianization Svarog was preserved in toponymy and vocabulary."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c07",
   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "In the Russian dialect (Novgorod) the obsolete word сва́рог, svarog meaning \"fire\" and \"blacksmith\", is preserved.",
   "locator": "Legacy",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam dialek Rusia Novgorod masih tersimpan kata usang svarog yang berarti \"api\" dan \"pandai besi\".",
    "en": "In the Russian dialect of Novgorod the obsolete word svarog, meaning \"fire\" and \"blacksmith\", survives."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c08",
   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "According to the glosses Svarog is: (1) the Slavic equivalent of Hephaestus, the Greek god of fire and smithing, (2) the father of Dazhbog, and (3) the creator of monogamy.",
   "locator": "Sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut glos tersebut, Svarog adalah padanan Slavia Hephaestus, ayah Dazhbog, dan pencipta aturan perkawinan monogami.",
    "en": "According to the glosses Svarog is the Slavic equivalent of Hephaestus, father of Dazhbog, and creator of monogamy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c09",
   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "Some scholars have suggested that Svarozhits means 'young Svarog' or is a diminutive of Svarog. ... However, most scholars disagree with this interpretation. The suffix -its, -ich (from Proto-Slavic *-iťь) is most often a patronymic suffix",
   "locator": "Svarog-Svarozhits",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian ahli menduga Svarozhits berarti \"Svarog muda\" atau bentuk kecil dari Svarog, tetapi kebanyakan ahli tidak setuju karena akhiran -its, -ich biasanya akhiran patronimik.",
    "en": "Some scholars suggest Svarozhits means \"young Svarog\" or is a diminutive of Svarog, but most disagree, as the suffix -its, -ich is usually patronymic."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c10",
   "source_id": "svarog-s2",
   "quote": "スヴァローグ（ロシア語: Сварог、 古東スラヴ語: Сварогъ、 英語: Svarog）とはスラヴ神話における太陽神であり、火の精霊または神でもある。",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Svarog (bahasa Rusia: Сварог) adalah dewa matahari dalam mitologi Slavia, sekaligus roh atau dewa api.",
    "en": "Svarog (Russian: Сварог) is a sun god in Slavic mythology, and also a fire spirit or god."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svarog-c11",
   "source_id": "svarog-s1",
   "quote": "Aleksander Gieysztor interpreted Svarog as celestial fire (the sun), Perun as atmospheric fire (the thunderbolt), and Svarozhits-Dazhbog as earthly fire (fire).",
   "locator": "Interpretations",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Aleksander Gieysztor menafsirkan Svarog sebagai api langit (matahari), Perun sebagai api di udara (petir), dan Svarozhits-Dazhbog sebagai api bumi.",
    "en": "Aleksander Gieysztor interpreted Svarog as celestial fire (the sun), Perun as atmospheric fire (the thunderbolt) and Svarozhits-Dazhbog as earthly fire."
   }
  },
  {
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Vodyanoy adalah roh air dalam mitologi Slavia, biasanya lelaki tua berjanggut hijau yang bisa menenggelamkan orang, tetapi juga dapat memberi tangkapan ikan berlimpah.",
  "en": "The vodyanoy is a water spirit of Slavic mythology, usually an old man with a green beard who may drown people but can also grant rich catches of fish.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "vodyanoy-c01",
   "vodyanoy-c08",
   "vodyanoy-c13"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, vodyanoy (secara harfiah \"yang dari air\") adalah roh air. Dalam dongeng Ceko dan Slowakia ia disebut vodník, dan dalam dongeng Ukraina vodyanyk; di Rusia ia kadang dijuluki dedushka vodyanoy, \"Kakek Air\". Ia dikatakan tinggal di lubuk atau pusaran sungai, kolam, atau danau, dan terutama suka hidup di dekat kincir air.",
   "en": "In Slavic mythology the vodyanoy (literally \"he from the water\") is a water spirit. Czech and Slovak fairy tales call him vodník, Ukrainian ones vodyanyk; in Russia he is sometimes called dedushka vodyanoy, \"Water-Grandfather\". He is said to live in a deep pool or whirlpool of a river, pond or lake, and to favour spots near a watermill.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vodyanoy-c01",
    "vodyanoy-c04",
    "vodyanoy-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Rupanya digambarkan berbeda-beda: lelaki telanjang berperut buncit dan berkepala botak bertopi dan bersabuk alang-alang, lelaki tua berambut dan berjanggut hijau, atau katak yang menyerupai manusia; perbedaan ini dijelaskan oleh kemampuannya berubah wujud. Janggut hijaunya memutih saat bulan menyusut, karena ia yang abadi menua atau menjadi muda lagi mengikuti fase bulan. Ia dapat muncul sebagai ikan raksasa berlumut, sebatang kayu, bahkan batang pohon bersayap kecil yang terbang di atas air.",
   "en": "Accounts of his looks differ: a naked, pot-bellied, bald man with a hat and belt of reeds, an old man with green hair and beard, or a man-like frog; the variety is put down to his shapeshifting. His green beard whitens as the moon wanes, since the immortal vodyanoy ages or grows young with the moon's phases. He may appear as a huge mossy fish, a log, or even a small-winged flying tree trunk skimming the water.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vodyanoy-c02",
    "vodyanoy-c06",
    "vodyanoy-c13",
    "vodyanoy-c14"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Saat marah ia menjebol bendungan, menghanyutkan kincir air, dan menenggelamkan manusia serta hewan, sehingga nelayan, penggiling, dan peternak lebah memberinya persembahan; kadang ia menyeret orang ke rumahnya di bawah air untuk dijadikan budak. Ketika kincir air dibangun, harus dikorbankan babi, sapi, domba, bahkan manusia atau ayam untuk menenangkannya. Sebaliknya, nelayan bisa mendapat tangkapan berlimpah darinya, misalnya setelah mengembalikan seorang anak yang tak sengaja terjaring.",
   "en": "When angry he breaks dams, washes away mills and drowns people and animals, so fishermen, millers and bee-keepers offer him sacrifices; at times he drags people to his underwater home as slaves. When a watermill was built, a pig, cattle, a sheep, even a human or a chicken had to be sacrificed to calm him. Fishermen, on the other hand, could receive bountiful catches from him, for instance after returning a child caught by accident in their nets.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vodyanoy-c03",
    "vodyanoy-c07",
    "vodyanoy-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Vodyanoy menguasai semua ikan dan makhluk air, dan ia memilih ikan lele besar sebagai tunggangan. Ia mengambil istri dari peri air atau gadis-gadis malang yang tenggelam, termasuk rusalka menurut Afanasyev. Ada pula penguasa para vodyanoy, Tsar Vodyanik, lelaki tua bersenjata gada yang bisa naik ke langit di atas awan hitam dan menciptakan sungai serta danau baru.",
   "en": "The vodyanoy owns all fish and water creatures and chooses the wels catfish as his mount.    "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia bisa tampil sebagai lelaki telanjang berperut buncit dan botak bertopi serta bersabuk alang-alang, sementara kisah lain memberinya rambut hijau dan janggut hijau panjang, dan banyak penggambaran menjadikannya katak berwujud manusia; perbedaan ini dikaitkan dengan kemampuannya berubah wujud.",

  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c03",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "When angered, the vodyanoy breaks dams, washes down water mills, and drowns people and animals. Consequently, fishermen, millers, and also bee-keepers make sacrifices to appease him. The vodyanoy would sometimes drag people down to his underwater dwelling to serve him as slaves.",

   "context": "traditional-belief",

    "en": "When angered the vodyanoy breaks dams, washes away mills and drowns people and animals, so fishermen, millers and bee-keepers sacrifice to appease him; he sometimes drags people to his underwater dwelling as slaves."

  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c04",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "In Russia, the vodyanoy is sometimes called the dedushka vodyanoy (Дѣдушка-водяной , \"Water-Grandfather\") or vodyanik (водяник ).",
   "locator": "Russia",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Rusia vodyanoy kadang disebut dedushka vodyanoy (\"Kakek Air\") atau vodyanik.",
    "en": "In Russia the vodyanoy is sometimes called dedushka vodyanoy (\"Water-Grandfather\") or vodyanik."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c05",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "He is said to dwell in a slough (омут ), kettle hole (Котловина ), or a whirlpool of a river, pond or lake, and liked especially to live near a watermill.",
   "locator": "Habitat",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dikatakan tinggal di lubuk, cekungan, atau pusaran sungai, kolam, atau danau, dan terutama suka tinggal di dekat kincir air.",
    "en": "He is said to dwell in a slough, kettle hole or whirlpool of a river, pond or lake, and especially liked to live near a watermill."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c06",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "He has the capability of shape-shifting, which has been suggested as an explanation of its varied descriptions.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia mampu berubah wujud, dan hal ini diajukan sebagai penjelasan atas gambaran rupanya yang beragam.",
    "en": "He can shape-shift, which has been suggested to explain his varied descriptions."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c07",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "When a watermill is built, a sacrifice of pig, cattle, sheep, or even human (or a chicken) must be made to appease the vodyanik.",
   "locator": "Offering and boon",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ketika kincir air dibangun, harus dipersembahkan babi, sapi, domba, bahkan manusia (atau ayam) untuk menenangkan vodyanik.",
    "en": "When a watermill is built, a pig, cattle, sheep or even a human (or a chicken) must be sacrificed to appease the vodyanik."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c08",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "The fisherman can also benefit from the boon of the vodyanoy, receiving a bountiful harvest in their fishing nets. He may receive this reward after returning a child which was accidentally netted.",
   "locator": "Offering and boon",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nelayan juga bisa mendapat anugerah vodyanoy berupa tangkapan berlimpah di jaringnya, misalnya setelah mengembalikan anak yang tak sengaja terjaring.",
    "en": "A fisherman can also gain the vodyanoy's boon of a bountiful catch, for example after returning a child accidentally netted."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c09",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "The vodyanik \"owns\" all the fish and aquatic creatures, and his control over them explains his ability to deliver fish. The vodyanik selects in particular the sheatfish (сом ; Silurus glanis, aka \"wels catfish\") as his mount to ride on.",
   "locator": "Mount",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vodyanik \"memiliki\" semua ikan dan makhluk air, dan kuasa itu menjelaskan kemampuannya memberi ikan; ia terutama memilih ikan lele besar (wels catfish) sebagai tunggangan.",
    "en": "The vodyanik \"owns\" all fish and aquatic creatures, which explains his ability to deliver fish; he especially chooses the wels catfish as his mount."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c10",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "He is known to take on a wife (or wives), and espouses \"water-nymphs or drowned and unhappy girls who have been cursed by their fathers or mothers\". According to Afanasyev, the \"water-nymph\" (\"water-maiden\") is known by various names in Russia, including the rusalka.",
   "locator": "Family",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia beristri satu atau lebih, yaitu peri air atau gadis malang yang tenggelam dan dikutuk ayah atau ibunya; menurut Afanasyev, peri air itu dikenal di Rusia dengan berbagai nama, termasuk rusalka.",
    "en": "He takes a wife or wives: water-nymphs or drowned, unhappy girls cursed by their parents; according to Afanasyev the water-nymph is known in Russia by various names, including rusalka."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c11",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "It is believed that vodyanoys have a ruler: the Tsar Vodyanik, or the Vodyan Tsar. He is described as an old man armed with a club, who can rise to the sky sitting on a black cloud and create new rivers and lakes.",
   "locator": "Family",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Para vodyanoy dipercaya punya penguasa, Tsar Vodyanik, lelaki tua bersenjata gada yang dapat naik ke langit di atas awan hitam dan menciptakan sungai serta danau baru.",
    "en": "Vodyanoys are believed to have a ruler, the Tsar Vodyanik, an old man with a club who can rise to the sky on a black cloud and create new rivers and lakes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c12",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "The farmer fording his livestock will make a sign of cross (emblem of Perun's weapon) over the river as protection from this happening.",
   "locator": "Mount",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Petani yang menyeberangkan ternaknya membuat tanda salib di atas sungai sebagai perlindungan agar ternaknya tidak ditunggangi vodyanik.",
    "en": "A farmer fording his livestock makes a sign of the cross over the river as protection against this."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c13",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "He is also described as an old man with green hair and (long) green beard The green beard turns white with when the moon wanes, as the immortal Vodyanoy ages or rejuvenates with the phases of the moon.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia juga digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua berambut dan berjanggut hijau panjang; janggutnya memutih saat bulan menyusut, karena vodyanoy yang abadi menua atau menjadi muda lagi mengikuti fase bulan.",
    "en": "He is also described as an old man with green hair and a long green beard, which whitens as the moon wanes, as the immortal vodyanoy ages or rejuvenates with the moon's phases."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c14",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "He can appear as a giant moss-covered fish, a log or even a flying tree-trunk with small-wings, skimming over the water's surface.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dapat muncul sebagai ikan raksasa berlumut, sebatang kayu, bahkan batang pohon terbang bersayap kecil yang meluncur di atas permukaan air.",
    "en": "He can appear as a giant moss-covered fish, a log or even a small-winged flying tree trunk skimming the water."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c15",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s1",
   "quote": "The Czech and Slovak vodníci (plural of vodník) can also take on an appearance of ordinary humans, but often with water dripping from their clothing, which makes their false identity easily discernable.",
   "locator": "Other folklores",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vodník Ceko dan Slowakia juga bisa tampil sebagai manusia biasa, tetapi pakaiannya sering meneteskan air sehingga penyamarannya mudah dikenali.",
    "en": "Czech and Slovak vodníci can also look like ordinary humans, but water often drips from their clothes, making the disguise easy to spot."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vodyanoy-c16",
   "source_id": "vodyanoy-s2",
   "quote": "Vodyanoy dipercaya sebagai penyebab orang tenggelam.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vodyanoy dipercaya menjadi penyebab orang tenggelam.",
    "en": "The vodyanoy was believed to cause people to drown."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],
 "gaps": [
  {
   "field": "images",
   "searched": "Tidak ada gambar Commons yang dibuktikan dalam sesi ini."
  }
 ]
}
```

```json
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Perkūnas adalah dewa guruh yang dipuja bangsa-bangsa Baltik, dewa terpenting kedua sesudah Dievas, yang memerintah langit, petir, badai, dan hujan.",
  "en": "Perkūnas is the thunder god honoured across the Baltic peoples, ranking just below Dievas and ruling sky, lightning, storm and rain.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "perkunas-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Perkūnas (bahasa Latvia: Pērkons) adalah dewa guruh yang dipuja bersama oleh bangsa-bangsa Baltik, kedua terpenting setelah Dievas. Dalam mitologi Lituania dan Latvia ia tercatat sebagai dewa langit, guruh, petir, badai, hujan, api, perang, hukum, ketertiban, kesuburan, gunung, dan pohon ek. Sebagian besar keterangan tentangnya berasal dari lagu rakyat, legenda, dan dongeng yang baru dikumpulkan pada abad ke-19.",
   "en": "Perkūnas (Latvian: Pērkons) was a thunder god honoured by all the Baltic peoples, outranked only by Dievas. Lithuanian and Latvian tradition make him master of the heavens and of thunder, lightning, storm and rain, and also tie him to fire, war, law and order, fertility, mountains and the oak. Most of our knowledge comes from songs, legends and tales that collectors only gathered in the 1800s.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perkunas-c01",
    "perkunas-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya muncul dalam banyak sumber tentang agama dan mitologi Lituania. Satu sumber menggambarkannya sebagai lelaki paruh baya bersenjata kapak dan anak panah yang mengendarai kereta beroda dua ditarik kambing, seperti Thor atau Taranis; sumber lain menyebutnya lelaki tua yang masih kuat, tinggi, dan berjanggut merah. Senjatanya antara lain kapak atau palu godam, batu, pedang, petir, busur dan panah, gada, serta pisau.",
   "en": "His name turns up in many sources on Lithuanian religion and myth. One source pictures him in middle age with axe and arrows, riding a goat-drawn two-wheeled chariot like Thor or Taranis; another calls him an elderly yet strong, tall, red-bearded man. His arsenal includes an axe or sledgehammer, stones, a sword, lightning, bow and arrows, a club and a knife.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perkunas-c04",
    "perkunas-c05",
    "perkunas-c07",
    "perkunas-c22"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat ia tinggal di antara langit dan bumi, di awan, dan mengendarai kereta beroda dua yang ditarik kuda atau kambing; derak rodanya menimbulkan guruh. Ia bertarung melawan iblis (velnias) dan kekuatan jahat; dalam beberapa kisah iblis mencuri batu atau kapaknya, yang oleh sebagian peneliti ditafsirkan sebagai pertarungan antara langit dan dunia bawah. Kamis dianggap hari Perkūnas, dan sebagian catatan menyebut Kamis seharusnya menjadi hari pernikahannya, yang batal karena iblis mencuri pengantinnya. Kadang pula disebut ada tujuh atau sembilan Perkūnai bersaudara.",
   "en": "Folk tales place his home between heaven and earth, among the clouds, and give him a two-wheeled cart pulled by horses or goats whose rumbling wheels make the thunder. He battles the devil (velnias) and evil forces; in some tales the devil steals his stone or axe, which some researchers read as a struggle between sky and underworld. Thursday was his day, and some records explain it as his planned wedding day, cancelled when the devil carried off his bride. Sometimes seven or nine Perkūnai are said to be brothers.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "perkunas-c09",

    "perkunas-c12",

    "perkunas-c18",
    "perkunas-c20"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Latvia ia disebut Bapa Perkūnas dan dikaitkan dengan Matahari sebagai istrinya. Dalam trias dewa Prusia ia melambangkan kesuburan, kejantanan, keberhasilan, dan unsur-unsur kosmos seperti langit, hujan, guruh, dan petir. Asal namanya tidak jelas: ada yang mengaitkannya dengan akar *per- \"memukul\" atau *perkʷ- \"pohon ek\", ada pula yang menganggapnya pinjaman dari bahasa Belarus Pertengahan.",
   "en": "In Latvian myth he is called Father Perkūnas and linked with the Sun as his wife. In the Prussian triad of gods he stands for fertility, manliness, success and cosmic elements such as sky, rain, thunder and lightning.
```
