 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c01",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "was the common Baltic god of thunder, and the second most important deity in the Baltic pantheon after Dievas. In both Lithuanian and Latvian mythology, he is documented as the god of sky, thunder, lightning, storms, rain, fire, war, law, order, fertility, mountains, and oak trees.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas (Perkūns, Parkuns) adalah dewa guruh bersama bangsa Baltik dan dewa terpenting kedua setelah Dievas; dalam mitologi Lituania dan Latvia ia tercatat sebagai dewa langit, guruh, petir, badai, hujan, api, perang, hukum, ketertiban, kesuburan, gunung, dan pohon ek.",
    "en": "Perkūnas was the common Baltic thunder god and second most important deity after Dievas; in Lithuanian and Latvian myth he is documented as god of sky, thunder, lightning, storms, rain, fire, war, law, order, fertility, mountains and oak trees."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c02",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "This theonym is said to derive from the root *per- \"to beat\" or from root *perkʷ- \"oak\", and the theonym itself was later given various extensions in different languages. Therefore, the theonym can be literally translated as \"the Striker\" or \"the Lord of Oaks\".",
   "locator": "Proto-Indo-European god *Perkʷúh₃nos",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama dewa ini dikatakan berasal dari akar *per- \"memukul\" atau *perkʷ- \"pohon ek\", sehingga dapat diterjemahkan secara harfiah \"Sang Pemukul\" atau \"Penguasa Pohon Ek\".",
    "en": "The theonym is said to come from the root *per- \"to beat\" or *perkʷ- \"oak\", so it can be translated literally as \"the Striker\" or \"the Lord of Oaks\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c03",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "Most information about Perkūnas comes from folklore songs, legends, and fairy tales. Because most of them were collected rather late in the 19th century ... Lithuanian Perkūnas has many alternative onomatopoeic names, like Dundulis",
   "locator": "Perkūnas in written sources",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian besar keterangan tentang Perkūnas berasal dari lagu rakyat, legenda, dan dongeng yang kebanyakan baru dikumpulkan pada abad ke-19; Perkūnas Lituania punya banyak nama tiruan bunyi, misalnya Dundulis.",
    "en": "Most information on Perkūnas comes from folk songs, legends and tales mostly collected late, in the 19th century; Lithuanian Perkūnas has many onomatopoeic names, such as Dundulis."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c04",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Perkūnas minimas daugelyje lietuvių religijos ir mitologijos šaltinių.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas disebut dalam banyak sumber tentang agama dan mitologi Lituania.",
    "en": "Perkūnas is mentioned in many sources on Lithuanian religion and mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c05",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "Perkūnas is pictured as middle-aged, armed with an axe and arrows, riding a two-wheeled chariot harnessed with goats, like Thor or Celtic Taranis.",
   "locator": "Weapons and vehicle",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas digambarkan sebagai lelaki paruh baya bersenjata kapak dan anak panah, mengendarai kereta beroda dua yang ditarik kambing, seperti Thor atau Taranis dari Kelt.",
    "en": "Perkūnas is pictured as middle-aged, armed with an axe and arrows, riding a two-wheeled goat-drawn chariot, like Thor or the Celtic Taranis."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c06",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Prūsų dievų trejete Perkūnas simbolizuoja aukščiausią derlingų (ir vegetatyvinių) jėgų pakilimą, vyriškumą, sėkmę, kosmologinius elementus – dangų, lietų, griaustinį, žaibą.",
   "locator": "Prūsų",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam trias dewa Prusia, Perkūnas melambangkan puncak kekuatan kesuburan (termasuk tumbuhan), kejantanan, keberhasilan, dan unsur kosmos: langit, hujan, guruh, dan petir.",
    "en": "In the Prussian triad of gods Perkūnas symbolizes the height of fertile (and vegetative) forces, manliness, success and cosmic elements: sky, rain, thunder and lightning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c07",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "They include an axe or sledgehammer, stones, a sword, lightning bolts, a bow and arrows, a club, and an iron or fiery knife.",
   "locator": "Perkūnas and Velnias",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Senjata Perkūnas antara lain kapak atau palu godam, batu, pedang, petir, busur dan panah, gada, serta pisau besi atau berapi.",
    "en": "His weapons include an axe or sledgehammer, stones, a sword, lightning bolts, a bow and arrows, a club and an iron or fiery knife."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c08",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Kovoja su velniu, piktosiomis galiomis. Tautosakos kūriniuose dažniausiai pasakojama, kad Perkūnas gyvena tarp dangaus ir žemės, debesyse, valdo griaustinį ir žaibus.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas bertarung melawan iblis (velnias) dan kekuatan jahat; dalam cerita rakyat ia paling sering dikisahkan tinggal di antara langit dan bumi, di awan, dan memerintah guruh dan petir.",
    "en": "He fights the devil and evil forces; folklore most often says Perkūnas lives between heaven and earth, in the clouds, ruling thunder and lightning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c09",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Turi dvirates vežėčias, pakinkytas žirgais arba ožiais, jomis važinėjasi dangumi taip, kad jo ratų (dažniausiai geležinių) dardėjimas sukelia griaustinį.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia punya kereta beroda dua yang ditarik kuda atau kambing, dan saat ia melaju di langit derak rodanya (biasanya dari besi) menimbulkan guruh.",
    "en": "He has a two-wheeled cart drawn by horses or goats, and as he drives across the sky the rumble of its (usually iron) wheels causes thunder."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c10",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Tautosakos kūriniuose dažniausiai pasakojama, kad Perkūnas gyvena tarp dangaus ir žemės, debesyse, valdo griaustinį ir žaibus.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat paling sering dikisahkan bahwa Perkūnas tinggal di antara langit dan bumi, di awan, dan memerintah guruh dan petir.",
    "en": "Folk works most often tell that Perkūnas lives between heaven and earth, in the clouds, and rules thunder and lightning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c11",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "Perkūnas is also connected to Thursday. Thursday is the day of the Thunderer in many traditions ... Perkūnas is associated with the Roman god Jupiter in early sources.",
   "locator": "Perkūnas and Velnias",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas juga dikaitkan dengan hari Kamis (Thursday), hari Sang Penguruh dalam banyak tradisi, dan dalam sumber awal ia disamakan dengan dewa Romawi Jupiter.",
    "en": "Perkūnas is also connected to Thursday, the Thunderer's day in many traditions, and early sources associate him with the Roman god Jupiter."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c12",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Perkūno diena laikytas ketvirtadienis, tą dieną buvo draudžiama dirbti kai kuriuos žemės ūkio darbus",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kamis dianggap hari Perkūnas, dan pada hari itu sebagian pekerjaan pertanian dilarang.",
    "en": "Thursday was held to be Perkūnas's day, when some farm work was forbidden."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c13",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s2",
   "quote": "adalah dewa petir Baltik dan merupakan salah satu dewa paling penting dalam mitologi Baltik. Ia berperan sebagai dewa guntur, hujan, pegunungan, pohon ek dan langit dalam mitologi Lituania dan Latvia.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas (Pērkons, Parkuns) adalah dewa petir Baltik, salah satu dewa terpenting dalam mitologi Baltik, yang berperan sebagai dewa guntur, hujan, pegunungan, pohon ek, dan langit dalam mitologi Lituania dan Latvia.",
    "en": "Perkūnas is the Baltic god of lightning, one of the most important Baltic gods, serving as god of thunder, rain, mountains, oak trees and the sky in Lithuanian and Latvian mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c14",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Latvių mitologijoje Perkūnas vadinamas Tėvu Perkūnu, Senuoju tėvu, Dievuliu. Jam priskiriama audros, darganų, lietaus sukėlimo funkcija. Tautosakoje siejamas su Saule, Perkūno mote",
   "locator": "Latvių",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Latvia, Perkūnas disebut Bapa Perkūnas (Tėvas Perkūnas), Bapa Tua, dan Dievulis; ia bertugas mendatangkan badai, cuaca buruk, dan hujan, dan dalam cerita rakyat dikaitkan dengan Matahari (Saulė) sebagai istrinya.",
    "en": "In Latvian mythology Perkūnas is called Father Perkūnas, Old Father and Dievulis; he brings storms, foul weather and rain, and folklore links him with the Sun, his wife."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c15",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "Smoczyński suggests that the basic form is the dialectal (eastern) word perū́nas \"lightning\", which is borrowed from Middle Belarusian",
   "locator": "Slavic borrowing",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Smoczyński mengusulkan bahwa bentuk dasarnya adalah kata dialek timur perū́nas, \"petir\", yang dipinjam dari bahasa Belarus Pertengahan.",
    "en": "Smoczyński suggests the base form is the eastern dialect word perū́nas, \"lightning\", borrowed from Middle Belarusian."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c16",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "Sometimes there are seven or nine Perkūnai referred to as brothers. It is said in Lithuanian \"Perkūnų yra daug\" (\"there are many thunders\").",
   "locator": "Perkūnas' family relations",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kadang disebut tujuh atau sembilan Perkūnai bersaudara; dalam bahasa Lituania dikatakan \"Perkūnų yra daug\" (\"ada banyak guruh\").",
    "en": "Sometimes seven or nine Perkūnai are called brothers; Lithuanian says \"Perkūnų yra daug\" (\"there are many thunders\")."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c17",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Kovoja su velniu, piktosiomis galiomis.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perkūnas bertarung melawan iblis (velnias) dan kekuatan-kekuatan jahat.",
    "en": "Perkūnas fights the devil and evil powers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c18",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Yra siužetų, kuriuose velnias pavagia iš Perkūno akmenį arba kirvį. Jie kai kurių tyrinėtojų aiškinami kaip mitinė dangaus ir chtoniškojo pasaulio kova",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada kisah ketika iblis (velnias) mencuri batu atau kapak Perkūnas; sebagian peneliti menafsirkannya sebagai pertarungan mitis antara langit dan dunia bawah.",
    "en": "In some plots the devil steals Perkūnas's stone or axe; some researchers read this as a mythic struggle between the sky and the chthonic world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c19",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Vardo etimologija nėra aiški, siejamas su lotynų kalbos žodžiu quercus – ąžuolas; kildinamas iš lietuvių kalbos žodžio perti – mušti, smogti.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Etimologi namanya tidak jelas; dikaitkan dengan kata Latin quercus, \"pohon ek\", atau diturunkan dari kata Lituania perti, \"memukul, menghantam\".",
    "en": "The etymology of the name is unclear; it is linked to Latin quercus, \"oak\", or derived from Lithuanian perti, \"to beat, to strike\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c20",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Kai kuriuose tautosakos užrašymuose ketvirtadienis aiškinamas ir kaip vestuvių diena, kurios neįvyko velniui pavogus Perkūno nuotaką.",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam beberapa catatan cerita rakyat, Kamis dijelaskan sebagai hari pernikahan Perkūnas yang batal karena iblis mencuri pengantinnya. Kadang pula disebut ada tujuh atau sembilan Perkūnai bersaudara.",
    "en": "In some folklore records Thursday is explained as Perkūnas's wedding day, which never happened because the devil stole his bride."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c21",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s1",
   "quote": "The main linguistic evidence is said to be the Baltic god of thunder Perkun , the Slavic god of thunder Perun",
   "locator": "Proto-Indo-European god *Perkʷúh₃nos",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bukti linguistik utama untuk dewa guruh rekonstruksi itu dikatakan antara lain dewa guruh Baltik Perkun dan dewa guruh Slavia Perun.",
    "en": "The main linguistic evidence for the reconstructed thunder god is said to include the Baltic thunder god Perkun and the Slavic thunder god Perun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c22",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Tautosakoje vaizduojamas kaip senyvas, bet dar stiprus, aukštas rudabarzdis vyras",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua yang masih kuat, tinggi, dan berjanggut merah.",
    "en": "In folklore he is depicted as an elderly but still strong, tall, red-bearded man."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "perkunas-c23",
   "source_id": "perkunas-s3",
   "quote": "Manoma, egzistavo Perkūno vardo tabu. Vengiant tarti jo vardą, jis būdavo vadinamas garsažodinės kilmės vardo pakaitalais: Brazdeikiu, Dunduliu",
   "locator": "entry",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Diduga ada tabu menyebut nama Perkūnas; untuk menghindarinya, ia disebut dengan nama pengganti tiruan bunyi seperti Brazdeikis dan Dundulis.",
    "en": "A taboo on Perkūnas's name is thought to have existed; to avoid saying it, people used onomatopoeic substitutes such as Brazdeikis and Dundulis."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Alkonost adalah burung legendaris berkepala perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, pasangan sekaligus kebalikan dari burung Sirin, dan melambangkan kebahagiaan.",
  "en": "The Alkonost is a legendary woman-headed bird of Russian folklore, the companion and opposite of the Sirin bird, symbolising happiness.",
  "claim_ids": [

  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, Alkonost adalah burung berkepala perempuan yang selalu dipasangkan dengan Sirin. Alkonost mewakili hal-hal baik seperti kebahagiaan dan surga, sedangkan Sirin mewakili bencana dan kemalangan; meski begitu, keduanya dikaitkan dengan meramalkan atau mendatangkan kematian. Alkonost dikatakan tinggal di Rai, tempat orang mati, dan menurut sumber lain berdiam di sungai surgawi Efrat. Sebuah sumber menyebut makhluk ini bersikap baik kepada manusia, berbeda dengan Sirin.",
   "en": "In Russian folklore the Alkonost is a woman-headed bird always paired with the Sirin. The Alkonost stands for good things such as happiness and paradise, while the Sirin stands for disaster and misfortune; yet both are linked with foretelling or bringing death. The Alkonost is said to live in Rai, where the dead dwell, and another source places it on the paradise river Euphrates. One source says it is well disposed towards people, unlike the Sirin.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "alkonost-c01",
    "alkonost-c03",
    "alkonost-c04",
    "alkonost-c14",
    "alkonost-c18"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Nyanyiannya begitu indah sehingga siapa pun yang mendengarnya melupakan segala hal. Alkonost bertelur di pantai lalu menggulirkan telurnya ke laut; selama telur itu berada di air, laut tenang, tetapi saat menetas timbul badai dahsyat. Menurut cerita rakyat, pada pesta apel Juru Selamat Sirin menangis di kebun apel pada pagi hari, lalu Alkonost datang sore harinya dengan gembira dan mengibaskan embun dari sayapnya sehingga buah di pohon tempatnya bertengger mendapat daya penyembuh.",
   "en": "Its song is so beautiful that anyone who hears it forgets everything. The Alkonost lays eggs on the shore and rolls them into the sea; while the eggs are in the water the sea is calm, but their hatching unleashes violent storms. According to folk tales, on the Apple Feast of the Saviour the Sirin weeps in the apple orchard in the morning, and in the afternoon the Alkonost arrives rejoicing and shakes dew from its wings, giving healing power to the fruit of the tree it sits on.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "alkonost-c05",
    "alkonost-c06",
    "alkonost-c08",
    "alkonost-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Nama Alkonost berasal dari salah baca teks gereja Slavia: frasa yang berarti \"alkuon adalah burung laut\" terbaca sebagai satu nama. Kata Yunani yang mendasarinya berarti burung raja-udang, dan sosok ini dikaitkan dengan mitos Yunani tentang Alkione yang diubah para dewa menjadi raja-udang; sumber lain menduga asalnya dari Xrafstar dalam cerita rakyat Persia. Dalam tradisi Kristen, Alkonost dipandang sebagai utusan Tuhan yang meringankan penderitaan atau sebagai lambang jiwa yang naik ke surga, dan bagi Gereja Ortodoks Rusia ia adalah personifikasi kehendak ilahi.",
   "en": "The name Alkonost arose from a misreading of Church Slavic text, where a phrase meaning \"the alkuon is a sea bird\" was read as one name. The underlying Greek word means kingfisher, and the figure is tied to the Greek myth of Alcyone, turned by the gods into a kingfisher; another source suggests an origin in Xrafstar of Persian folklore. After Christianity reached Russia, the Alkonost was seen as a messenger from God easing hardship or as an image of the soul rising to Heaven, and for the Russian Orthodox Church it personifies the divine will.",
   "claim_ids": [

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c01",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "is a legendary woman-headed bird in Russian folklore, related to the Sirin.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost (Алконост) adalah burung legendaris berkepala perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, berkerabat dengan Sirin.",
    "en": "The Alkonost (Алконост) is a legendary woman-headed bird in Russian folklore, related to the Sirin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c02",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "It possibly originated from Xrafstar in Persian folklore.",
   "locator": "Origins and name",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost mungkin berasal dari Xrafstar dalam cerita rakyat Persia.",
    "en": "The Alkonost possibly originated from Xrafstar in Persian folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c03",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "The Alkonost often symbolizes positive things like happiness and paradise, while the Sirin tends to symbolize negative forces like natural disasters and misfortune.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost sering melambangkan hal-hal baik seperti kebahagiaan dan surga, sedangkan Sirin melambangkan kekuatan negatif seperti bencana alam dan kemalangan.",
    "en": "The Alkonost often symbolises good things like happiness and paradise, while the Sirin symbolises negative forces like natural disasters and misfortune."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c04",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "Both creatures are associated with foretelling or causing death. The Alkonost, along with many other mythological bird human hybrids, is said to live in Rai, where the dead live.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kedua makhluk dikaitkan dengan meramalkan atau mendatangkan kematian; Alkonost, bersama banyak makhluk campuran burung-manusia lain, dikatakan tinggal di Rai, tempat orang mati.",
    "en": "Both creatures are associated with foretelling or causing death; the Alkonost, with many other bird-human hybrids, is said to live in Rai, where the dead live."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c05",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "The Alkonost sings beautiful songs, and those who hear these songs forget everything they know and want nothing more ever again.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost menyanyikan lagu yang indah, dan yang mendengarnya melupakan semua yang diketahuinya dan tidak menginginkan apa pun lagi.",
    "en": "The Alkonost sings beautiful songs; those who hear them forget everything they know and want nothing more ever again."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c06",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "The Alkonost lays her eggs on a beach and then rolls them into the sea. During the time when the eggs are in the ocean, there are no storms or disasters. However, when the eggs hatch, the hatching causes very violent storms.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost bertelur di pantai lalu menggulirkan telurnya ke laut; selama telur di laut tidak ada badai atau bencana, tetapi saat menetas timbul badai yang sangat dahsyat.",
    "en": "The Alkonost lays eggs on a beach and rolls them into the sea; while they are in the ocean there are no storms or disasters, but their hatching causes very violent storms."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c07",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "The Alkonost became a popular figure when Christianity was introduced in Russia, as the Alkonost was believed to be a messenger from God meant to take away the pain of hardship. Alternately, the Alkonost was used to represent the human soul ascending to Heaven.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost menjadi populer saat agama Kristen masuk ke Rusia karena diyakini sebagai utusan Tuhan untuk menghapus derita kesusahan, atau dipakai sebagai lambang jiwa manusia yang naik ke surga.",
    "en": "The Alkonost became popular when Christianity reached Russia, as it was believed to be a messenger from God to take away the pain of hardship, or was used to represent the human soul ascending to Heaven."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c08",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "According to folk tales, on the morning of the Apple Feast of the Saviour, the Sirin flies into the apple orchard and cries sadly. In the afternoon, the Alkonost flies to this place, beginning to rejoice and laugh. The Alkonost brushes dew from her wings, granting healing powers to all fruits on the tree she is sitting on.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat, pada pagi pesta apel Juru Selamat Sirin terbang ke kebun apel dan menangis; sore harinya Alkonost terbang ke sana, bergembira dan tertawa, lalu mengibaskan embun dari sayapnya sehingga semua buah di pohon tempatnya bertengger mendapat daya penyembuh.",
    "en": "According to folk tales, on the morning of the Apple Feast of the Saviour the Sirin flies into the orchard and weeps; in the afternoon the Alkonost flies there rejoicing and laughing, and brushes dew from its wings, granting healing powers to all fruits on its tree."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c09",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s2",
   "quote": "от греч. ἀλκυών — зимородок) — морская птица, символизирующая Божий промысел и Божие милосердие; мифическая райская птица из книжных византийских и славянских легенд.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama ini berasal dari kata Yunani ἀλκυών, \"raja-udang\"; Alkonost adalah burung laut yang melambangkan pemeliharaan dan belas kasih Tuhan, burung surga mitos dari legenda tertulis Bizantium dan Slavia.",
    "en": "The name derives from Greek ἀλκυών, \"kingfisher\"; the Alkonost is a sea bird symbolising God's providence and mercy, a mythical bird of paradise from Byzantine and Slavic literary legends."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c10",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s2",
   "quote": "Алконост несёт яйца на берегу моря, погружает их в глубину моря и делает его спокойным на шесть дней. Пение этой птицы настолько прекрасно, что услышавший его забывает обо всём на свете.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost bertelur di tepi laut, membenamkan telurnya ke kedalaman laut dan membuat laut tenang selama enam hari; nyanyiannya begitu indah sehingga yang mendengarnya melupakan segala hal di dunia.",
    "en": "The Alkonost lays eggs on the seashore, sinks them into the deep and makes the sea calm for six days; its song is so beautiful that whoever hears it forgets everything in the world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c11",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s2",
   "quote": "Образ возник из древнегреческого мифа об Алкионе, превращённой богами в зимородка.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sosok ini muncul dari mitos Yunani kuno tentang Alkione, yang diubah para dewa menjadi burung raja-udang.",
    "en": "The figure arose from the ancient Greek myth of Alcyone, turned by the gods into a kingfisher."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c12",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s2",
   "quote": "Русский лубок изображает Алконоста полуптицей-полуженщиной с большими перьями, имеющими различную окраску. Голову венчает корона, в руках — райские цветы.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Lubok Rusia menggambarkan Alkonost sebagai setengah burung setengah perempuan dengan bulu besar beraneka warna, kepala bermahkota dan memegang bunga surga.",
    "en": "The Russian lubok depicts the Alkonost as half bird, half woman with large feathers of various colours, a crown on its head and flowers of paradise in its hands."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c13",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s2",
   "quote": "в котором в результате прочтения «алкуон(ъ) есть морская птица» как «алкуонестъ морская птица» впервые читается имя «Алконост», закрепившееся в древнерусской литературе.",
   "locator": "В книжности",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena frasa \"alkuon adalah burung laut\" terbaca sebagai \"alkuonest burung laut\", nama \"Alkonost\" untuk pertama kali muncul dan kemudian mapan dalam sastra Rusia Kuno.",
    "en": "Because the phrase \"the alkuon is a sea bird\" was read as \"alkuonest sea bird\", the name \"Alkonost\" first appeared and then became fixed in Old Russian literature."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c14",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s3",
   "quote": "Die Alkonosty sind die magischen Vögel des Glücks und der Hoffnung. Im Gegensatz dazu stehen die Sirin, die Vögel der Trauer und des Kummers. Die Alkonosty sind den Menschen gut gesinnt, die Sirin hingegen weniger.",
   "locator": "Merkmale",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost adalah burung ajaib kebahagiaan dan harapan, berlawanan dengan Sirin, burung duka dan kesedihan; Alkonost bersikap baik kepada manusia, Sirin kurang.",
    "en": "The Alkonosts are magical birds of happiness and hope, opposed to the Sirin, birds of grief and sorrow; the Alkonosts are well disposed to people, the Sirin less so."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c15",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s3",
   "quote": "Für die Russisch-Orthodoxe Kirche ist die Alkonost die Personifikation des göttlichen Willens. Sie lebt im Paradies und verlässt es nur, um in unserer Welt Botschaften zu überbringen.",
   "locator": "Interpretation",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bagi Gereja Ortodoks Rusia, Alkonost adalah personifikasi kehendak ilahi; ia tinggal di surga dan hanya meninggalkannya untuk menyampaikan pesan ke dunia kita.",
    "en": "For the Russian Orthodox Church the Alkonost personifies the divine will; it lives in Paradise and leaves only to bring messages to our world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c16",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s3",
   "quote": "Daraufhin beruhigt sich die See für sechs oder sieben Tage und wird durch einen Sturm aufgewirbelt, sobald die Jungen schlüpfen.",
   "locator": "Merkmale",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah telur diletakkan, laut tenang selama enam atau tujuh hari dan diguncang badai begitu anak-anaknya menetas.",
    "en": "The sea then calms for six or seven days and is stirred up by a storm as soon as the young hatch."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c17",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "The Alkonost is a minor character from the opera The Legend of the Invisible City of Kitezh and the Maiden Fevroniya",
   "locator": "In popular culture",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost adalah tokoh kecil dalam opera The Legend of the Invisible City of Kitezh and the Maiden Fevroniya.",
    "en": "The Alkonost is a minor character in the opera The Legend of the Invisible City of Kitezh and the Maiden Fevroniya."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c18",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s2",
   "quote": "Обитает на райской реке Евфрат. Часто упоминается вместе с другой райской птицей — Сирином.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost berdiam di sungai surgawi Efrat (Евфрат) dan sering disebut bersama burung surga lain, Sirin.",
    "en": "The Alkonost dwells on the paradise river Euphrates (Евфрат) and is often mentioned with another bird of paradise, the Sirin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "alkonost-c19",
   "source_id": "alkonost-s1",
   "quote": "Alkonost is featured in the digital card game Mythgard (2019) as a rare minion in the Dreni faction.",
   "locator": "In popular culture",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alkonost muncul dalam gim kartu digital Mythgard (2019) sebagai kartu langka di faksi Dreni.",
    "en": "Alkonost is featured in the digital card game Mythgard (2019) as a rare minion in the Dreni faction."
   }
  }
 ],

 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Jarilo (Yarilo) adalah tokoh mitologi Slavia yang dikaitkan dengan kesuburan musim semi; sebagian sumber menyebutnya dewa vegetasi dan musim semi, sementara yang lain menganggapnya tokoh ritual.",
  "en": "Jarilo (Yarilo) is a Slavic mythological figure linked with spring fertility; some sources call him a god of vegetation and springtime, others a ritual character.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "jarilo-c01",
   "jarilo-c03",
   "jarilo-c04"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Jarilo, juga dieja Yarilo atau Yaryla dan disebut pula Gerovit, adalah dewa vegetasi, kesuburan, dan musim semi yang keberadaannya sebagai dewa masih diperdebatkan, dari kalangan Slavia Timur dan Selatan. Satu sumber melihatnya terutama sebagai tokoh mitos dan ritual yang terkait dengan kesuburan musim semi dan daya seksual, sekaligus personifikasi salah satu perayaan musim panas dalam kalender rakyat Rusia; tafsiran lain menganggapnya dewa kesuburan musim semi. Sumber yang sama menegaskan bahwa ia bukan dewa matahari, sedangkan sumber lain justru menyebutnya dewa matahari di wilayah berbahasa Rusia. Sumber autentik tentang Jarilo berasal dari Rusia Raya dan menggambarkan perayaan untuknya, sementara satu sumber menyebut biografi abad ke-12 tentang uskup Otto dari Bamberg sebagai satu-satunya catatan sejarah yang menyebut dewa ini.",
   "en": "Jarilo, also spelled Yarilo or Yaryla and called Gerovit, is a disputed deity of plant growth, fertility and spring in East and South Slavic belief. One source sees him mainly as a mythological and ritual character tied to spring fertility and sexual power, and as the personification of a summer festival in the Russian folk calendar; another reading makes him a deity of spring fertility. The same source insists he was not a sun god, while another calls him the sun god of the Russian-speaking area. The authentic sources on Jarilo are Great Russian and describe a festival in his honour, while one source names a 12th-century biography of bishop Otto of Bamberg as the only historical record of the god.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jarilo-c01",
    "jarilo-c03",
    "jarilo-c04",
    "jarilo-c07",
    "jarilo-c08",
    "jarilo-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya berasal dari akar kata Proto-Slavia *jarъ yang bermakna musim semi atau musim panas, kuat, garang, dan penuh daya hidup muda. Daya hidup muda itu dianggap suci dalam agama Slavia pra-Kristen, sehingga dewa yang mempersonifikasikannya disebut Jarovit, atau dengan nama akrab Jarilo.",
   "en": "His name comes from the Proto-Slavic root *jarъ, meaning spring or summer, strong, furious and full of youthful life-force. That life-force was held sacred in Slavic pre-Christian religion, so the god who personified it was called Jarovit, or affectionately Jarilo.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jarilo-c02",
    "jarilo-c14"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Jarilo berperan khusus dalam upacara pertanian, terutama pada musim semi, dan sosoknya mirip dengan Kostroma dan boneka jerami Maslenitsa. Di sebuah desa Kostroma, boneka bernama Jarilo dimasukkan ke peti mati, diarak keliling desa oleh seorang lelaki tua berpakaian compang-camping sambil diratapi, lalu dikubur di ladang. Hingga abad ke-19, festival rakyat bernama Jarilo dirayakan di Rusia, Belarusia, dan Serbia pada akhir musim semi atau awal musim panas.",
   "en": "Jarilo has a special place in agricultural rites, especially in spring, and his figure resembles Kostroma and the Maslenitsa straw effigy. In one Kostroma village an effigy called Jarilo was laid in a coffin, carried through the village by an old man dressed in rags amid lamentation, and then buried in the field. Until the 19th century a folk festival named Jarilo was held in Russia, Belarus and Serbia in late spring or early summer.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jarilo-c05",
    "jarilo-c06",
    "jarilo-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut satu sumber, Jarilo adalah putra dewa guntur Perun dan istrinya, dewi Mokosch; wujud hewannya kuda dan ia dikaitkan dengan warna hijau. Dalam siklus tahunan ia datang dari jauh untuk bersatu dalam pernikahan suci dengan saudarinya Mara/Morana, sehingga kesuburan musim semi terlepas. Dalam tafsiran Kristen, ia dipadankan dengan Santo Georg dan Yohanes; di Kroasia utara dan Slovenia selatan, perayaan musim semi serupa disebut Jurjevo atau Zeleni Juraj dan dipersembahkan untuk St. George.",
   "en": "According to one source, Jarilo is the son of the thunder god Perun and his wife, the goddess Mokosch; his animal form is a horse and he is associated with the colour green. In the yearly cycle he comes from afar to unite in sacred marriage with his sister Mara/Morana, releasing spring fertility. In Christian interpretation he corresponds to Saint George (Georg) and John (Johannes); in northern Croatia and southern Slovenia a similar spring festival called Jurjevo or Zeleni Juraj is dedicated to St. George.",

    "jarilo-c09",
    "jarilo-c10",

   ]
  }

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c01",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s1",
   "quote": "alternatively Yaryla, Yarilo, Iarilo, Juraj, Jurij, or Gerovit, is an alleged East and South Slavic god of vegetation, fertility and springtime.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jarilo, juga disebut Yaryla, Yarilo, Iarilo, Juraj, Jurij, atau Gerovit, adalah dewa vegetasi, kesuburan, dan musim semi Slavia Timur dan Selatan yang keberadaannya masih diduga.",
    "en": "Jarilo, also Yaryla, Yarilo, Iarilo, Juraj, Jurij or Gerovit, is an alleged East and South Slavic god of vegetation, fertility and springtime."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c02",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s1",
   "quote": "means \"spring\" or \"summer\", \"strong\", \"furious\", \"imbued with youthful life-force\".",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Akar kata Proto-Slavia *jarъ berarti \"musim semi\" atau \"musim panas\", \"kuat\", \"garang\", \"penuh daya hidup muda\".",
    "en": "The Proto-Slavic root *jarъ means \"spring\" or \"summer\", \"strong\", \"furious\", \"imbued with youthful life-force\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c03",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s2",
   "quote": "славянский мифологический и ритуальный персонаж, связанный с идеей плодородия, прежде всего весеннего, и сексуальной мощи, персонификация одного из летних праздников в русском народном календаре",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jarilo adalah tokoh mitologi dan ritual Slavia yang terkait dengan gagasan kesuburan, terutama kesuburan musim semi, dan daya seksual, serta personifikasi salah satu perayaan musim panas dalam kalender rakyat Rusia.",
    "en": "Jarilo is a Slavic mythological and ritual character tied to fertility, above all spring fertility, and sexual power, and the personification of one of the summer festivals in the Russian folk calendar."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c04",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s2",
   "quote": "по другой интерпретации славянское божество весеннего плодородия. Вопреки распространённому мнению, Ярила не был богом солнца.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut tafsiran lain, Jarilo adalah dewa Slavia untuk kesuburan musim semi; berlawanan dengan anggapan umum, ia bukan dewa matahari.",
    "en": "By another interpretation Jarilo is a Slavic deity of spring fertility; contrary to popular belief, he was not a sun god."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c05",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s2",
   "quote": "Ярила играет особую роль в сельскохозяйственной обрядности, особенно весенней. Образ Ярилы сходен с образами Костромы, Кострубоньки, чучела Масленицы.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jarilo berperan khusus dalam upacara pertanian, terutama musim semi; sosoknya mirip dengan Kostroma (Кострома), Kostrubonka, dan boneka jerami Maslenitsa (Масленица).",
    "en": "Jarilo has a special role in agricultural rites, especially in spring; his figure resembles Kostroma, Kostrubonka and the Maslenitsa effigy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c06",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s2",
   "quote": "Так, в Костромской деревне такое чучело под названием Ярило клали в гроб и давали носить по деревне старику, которого одевали в лохмотья, а вместо песен было оплакивание. После этого Ярило зарывали в поле.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di sebuah desa Kostroma, boneka bernama Jarilo dimasukkan ke peti mati dan diarak keliling desa oleh lelaki tua berpakaian compang-camping, diiringi ratapan alih-alih nyanyian, lalu dikubur di ladang.",
    "en": "In a Kostroma village an effigy called Jarilo was laid in a coffin and carried around the village by an old man in rags, with lamentation instead of songs, and then buried in the field."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c07",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s2",
   "quote": "Аутентичные источники по Яриле — великорусские. В них описывается посвящённое Яриле празднество.",
   "locator": "Источники",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sumber autentik tentang Jarilo berasal dari Rusia Raya (великорусские) dan menggambarkan perayaan yang dipersembahkan untuknya.",
    "en": "The authentic sources on Jarilo are Great Russian and describe a festival dedicated to him."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c08",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s3",
   "quote": "Jarilo (russ: Ярила) oder Jarovit, auch Yarilo ist in der slawischen Mythologie Sohn des Donnergottes Perun und seiner Gattin, der Göttin Mokosch und der Sonnengott im Russischen Sprachraum.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jarilo (Ярила), juga Jarovit atau Yarilo, dalam mitologi Slavia adalah putra dewa guntur Perun dan istrinya, dewi Mokosch, serta dewa matahari di wilayah berbahasa Rusia.",
    "en": "Jarilo (Ярила), or Jarovit, also Yarilo, is in Slavic mythology the son of the thunder god Perun and his wife the goddess Mokosch, and the sun god of the Russian-speaking area."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c09",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s3",
   "quote": "Seine Tiergestalt ist ein Pferd und er wird mit der Farbe grün (Vegetation) assoziiert.",
   "locator": "lead",

    "en": "Jarilo's animal form is a horse, and he is associated with the colour green (vegetation)."

  },
  {
   "id": "jarilo-c10",
   "source_id": "jarilo-s3",
   "quote": "um sich mit seiner Schwester Mara/Morana in heiliger Ehe zu vereinen und dadurch die Fruchtbarkeit im Frühling zu entfesseln.",
   "locator": "lead",

  "id": "Mokosh adalah dewi Slavia yang menurut rekonstruksi berhubungan dengan air dan kesuburan; ia satu-satunya dewi yang tercatat dalam jajaran dewa Kiev.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Mokosh adalah dewi Slavia yang kisahnya tidak bertahan. Namanya muncul dalam khotbah-khotbah anti-pagan bersama para vila, tetapi tanpa penjelasan, sehingga semua gambaran modern tentangnya merupakan hasil rekonstruksi. Satu sumber menyebutnya, bersama dewi Polabia Siva, sebagai satu-satunya dewi Slavia yang terbukti secara historis, seorang dewi ibu yang mengurus kesuburan, kewanitaan, serta perlindungan domba, pemintalan, dan tenun.",
   "en": "Mokosh is a Slavic goddess whose myths have not survived. Her name appears in sermons against paganism alongside the vilas, but without description, so every modern picture of her is a reconstruction. One source counts her, together with the Polabian goddess Siva, as the only historically attested Slavic goddess, a mother goddess in charge of fertility, womanhood and the protection of sheep, spinning and weaving.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mokosh-c01",
    "mokosh-c13",
    "mokosh-c18"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Mokosh termasuk enam dewa yang patungnya didirikan oleh Pangeran Vladimir I di Kiev pada tahun 980, sehingga ia tergolong dewa utama bangsa Slavia Timur. Dalam sumber tertulis ia biasanya disebut bersama roh-roh air, yakni vila dan kemudian rusalka. Setelah Kristenisasi, perlindungan ladang dan ternak yang dulu menjadi urusannya dialihkan kepada Santa Paraskeva, namun pada abad ke-16 perempuan masih ditanya dalam pengakuan dosa apakah mereka pergi kepada Mokosh.",
   "en": "Mokosh was one of six deities whose statues Prince Vladimir I set up in Kiev in 980, making her one of the chief gods of the East Slavs. Written sources usually mention her with water spirits, the vilas and later the rusalkas. After Christianisation the protection of fields and livestock that had been hers passed to Saint Paraskeva, yet in the 16th century women were still asked at confession whether they went to Mokosh.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mokosh-c14",
    "mokosh-c15",
    "mokosh-c16",
    "mokosh-c18"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Nama Mokosh paling umum dijelaskan berasal dari akar Proto-Slavia *mok- yang berarti \"basah\", sehingga ia dipahami sebagai \"dia yang basah\". Dari situ ia direkonstruksi sebagai dewi air dan kesuburan, yang kemudian tercermin dalam sosok Mat Zemlya, personifikasi Bumi dalam cerita rakyat Slavia Timur, dan dilambangkan sebagai \"Ibu Bumi yang lembap\" yang sepadan dengan dewi Iran Ardvi Sur Anahita.",
   "en": "The most widely accepted explanation derives Mokosh from the Proto-Slavic stem *mok-, \"wet\", so that she is understood as \"She who is wet\". On that basis she is reconstructed as a goddess of waters and fertility, later reflected in Mat Zemlya, the personification of Earth in East Slavic folklore, and symbolised as the \"moist Mother Earth\" corresponding to the Iranian goddess Ardvi Sur Anahita.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mokosh-c04",
    "mokosh-c09",
    "mokosh-c19"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Rekonstruksi lain bertumpu pada catatan etnografi: pada akhir abad ke-19, kikimora di Rusia Utara disebut Mokusha atau Mokosha, dan kikimora dianggap versi dewi ini yang telah dijadikan iblis. Hingga abad ke-19 dikenal pula iblis perempuan bernama Mokusch, berkepala besar dan bertangan panjang, yang datang ke rumah petani pada malam hari untuk memintal serat yang tergeletak. Dari sini para peneliti menggambarkan Mokosh sebagai dewi cinta dan kelahiran yang terkait dengan malam, bulan, pemintalan, peternakan domba, dan urusan rumah tangga perempuan, meski data etimologi menunjukkan pemintalan tidak mungkin menjadi peran utamanya.",
   "en": "Another reconstruction rests on ethnography: at the end of the 19th century the kikimora was called Mokusha or Mokosha in the Russian North, and the kikimora is seen as a demonised version of the goddess. Until the 19th century there was also a female demon called Mokusch, with a big head and long hands, who came to peasant houses at night to spin loose fibre. From this, researchers portrayed Mokosh as a goddess of love and birth tied to night, the moon, spinning, sheep farming and women's economy, though etymological data show spinning could not have been her main role.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mokosh-c05",
    "mokosh-c06",
    "mokosh-c07",
    "mokosh-c17"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c01",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "is a Slavic goddess. No narratives about this deity have survived: Mokosh was mentioned in various sermons (called Words and Teachings) against Paganism along with the vilas, but is not described by them, and all modern descriptions are reconstructions.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mokosh adalah dewi Slavia yang kisahnya tidak bertahan; ia disebut dalam khotbah-khotbah anti-pagan bersama para vila tanpa dijelaskan, dan semua gambaran modern tentangnya adalah rekonstruksi.",
    "en": "Mokosh is a Slavic goddess with no surviving narratives; she was named in sermons against paganism with the vilas but not described, and all modern descriptions are reconstructions."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c02",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "In academia, the opinion has spread that the cult of Mokosh has passed to the folk-Christian Paraskeva Friday, the personification of Friday associated with water and spinning.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di kalangan akademik tersebar pendapat bahwa kultus Mokosh beralih kepada Paraskeva Friday dalam Kristen rakyat, personifikasi hari Jumat yang dikaitkan dengan air dan pemintalan.",
    "en": "In academia the view spread that Mokosh's cult passed to the folk-Christian Paraskeva Friday, the personification of Friday associated with water and spinning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c03",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "In later studies, the idea of an identification with Paraskeva was criticized because Paraskeva's association with spinning, water, and Friday has Christian rather than pagan roots.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kajian lanjutan mengkritik penyamaan dengan Paraskeva karena kaitan Paraskeva dengan pemintalan, air, dan hari Jumat berakar Kristen, bukan pagan.",
    "en": "Later studies criticised the identification with Paraskeva, because her link with spinning, water and Friday has Christian rather than pagan roots."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c04",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "According to etymological reconstruction, Mokosh was the goddess of waters and fertility. Later, according to most researchers, she was reflected in bylinas and zagovory as Mat Zemlya, the personification of Earth in East Slavic folklore.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut rekonstruksi etimologis, Mokosh adalah dewi air dan kesuburan; menurut sebagian besar peneliti, ia kemudian tercermin dalam bylina dan zagovory sebagai Mat Zemlya, personifikasi Bumi dalam cerita rakyat Slavia Timur.",
    "en": "By etymological reconstruction Mokosh was a goddess of waters and fertility; most researchers say she was later reflected in bylinas and zagovory as Mat Zemlya, the personification of Earth in East Slavic folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c05",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "at the end of the 19th century, the names kikimora as Mokusha or Mokosha were recorded in the Russian North. The coincidence is explained by kikimora being a demonized version of the goddess",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada akhir abad ke-19, di Rusia Utara tercatat nama kikimora sebagai Mokusha atau Mokosha; kesamaan ini dijelaskan dengan menganggap kikimora versi dewi ini yang dijadikan iblis.",
    "en": "At the end of the 19th century kikimora was recorded as Mokusha or Mokosha in the Russian North; this is explained by the kikimora being a demonised version of the goddess."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c06",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "researchers have portrayed Mokosh as the goddess of love and birth, with a connection to night, the moon, spinning, sheep farming and women's economy.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Para peneliti menggambarkan Mokosh sebagai dewi cinta dan kelahiran yang terkait dengan malam, bulan, pemintalan, peternakan domba, dan ekonomi perempuan.",
    "en": "Researchers have portrayed Mokosh as a goddess of love and birth, linked with night, the moon, spinning, sheep farming and women's economy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c07",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "This reconstruction disagrees with data on her etymology, which shows spinning could not have been the deity's main role.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Rekonstruksi ini bertentangan dengan data etimologi, yang menunjukkan pemintalan tidak mungkin menjadi peran utama sang dewi.",
    "en": "This reconstruction conflicts with the etymological data, which show spinning could not have been her main role."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c08",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "based on ethnographic and linguistic data, depicts Mokosh as Perun's wife. It is believed Mokosh cheated on Perun with Veles, causing Perun to kill Mokosh's children. The theory has not been recognized in academia.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Versi Slavia dari teori mitos dasar menggambarkan Mokosh sebagai istri Perun yang berselingkuh dengan Veles sehingga Perun membunuh anak-anak Mokosh; teori ini tidak diakui kalangan akademik.",
    "en": "The Slavic version of the basic myth theory depicts Mokosh as Perun's wife who cheated on him with Veles, so Perun killed her children; the theory is not recognised in academia."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c09",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "According to the most-reasonable and widespread etymology the theonym was formed by the suffixal method from the Proto-Slavic stem *mok- meaning \"wet\" with the suffix *-ošь. Vladimir Toporov and Vyacheslav Ivanov comment this etymology is \"indisputable\", understanding her name as \"She who is wet\".",
   "locator": "Name and characteristics",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut etimologi yang paling masuk akal dan tersebar luas, nama ini dibentuk dari akar Proto-Slavia *mok- \"basah\" dengan akhiran *-ošь; Vladimir Toporov dan Vyacheslav Ivanov menganggapnya tak terbantahkan dan mengartikan namanya \"dia yang basah\".",
    "en": "By the most reasonable and widespread etymology the name was formed from the Proto-Slavic stem *mok- \"wet\" with the suffix *-ošь; Vladimir Toporov and Vyacheslav Ivanov call this indisputable, reading the name as \"She who is wet\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c10",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "In Old East Slavic texts, the name Mokosh is rendered as Mokošĭ ( мокошь ), Mokŭšĭ ( мокъшь )",
   "locator": "Name and characteristics",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam teks Slavia Timur Kuno, nama Mokosh ditulis Mokošĭ (мокошь) dan Mokŭšĭ (мокъшь).",
    "en": "In Old East Slavic texts the name Mokosh is written Mokošĭ (мокошь) and Mokŭšĭ (мокъшь)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c11",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "Mokosh, unlike Macha, was not a warrior, but from the 16th century her name was used to refer to witches and healers, indicating a possible function as a prophetess.",
   "locator": "Name and characteristics",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Berbeda dengan Macha, Mokosh bukan pejuang, tetapi sejak abad ke-16 namanya dipakai untuk menyebut penyihir dan penyembuh, yang menandakan kemungkinan fungsi sebagai peramal.",
    "en": "Unlike Macha, Mokosh was not a warrior, but from the 16th century her name was used for witches and healers, suggesting a possible role as a prophetess."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c12",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s1",
   "quote": "Celtologist Viktor Kalygin approximated Mokosh to the Irish goddess Macha, in his opinion originally the goddess of fertility.",
   "locator": "Name and characteristics",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ahli Kelt Viktor Kalygin menyamakan Mokosh dengan dewi Irlandia Macha, yang menurutnya semula dewi kesuburan.",
    "en": "Celtologist Viktor Kalygin likened Mokosh to the Irish goddess Macha, originally in his view a fertility goddess."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c13",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "Sie ist neben der polabischen Göttin Siva die einzige bekannte historisch belegbare weibliche Gottheit des slawischen Pantheons und als Muttergöttin zuständig für Fruchtbarkeit, Weiblichkeit und den Schutz der Schafe und des Spinnens und Webens.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bersama dewi Polabia Siva, Mokosh (Mokosch) adalah satu-satunya dewi yang terbukti secara historis dalam jajaran dewa Slavia, dan sebagai dewi ibu ia mengurus kesuburan, kewanitaan, serta perlindungan domba, pemintalan, dan tenun.",
    "en": "With the Polabian goddess Siva, Mokosh (Mokosch) is the only historically attested female deity of the Slavic pantheon, and as mother goddess she oversees fertility, womanhood and the protection of sheep, spinning and weaving."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c14",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "Mokosch war eine der sechs Gottheiten, denen Fürst Vladimir I. 980 in Kiew Standbilder aufstellen ließ. Sie zählte also zu den Hauptgöttern der Ostslawen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mokosh termasuk enam dewa yang patungnya didirikan Pangeran Vladimir I di Kiev (Kiew) pada tahun 980, sehingga ia tergolong dewa utama Slavia Timur.",
    "en": "Mokosh was one of six deities for whom Prince Vladimir I set up statues in Kiev (Kiew) in 980, so she was one of the chief gods of the East Slavs."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c15",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "Nach der Christianisierung wurde der Schutz der Felder und des Viehs, der Mokosch oblag, der Heiligen Paraskeva übertragen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah Kristenisasi, perlindungan ladang dan ternak yang menjadi tugas Mokosh dialihkan kepada Santa Paraskeva.",
    "en": "After Christianisation the protection of fields and livestock that had been Mokosh's task passed to Saint Paraskeva."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c16",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "Doch noch im 16. Jahrhundert wurden Frauen bei der Beichte gefragt, ob sie zu Mokosch gingen",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada abad ke-16 perempuan masih ditanya dalam pengakuan dosa apakah mereka pergi kepada Mokosh.",
    "en": "In the 16th century women were still asked at confession whether they went to Mokosh."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c17",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "bis zum 19. Jahrhundert hielt sich in der nordrussischen und ukrainischen Folklore die Vorstellung eines weiblichen Dämons namens Mokusch, mit großem Kopf und langen Händen, der nachts in die Bauernhäuser kam und das Werg spann, das offen herumlag",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hingga abad ke-19, cerita rakyat Rusia Utara dan Ukraina mengenal iblis perempuan bernama Mokusch, berkepala besar dan bertangan panjang, yang datang ke rumah petani pada malam hari dan memintal serat yang tergeletak.",
    "en": "Until the 19th century North Russian and Ukrainian folklore knew a female demon called Mokusch, with a big head and long hands, who came to peasant houses at night and spun loose fibre."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c18",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "In den schriftlichen Quellen wird sie meist zusammen mit Wassergeistern erwähnt, den Vilen, später den Rusalken.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam sumber tertulis Mokosh biasanya disebut bersama roh air, yaitu vila (Vilen) dan kemudian rusalka (Rusalken).",
    "en": "In written sources she is usually mentioned together with water spirits, the vilas (Vilen) and later the rusalkas (Rusalken)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mokosh-c19",
   "source_id": "mokosh-s2",
   "quote": "Sie symbolisiert demnach die „feuchte Mutter Erde“ und entspricht damit der iranischen Fruchtbarkeitsgöttin Ardvi Sur Anahita.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {

    "en": "She thus symbolises the \"moist Mother Earth\" and corresponds to the Iranian fertility goddess Ardvi Sur Anahita."
   }

 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Svetovit atau Svantovit adalah dewa kelimpahan dan perang, dewa tertinggi suku Slavia Rani dan kemudian seluruh Slavia Polabia, dengan pusat pemujaan di Tanjung Arkona, Rügen.",
  "en": "Svetovit, or Svantovit, is the god of abundance and war, chief god of the Slavic Rani tribe and later of all the Polabian Slavs, worshipped at Cape Arkona on Rügen.",
  "claim_ids": [

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Svetovit, yang juga dikenal sebagai Sventovit atau Svantovit, adalah dewa kelimpahan dan perang. Ia dewa tertinggi suku Rani, lalu seluruh Slavia Polabia, dan juga pemberi orakel; satu sumber membandingkannya dengan Perun, dewa tertinggi suku-suku Slavia lain, dan banyak ahli memandangnya sebagai perwujudan Perun di kalangan Polabia. Pemujaannya yang terorganisasi berpusat di pulau Rügen, di Tanjung Arkona, tempat kuil utamanya berdiri.",
   "en": "Svetovit, also called Sventovit or Svantovit, is a god of abundance and war. He was the supreme god of the Rani tribe and later of all the Polabian Slavs, and a giver of oracles; one source compares him with Perun, the highest god of other Slavic tribes, and many scholars see him as a Polabian form of Perun. His organised worship had its centre at Cape Arkona on Rügen, the site of his chief temple.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svetovit-c01",
    "svetovit-c02",
    "svetovit-c06",
    "svetovit-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut para penulis kronik abad pertengahan, patungnya berkepala empat serta memegang tanduk dan pedang. Satu sumber menyebut patung kayu di Arkona itu bermuka empat dengan tanduk berisi anggur, berdiri dalam kuil persegi bertiang di dalam Jaromarsburg; tiap kepala menghadap satu arah mata angin yang masing-masing punya warna sendiri. Kuda putih, pelana, tali kekang, panji, dan burung elang dipersembahkan kepadanya.",
   "en": "Medieval chroniclers describe his statue as four-headed, holding a horn and a sword. One source says the wooden statue at Arkona had four faces and a horn filled with wine, and stood in a square pillared temple inside the Jaromarsburg; each head faced one cardinal direction, each with its own colour. A white horse, a saddle, a bit, a banner and eagles were dedicated to him.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svetovit-c03",
    "svetovit-c11",
    "svetovit-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sekali setahun, setelah panen, diadakan perayaan besar untuknya. Para pendeta meramal dengan tanduk dan kuda milik sang dewa, dan dipercaya bahwa pada malam hari dewa itu sendiri menunggang kuda untuk melawan musuh-musuhnya. Orakel Svantovit menerima banyak hadiah dan persembahan dari dunia Slavia maupun tetangganya. Tempat suci itu dianggap pusat rohani bangsa Slavia, terutama suku Rani, hingga dihancurkan pada tahun 1168 oleh orang Denmark di bawah Uskup Absalon dan Raja Valdemar.",
   "en": "Once a year, after the harvest, a great festival was held for him. Priests divined with the god's horn and horse, and the god himself was believed to ride out at night against his enemies. The Svantovit oracle received many gifts and offerings from the Slavic world and its neighbours. The sanctuary was seen as the spiritual centre of the Slavs, especially the Rani, until the Danes under Bishop Absalon and King Valdemar destroyed it in 1168.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "svetovit-c04",
    "svetovit-c06",
    "svetovit-c13",
    "svetovit-c14"
   ]

   "id": "Namanya dapat diartikan \"Tuan yang Kuat\" atau \"Tuan yang Suci\". Pandangan yang dominan menyatakan bahwa kata *svętъ semula berarti \"kuat\" dan baru bermakna \"suci\" karena pengaruh Kristen, tetapi kini sebagian ahli menganggap makna \"suci\" sebagai makna asli. Dahulu orang keliru mengira kultus Svetovit berasal dari Santo Vitus, sedangkan bacaan Światowid, \"dewa yang melihat empat penjuru dunia\", adalah ciptaan kaum Romantik Polandia abad ke-19 dan dinilai keliru sama sekali.",
   "en": "His name can be rendered \"Strong Lord\" or \"Holy Lord\".   {
   "id": "svetovit-c01",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "Svetovit, also known as Sventovit and Svantovit amongst other variants, is the god of abundance and war, and the chief god of the Slavic tribe of the Rani, and later of all the Polabian Slavs.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Svetovit, juga dikenal sebagai Sventovit dan Svantovit, adalah dewa kelimpahan dan perang, dewa tertinggi suku Slavia Rani dan kemudian seluruh Slavia Polabia.",
    "en": "Svetovit, also Sventovit and Svantovit, is the god of abundance and war, chief god of the Slavic Rani tribe and later of all the Polabian Slavs."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c02",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "His organized cult was located on the island of Rügen, at Cape Arkona, where his main temple was also located.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pemujaan terorganisasinya berada di pulau Rügen, di Tanjung Arkona, tempat kuil utamanya juga berada.",
    "en": "His organised cult was on the island of Rügen at Cape Arkona, where his main temple was also located."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c03",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "According to the descriptions of medieval chroniclers, the statue representing this god had four heads and held a horn and a sword. Dedicated to the deity were a white horse, a saddle, a bit, a flag, and eagles.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut penulis kronik abad pertengahan, patung dewa ini berkepala empat serta memegang tanduk dan pedang; kuda putih, pelana, tali kekang, panji, dan elang dipersembahkan kepadanya.",
    "en": "According to medieval chroniclers, the god's statue had four heads and held a horn and a sword; a white horse, a saddle, a bit, a flag and eagles were dedicated to him."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c04",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "Once a year, after the harvest, a large festival was held in his honor. With the help of a horn and a horse belonging to the god, the priests carried out divinations, and at night the god himself rode a horse to fight his enemies.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sekali setahun setelah panen diadakan perayaan besar untuknya; para pendeta meramal dengan tanduk dan kuda milik sang dewa, dan pada malam hari sang dewa sendiri menunggang kuda melawan musuh-musuhnya.",
    "en": "Once a year after the harvest a great festival was held for him; priests divined with the god's horn and horse, and at night the god himself rode a horse to fight his enemies."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c05",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "His name can be translated as \"Strong Lord\" or \"Holy Lord\". In the past it was often mistakenly believed that the cult of Svetovit originated from St. Vitus.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namanya dapat diterjemahkan \"Tuan yang Kuat\" atau \"Tuan yang Suci\"; dahulu sering keliru dipercaya bahwa kultus Svetovit berasal dari Santo Vitus.",
    "en": "His name can be translated \"Strong Lord\" or \"Holy Lord\"; it was often wrongly believed that his cult derived from St. Vitus."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c06",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "Among scholars of Slavic mythology, Svetovit is often regarded as a Polabian hypostasis of Pan-Slavic god Perun. His cult was destroyed in 1168 by the Danes under Bishop Absalon and King Valdemar the Great.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di kalangan ahli mitologi Slavia, Svetovit sering dianggap perwujudan Polabia dari dewa pan-Slavia Perun; kultusnya dihancurkan pada tahun 1168 oleh orang Denmark di bawah Uskup Absalon dan Raja Valdemar Agung.",
    "en": "Scholars of Slavic mythology often regard Svetovit as a Polabian hypostasis of the pan-Slavic god Perun; his cult was destroyed in 1168 by the Danes under Bishop Absalon and King Valdemar the Great."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c07",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "A completely incorrect reading is Sviatovid / Svietovid (Polish Światowid) \"God seeing the four directions of the world\" invented by 19th century Polish Romantics",
   "locator": "Other propositions",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bacaan Sviatovid/Svietovid (Polandia: Światowid), \"dewa yang melihat empat penjuru dunia\", sepenuhnya keliru dan diciptakan kaum Romantik Polandia abad ke-19.",
    "en": "The reading Sviatovid/Svietovid (Polish Światowid), \"God seeing the four directions of the world\", is entirely wrong and was invented by 19th-century Polish Romantics."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c08",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "The prevailing view in the literature is that *svętъ in Proto-Slavic language meant \"strong, mighty\", and only under the influence of Christianity did it acquire the religious meaning of \"holy, sacred\".",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pandangan yang dominan menyatakan bahwa *svętъ dalam bahasa Proto-Slavia berarti \"kuat, perkasa\" dan baru memperoleh makna religius \"suci\" karena pengaruh Kristen.",
    "en": "The prevailing view is that Proto-Slavic *svętъ meant \"strong, mighty\" and acquired the religious meaning \"holy, sacred\" only under Christian influence."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c09",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s1",
   "quote": "Nowadays, however, this view is sometimes criticized and it is suggested that the meaning of \"holy, sacred\" should be considered original, Proto-Slavic.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kini pandangan itu kadang dikritik, dan diusulkan bahwa makna \"suci\" adalah makna asli Proto-Slavia.",
    "en": "Nowadays this view is sometimes criticised, and it is suggested that \"holy, sacred\" is the original Proto-Slavic meaning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c10",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s2",
   "quote": "Svantovit (auch Svantevit, Swantewit, Sventevit, Svetovit, Svatovit, Świętowit, Svątevit, Suvid oder Святовит) ist eine slawische Gottheit. Er war der Kriegsgott, Orakelspender und die oberste Gottheit der Ranen auf Rügen und anderer Elb- und Ostseeslawen, vergleichbar mit dem obersten Gott Perun anderer slawischer Stämme.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Svantovit (juga Svantevit, Swantewit, Sventevit, Svetovit, Svatovit, Świętowit, Svątevit, Suvid, atau Святовит) adalah dewa Slavia: dewa perang, pemberi orakel, dan dewa tertinggi suku Rani di Rügen serta Slavia Elbe dan Baltik lainnya, sebanding dengan Perun pada suku Slavia lain.",
    "en": "Svantovit (also Svantevit, Swantewit, Sventevit, Svetovit, Svatovit, Świętowit, Svątevit, Suvid or Святовит) is a Slavic deity: war god, oracle giver and supreme god of the Rani on Rügen and other Elbe and Baltic Slavs, comparable to Perun among other Slavic tribes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c11",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s2",
   "quote": "Svantovit ist vierköpfig, jeder Kopf schaut in eine Himmelsrichtung. Dabei wird jeder Himmelsrichtung eine eigene Farbe zugeordnet: Norden weiß, Westen rot, Süden schwarz und Osten grün.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Svantovit berkepala empat, tiap kepala menghadap satu arah mata angin, dan tiap arah punya warna sendiri: utara putih, barat merah, selatan hitam, dan timur hijau.",
    "en": "Svantovit is four-headed, each head facing a cardinal direction, and each direction has its own colour: north white, west red, south black and east green."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c12",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s2",
   "quote": "Auf Kap Arkona auf der Insel Rügen stand eine hölzerne Statue, die ihn mit vier Gesichtern und einem mit Wein gefüllten Horn darstellte. Die Statue befand sich in einem quadratischen, säulengetragenen Tempel innerhalb der Jaromarsburg.",
   "locator": "Svantovit-Kultstätte auf Rügen",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Tanjung Arkona (Kap Arkona), pulau Rügen, berdiri patung kayu yang menggambarkannya bermuka empat dengan tanduk berisi anggur, di dalam kuil persegi bertiang di Jaromarsburg.",
    "en": "At Cape Arkona (Kap Arkona) on Rügen stood a wooden statue showing him with four faces and a wine-filled horn, inside a square pillared temple within the Jaromarsburg."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c13",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s2",
   "quote": "Das Heiligtum galt als geistiges Zentrum der Slawen und insbesondere der auf Rügen ansässigen Ranen. Es wurde 1168 vom dänischen König Waldemar zerstört.",
   "locator": "Svantovit-Kultstätte auf Rügen",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tempat suci itu dianggap pusat rohani bangsa Slavia, terutama suku Rani di Rügen, dan dihancurkan pada tahun 1168 oleh Raja Denmark Valdemar (Waldemar).",
    "en": "The sanctuary was considered the spiritual centre of the Slavs, especially the Rani of Rügen, and was destroyed in 1168 by the Danish King Valdemar (Waldemar)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c14",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s2",
   "quote": "Die Chronica Slavorum des Chronisten Helmold von Bosau (12. Jh.) beschreibt das Svantovit-Orakel, dem zahlreiche Geschenke und Opfergaben aus der gesamten slawischen und der benachbarten nichtslawischen Welt dargebracht wurden.",
   "locator": "Belege des Svantovit-Kults",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chronica Slavorum karya Helmold von Bosau (abad ke-12) menggambarkan orakel Svantovit yang menerima banyak hadiah dan persembahan dari seluruh dunia Slavia dan tetangganya yang bukan Slavia.",
    "en": "The Chronica Slavorum of Helmold von Bosau (12th century) describes the Svantovit oracle, which received many gifts and offerings from the whole Slavic and neighbouring non-Slavic world."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "svetovit-c15",
   "source_id": "svetovit-s2",
   "quote": "Ein Steinrelief, das sich in der Kirche von Altenkirchen auf Rügen befindet und von dem man annehmen könnte, dass es Svantovit abbildet, zeigt ebenfalls eine männliche Figur mit einem großen Trinkhorn. Wahrscheinlicher ist aber, dass es sich dabei um einen Priester der Gottheit handelt.",
   "locator": "Svantovit-Kultstätte auf Rügen",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Relief batu di gereja Altenkirchen, Rügen, yang mungkin dianggap menggambarkan Svantovit, menampilkan sosok lelaki dengan tanduk minum besar; namun lebih mungkin itu pendeta sang dewa.",
    "en": "A stone relief in the church of Altenkirchen on Rügen, which one might take for Svantovit, shows a male figure with a large drinking horn; more likely it depicts a priest of the god."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Väinämöinen adalah setengah dewa dan pahlawan, tokoh sentral cerita rakyat Finlandia dan tokoh utama epos Kalevala, digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua bijak bersuara nyanyian magis.",
  "en": "Väinämöinen is a demigod and hero, the central figure of Finnish folklore and main character of the Kalevala, pictured as a wise old man with a magical singing voice.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "vainamoinen-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Väinämöinen adalah setengah dewa dan pahlawan, tokoh sentral cerita rakyat Finlandia, serta tokoh utama epos nasional Kalevala karya Elias Lönnrot. Ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua yang bijak dengan suara nyanyian yang sakti. Catatan tertulis pertama tentangnya terdapat dalam daftar dewa Tavastia yang disusun Mikael Agricola pada tahun 1551, dan para penulis menyebutnya dewa mantra, lagu, dan puisi; dalam banyak cerita ia menjadi tokoh pusat saat dunia lahir.",
   "en": "Väinämöinen is a demigod and hero, the central figure of Finnish folklore and the main character of Elias Lönnrot's national epic Kalevala. He is pictured as a wise old man with a powerful, magical singing voice. His first written mention is in Mikael Agricola's list of Tavastian gods from 1551, and writers called him the god of chants, songs and poetry; in many tales he stands at the centre of the world's birth.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vainamoinen-c01",
    "vainamoinen-c03",
    "vainamoinen-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia sering digambarkan sebagai pahlawan dukun, seorang tietäjä mitis dengan kemampuan magis, pengetahuan rahasia, dan keahlian menyanyi tertinggi; ada pula mitos lama tentang perjalanannya ke dunia bawah. Di Karelia, seekor burung bertelur di lututnya, dan ketika ia bergerak telur itu jatuh dan pecah menjadi bumi dan langit. Air adalah unsurnya, dan di Savonia ia dipandang sebagai penguasa air. Dalam adu nyanyian ia mengalahkan Joukahainen karena dialah yang membentuk dasar laut, menaruh bintang di langit, dan menciptakan gunung.",
   "en": "He is often described as a shaman hero, a mythical tietäjä with magical powers, secret knowledge and unmatched singing skill, and an old myth tells of his journey to the underworld. In Karelia a bird lays an egg on his knee, and when he moves, the eggs fall and break, forming earth and sky. Water is his element, and in Savonia he is seen as the ruler of water. In a singing contest he defeats Joukahainen, since it was he who shaped the sea floor, set the stars in the sky and made the mountains.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vainamoinen-c06",
    "vainamoinen-c08",
    "vainamoinen-c09",
    "vainamoinen-c19"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sebuah surat tahun 1674 dari Johan Cajanus menyebut bahwa di Kainuu ia dianggap putra raksasa bernama Kaleva. Dalam mitos yang banyak tercatat, ia berlayar, menciptakan kantele, dan memainkannya begitu indah hingga para dewi datang mendengarkan; dalam Kalevala ia membuat kantele dari tulang rahang ikan tombak raksasa dan bersama Lemminkäinen dan Ilmarinen berusaha merampas Sampo dari Louhi. Pada akhir Kalevala ia memanggil perahu tembaga dengan nyanyiannya, meninggalkan alam fana, dan berjanji akan kembali bila diperlukan.",
   "en": "A 1674 letter by Johan Cajanus says that in Kainuu he was called the son of a giant named Kaleva. In a widely attested myth he sails off, creates the kantele and plays it so well that even goddesses come to listen; in the Kalevala he makes the kantele from the jawbone of a huge pike and, with Lemminkäinen and Ilmarinen, tries to seize the Sampo from Louhi. At the end of the Kalevala he sings up a copper boat, leaves the mortal realm and promises to return when needed.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vainamoinen-c05",
    "vainamoinen-c07",
    "vainamoinen-c14",
    "vainamoinen-c15"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Pada abad kesembilan belas, Elias Lönnrot meragukan latar mitologis Väinämöinen dan menganggapnya pahlawan kuno atau dukun berpengaruh. Lönnrot menghapus sifat-sifat ketuhanannya yang langsung dan menjadikannya putra dewi purba Ilmatar, ciptaan Lönnrot sendiri; menurut Kalevala ia tumbuh dalam rahim ibunya selama 730 tahun dan sejak lahir telah bijak. Dalam epos Estonia Kalevipoeg, padanannya adalah dewa Vanemuine.",
   "en": "In the nineteenth century Elias Lönnrot questioned Väinämöinen's mythological background, treating him as an ancient hero or influential shaman. Lönnrot stripped away his direct godlike traits and made him the son of the primal goddess Ilmatar, whom Lönnrot invented himself; in the Kalevala he grows in his mother's womb for 730 years and is wise from birth. In the Estonian epic Kalevipoeg his counterpart is the god Vanemuine.",
   "claim_ids": [
