  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c08",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s1",
   "quote": "In Karelia, Väinämöinen is involved with events before the creation of the world ... A bird lays an egg on Väinämöinen's knee, and when Väinämöinen moves it, the eggs fall down and break, forming the earth and the sky. Väinämöinen's element is water, and he is widely connected to it in many regions.",
   "locator": "In Finnish mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Karelia, seekor burung bertelur di lutut Väinämöinen; ketika ia menggerakkannya, telur-telur jatuh dan pecah membentuk bumi dan langit. Unsur Väinämöinen adalah air, dan di banyak daerah ia dikaitkan dengannya.",
    "en": "In Karelia a bird lays an egg on Väinämöinen's knee; when he moves it, the eggs fall and break, forming earth and sky. His element is water, and many regions connect him with it."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c09",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s1",
   "quote": "Väinämöinen defeats Joukahainen in a runic singing battle with his superior knowledge over the events in the beginning of the world; after all, it was Väinämöinen himself who formed the bottom of the sea, set the stars in the sky and created mountains.",
   "locator": "In Finnish mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Väinämöinen mengalahkan Joukahainen dalam adu nyanyian rune berkat pengetahuannya tentang awal dunia, sebab dialah yang membentuk dasar laut, menaruh bintang di langit, dan menciptakan gunung.",
    "en": "Väinämöinen defeats Joukahainen in a rune-singing battle through his superior knowledge of the world's beginning, since he himself formed the sea floor, set the stars in the sky and created mountains."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c10",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s1",
   "quote": "Some runic singers thought this meant the death of Väinämöinen, while others believed he was still alive and would return one day, real death still waiting for him in the future.",
   "locator": "In Finnish mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian penyanyi rune menganggap kepergiannya ke pusaran air sebagai kematian Väinämöinen, sedangkan yang lain percaya ia masih hidup dan suatu hari akan kembali.",
    "en": "Some rune singers took this as Väinämöinen's death, while others believed he was still alive and would return one day."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c11",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s1",
   "quote": "In the nineteenth century, some folklorists, most notably Elias Lönnrot, the writer of Kalevala, disputed Väinämöinen's mythological background, claiming that he was an ancient hero, or an influential shaman who lived perhaps in the ninth century.",
   "locator": "In the Kalevala",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Elias Lönnrot, penulis Kalevala, membantah latar mitologis Väinämöinen dan menganggapnya pahlawan kuno atau dukun berpengaruh yang mungkin hidup pada abad kesembilan.",
    "en": "Elias Lönnrot, author of the Kalevala, disputed Väinämöinen's mythological background, claiming he was an ancient hero or an influential shaman who perhaps lived in the ninth century."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c12",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s1",
   "quote": "Stripping Väinämöinen from his direct godlike characteristics, Lönnrot turned Väinämöinen into the son of the primal goddess Ilmatar, whom Lönnrot had invented himself.",
   "locator": "In the Kalevala",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dengan menghapus sifat ketuhanan Väinämöinen yang langsung, Lönnrot menjadikannya putra dewi purba Ilmatar, yang diciptakan Lönnrot sendiri.",
    "en": "Stripping away his direct godlike traits, Lönnrot made Väinämöinen the son of the primal goddess Ilmatar, whom Lönnrot himself invented."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c13",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s2",
   "quote": "Er wuchs 730 Jahre lang im Leib seiner Mutter, der Lüftetochter Ilmatar, heran und besitzt seit seiner Geburt die Weisheit und eine magische Stimme.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Väinämöinen tumbuh selama 730 tahun dalam rahim ibunya, Ilmatar sang putri udara, dan sejak lahir memiliki kebijaksanaan serta suara magis.",
    "en": "He grew for 730 years in the womb of his mother Ilmatar, daughter of the air, and has had wisdom and a magical voice since birth."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c14",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s2",
   "quote": "Im Kalevala wird berichtet, dass Väinämöinen einen großen Hecht tötet und aus dessen Kieferknochen eine Kantele macht. Außerdem versucht er zusammen mit Lemminkäinen und Ilmarinen, das Sampo von Louhi zu rauben.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kalevala mengisahkan Väinämöinen membunuh ikan tombak besar dan membuat kantele dari tulang rahangnya; ia juga bersama Lemminkäinen dan Ilmarinen berusaha merampas Sampo dari Louhi.",
    "en": "The Kalevala tells that Väinämöinen kills a great pike and makes a kantele from its jawbone; with Lemminkäinen and Ilmarinen he also tries to steal the Sampo from Louhi."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c15",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s2",
   "quote": "Väinämöinen geht daraufhin an das Ufer des Meeres, singt sich mit seiner magischen Stimme ein Boot aus Kupfer herbei und verlässt das Reich der Sterblichen. In seinen letzten Worten sagt er, dass er irgendwann noch einmal gebraucht werden würde und dann zurückkehren werde.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Väinämöinen lalu pergi ke tepi laut, memanggil perahu tembaga dengan suara magisnya, dan meninggalkan alam fana; dalam kata-kata terakhirnya ia berkata suatu hari akan dibutuhkan lagi dan akan kembali.",
    "en": "Väinämöinen then goes to the seashore, sings up a copper boat with his magical voice and leaves the realm of mortals; in his last words he says he will be needed again some day and will return."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c16",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s2",
   "quote": "Im estnischen Nationalepos Kalevipoeg entspricht Väinämöinen dem Gott Vanemuine.",
   "locator": "Estnische Parallele",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam epos nasional Estonia Kalevipoeg, Väinämöinen sepadan dengan dewa Vanemuine.",
    "en": "In the Estonian national epic Kalevipoeg, Väinämöinen corresponds to the god Vanemuine."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c17",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s2",
   "quote": "So wird seine Geschichte in dem Fantasy-Roman Hammered (2011) des US-amerikanischen Autors Kevin Hearne fortgesponnen.",
   "locator": "Populärkultur",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah Väinämöinen dilanjutkan dalam novel fantasi Hammered (2011) karya penulis Amerika Kevin Hearne.",
    "en": "His story is continued in the fantasy novel Hammered (2011) by the American author Kevin Hearne."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c18",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s2",
   "quote": "Dies zeigt er unter anderem, indem er den ungestümen Joukahainen durch sein Singen in einem Sumpf versinken ließ.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kekuatan suaranya tampak antara lain ketika ia menenggelamkan Joukahainen yang gegabah ke rawa dengan nyanyiannya.",
    "en": "He shows this, among other things, by singing the rash Joukahainen down into a bog."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vainamoinen-c19",
   "source_id": "vainamoinen-s1",
   "quote": "Väinämöinen is also seen as the ruler of water in Savonia.",
   "locator": "In Finnish mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Savonia, Väinämöinen juga dipandang sebagai penguasa air.",
    "en": "In Savonia Väinämöinen is also seen as the ruler of water."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Kikimora adalah roh rumah perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia yang bisa bersikap baik atau jahat tergantung perilaku pemilik rumah; belakangan ia juga dibayangkan sebagai roh rawa.",
  "en": "The kikimora is a female house spirit of Slavic mythology who may be good or bad depending on the homeowner's behaviour; later she was also imagined as a swamp spirit.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "kikimora-c01",
   "kikimora-c02"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Kikimora adalah roh rumah perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia, yang perannya biasanya disandingkan dengan domovoi. Ia bisa menjadi roh \"baik\" atau \"jahat\", bergantung pada perilaku penghuni rumah, dan biasanya tinggal di balik tungku atau di ruang bawah tanah. Kepercayaan ini bermula pada masa ketika paganisme Slavia perlahan tergantikan oleh agama Kristen antara abad ke-8 dan ke-13, dan tetap kuat sesudahnya.",
   "en": "The kikimora is a female house spirit of Slavic mythology whose role is usually set beside that of the domovoy. She can be a \"good\" or a \"bad\" spirit depending on how the household behaves, and usually lives behind the stove or in the cellar. The belief arose as Slavic paganism slowly gave way to Christianity between the 8th and 13th centuries, and stayed strong afterwards.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kikimora-c01",
    "kikimora-c02",
    "kikimora-c04",
    "kikimora-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Wujudnya beragam: ia bisa bermoncong anjing, berparuh ayam, atau menyerupai kambing bermata menyala dan bertanduk, tetapi selalu perempuan, entah nenek tua atau gadis cantik. Ada dua jenis kikimora: yang dari hutan bersuami domovoi, sedangkan yang dari rawa bersuami leshy. Kikimora rawa digambarkan sebagai nenek kecil, buruk rupa, bungkuk, kurus, dan kusut, berhidung runcing dan berambut acak-acakan; ia menakuti orang, menjatuhkan pelancong dari jalan atau bahkan menenggelamkan mereka, dan menculik anak-anak.",
   "en": "Her looks vary: she may have a dog's snout, a chicken's beak or resemble a goat with glowing eyes and horns, but she is always female, either an old woman or a lovely girl. There are two kinds: the forest kikimora is married to the domovoi, while the swamp kikimora is married to the leshy. The swamp kikimora is pictured as a small, ugly, hunchbacked, thin, scruffy old woman with a pointed nose and tangled hair; she frightens people, knocks travellers off the road or even drowns them, and steals children.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kikimora-c05",
    "kikimora-c06",
    "kikimora-c07",
    "kikimora-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Bila rumah terurus, kikimora membantu menjaga ayam dan pekerjaan rumah; bila tidak, ia bersiul, memecahkan piring, dan membuat keributan pada malam hari, dan ia juga keluar malam-malam untuk memintal. Ia menghantui rumah tangga yang kotor, suami yang malas atau kasar, atau anak yang tidak disiplin, dan kepergiannya dipercaya bisa diupayakan dengan memperbaiki perilaku. Tukang bangunan yang tidak senang pada pemilik rumah bisa mengundangnya dengan menaruh boneka mirip kikimora di balok utama, dan sekali masuk ia sulit diusir.",
   "en": "In a tidy home the kikimora tends the hens and helps with chores; in a messy one she whistles, smashes crockery and stirs up a racket after dark, and she also comes out at night to spin. She haunts households that are kept dirty, with a lazy or abusive husband or undisciplined children, and mending one's ways is believed to drive her out. Builders displeased with a homeowner could summon her by placing a kikimora-like doll in the main beam, and once inside she is hard to remove.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "kikimora-c09",
    "kikimora-c10",
    "kikimora-c14",
    "kikimora-c17"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, siapa pun yang melihat kikimora sedang menenun akan segera mati. Untuk menenangkannya, semua panci dan wajan dicuci dengan teh pakis, sedangkan batu ular atau ranting juniper digantung di atas kandang ayam untuk melindungi ayam darinya.",
   "en": "It is believed that anyone who sees the kikimora weaving will soon die. To calm her, all pots and pans are washed with fern tea, while a snake stone or juniper twigs are hung over the henhouse to protect the chickens from her.",

   ]

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c01",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "Kikimora is a legendary creature, a female house spirit in Slavic mythology. Her role in the house is usually juxtaposed with that of the domovoy.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora adalah makhluk legenda, roh rumah perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia, yang perannya di rumah biasanya disandingkan dengan domovoi.",
    "en": "The kikimora is a legendary creature, a female house spirit in Slavic mythology, whose role in the house is usually set beside the domovoy's."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c02",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "The kikimora can either be a \"bad\" or a \"good\" spirit, which will depend on the behavior of the homeowner. In more recent times, an image of kikimora as a female swamp spirit has developed.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora bisa menjadi roh \"jahat\" atau \"baik\" bergantung pada perilaku pemilik rumah; belakangan berkembang gambaran kikimora sebagai roh rawa perempuan.",
    "en": "The kikimora can be a \"bad\" or \"good\" spirit depending on the homeowner's behaviour; more recently an image of her as a female swamp spirit has developed."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c03",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "Most sources link the suffix -mora with the Proto-Slavic *morà ('nightly spirit, bad dream') and the Proto-Germanic *marōn (id.), as in the modern English nightmare or French cauchemar.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian besar sumber mengaitkan akhiran -mora dengan Proto-Slavia *morà (\"roh malam, mimpi buruk\") dan Proto-Jermanik *marōn, seperti dalam kata Inggris nightmare atau Prancis cauchemar.",
    "en": "Most sources link the suffix -mora with Proto-Slavic *morà (\"nightly spirit, bad dream\") and Proto-Germanic *marōn, as in English nightmare or French cauchemar."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c04",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "The origin of the kikimora comes from the 8th to 13th century when Slavic paganism was slowly subsumed by Christianity. However, even after Christianity took hold in the Slavic regions, the belief of the kikimora was still strong.",
   "locator": "History",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Asal-usul kikimora berasal dari abad ke-8 hingga ke-13, saat paganisme Slavia perlahan tergantikan agama Kristen; bahkan setelah Kristen mapan, kepercayaan pada kikimora tetap kuat.",
    "en": "The kikimora originates from the 8th to 13th century, when Slavic paganism was slowly absorbed by Christianity; even after Christianity took hold, belief in her remained strong."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c05",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "The kikimora might have the snout of a dog, a chicken beak or can even resemble a goat-like entity with glowing eyes and horns. In fact, it can take any part of an animal's face or body. It is always feminine and can appear as an old woman or a beautiful girl.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora bisa bermoncong anjing, berparuh ayam, atau menyerupai makhluk mirip kambing bermata menyala dan bertanduk; ia bisa memiliki bagian wajah atau tubuh hewan mana pun, selalu perempuan, dan bisa tampak sebagai nenek tua atau gadis cantik.",
    "en": "The kikimora may have a dog's snout, a chicken's beak or resemble a goat-like being with glowing eyes and horns; she can have any animal's face or body part, is always female, and may appear as an old woman or a beautiful girl."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c06",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "The swamp kikimora is usually described as a small, ugly, hunchbacked, thin, and scruffy old woman with a pointed nose and disheveled hair.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora rawa biasanya digambarkan sebagai nenek kecil, buruk rupa, bungkuk, kurus, dan kusut, berhidung runcing dan berambut acak-acakan.",
    "en": "The swamp kikimora is usually described as a small, ugly, hunchbacked, thin and scruffy old woman with a pointed nose and dishevelled hair."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c07",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "It was believed that she frightens people, knocks travelers off the road or even drowns them. She also kidnaps children.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora rawa dipercaya menakuti orang, menjatuhkan pelancong dari jalan, bahkan menenggelamkan mereka, dan juga menculik anak-anak.",
    "en": "The swamp kikimora was believed to frighten people, knock travellers off the road or even drown them, and to kidnap children."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c08",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "There are two different kinds of kikimoras. The one that comes from the forest is married to the domovoi. The other one comes from the swamp ... and is married to leshy.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada dua jenis kikimora: yang berasal dari hutan bersuami domovoi, dan yang berasal dari rawa bersuami leshy.",
    "en": "There are two kinds of kikimora: the one from the forest is married to the domovoi, and the one from the swamp is married to the leshy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c09",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "When the house is in order, a kikimora looks after the chickens and housework. If not, she whistles, breaks dishes, and makes noises at night. She also comes out at night to spin thread.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bila rumah teratur, kikimora menjaga ayam dan pekerjaan rumah; bila tidak, ia bersiul, memecahkan piring, dan membuat keributan pada malam hari; ia juga keluar pada malam hari untuk memintal benang.",
    "en": "When the house is in order a kikimora looks after the chickens and housework; if not, she whistles, breaks dishes and makes noise at night; she also comes out at night to spin thread."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c10",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "A kikimora would haunt a household if a woman did not keep a clean house, if a husband was lazy or abusive, or if children were poorly disciplined. Reversing one's behaviour is believed to remove her presence from the house.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora menghantui rumah tangga bila rumah tidak dijaga bersih, suami malas atau kasar, atau anak tidak disiplin; mengubah perilaku dipercaya mengusirnya dari rumah.",
    "en": "A kikimora would haunt a household if the house was not kept clean, the husband was lazy or abusive, or the children undisciplined; changing one's behaviour is believed to remove her."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c11",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "The legendary kikimora provides the basis of Kikimora (op. 63), a tone poem for orchestra by Anatoly Lyadov.",
   "locator": "Legacy",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Legenda kikimora menjadi dasar Kikimora (op. 63), puisi nada untuk orkestra karya Anatoly Lyadov.",
    "en": "The kikimora legend is the basis of Kikimora (op. 63), a tone poem for orchestra by Anatoly Lyadov."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c12",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "The 2015 video game The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt and 2019 television series The Witcher both include depictions of kikimory, which are spider-like monsters that have no relation to folkloric kikimoras.",
   "locator": "Legacy",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gim video The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt (2015) dan serial televisi The Witcher (2019) menampilkan kikimory sebagai monster mirip laba-laba yang tidak berhubungan dengan kikimora cerita rakyat.",
    "en": "The 2015 video game The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt and the 2019 TV series The Witcher depict kikimory as spider-like monsters unrelated to folkloric kikimoras."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c13",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s2",
   "quote": "adalah makhluk perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia. Kikimora termasuk dalam makhluk penghun rumah dan kadang-kadang dikatakan menikah dengan Domovoi. Kikimora biasanya tinggal di balik kompor atau di gudang bawah tanah.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora adalah makhluk perempuan mitologi Slavia penghuni rumah, kadang dikatakan menikah dengan Domovoi, dan biasanya tinggal di balik tungku atau di ruang bawah tanah.",
    "en": "The kikimora is a female being of Slavic mythology who dwells in houses, is sometimes said to be married to the Domovoi, and usually lives behind the stove or in the cellar."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c14",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s2",
   "quote": "Jika para pekerja yang membangun rumah merasa tidak senang pada pemlik rumah maka mereka bisa mengundang Kikimora untuk menghantui rumah itu dengan cara menaruh sebuah boneka mirip Kikimora di balok utama atau di bawah sudut depan rumah.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pekerja yang membangun rumah dan tidak senang pada pemiliknya bisa mengundang kikimora untuk menghantui rumah itu dengan menaruh boneka mirip kikimora di balok utama atau di bawah sudut depan rumah.",
    "en": "Builders displeased with a homeowner could invite a kikimora to haunt the house by placing a kikimora-like doll in the main beam or under the front corner."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c15",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s2",
   "quote": "Barang siapa yang melihat Kikimora sedang menenun maka dia akan segera mati. Untuk menentramkan Kikimora, seseorang harus mencuci semua panci dan wajan dengan teh pakis.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Siapa pun yang melihat kikimora sedang menenun akan segera mati; untuk menenangkannya, semua panci dan wajan harus dicuci dengan teh pakis.",
    "en": "Whoever sees the kikimora weaving will soon die; to calm her, all pots and pans must be washed with fern tea."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c16",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s2",
   "quote": "Batu ular atau ranting junifer digantung di atas kadang ayam untuk melindungi ayam dari Kikimora.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Batu ular atau ranting juniper digantung di atas kandang ayam untuk melindungi ayam dari kikimora.",
    "en": "A snake stone or juniper twigs are hung over the henhouse to protect the chickens from the kikimora."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "kikimora-c17",
   "source_id": "kikimora-s1",
   "quote": "It is said that she can be identified by her wet footprints. When home builders wanted to cause harm to someone buying a house, they would bring in kikimora. Once she is inside, it is difficult to get her to leave.",
   "locator": "Features and behaviours",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kikimora konon bisa dikenali dari jejak kakinya yang basah; tukang bangunan yang ingin mencelakai pembeli rumah akan mendatangkan kikimora, dan sekali masuk ia sulit diusir.",
    "en": "She can reportedly be identified by her wet footprints; builders wanting to harm a house buyer would bring in a kikimora, and once inside she is hard to make leave."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Chernobog (\"dewa hitam\") dan Belobog adalah pasangan dewa yang saling berlawanan dan diduga berasal dari mitologi Polabia; keasliannya masih diperdebatkan.",
  "en": "Chernobog (\"black god\") and Belobog are a pair of opposing deities alleged to belong to Polabian mythology; their authenticity is still debated.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Chernobog dan Belobog adalah sepasang dewa yang saling berlawanan dan diduga termasuk mitologi Polabia. Chernobog pertama kali disebut dalam Kronik Bangsa Slavia abad ke-12 sebagai dewa kemalangan yang dipuja suku Wagri dan Obodrit, sedangkan Belobog baru muncul dalam tulisan abad ke-16 sebagai lawannya. Sebagian peneliti merekonstruksi pasangan ini sebagai pertentangan terang dan gelap, baik dan jahat, serta untung dan malang.",
   "en": "Chernobog and Belobog are a pair of opposed deities said to belong to Polabian mythology. Chernobog is first recorded in the 12th-century Chronicle of the Slavs, which calls him a bringer of ill fortune revered by the Wagri and Obodrites, while Belobog only turns up in 16th-century writing as his opposite. Some researchers reconstruct the pair as a contrast of light and dark, good and evil, luck and misfortune.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c01",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c02",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Helmold, penulis kronik itu, menceritakan bahwa dalam pesta dan perjamuan orang Slavia mengedarkan cawan persembahan sambil mengucapkan mantra atas nama dewa yang baik dan dewa yang jahat; dewa jahat itu mereka sebut iblis atau Chernobog, yaitu dewa hitam. Menurut sebuah kronik tahun 1590, mereka memuja Chernobog sebagai dewa jahat karena semua keburukan dianggap berada dalam kuasanya, sehingga mereka memohon belas kasihnya. Satu-satunya sumber sejarah berasal dari penulis Kristen yang menggambarkannya sebagai dewa jahat dan terkutuk, dan tidak diketahui apakah orang Slavia kuno sungguh menganggapnya jahat.",
   "en": "Helmold, the chronicle's author, relates that at feasts the Slavs passed round a sacrificial cup while uttering spells in the name of a good god and an evil god; the evil one they called the devil or Chernobog, that is, the black god. According to a chronicle of 1590, they worshipped Chernobog as an evil deity because all harm was thought to lie in his power, so they begged his mercy. The only historical sources are Christian writers who show him as wicked and accursed, and it is unknown whether the ancient Slavs truly saw him as evil.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c05",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c09",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c10",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c11",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c19"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Belobog disebut sebagai dewa untung dan kebahagiaan Slavia Barat yang keberadaannya hanya diduga; ia direkonstruksi antara lain dari nama tempat, dan di kalangan Sorbia terdapat bukit Bjely boh yang bermakna positif serta Corny boh yang bermakna negatif. Sumber abad ke-17 menyamakan Belobog dengan Vit (Svetovit), yang kuilnya berada di Arkona, pulau Rügen.",
   "en": "Belobog is described as a supposed West Slavic god of luck and happiness, reconstructed partly from place names; among the Sorbs there were hills called Bjely boh, with a positive meaning, and Corny boh, with a negative one. A 17th-century source equates Belobog with Vit (Svetovit), whose temple stood at Arkona on Rügen.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c14",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c15",
    "chernobog-and-belobog-c17"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Para peneliti berbeda pendapat apakah kedua dewa ini sungguh bagian dari kepercayaan Slavia: banyak ahli menganggap kedua nama itu asli, tetapi ada yang menganggapnya dewa semu, dan satu sumber menyebut Chernobog muncul karena salah tafsir atas kronik Helmold. Karena namanya baru tercatat pada abad ke-12, Chernobog diduga bukan dewa penting atau dewa tua. Dalam sumber-sumber kemudian, Chernobog dikaitkan dengan iblis dalam agama Kristen.",
   "en": "Researchers disagree on whether the two gods truly belonged to Slavic belief: many scholars accept both names as authentic, others see them as pseudo-deities, and one source says Chernobog arose from a misreading of Helmold's chronicle. Because his name is only recorded from the 12th century, Chernobog is thought not to have been an important or ancient god. Later sources associate Chernobog with the Christian devil.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c01",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s1",
   "quote": "are a pair of opposing deities alleged to be part of Polabian mythology.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chernobog dan Belobog adalah sepasang dewa yang saling berlawanan dan diduga merupakan bagian mitologi Polabia.",
    "en": "Chernobog and Belobog are a pair of opposing deities alleged to be part of Polabian mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c02",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s1",
   "quote": "The first written account of Chernobog is in the 12th-century Chronicle of the Slavs, where he is described as a god of misfortune worshipped by the Wagri and Obodrites; while Belobog is first noted in 16th century literature in opposition to Chernobog.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Catatan tertulis pertama tentang Chernobog ada dalam Kronik Bangsa Slavia abad ke-12, yang menyebutnya dewa kemalangan yang dipuja suku Wagri dan Obodrit; Belobog baru tercatat dalam tulisan abad ke-16 sebagai lawan Chernobog.",
    "en": "The first written account of Chernobog is in the 12th-century Chronicle of the Slavs, describing him as a god of misfortune worshipped by the Wagri and Obodrites; Belobog is first noted in 16th-century literature as his opposite."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c03",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s1",
   "quote": "Researchers disagree on whether the two deities were indeed part of Slavic folklore: while many scholars believe the two theonyms are authentic, others believe they are pseudo-deities. Both deities appear in later sources, however, with Chernobog being associated with the Christian devil.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Para peneliti berbeda pendapat apakah kedua dewa ini sungguh bagian kepercayaan Slavia: banyak ahli menganggap kedua nama itu asli, yang lain menganggapnya dewa semu; keduanya muncul dalam sumber kemudian, dan Chernobog dikaitkan dengan iblis Kristen.",
    "en": "Researchers disagree whether the two were truly part of Slavic folklore: many think both names authentic, others call them pseudo-deities; both appear in later sources, with Chernobog associated with the Christian devil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c04",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s2",
   "quote": "Chernobog (dari bahasa Proto-Slavia \"čĭrnŭ\" yang berarti \"hitam\", dan \"bogŭ\" yang berarti \"dewa\")",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama Chernobog berasal dari kata Proto-Slavia \"čĭrnŭ\" (hitam) dan \"bogŭ\" (dewa).",
    "en": "The name Chernobog comes from Proto-Slavic \"čĭrnŭ\" (black) and \"bogŭ\" (god)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c05",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s2",
   "quote": "Satu-satunya sumber sejarah berasal dari sumber Kristen yang menggambarkan dewa ini sebagai dewa yang jahat dan terkutuk, tetapi tidak diketahui apakah dewa ini benar-benar dianggap sebagai dewa yang jahat oleh orang-orang Slavia kuno.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Satu-satunya sumber sejarah tentang Chernobog berasal dari sumber Kristen yang menggambarkannya jahat dan terkutuk; tidak diketahui apakah orang Slavia kuno sungguh menganggapnya jahat.",
    "en": "The only historical sources on Chernobog are Christian ones depicting him as evil and accursed; it is unknown whether the ancient Slavs truly regarded him as evil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c06",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s2",
   "quote": "Nama ini disebutkan oleh suku-suku Slavia Barat pada abad ke-12, sehingga diduga ia bukan dewa yang sangat penting atau bukan dewa lama.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama Chernobog disebut oleh suku Slavia Barat pada abad ke-12, sehingga ia diduga bukan dewa yang sangat penting atau bukan dewa tua.",
    "en": "The name was used by West Slavic tribes in the 12th century, so he is thought not to have been a very important or old god."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c07",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Чернобог (Czorneboh, Czernebog) — в мифологии балтийских славян злой бог, приносящий несчастье.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chernobog (Чернобог; Czorneboh, Czernebog) dalam mitologi Slavia Baltik adalah dewa jahat yang membawa kemalangan.",
    "en": "Chernobog (Чернобог; Czorneboh, Czernebog) is, in the mythology of the Baltic Slavs, an evil god who brings misfortune."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c08",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Некоторыми учёными реконструируется пара Белобог/Чернобог, которая представляет собой противопоставление «светлое/тёмное», «добро/зло», «счастье/несчастье».",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian ahli merekonstruksi pasangan Belobog/Chernobog sebagai pertentangan \"terang/gelap\", \"baik/jahat\", dan \"untung/malang\".",
    "en": "Some scholars reconstruct the Belobog/Chernobog pair as an opposition of \"light/dark\", \"good/evil\" and \"luck/misfortune\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c09",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "во время пиров и возлияний они пускают вкруговую жертвенную чашу, произнося при этом, не скажу благословения, а скорее заклинания от имени богов, а именно, доброго бога и злого",
   "locator": "В мифологии",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Helmold, dalam pesta dan perjamuan orang Slavia mengedarkan cawan persembahan sambil mengucapkan mantra, bukan berkat, atas nama dewa yang baik dan dewa yang jahat.",
    "en": "According to Helmold, at feasts and drinking the Slavs passed round a sacrificial cup, uttering not blessings but spells in the name of a good god and an evil god."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c10",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Поэтому злого бога они на своём языке называют дьяволом, или Чернобогом, то есть чёрным богом.",
   "locator": "В мифологии",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena itu, dalam bahasa mereka dewa jahat disebut iblis atau Chernobog, yaitu dewa hitam.",
    "en": "Therefore in their language they call the evil god the devil, or Chernobog, that is, the black god."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c11",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Славяне для того почитали Чернобога, как злое божество, что они воображали, будто всякое зло находится в его власти, и потому просили его о помиловании",
   "locator": "В мифологии",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Slavia memuja Chernobog sebagai dewa jahat karena mengira semua keburukan berada dalam kuasanya, sehingga memohon belas kasihnya.",
    "en": "The Slavs worshipped Chernobog as an evil deity because they imagined all evil lay in his power, and so begged his mercy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c12",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Чернобог является главным злодеем и финальным боссом в шутере от первого лица Blood 1997 года выпуска.",
   "locator": "В массовой культуре",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chernobog adalah penjahat utama dan bos terakhir dalam gim tembak orang pertama Blood (1997).",
    "en": "Chernobog is the main villain and final boss of the 1997 first-person shooter Blood."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c13",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Чернобог — главный антагонист сегмента «Ночь на Лысой Горе/Аве Мария» из диснеевского полнометражного мультфильма 1940 года «Фантазия».",
   "locator": "В массовой культуре",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chernobog adalah antagonis utama segmen \"Ночь на Лысой Горе/Аве Мария\" dalam film animasi panjang tahun 1940 \"Фантазия\".",
    "en": "Chernobog is the main antagonist of the \"Ночь на Лысой Горе/Аве Мария\" segment of the 1940 animated feature \"Фантазия\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c14",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s4",
   "quote": "Белобог — предполагаемый западнославянский бог удачи и счастья. ... Ряд ученых считают имя Белобог вымышленным.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Belobog (Белобог) adalah dewa untung dan kebahagiaan Slavia Barat yang keberadaannya hanya diduga; sejumlah ahli menganggap nama Belobog rekaan.",
    "en": "Belobog (Белобог) is a supposed West Slavic god of luck and happiness; several scholars consider the name Belobog invented."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c15",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s4",
   "quote": "У лужицких сербов существовали названия гор Bjely boh и Corny boh — с первым из них связывалась положительная семантика, со вторым — отрицательная.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di kalangan Sorbia (лужицких сербов) terdapat nama bukit Bjely boh yang bermakna positif dan Corny boh yang bermakna negatif.",
    "en": "Among the Sorbs there were hills named Bjely boh, with positive meaning, and Corny boh, with negative meaning."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c16",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s4",
   "quote": "в которой говорится о божествах славян, в частности — «Белобог злой, а Чернобог добрый». Однако данные выдержки считаются рядом историков поддельными.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebuah kronik menyebut tentang dewa Slavia bahwa \"Belobog jahat, dan Chernobog baik\", tetapi kutipan itu dianggap palsu oleh sejumlah sejarawan.",
    "en": "A chronicle says of the Slavic gods that \"Belobog is evil and Chernobog good\", but several historians consider these excerpts forged."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c17",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "При этом Белобог выступает как второе имя Вита (Святовита), храм которого располагался в городе Арконе на острове Рюгене (Руяне).",
   "locator": "В мифологии",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam sumber abad ke-17 itu, Belobog muncul sebagai nama kedua Vit (Svetovit), yang kuilnya berada di kota Arkona di pulau Rügen.",
    "en": "In that 17th-century source Belobog appears as a second name of Vit (Svetovit), whose temple stood in Arkona on Rügen."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c18",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Появился из-за неправильной интерпретации \"Славянской хроники Гельмольда\"",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chernobog muncul karena salah tafsir atas \"Kronik Slavia\" karya Helmold.",
    "en": "Chernobog arose from a misinterpretation of Helmold's \"Slavic Chronicle\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chernobog-and-belobog-c19",
   "source_id": "chernobog-and-belobog-s3",
   "quote": "Чернобога упоминает Пётр Альбин в «Миснейской хронике» 1590 года:",
   "locator": "В мифологии",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Petrus Albinus menyebut Chernobog dalam Kronik Meissen tahun 1590.",
    "en": "Petrus Albinus mentions Chernobog in his Meissen Chronicle of 1590."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Turul adalah burung pemangsa mitologis dalam tradisi Hungaria dan Turk, umumnya digambarkan sebagai falkon, dan menjadi lambang nasional bangsa Hungaria.",
  "en": "The Turul is a mythological bird of prey of Hungarian and Turkic tradition, usually shown as a falcon, and a national symbol of the Hungarians.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "turul-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Turul adalah burung pemangsa mitologis dalam tradisi Hungaria dan Turk yang umumnya digambarkan sebagai falkon, dan kini menjadi lambang nasional bangsa Hungaria. Burung ini kemungkinan besar didasarkan pada falkon besar. Dalam bahasa Hungaria, turul adalah sebutan untuk satu jenis falkon, dan asal katanya kini dianggap paling mungkin dari bahasa Turk, dengan bentuk toġrïl atau toğrul yang berarti burung pemangsa sedang hingga besar dari famili Accipitridae.",
   "en": "The Turul is a mythological bird of prey of Hungarian and Turkic tradition, usually pictured as a falcon, and today a national symbol of the Hungarians. It is probably modelled on a large falcon. In Hungarian, turul named one kind of falcon, and the word is now thought most likely to be of Turkic origin, from toġrïl or toğrul, a medium to large bird of prey of the family Accipitridae.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "turul-c01",
    "turul-c02",
    "turul-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam legenda Emese yang tercatat dalam Gesta Hungarorum dan Chronicon Pictum, turul muncul dalam mimpi Emese ketika ia sudah hamil. Peran turul di sini adalah roh pelindung yang menjaga bayi Álmos dari bahaya, motif yang sangat mirip dengan peran Simurgh dalam epos Iran Shahnameh. Dalam mimpi kedua pemimpin suku Hungaria, elang-elang lambang bangsa Pecheneg menyerang kuda mereka, lalu turul datang menyelamatkannya.",
   "en": "Both the Gesta Hungarorum and the Chronicon Pictum preserve the tale of Emese, who sees the turul in a dream while already carrying her child. Its role there is that of a guardian spirit shielding the infant Álmos from harm, a motif very close to the Simurgh's role in the Iranian epic Shahnameh. In a second dream of the Hungarian tribal leader, eagles, the emblem of the Pechenegs, attacked their horses and the turul came to rescue them.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "turul-c04",
    "turul-c05",
    "turul-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam tradisi Hungaria, turul bermula sebagai lambang klan wangsa Árpád yang berkuasa pada abad ke-9 dan ke-10. Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum menyebut wangsa itu keturunan klan Turul, dan Gesta Hungarorum mencatat turul sebagai leluhur totemik Árpád. Sepasang cakram perak bermotif turul ditemukan di Rakamaz dari pemakaman Hungaria abad ke-10.",
   "en": "In Hungarian tradition the turul began as the clan emblem of the ruling Árpád dynasty in the 9th and 10th centuries. The Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum says the dynasty descended from the Turul clan, and the Gesta Hungarorum records the turul as Árpád's totemic ancestor. A pair of silver discs with a turul motif was found at Rakamaz in a 10th-century Hungarian cemetery.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "turul-c08",
    "turul-c09",
    "turul-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kini turul menjadi unsur utama lambang Angkatan Bersenjata Hungaria: burung bersayap terbentang yang mencengkeram pedang Raja Santo Stefanus. Patung turul di sebuah gunung dekat Tatabánya disebut patung burung terbesar di Eropa.",
   "en": "Today the turul is the centrepiece of the Hungarian Defence Forces emblem: a bird with outspread wings gripping the sword of King Saint Stephen. The turul statue on a mountain near Tatabánya is described as the largest bird statue in Europe.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "turul-c12",
    "turul-c13"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "turul-c01",

   "quote": "The Turul is a mythological bird of prey, mostly depicted as a falcon, in Hungarian tradition and Turkic tradition, and a national symbol of Hungarians.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",

    "en": "The Turul is a mythological bird of prey, mostly depicted as a falcon, in Hungarian and Turkic tradition, and a national symbol of Hungarians."

  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c02",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "The Turul is probably based on a large falcon. The Hungarian word turul meant one kind of falcon and the origin of the word is currently thought to be most likely Turkic",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {

    "en": "The Turul is probably based on a large falcon; the Hungarian word turul meant one kind of falcon, and its origin is now thought most likely Turkic."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c03",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "Toġrïl or toğrul means a medium to large bird of prey of the family Accipitridae, goshawk or red kite.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Toġrïl atau toğrul berarti burung pemangsa sedang hingga besar dari famili Accipitridae, seperti elang alap atau elang merah.",
    "en": "Toġrïl or toğrul means a medium to large bird of prey of the family Accipitridae, a goshawk or red kite."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c04",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "In the legend of Emese, recorded in the Gesta Hungarorum and the Chronicon Pictum, the turul is mentioned as occurring in a dream of Emese, when she was already pregnant.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam legenda Emese yang tercatat di Gesta Hungarorum dan Chronicon Pictum, turul disebut muncul dalam mimpi Emese ketika ia sudah hamil.",
    "en": "In the legend of Emese, recorded in the Gesta Hungarorum and the Chronicon Pictum, the turul appears in Emese's dream when she was already pregnant."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c05",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "The Turul's role is one of a protector spirit that protects the infant Álmos from harm. This is a very similar motif to the role of the Simurgh in the Iranian epic Shahnameh.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Peran turul adalah roh pelindung yang menjaga bayi Álmos dari bahaya, motif yang sangat mirip dengan peran Simurgh dalam epos Iran Shahnameh.",
    "en": "The Turul's role is a protector spirit shielding the infant Álmos from harm, a motif very similar to the Simurgh's role in the Iranian epic Shahnameh."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c06",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "In a second dream by the leader of the Hungarian tribes, in which eagles (the emblem of the Pechenegs, enemies of the Hungarians ) attacked their horses and the Turul came and saved them.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mimpi kedua pemimpin suku Hungaria, elang-elang (lambang bangsa Pecheneg, musuh Hungaria) menyerang kuda-kuda mereka, lalu turul datang menyelamatkannya.",
    "en": "In a second dream of the Hungarian tribal leader, eagles (the emblem of the Pechenegs, enemies of the Hungarians) attacked their horses and the Turul came and saved them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c07",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "The image of the Turul and its role is similar to that of the Norse Vedfolnir, which like it perched on the tree of life.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {

    "en": "The Turul's image and role are similar to the Norse Vedfolnir, which likewise perched on the tree of life."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c08",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "In Hungarian tradition, it originated as the clan symbol used in the 9th and 10th centuries by the ruling Árpád dynasty.",
   "locator": "Turul dynasty",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam tradisi Hungaria, turul bermula sebagai lambang klan yang dipakai wangsa Árpád yang berkuasa pada abad ke-9 dan ke-10.",
    "en": "In Hungarian tradition it originated as the clan symbol of the ruling Árpád dynasty in the 9th and 10th centuries."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c09",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "The Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum mentioned that the Árpád dynasty descended from the gens (clan) Turul, and the Gesta Hungarorum recorded that the Árpád's totemic ancestor was the Turul.",
   "locator": "Turul dynasty",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum menyebut wangsa Árpád keturunan klan Turul, dan Gesta Hungarorum mencatat bahwa leluhur totemik Árpád adalah turul.",
    "en": "The Gesta Hunnorum et Hungarorum says the Árpád dynasty descended from the Turul clan, and the Gesta Hungarorum records the Turul as Árpád's totemic ancestor."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c10",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "a divine vision appeared to his mother in a dream in the form of a falcon that, as if coming to her, impregnated her and made known to her that from her womb a torrent would come forth and from her loins glorious kings be generated",
   "locator": "Turul dynasty",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut Gesta Hungarorum, penampakan ilahi berwujud falkon muncul dalam mimpi ibu Álmos, seakan mendatangi dan membuahinya, serta memberitahukan bahwa dari rahimnya akan mengalir arus deras dan lahir raja-raja mulia.",
    "en": "According to the Gesta Hungarorum, a divine vision in the form of a falcon appeared in a dream to Álmos's mother, as if coming to her and impregnating her, and revealed that a torrent would come from her womb and glorious kings from her loins."

  },

   "statement": {

    "en": "A pair of silver discs with a Turul motif was found at Rakamaz, Hungary, in a 10th-century Hungarian cemetery."
   }
  },
  {

   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "The central element of the emblem of the Hungarian Defence Forces is the Turul bird with extended wings holding the sword of King Saint Stephen in its claws.",
   "locator": "Modern use",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {

    "en": "The central element of the Hungarian Defence Forces emblem is the Turul with outspread wings holding King Saint Stephen's sword in its claws."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "turul-c13",
   "source_id": "turul-s1",
   "quote": "The last of the three stands on a mountain near Tatabánya, Hungary, but the other two were destroyed. It is the largest bird statue in Europe, and the largest bronze statue in Central Europe.",
   "locator": "Modern use",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dari tiga patung turul besar, yang terakhir berdiri di gunung dekat Tatabánya, sedangkan dua lainnya hancur; patung itu adalah patung burung terbesar di Eropa dan patung perunggu terbesar di Eropa Tengah.",
    "en": "Of three large Turul statues, the last stands on a mountain near Tatabánya while the other two were destroyed; it is the largest bird statue in Europe and the largest bronze statue in Central Europe."

   "source_id": "turul-s2",
   "quote": "Turul adalah burung mitologis yang sering kali digambarkan seperti rajawali dalam tradisi Hungaria. Burung ini merupakan lambang nasional Hungaria dan Transilvania.",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Jiangshi adalah mayat hidup dalam legenda dan cerita rakyat Tiongkok, dikenal pula sebagai vampir melompat; dalam budaya populer ia digambarkan sebagai mayat kaku berbusana pejabat dinasti Qing.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Jiangshi adalah jenis mayat hidup atau mayat yang bangkit kembali dalam legenda dan cerita rakyat Tiongkok, yang juga dijuluki vampir melompat. Kata jiang dalam namanya secara harfiah berarti keras atau kaku, dan memang jiangshi selalu tidak lentur seperti orang hidup. Ia digambarkan berkulit putih kehijauan, mungkin dari jamur yang tumbuh pada mayat, berambut panjang, buas, rakus, dan sangat kuat.",
   "en": "The jiangshi is a type of undead being or reanimated corpse in Chinese legend and folklore, also nicknamed the hopping vampire. The syllable jiang in its name literally means hard or stiff, and the jiangshi indeed never has a living person's suppleness. It is said to have greenish-white skin, perhaps from fungus growing on corpses, long hair, and a fierce, ravenous nature with enormous strength.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jiangshi-c01",
    "jiangshi-c11",
    "jiangshi-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut sarjana dinasti Qing Ji Xiaolan, mayat bangkit karena dua sebab: orang yang baru meninggal hidup kembali, atau mayat yang lama dikubur tetapi tidak membusuk. Sebab lain yang disebut antara lain roh hun yang meninggalkan tubuh sementara roh po tetap tinggal dan mengendalikannya, atau mayat yang belum dikubur tersambar petir atau dilompati kucing hamil. Jiangshi biasanya membunuh makhluk hidup untuk menyerap qi mereka pada malam hari, dan siang harinya bersembunyi di peti mati atau tempat gelap seperti gua.",
   "en": "According to the Qing dynasty scholar Ji Xiaolan, a corpse is reanimated for one of two reasons: a recently dead person returns to life, or a long-buried body fails to decay. Other causes include the hun soul leaving the body while the po soul stays and takes control, or an unburied corpse being struck by lightning or leapt over by a pregnant cat. The jiangshi usually kills the living to absorb their qi at night, and by day hides in a coffin or a dark place such as a cave.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jiangshi-c08",
    "jiangshi-c09",
    "jiangshi-c10",
    "jiangshi-c18"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam budaya populer, jiangshi umumnya bergerak dengan melompat, sedangkan dalam cerita rakyat ia lebih menakutkan: bisa mengejar dengan berlari, dan bila sudah sangat tua atau cukup menyerap energi yang, ia bahkan bisa terbang. Cerita rakyat menyebut jiangshi datang dari perbukitan, melayang di udara, untuk memangsa bayi. Gambaran mayat kaku berbusana resmi dinasti Qing berasal dari pengaruh sinema Hong Kong, dan film seperti Mr. Vampire beserta sekuelnya menjadi film kultus komedi-horor.",
   "en": "In popular culture the jiangshi usually moves by hopping, but folklore makes it more fearsome: it can give chase by running and, if old enough or full of enough yang energy, can even fly. Folk tales describe one flying down from the hills to prey on babies. The image of a stiff corpse in Qing dynasty official dress comes from the influence of Hong Kong cinema, and films such as Mr. Vampire and its sequels became comedy-horror cult classics.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jiangshi-c02",
    "jiangshi-c03",
    "jiangshi-c04",
    "jiangshi-c17"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sejarawan J. J. M. de Groot berpendapat kepercayaan ini lahir dari rasa ngeri alami terhadap mayat, diperkuat oleh banyaknya jenazah yang tak dikuburkan di Tiongkok masa kekaisaran. Kepercayaan ini juga sebagian bersumber dari tradisi \"mengantar mayat\", yaitu memulangkan jenazah para buruh di provinsi Xiang (kini Hunan) ke kampung halaman: mayat diikat pada batang bambu dan dipikul sehingga tampak seperti melompat.",
   "en": "Historian J. J. M. de Groot suggested the belief grew from a natural horror of dead bodies, fed by the many unburied corpses of imperial China. It also drew partly on \"corpse-driving\", the return of dead labourers' bodies across Xiang province (now Hunan) to their home villages: the corpses were tied to bamboo poles and carried, which made them seem to hop.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "jiangshi-c05",
    "jiangshi-c06",
    "jiangshi-c07"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c01",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "A jiāngshī (simplified Chinese: 僵尸; traditional Chinese: 殭屍; pinyin: jiāngshī; Jyutping: goeng1 si1), also known as a Chinese hopping vampire, is a type of undead creature or reanimated corpse in Chinese legends and folklore.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jiangshi (僵尸; 殭屍), juga dikenal sebagai vampir melompat Tiongkok, adalah jenis makhluk mayat hidup atau mayat yang bangkit kembali dalam legenda dan cerita rakyat Tiongkok.",
    "en": "A jiangshi (僵尸; 殭屍), also known as a Chinese hopping vampire, is a type of undead creature or reanimated corpse in Chinese legends and folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c02",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Due to the influence of Hong Kong cinema, it is typically depicted in modern popular culture as a stiff corpse dressed in official garments from the Qing dynasty.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena pengaruh sinema Hong Kong, dalam budaya populer modern jiangshi biasanya digambarkan sebagai mayat kaku berbusana resmi dinasti Qing.",
    "en": "Due to Hong Kong cinema, modern popular culture usually depicts it as a stiff corpse in Qing dynasty official garments."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c03",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "In popular culture, it is commonly represented as hopping or leaping. In folkloric accounts, however, it is more formidable, capable of giving chase by running, and if sufficiently ancient or if it has absorbed sufficient yang energy, capable also of flight.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam budaya populer jiangshi umumnya digambarkan melompat; dalam cerita rakyat ia lebih menakutkan, bisa berlari mengejar, dan bila cukup tua atau cukup menyerap energi yang, juga bisa terbang.",
    "en": "In popular culture it is usually shown hopping; in folklore it is more formidable, able to run in pursuit and, if old enough or full of enough yang energy, to fly."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c04",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "According to folkloric understandings, \"jiāngshī came from the hills, soaring through the air, to devour the infants of the people\".",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut cerita rakyat, jiangshi datang dari perbukitan, melayang di udara, untuk memangsa bayi-bayi penduduk.",
    "en": "According to folklore, jiangshi came from the hills, soaring through the air, to devour the people's infants."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c05",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Historian J. J. M. de Groot suggests that the belief in jiangshi was the result of the natural horror at the sight of dead bodies, nourished by the presence of unburied corpses in imperial China",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sejarawan J. J. M. de Groot berpendapat bahwa kepercayaan pada jiangshi berasal dari kengerian alami saat melihat mayat, diperkuat oleh adanya jenazah yang tak dikuburkan di Tiongkok masa kekaisaran (imperial China).",
    "en": "Historian J. J. M. de Groot suggests the belief arose from natural horror at dead bodies, fed by unburied corpses in imperial China."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c06",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "The belief in jiangshi and its representation in the popular imagination was also partly derived from the habit of \"corpse-driving\", a practice involving the repatriation of the corpses of dead laborers across Xiang province (present-day Hunan) to their hometowns for burial in family gravesites.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kepercayaan pada jiangshi sebagian juga berasal dari kebiasaan \"mengantar mayat\", yaitu memulangkan jenazah buruh melintasi provinsi Xiang (kini Hunan) ke kampung halaman untuk dimakamkan di makam keluarga.",
    "en": "The belief also partly derived from \"corpse-driving\", the repatriation of dead labourers' corpses across Xiang province (now Hunan) to their hometowns for burial in family graves."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c07",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "The corpses were trussed up against bamboo sticks and carried by professionals known as corpse-drivers and transported over thousands of miles to their ancestral villages, which gave the impression of a hopping corpse.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jenazah diikat pada batang bambu dan dipikul oleh para pengantar mayat profesional sejauh ribuan mil ke desa leluhurnya, sehingga tampak seperti mayat yang melompat.",
    "en": "The corpses were tied to bamboo sticks and carried by professional corpse-drivers for thousands of miles to their ancestral villages, giving the impression of a hopping corpse."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c08",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "The Qing dynasty scholar Ji Xiaolan mentioned in his book Yuewei Caotang Biji ... that the causes for a corpse to be reanimated can be classified in either of two categories: a recently deceased person returning to life, or a corpse that has been buried for a long time but does not decompose.",
   "locator": "Origins",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sarjana dinasti Qing Ji Xiaolan dalam bukunya Yuewei Caotang Biji menggolongkan sebab mayat bangkit menjadi dua: orang yang baru meninggal hidup kembali, atau mayat yang lama dikubur tetapi tidak membusuk.",
    "en": "Qing dynasty scholar Ji Xiaolan, in his Yuewei Caotang Biji, classified reanimation into two kinds: a recently dead person returning to life, or a long-buried corpse that does not decompose."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c09",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "When the hun leaves the body after death but the po remains and takes control of the body, the dead person becomes a jiangshi.",
   "locator": "Origins",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bila roh hun meninggalkan tubuh setelah mati tetapi roh po tetap tinggal dan mengendalikan tubuh, orang itu menjadi jiangshi.",
    "en": "When the hun leaves the body after death but the po remains and takes control, the dead person becomes a jiangshi."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c10",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "The corpse comes to life after it is struck by a bolt of lightning, or when a pregnant cat (or a black cat in some tales) leaps across the coffin.",
   "locator": "Origins",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mayat yang belum dikubur bisa hidup kembali setelah tersambar petir, atau bila kucing hamil (atau kucing hitam dalam sebagian kisah) melompati peti matinya.",
    "en": "An unburied corpse comes to life when struck by lightning, or when a pregnant cat (or a black cat in some tales) leaps across the coffin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c11",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "in \"jiangshi\" literally means \"hard\" or \"stiff\".",
   "locator": "Appearance and attributes",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Aksara jiang dalam \"jiangshi\" secara harfiah berarti \"keras\" atau \"kaku\".",
    "en": "The character jiang in \"jiangshi\" literally means \"hard\" or \"stiff\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c12",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Jiangshi are described as having greenish-white skin, possibly deriving from fungus or mould growing on corpses. They have long hair and may behave like animals. They are ferocious, ravenous beings possessing extreme strength",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jiangshi digambarkan berkulit putih kehijauan, mungkin dari jamur yang tumbuh pada mayat, berambut panjang, bisa berperilaku seperti hewan, buas, rakus, dan sangat kuat.",
    "en": "Jiangshi are described with greenish-white skin, perhaps from fungus or mould on corpses, long hair and animal-like behaviour; they are ferocious, ravenous and extremely strong."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c13",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Jiangshi are also said to be terrified of their own reflections.",
   "locator": "Methods and items used to counter jiangshi",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jiangshi juga dikatakan takut pada bayangannya sendiri.",
    "en": "Jiangshi are also said to be terrified of their own reflections."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c14",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Fulu (Taoist talisman), stuck on the forehead to immobilise them.",
   "locator": "Methods and items used to counter jiangshi",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Fulu, jimat Tao, ditempel di dahi untuk melumpuhkan jiangshi.",
    "en": "Fulu, a Taoist talisman, is stuck on the forehead to immobilise jiangshi."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c15",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "According to Yuan Mei's book Zi Bu Yu, \"Evil spirits withdraw when they hear a rooster's call\"",
   "locator": "Methods and items used to counter jiangshi",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut buku Zi Bu Yu karya Yuan Mei, roh jahat mundur ketika mendengar kokok ayam jantan.",
    "en": "According to Yuan Mei's Zi Bu Yu, evil spirits withdraw when they hear a rooster's call."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c16",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "\"Peach is the essence of the Five Elements. It can subjugate evil auras and deter evil spirits.\"",
   "locator": "Methods and items used to counter jiangshi",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Persik adalah inti Lima Unsur, mampu menundukkan aura jahat dan menangkal roh jahat.",
    "en": "Peach is the essence of the Five Elements and can subdue evil auras and deter evil spirits."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c17",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Movies such as Mr. Vampire and its various sequels (Mr. Vampire II, Mr. Vampire III, and Mr. Vampire IV) became cult classics in comedy-horror and inspired a vampire craze in East Asia, including Taiwan and Japan.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Film seperti Mr. Vampire dan sekuelnya (Mr. Vampire II, III, dan IV) menjadi film kultus komedi-horor dan memicu demam vampir di Asia Timur, termasuk Taiwan dan Jepang.",
    "en": "Films such as Mr. Vampire and its sequels (Mr. Vampire II, III and IV) became comedy-horror cult classics and sparked a vampire craze in East Asia, including Taiwan and Japan."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c18",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s2",
   "quote": "Mereka biasanya digambarkan sebagai mayat kaku mengenakan pakaian resmi dari Dinasti Qing, dan bergerak dengan melompat-lompat, dengan lengan terentang. Membunuh makhluk hidup untuk menyerap qi, atau \"kekuatan hidup\" mereka, biasanya pada malam hari, sedangkan di siang hari, mereka ada di dalam peti mati atau bersembunyi di tempat gelap seperti gua.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jiangshi biasanya digambarkan sebagai mayat kaku berpakaian resmi Dinasti Qing yang melompat dengan lengan terentang; mereka membunuh makhluk hidup untuk menyerap qi pada malam hari, dan siang harinya berada di peti mati atau bersembunyi di tempat gelap seperti gua.",
    "en": "Jiangshi are usually depicted as stiff corpses in Qing dynasty official dress, hopping with arms outstretched; they kill the living to absorb their qi, usually at night, and by day stay in coffins or hide in dark places like caves."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c19",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s1",
   "quote": "Holding one's breath, which makes the jiangshi unable to find you as they are blind, though this contradicts the earlier statement that they are afraid of their own reflection.",
   "locator": "Methods and items used to counter jiangshi",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menahan napas membuat jiangshi tidak bisa menemukan orang karena mereka buta, meski hal ini bertentangan dengan pernyataan bahwa mereka takut pada bayangannya sendiri.",
    "en": "Holding one's breath keeps jiangshi from finding you since they are blind, though this contradicts the claim that they fear their own reflection."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "jiangshi-c20",
   "source_id": "jiangshi-s2",
   "quote": "\"Jiangshi\" dibaca goeng-si dalam bahasa Kanton.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Kanton, \"jiangshi\" dibaca goeng-si.",
    "en": "In Cantonese, \"jiangshi\" is pronounced goeng-si."
   }
  }
 ],

  "id": "Vila adalah peri perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Slavia, roh alam yang bisa bersikap ramah maupun berbahaya terhadap manusia.",
  "en": "The vila is a female fairy of Slavic folklore, a nature spirit who can be either friendly or dangerous towards people.",

  {
   "id": "Vila adalah peri Slavia yang mirip nimfa. Ia terutama dikenal di kalangan Slavia Selatan, tetapi beberapa variannya juga muncul dalam mitologi Slavia Barat. Menurut satu sumber, vila adalah roh alam perempuan yang biasanya digambarkan berkelompok, terutama dikaitkan dengan unsur air, tetapi juga hadir di hutan, ladang, dan pegunungan.",
   "en": "The vila is a Slavic fairy resembling a nymph. She is chiefly known among the South Slavs, though some variants also occur in West Slavic mythology. One source describes vile as female nature spirits usually pictured in groups, mainly tied to the element of water but also found in woods, fields and mountains.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vila-fairy-c01",
    "vila-fairy-c02",
    "vila-fairy-c14",

  {
   "id": "Dalam kisah rakyat, vila adalah gadis cantik bertubuh tembus pandang dan berambut panjang; kehilangan sehelai rambut saja berarti kematian baginya. Ada tiga golongan vila: penghuni darat dan hutan, nimfa air, serta nimfa awan atau udara. Mereka tampil sebagai angsa, elang, kuda, atau serigala, sedangkan vila awan muncul sebagai angin puyuh.",
   "en": "In folk tales the vila is a beautiful girl with a translucent body and long hair; losing even a single hair means her death. There are three kinds of vile: dwellers of land and forest, water nymphs, and cloud or air nymphs. They appear as swans, falcons, horses or wolves, while cloud vile appear as a whirlwind.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "vila-fairy-c07",
    "vila-fairy-c08",

   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Hubungan vila dengan manusia bersifat mendua. Mereka biasanya ramah, namun membalas dengan kejam bila dihina atau bila tarian lingkaran mereka didekati tanpa undangan. Mereka juga memiliki kekuatan gaib dan pandai menyembuhkan.",
   "en": "The vila's relationship with humans is ambivalent. In fairy tales they may kill people, ruin crops, confuse men's spirits with arrows and swap children for changelings, yet they may also aid a hero with magical objects and mounts.    "en": "Many heroes of Croatian and Serbian epic poetry count a vila as a sworn or blood sister. The vilas' love of fighting is considered unique in Slavic mythology.    "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "In fairy tales, they may act with malice towards them (killing people, destroying crops) but may also help the hero by giving him magical objects and mounts.",

   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam dongeng, vila bisa berbuat jahat kepada manusia (membunuh orang, merusak panen), tetapi juga bisa menolong pahlawan dengan memberi benda ajaib dan tunggangan.",

  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c05",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "They confuse men's spirits with their arrows. They steal children and substitute them with changelings.",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vila mengacaukan jiwa laki-laki dengan panah mereka, serta mencuri anak dan menggantinya dengan anak tukaran.",
    "en": "Vilas confuse men's spirits with their arrows and steal children, leaving changelings in their place."

  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c06",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "Their fondness for fighting is reminiscent of the teutonic Valkyrie and is unique in Slavic mythology.",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kegemaran vila bertempur mengingatkan pada Valkyrie dan unik dalam mitologi Slavia.",
    "en": "The vilas' fondness for fighting recalls the Valkyrie and is unique in Slavic mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c07",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "There are three kinds, those living on land and in forests ... water nymphs ... and cloud or air nymphs",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada tiga jenis vila: yang hidup di darat dan hutan, nimfa air, serta nimfa awan atau udara.",

   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c08",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "They appear as swans, falcons, horses, or wolves; cloud nymphs appear as a whirlwind.",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",

  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c09",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "In Croatian and Serbian epic poetry, many heroes have a vila as an elective or blood sister",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam puisi epik Kroasia dan Serbia, banyak pahlawan memiliki vila sebagai saudari angkat atau saudari sedarah.",
    "en": "In Croatian and Serbian epic poetry, many heroes have a vila as an elective or blood sister."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c10",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "They possess supernatural powers and are able in the art of healing. They build splendid castles at the edges of clouds.",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vila memiliki kekuatan gaib dan pandai menyembuhkan; mereka membangun istana megah di tepi awan.",
    "en": "Vilas have supernatural powers and are skilled in healing; they build splendid castles at the edges of clouds."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c11",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "In Slovakia, vile are the restless souls of deceased girls who lure young men into a deadly circle dance.",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Slowakia, vila adalah arwah gelisah gadis yang telah meninggal, yang memikat pemuda ke tarian lingkaran yang mematikan.",

  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c12",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "The etymology is unclear. Possible explanations are from the Slavic verb viti (вити, \"to wind around, twist, weave\")",

   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {

  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c13",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "Within the Czech tradition, víly are almost always malicious, unless respected and avoided.",

   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {

  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c14",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s2",
   "quote": "Die Wila (russisch вила, ukrainisch віла, polnisch wiła, tschechisch víla, serbokroatisch-kyrillisch vila), auch Samowila oder Samodiwa, ist ein weiblicher Naturgeist der slawischen Mythologie.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Wila (bahasa Rusia вила, Polandia wiła, Ceko víla), juga disebut Samowila atau Samodiwa, adalah roh alam perempuan dalam mitologi Slavia.",
    "en": "The Wila (Russian вила, Polish wiła, Czech víla), also called Samowila or Samodiwa, is a female nature spirit of Slavic mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c15",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s2",
   "quote": "In den Volkserzählungen sind Wilen schöne Mädchen mit durchsichtigem Körper und langen Haaren. Verlieren sie auch nur ein einziges Haar, bedeutet dies ihren Tod.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kisah rakyat, vila adalah gadis cantik bertubuh tembus pandang dan berambut panjang; kehilangan sehelai rambut saja berarti kematian baginya.",
    "en": "In folk tales vile are beautiful girls with translucent bodies and long hair; losing even one hair means their death."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c16",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s2",
   "quote": "Über ihre Verehrung im Rahmen des vorchristlichen Kultes berichtet bereits Prokopios von Caesarea im 6. Jahrhundert. Er nennt allerdings nicht den slawischen Namen, sondern bezeichnet sie als Nymphen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Prokopios von Caesarea sudah melaporkan pemujaan mereka dalam kultus pra-Kristen pada abad ke-6, tetapi ia tidak memakai nama Slavia dan menyebut mereka nimfa.",
    "en": "Prokopios von Caesarea already reported their worship in pre-Christian cult in the 6th century, though he called them nymphs rather than using the Slavic name."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c17",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s2",
   "quote": "Verwandte Geister sind die Rusalkas, deren Verehrung eng mit dem Totenkult verknüpft ist und die im Gegensatz zu den Wilen meist verhängnisvoll wirken.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Roh yang berkerabat adalah rusalka, yang pemujaannya terkait erat dengan kultus orang mati dan yang, berbeda dari vila, umumnya membawa celaka.",
    "en": "Related spirits are the rusalkas, whose worship is closely tied to the cult of the dead and who, unlike vile, are mostly harmful."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c18",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s2",
   "quote": "Den Menschen gegenüber sind sie meist wohlgesinnt, auch Hochzeiten zwischen einer Wila und einem Menschen kommen in den Erzählungen vor.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vila umumnya bersikap baik kepada manusia, dan pernikahan antara vila dan manusia juga muncul dalam kisah-kisah.",
    "en": "Vile are mostly well-disposed towards humans, and marriages between a vila and a human also occur in the tales."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c19",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s2",
   "quote": "Wilen oder Wil(l)is werden als Gruppenwesen beschrieben und überwiegend mit dem Wasserelement verbunden, treten aber auch in Wäldern, auf dem Feld und im Gebirge auf.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vila digambarkan sebagai makhluk berkelompok yang terutama dikaitkan dengan unsur air, tetapi juga muncul di hutan, ladang, dan pegunungan.",
    "en": "Vile are described as group beings mainly tied to the water element, but also appear in forests, fields and mountains."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "vila-fairy-c20",
   "source_id": "vila-fairy-s1",
   "quote": "Vile are usually friendly to people, but they can take horrible revenge on those who insult them, disregard their orders, or approach their circle dances uninvited. Their general amiability distinguishes them from the rusalki.",
   "locator": "Folkloric accounts",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Vila biasanya ramah kepada manusia, tetapi bisa membalas dendam dengan mengerikan kepada orang yang menghina mereka, mengabaikan perintah mereka, atau mendekati tarian lingkaran mereka tanpa diundang; keramahan inilah yang membedakan mereka dari rusalka.",
    "en": "Vile are usually friendly to people but can take horrible revenge on those who insult them, ignore their orders or approach their circle dances uninvited; this general amiability sets them apart from the rusalki."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

  "id": "Dhampir adalah makhluk dalam cerita rakyat Balkan yang lahir dari hubungan antara vampir dan manusia, dan dipercaya mampu melihat serta memburu vampir.",
