 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Balkan, dhampir adalah makhluk hasil hubungan antara vampir dan manusia. Biasanya ayahnya vampir laki-laki dan ibunya perempuan manusia; kisah tentang vampir perempuan yang mendapat anak dari laki-laki manusia jarang ada. Kata dhampir berasal dari bahasa Albania, yang meminjamnya dari kata Slavia Selatan vampir.",
   "en": "In Balkan folklore the dhampir is a creature born of a union between a vampire and a human. Usually the father is a male vampire and the mother a human woman; tales of female vampires bearing children by human men are rare. The word dhampir is Albanian, itself borrowed from South Slavic vampir.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "dhampir-c01",
    "dhampir-c02",
    "dhampir-c03"

  {
   "id": "Menurut legenda, dhampir umumnya hidup sebagai warga biasa. Di Kosovo, putra dhampir dipandang sebagai agen magis utama untuk membinasakan vampir, bahkan kadang satu-satunya yang mampu melihatnya.",
   "en": "Legends say dhampirs mostly lived as ordinary members of the community. Yet they, especially sons with a vampire father, could see invisible vampires and work sorcery, and often became vampire hunters, a calling passed down the generations.   {
   "id": "Beberapa tradisi menyebut tanda-tanda anak vampir.    "en": "Some traditions list signs of a vampire's child. In Bulgarian folklore these include a soft body without nails or bones; other signs were a prominent nose or unusually large ears, teeth or eyes. In some areas a true dhampir was said to have a slippery, jelly-like body and a short life.",

   "id": "Satu sumber menggambarkan dhampir sebagai makhluk dari legenda Eropa Timur dan Rusia yang tampak seperti manusia biasa, tetapi mampu mendeteksi dan membunuh vampir.    "en": "One source describes the dhampir as a being from Eastern European and Russian legend who looks like an ordinary human yet can detect and kill vampires.    "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "In Balkan folklore, a dhampir ... is a mythical creature that is the result of a union between a vampire and a human.",
   "locator": "lead",

   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Balkan, dhampir adalah makhluk mitos hasil hubungan antara vampir dan manusia.",

  {

   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "This union was usually between male vampires and female humans, while stories of female vampires mating with male humans are rare.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {

    "en": "This union was usually between male vampires and human women; stories of female vampires mating with human men are rare."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c03",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "The word dhampir is an Albanian word which in turn is borrowed from South Slavic vampir. The shift v > dh is a feature of Albanian.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata dhampir adalah kata bahasa Albania yang dipinjam dari kata Slavia Selatan vampir; perubahan v menjadi dh adalah ciri bahasa Albania.",
    "en": "The word dhampir is Albanian, borrowed from South Slavic vampir; the shift v > dh is a feature of Albanian."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c04",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "In the rest of the region, terms such as Serbian vampirović, vampijerović, vampirić (thus, Bosnian lampijerović, etc.) literally meaning \"vampire's son\", are used.",
   "locator": "Variants",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di wilayah lain dipakai istilah seperti vampirović, vampijerović, dan vampirić dalam bahasa Serbia (dan lampijerović dalam bahasa Bosnia), yang berarti \"putra vampir\".",
    "en": "Elsewhere in the region, terms such as Serbian vampirović, vampijerović and vampirić (and Bosnian lampijerović), meaning \"vampire's son\", are used."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c05",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "Legends state that dhampirs were, for the most part, normal members of the community. But dhampirs, especially males of paternal vampire descent, could see invisible vampires and practise sorcery, often starting careers as vampire hunters, which would be practiced for generations from father to son.",
   "locator": "Features in folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut legenda, dhampir umumnya warga biasa, tetapi mereka, terutama laki-laki keturunan ayah vampir, dapat melihat vampir tak kasatmata dan mempraktikkan sihir, sering menjadi pemburu vampir turun-temurun dari ayah ke anak.",
    "en": "Legends say dhampirs were mostly ordinary community members, but they, especially males with a vampire father, could see invisible vampires and practise sorcery, often becoming vampire hunters from father to son."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c06",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "In Kosovo, a dhampir (specifically a son) was regarded as a \"chief magical agent for the destruction of vampires\", and sometimes the dhampir was thought to be the only one capable of seeing a vampire.",
   "locator": "Features in folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Kosovo, dhampir (khususnya putra) dipandang sebagai agen magis utama untuk membinasakan vampir, dan kadang dianggap satu-satunya yang mampu melihat vampir.",
    "en": "In Kosovo a dhampir (specifically a son) was seen as the chief magical agent for destroying vampires, and sometimes the only one able to see a vampire."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c07",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "In Bulgarian folklore, possible indications include being \"very dirty\", having a soft body, no nails or bones",
   "locator": "Features in folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Bulgaria, tanda anak vampir antara lain sangat kotor, bertubuh lunak, serta tidak berkuku dan tidak bertulang.",
    "en": "In Bulgarian folklore, possible signs include being very dirty and having a soft body with no nails or bones."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c08",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "In contrast, a pronounced nose was often a sign, as were larger than normal ears, teeth, or eyes.",
   "locator": "Features in folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tanda lain adalah hidung yang menonjol serta telinga, gigi, atau mata yang lebih besar dari biasa.",
    "en": "Other signs were a prominent nose and unusually large ears, teeth or eyes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c09",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "in some areas, a true dhampir possessed a \"slippery, jelly-like body and lived only a short life",
   "locator": "Features in folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di beberapa daerah, dhampir sejati dikatakan bertubuh licin seperti jeli dan berumur pendek.",
    "en": "In some areas a true dhampir had a slippery, jelly-like body and lived only a short life."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c10",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s1",
   "quote": "Serbian ethnologist Tatomir Vukanović recorded that Muslims called half-vampire people \"Dhampir\" (masculine) and \"Dhampiresa\" (feminine).",
   "locator": "Variants",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Etnolog Serbia Tatomir Vukanović mencatat bahwa kaum Muslim menyebut orang setengah vampir \"Dhampir\" (laki-laki) dan \"Dhampiresa\" (perempuan).",
    "en": "Serbian ethnologist Tatomir Vukanović recorded that Muslims called half-vampire people \"Dhampir\" (male) and \"Dhampiresa\" (female)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c11",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s2",
   "quote": "外見は普通の人間と変わらないが、不死である吸血鬼を殺す力を持つ。また、吸血鬼を探知する能力も備える。",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Penampilan dhampir tidak berbeda dari manusia biasa, tetapi ia memiliki kekuatan untuk membunuh vampir yang abadi serta kemampuan mendeteksi vampir.",
    "en": "A dhampir looks like an ordinary human but has the power to kill the undying vampire and the ability to detect vampires."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c12",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s2",
   "quote": "大抵は生まれてもすぐに死んでしまうが、成長した者は前述の能力を活かし、吸血鬼ハンターを生業とすることもある。",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kebanyakan dhampir meninggal tak lama setelah lahir, tetapi yang tumbuh dewasa kadang memanfaatkan kemampuannya untuk mencari nafkah sebagai pemburu vampir.",
    "en": "Most dhampirs die soon after birth, but those who grow up sometimes use their abilities to make a living as vampire hunters."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c13",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s2",
   "quote": "ダンピールによる吸血鬼退治は、儀式のような形式を取る。笛を吹いたり、走り回ったり、目に見えない敵と戦った後、勝利を宣言する。",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perburuan vampir oleh dhampir berbentuk seperti ritual: meniup seruling, berlari ke sana kemari, dan bertarung dengan musuh tak terlihat, lalu menyatakan kemenangan.",
    "en": "A dhampir's vampire hunt takes a ritual form: blowing a flute, running about and fighting an invisible foe, then declaring victory."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c14",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s2",
   "quote": "吸血鬼になった者は、人間だったころの家族の下に戻ってくることがある。その際、血液ではなく性行為を要求する吸血鬼がいる。",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang yang telah menjadi vampir kadang kembali kepada keluarganya semasa hidup, dan ada vampir yang saat itu meminta hubungan badan, bukan darah.",
    "en": "Someone who has become a vampire may return to the family of their human life, and some such vampires demand intercourse rather than blood."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "dhampir-c15",
   "source_id": "dhampir-s2",
   "quote": "ダンピール (Dhampir) は、東欧やロシアの伝説に登場する、ヒトと吸血鬼の混血。",

  "id": "Sirin adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, berkepala perempuan cantik dan bertubuh burung, yang nyanyiannya bisa membuat orang melupakan segalanya.",
  "en": "The Sirin is a legendary creature of Russian folklore with a beautiful woman's head and a bird's body, whose song can make people forget everything.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Sirin adalah makhluk legenda dari cerita rakyat Rusia. Ia berkepala perempuan cantik dan bertubuh burung, biasanya burung hantu, dan sering disamakan dengan siren dalam mitologi Yunani. Menurut mitos, Sirin tinggal di Iriy atau di sekitar Sungai Efrat. Satu sumber menyebut legendanya mungkin berasal dari Persia.",
   "en": "The Sirin is a legendary creature of Russian folklore. She is pictured as a bird, usually an owl, bearing a lovely woman's head, and is often equated with the sirens of Greek mythology. Myth places her home in Iriy or near the Euphrates River. One source suggests her legend may be of Persian origin.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "sirin-c01",
    "sirin-c02",
    "sirin-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Berbeda dari siren yang memikat pelaut menuju maut, Sirin memakai suaranya untuk mengganjar orang saleh. Ia menyanyikan lagu indah bagi para santo dan meramalkan kebahagiaan yang akan datang. Namun bagi manusia biasa ia berbahaya: laki-laki yang mendengarnya melupakan segala hal duniawi, mengikutinya, dan akhirnya mati. Orang berusaha mengusirnya dengan tembakan meriam, dentang lonceng, dan bunyi keras lainnya.",
   "en": "Unlike the sirens who lure sailors to death, the Sirin uses her voice to reward the virtuous. She sang lovely songs to the saints, foretelling joys to come. To ordinary mortals, however, she was dangerous: men who heard her forgot everything earthly, followed her and finally died. People tried to drive her off with cannon fire, ringing bells and other loud noises.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "sirin-c06",
    "sirin-c07",
    "sirin-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sumber berbeda pendapat tentang sifatnya. Pada abad ke-17 hingga ke-18, citranya berubah menjadi lambang keselarasan dunia, dan orang percaya hanya orang bahagia yang dapat mendengarnya.",
   "en": "Sources disagree about her nature. One calls the Sirin the bird of happiness and the Alkonost the bird of sorrow, while another makes the Sirin the bird of grief, rather ill-disposed to humans, and the Alkonost the bird of happiness.     "sirin-c12"

  {
   "id": "Sirin kadang dipandang sebagai kiasan firman Tuhan yang masuk ke jiwa manusia, kadang sebagai kiasan bidah yang menggoda orang lemah. Bersama Alkonost dan Gamayun, ia termasuk burung peramal dalam cerita rakyat Rusia. Pengarang Vladimir Nabokov pun pernah memakai Sirin sebagai nama samaran.",
   "en": "The Sirin is sometimes read as a metaphor for God's word entering a person's soul, and sometimes for heretics tempting the weak.  "claims": [
  {
   "id": "sirin-c01",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "is a legendary creature in Russian folklore, with the head of a beautiful woman and the body of a bird (usually an owl), often equated with sirens in Greek mythology.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sirin adalah makhluk legenda dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, berkepala perempuan cantik dan bertubuh burung (biasanya burung hantu), sering disamakan dengan siren dalam mitologi Yunani.",
    "en": "The Sirin is a legendary creature in Russian folklore with a beautiful woman's head and a bird's body (usually an owl), often equated with the sirens of Greek mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c02",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "According to myth, the Sirin lived in Iriy or around the Euphrates River. The Sirin is considered the bird of happiness; it is related to the Alkonost, the bird of sorrow.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut mitos, Sirin tinggal di Iriy atau di sekitar Sungai Efrat; ia dianggap burung kebahagiaan dan berkaitan dengan Alkonost, burung kesedihan.",
    "en": "According to myth the Sirin lived in Iriy or around the Euphrates; she is considered the bird of happiness, related to the Alkonost, the bird of sorrow."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c03",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "The legend of the Sirin may have a Persian origin. The Pomors often depicted the Sirin on illustrations in the Book of Genesis as birds sitting in paradise trees.",
   "locator": "History",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Legenda Sirin mungkin berasal dari Persia; orang Pomor sering menggambarkannya dalam ilustrasi Kitab Kejadian sebagai burung yang bertengger di pohon surga.",
    "en": "The Sirin legend may be of Persian origin; the Pomors often drew her in Book of Genesis illustrations as birds sitting in paradise trees."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c04",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "Later, in the 17th–18th centuries, the image of the Sirin changed and she started to symbolize world harmony (as she lives near paradise).",
   "locator": "History",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada abad ke-17–18, citra Sirin berubah dan ia mulai melambangkan keselarasan dunia karena tinggal di dekat surga.",
    "en": "In the 17th–18th centuries the image of the Sirin changed and she came to symbolize world harmony, as she lives near paradise."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c05",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "People in those times believed only happy people could hear the Sirin, while only very few could see one because she is as fast and difficult to catch as human happiness.",
   "locator": "History",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang pada masa itu percaya hanya orang bahagia yang dapat mendengar Sirin, dan sangat sedikit yang bisa melihatnya karena ia secepat dan sesulit ditangkap seperti kebahagiaan manusia.",
    "en": "People then believed only happy people could hear the Sirin, and very few could see her, as she is as quick and elusive as human happiness."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c06",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "However, instead of using its voice to lure sailors to their deaths, the Sirin uses it to reward the virtuous. She was usually portrayed wearing a crown or with a nimbus.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Alih-alih memakai suaranya untuk memikat pelaut menuju maut, Sirin memakainya untuk mengganjar orang saleh; ia biasanya digambarkan bermahkota atau berlingkaran cahaya.",
    "en": "Instead of using her voice to lure sailors to death, the Sirin uses it to reward the virtuous; she was usually shown wearing a crown or a nimbus."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c07",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "The Sirin sang beautiful songs to the saints, foretelling future blisses. The bird was dangerous. Men who heard her would forget everything on earth, follow her, and ultimately die.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sirin menyanyikan lagu indah bagi para santo, meramalkan kebahagiaan mendatang; burung ini berbahaya, karena laki-laki yang mendengarnya melupakan segala hal di bumi, mengikutinya, dan akhirnya mati.",
    "en": "The Sirin sang beautiful songs to the saints, foretelling future bliss; the bird was dangerous, for men who heard her forgot everything on earth, followed her and finally died."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c08",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "People would attempt to save themselves from the Sirin by shooting cannons, ringing bells and making other loud noises to scare the bird off.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang berusaha menyelamatkan diri dari Sirin dengan menembakkan meriam, membunyikan lonceng, dan membuat bunyi keras lain untuk mengusirnya.",
    "en": "People tried to save themselves from the Sirin by firing cannons, ringing bells and making other loud noises to scare her off."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c09",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "According to folk tales, on the morning of the Apple Feast of the Saviour, the Sirin flies into the apple orchard and cries sadly.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kisah rakyat, pada pagi Pesta Apel Sang Juru Selamat, Sirin terbang ke kebun apel dan menangis sedih.",
    "en": "According to folk tales, on the morning of the Apple Feast of the Saviour, the Sirin flies into the apple orchard and cries sadly."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c10",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "The Sirin is sometimes seen as a metaphor for God's word going into the soul of a man. Sometimes she is seen as a metaphor of heretics tempting the weak.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sirin kadang dipandang sebagai kiasan firman Tuhan yang masuk ke jiwa manusia, kadang sebagai kiasan bidah yang menggoda orang lemah.",
    "en": "The Sirin is sometimes seen as a metaphor for God's word entering a man's soul, and sometimes for heretics tempting the weak."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c11",
   "source_id": "sirin-s1",
   "quote": "In Russian folklore, Sirin was mixed with the revered religious writer, Ephrem the Syrian. Thus, peasant lyrists such as Nikolay Klyuev often used the Sirin as a synonym for poet.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rusia, Sirin dicampuradukkan dengan penulis religius Ephrem the Syrian, sehingga penyair petani seperti Nikolay Klyuev memakai Sirin sebagai sinonim untuk penyair.",
    "en": "In Russian folklore the Sirin was mixed up with the religious writer Ephrem the Syrian, so peasant poets such as Nikolay Klyuev used Sirin as a synonym for poet."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c12",
   "source_id": "sirin-s2",
   "quote": "Die Sirin sind die magischen Vögel der Trauer und des Kummers. Im Gegensatz dazu stehen die Alkonost, die Vögel des Glücks und der Hoffnung. Die Sirin sind den Menschen eher böse gesinnt, die Alkonost hingegen nicht.",
   "locator": "Merkmale",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sirin adalah burung ajaib duka dan kesedihan, berlawanan dengan Alkonost, burung kebahagiaan dan harapan; Sirin cenderung berniat buruk kepada manusia, sedangkan Alkonost tidak.",
    "en": "The Sirin are magic birds of grief and sorrow, in contrast to the Alkonost, birds of happiness and hope; the Sirin are rather ill-disposed to humans, the Alkonost are not."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "sirin-c13",
   "source_id": "sirin-s2",
   "quote": "Sirin (russisch Сирин) ist der Name einer Sagengestalt des russischen Volksglaubens. Es handelt sich um Vögel, die rein äußerlich Eulen ähneln, aber Kopf und Brust einer schönen Frau besitzen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sirin (bahasa Rusia Сирин) adalah tokoh legenda kepercayaan rakyat Rusia, berupa burung yang mirip burung hantu tetapi berkepala dan berdada perempuan cantik.",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Naga Wawel (Smok Wawelski) adalah naga terkenal dalam legenda Polandia yang tinggal di gua di bawah Bukit Wawel dan meneror kota Kraków sampai dibunuh dengan umpan berisi belerang.",
  "en": "The Wawel Dragon (Smok Wawelski) is a famous dragon of Polish legend who lived in a cave beneath Wawel Hill and plagued Kraków until killed with a sulfur-filled bait.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "wawel-dragon-c01",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Naga Wawel adalah naga terkenal dalam legenda Polandia. Kisah tertua tentangnya berasal dari abad ke-13, dalam karya yang dikaitkan dengan Wincenty Kadłubek. Menurut catatan itu, naga pemakan manusia tersebut mengganggu kota Kraków yang didirikan Raja Krak, dan ditenangkan dengan jatah ternak setiap minggu.",
   "en": "The Wawel Dragon is a famous dragon of Polish legend. The oldest known telling dates from the 13th century, in a work attributed to Wincenty Kadłubek. In that account the man-eating beast troubled the city of Kraków founded by King Krak and was kept calm with a weekly share of cattle.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "wawel-dragon-c01",
    "wawel-dragon-c02",
    "wawel-dragon-c03",
    "wawel-dragon-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam versi tertua, putra-putra raja mengalahkannya dengan memberinya kulit sapi berisi belerang yang membara sehingga naga mati terbakar; lalu putra yang lebih muda membunuh kakaknya agar mendapat pujian sendirian, dan akhirnya diusir. Kronik abad ke-15 karya Jan Długosz menukar peran kedua pangeran dan menyebut Raja Krak sendiri sebagai perancang siasat; naga itu menyemburkan api sesaat sebelum mati.",
   "en": "In the oldest version the king's sons defeat it by feeding it a cattle skin packed with smouldering sulfur, so the dragon dies in flames; the younger son then kills his brother to take all the credit and is later banished. Jan Długosz's 15th-century chronicle swaps the princes' roles and credits King Krak himself with the plan; the dragon breathes fire just before it dies.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "wawel-dragon-c04",
    "wawel-dragon-c06",
    "wawel-dragon-c07",
    "wawel-dragon-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kronika Polska karya Marcin Bielski (1597) memberi jasa itu kepada Skub atau Skuba, seorang tukang sepatu. Dalam versi ini naga tidak dapat menelan umpan belerang dan terus minum air sampai mati, lalu si tukang sepatu diberi hadiah besar. Versi populer lain menyebut seorang magang tukang sepatu bernama Dratewka yang mengisi bangkai domba dengan belerang; naga minum di Sungai Vistula sampai perutnya pecah, dan Dratewka menikahi putri raja.",
   "en": "Marcin Bielski's Kronika Polska (1597) gives the credit to Skub or Skuba, a cobbler. In this version the dragon cannot swallow the sulfur bait and drinks water until it dies, and the shoemaker is richly rewarded. Another popular version names a young shoemaker's apprentice, Dratewka, who stuffs a dead lamb with sulfur; the dragon drinks from the Vistula until its belly bursts, and Dratewka marries the king's daughter.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "wawel-dragon-c09",
    "wawel-dragon-c10",
    "wawel-dragon-c12",
    "wawel-dragon-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Naga itu dikatakan tinggal di Smocza Jama, gua naga di bawah Bukit Wawel di tepi Sungai Vistula. Dalam kebanyakan versi populer, ia lebih suka memangsa gadis muda dan hanya bisa ditenangkan dengan persembahan seorang gadis setiap bulan. Pada tahun 1970 sebuah patung logam naga ini karya Bronisław Chromy dipasang di depan gua; patung itu menyemburkan api dari lubang hidungnya.",
   "en": "The dragon is said to have lived in Smocza Jama, the dragon's cave beneath Wawel Hill on the banks of the Vistula. In most popular versions it preferred to eat young girls and could only be soothed by a monthly offering of a girl. In 1970 a metal sculpture of the dragon by Bronisław Chromy was set up before the cave; it breathes fire from its nostrils.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c01",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "also known as the Dragon of Wawel Hill, is a famous dragon in Polish legend.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga Wawel, juga dikenal sebagai Naga Bukit Wawel (Dragon of Wawel Hill), adalah naga terkenal dalam legenda Polandia.",
    "en": "The Wawel Dragon, also known as the Dragon of Wawel Hill, is a famous dragon in Polish legend."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c02",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "According to the earliest account (13th century), a dragon (holophagos, \"one who swallows whole\") plagued the capital city of Kraków established by legendary King Krak (or Krakus, Gracchus, etc.).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kisah tertua (abad ke-13), seekor naga (holophagos, \"yang menelan bulat-bulat\") mengganggu ibu kota Kraków yang didirikan Raja Krak yang legendaris.",
    "en": "According to the earliest account (13th century), a dragon (holophagos, \"one who swallows whole\") plagued the capital Kraków, founded by the legendary King Krak."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c03",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "The man-eating monster was being appeased with a weekly ration of cattle, until finally being defeated by the king's sons using decoy cows stuffed with sulfur.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Monster pemakan manusia itu ditenangkan dengan jatah ternak setiap minggu, sampai akhirnya dikalahkan putra-putra raja dengan sapi umpan berisi belerang.",
    "en": "The man-eating monster was appeased with a weekly ration of cattle until the king's sons defeated it with decoy cows stuffed with sulfur."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c04",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "But the younger prince (\"Krak the younger\" or \"Krak junior\") murdered his elder brother to take sole credit, and was banished afterwards.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namun pangeran yang lebih muda membunuh kakaknya agar mendapat pujian sendirian, lalu diusir.",
    "en": "But the younger prince murdered his elder brother to take sole credit and was afterwards banished."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c05",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "The oldest known telling of the story comes from the 13th-century work attributed to Bishop of Kraków and historian of Poland, Wincenty Kadłubek.",
   "locator": "Literary history",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah tertua yang diketahui berasal dari karya abad ke-13 yang dikaitkan dengan Uskup Kraków dan sejarawan Polandia, Wincenty Kadłubek.",
    "en": "The oldest known telling comes from a 13th-century work attributed to the Bishop of Kraków and Polish historian Wincenty Kadłubek."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c06",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "They fed him a cattle skin stuffed with smoldering sulfur, causing his fiery death.",
   "locator": "Polish Chronicle (13th c.)",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka memberinya kulit sapi berisi belerang membara sehingga naga mati terbakar.",
    "en": "They fed him a cattle skin stuffed with smouldering sulfur, causing his fiery death."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c07",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "Jan Długosz's 15th-century chronicle, however, swapped the roles of the princes, claiming that the younger son named Lech was the killer, while the elder son named Krak, Jr. became the victim.",
   "locator": "Derivative chronicles",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kronik abad ke-15 karya Jan Długosz menukar peran kedua pangeran: putra bungsu bernama Lech menjadi pembunuh, sedangkan putra sulung Krak muda menjadi korban.",
    "en": "Jan Długosz's 15th-century chronicle swapped the princes' roles: the younger son Lech was the killer and the elder son Krak Jr. the victim."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c08",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "The dragon ate the burning meal and died breathing fire just before death. Długosz also adds the detail that the dragon lived in a cave of Mount Wawel upon which King Krak had built his castle.",
   "locator": "Derivative chronicles",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga memakan umpan yang menyala dan mati sambil menyemburkan api sesaat sebelumnya; Długosz menambahkan bahwa naga tinggal di gua Gunung Wawel, tempat Raja Krak membangun istananya.",
    "en": "The dragon ate the burning bait and breathed fire just before dying; Długosz adds that it lived in a cave of Mount Wawel, where King Krak had built his castle."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c09",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "Later, Marcin Bielski's Kronika Polska (1597) gave credit to Skub or Skuba the Cobbler",
   "locator": "Shoemaker version",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kemudian Kronika Polska karya Marcin Bielski (1597) memberi jasa kepada Skub atau Skuba, si tukang sepatu.",
    "en": "Later, Marcin Bielski's Kronika Polska (1597) credited Skub or Skuba the Cobbler."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c10",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "The dragon was unable to swallow this, and drank water until it died. Afterwards, the shoemaker was rewarded handsomely.",
   "locator": "Shoemaker version",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga tidak dapat menelan umpan itu dan minum air sampai mati; tukang sepatu itu kemudian diberi hadiah besar.",
    "en": "The dragon could not swallow it and drank water until it died; the shoemaker was then richly rewarded."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c11",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Er soll in der Smocza Jama (Drachenhöhle) unter dem Wawelhügel an den Ufern der Weichsel gehaust haben.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga itu konon tinggal di Smocza Jama (gua naga) di bawah Bukit Wawel di tepi Sungai Vistula (Weichsel).",
    "en": "It is said to have lived in Smocza Jama (the dragon's cave) beneath Wawel Hill on the banks of the Vistula."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c12",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Eines Tages meldete sich ein junger Schusterlehrling namens Dratewka für die Aufgabe. Er füllte ein totes Lamm mit Schwefel (nach manchen Versionen auch Ätzkali) und ließ es vor dem Drachenhort liegen.",
   "locator": "Geschichte",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Suatu hari seorang magang tukang sepatu muda bernama Dratewka menyanggupi tugas itu; ia mengisi bangkai domba dengan belerang dan meletakkannya di depan sarang naga.",
    "en": "One day a young shoemaker's apprentice named Dratewka took on the task; he filled a dead lamb with sulfur and left it before the dragon's lair."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c13",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Er trank so lange, bis er vor lauter Wasser im Bauch platzte und verendete. Dratewka durfte nun die Königstochter heiraten und lebte fortan glücklich mit ihr weiter.",
   "locator": "Geschichte",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga minum terus sampai perutnya pecah karena air dan mati; Dratewka lalu boleh menikahi putri raja dan hidup bahagia.",
    "en": "The dragon drank until its belly burst with water and it died; Dratewka was allowed to marry the king's daughter and lived happily."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "wawel-dragon-c14",
   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "In den meisten Versionen bevorzugte der Drache junge Mädchen zu fressen, man konnte ihn nur durch die monatliche Darbietung eines Mädchens vor seiner Höhle besänftigen.",
   "locator": "Geschichte",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {

    "en": "In most versions the dragon preferred to eat young girls and could only be soothed by a monthly offering of a girl in front of its cave."

   "source_id": "wawel-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "An jedem Tag schlug der böse Drache einen Pfad der Zerstörung durch das Land, tötete die Bürger, steckte ihre Heime in Brand und verschlang ihr Vieh.",
   "locator": "Geschichte",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {

 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Mavka adalah roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat Ukraina, \"jiwa hutan\" berambut panjang yang memikat laki-laki muda ke dalam hutan hingga menemui ajal.",
  "en": "The mavka is a female spirit of Ukrainian folklore, a long-haired \"soul of the forest\" who lures young men into the woods to their death.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Mavka adalah sejenis roh perempuan dalam cerita rakyat dan mitologi Ukraina. Ia dikenal sebagai \"jiwa hutan\" berambut panjang dan biasanya digambarkan sebagai penggoda yang menuntun laki-laki menuju kematian. Namanya berasal dari kata yang berarti \"orang mati\", istilah untuk arwah mereka yang meninggal secara tragis dan terlalu cepat.",
   "en": "The mavka is a kind of female spirit in Ukrainian folklore and mythology. Known as a long-haired \"soul of the forest\", she is usually pictured as a temptress who leads men to their deaths. Her name derives from a word meaning \"the dead\", a term for the spirits of those who died tragically and too early.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mavka-c01",
    "mavka-c02",
    "mavka-c14"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Roh ini dianggap sebagai jiwa gadis yang mati secara tidak wajar, tragis, atau terlalu dini, terutama bayi yang belum dibaptis. Bila jiwa bayi itu tidak masuk surga dalam tujuh tahun, ia berubah menjadi mavka dan bergentayangan. Untuk menyelamatkannya, orang harus melempar kain kepala ke atas saat hari raya Pentakosta, menyebut namanya, dan mengucapkan kata pembaptisan.",
   "en": "These spirits were held to be the souls of girls who died unnatural, tragic or early deaths, especially unbaptized babies. If such a baby's soul did not reach heaven within seven years, it became a mavka and haunted the earth. To save it, one had to toss a kerchief up during the Pentecost holidays, say its name and speak words of baptism.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mavka-c03",
    "mavka-c09",
    "mavka-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Mavka sering tampil sebagai gadis muda yang cantik, tinggi, berwajah bulat, berambut panjang, dan kadang telanjang. Ia memikat pemuda ke hutan lalu \"menggelitik\" mereka sampai mati. Mavka tidak memantulkan bayangan di air dan tidak berbayang. Subjenisnya, nyavka, berperilaku sama tetapi \"tidak berpunggung\", sehingga tulang belakang dan organ dalamnya terlihat.",
   "en": "The mavka often appears as a beautiful young girl, tall, round-faced, long-haired and sometimes naked. She lures young men into the woods and \"tickles\" them to death.    "claim_ids": [
    "mavka-c04",
    "mavka-c06",
    "mavka-c15"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Mavka dipercaya hidup berkelompok di hutan, gua gunung, atau gudang yang mereka hiasi permadani. Pada musim semi mereka menanam bunga di pegunungan untuk memikat pemuda. Saat Pentakosta mereka mengadakan permainan, tarian, dan pesta pora dengan iringan seruling setan. Namun menurut beberapa kisah, mereka juga membantu petani dengan menjaga ternak dan mengusir binatang liar. Dalam sastra, mavka antara lain muncul dalam The Forest Song karya Lesia Ukrainka.",
   "en": "Mavkas were thought to dwell together in woods, in mountain caves or in sheds adorned with rugs. In spring they planted flowers in the mountains to lure young men.    "quote": "Das Wort Mawka kommt von Naw, was auf Altslawisch „Toter“ bedeutet und ein Begriff für Geister der Sterblichen, die tragisch und zu früh gestorben waren, ist.",

  {
   "id": "mavka-c15",
   "source_id": "mavka-s2",
   "quote": "Äußerlich werden sie als weibliche Figur, groß, mit einem runden Gesicht, langem Haar und manchmal nackt beschrieben.",
   "locator": "Eigenschaften und Rezeption",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Secara lahiriah mereka digambarkan sebagai sosok perempuan yang tinggi, berwajah bulat, berambut panjang, dan kadang telanjang.",
    "en": "Outwardly they are described as female figures, tall, round-faced, long-haired and sometimes naked."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mavka-c16",
   "source_id": "mavka-s1",
   "quote": "Mavka is different from other types of female spirits in that her evil is not intentional. At the sight of a young man, she falls into a trance and realizes her actions too late to change anything.",
   "locator": "Notes",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mavka berbeda dari roh perempuan lain karena kejahatannya tidak disengaja; saat melihat pemuda ia jatuh dalam keadaan tak sadar dan baru menyadari perbuatannya ketika sudah terlambat.",
    "en": "The mavka differs from other female spirits in that her evil is unintentional; on seeing a young man she falls into a trance and realizes her actions too late."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mavka-c17",
   "source_id": "mavka-s1",
   "quote": "they held games, dances, and orgies. A demon accompanied them on a flute or pipes.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Saat Pentakosta mereka mengadakan permainan, tarian, dan pesta pora, diiringi setan yang memainkan seruling atau pipa.",
    "en": "At Pentecost they held games, dances and orgies, accompanied by a demon on flute or pipes."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Naga Slavia adalah sebutan untuk naga mana pun dalam mitologi Slavia, seperti zmei dari Rusia atau żmij dari Polandia, yang sering digambarkan sebagai ular bersayap.",
  "en": "Slavic dragon is the name for any dragon in Slavic mythology, such as the Russian zmei or the Polish żmij, often pictured as a winged serpent.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Naga Slavia mencakup semua naga dalam mitologi Slavia, termasuk żmij dari Polandia, zmei dari Rusia, dan padanannya di budaya Slavia lain. Istilah-istilah itu berasal dari kata Proto-Slavia *zmьjь, dan kebanyakan merupakan bentuk maskulin dari kata Slavia untuk \"ular\". Wujudnya memadukan naga klasik dan ular, sebagai ular bersayap, dan kadang digambarkan berkaki dua atau berkepala lebih dari satu.",
   "en": "The Slavic dragon covers every dragon of Slavic mythology, including the Polish żmij, the Russian zmei and their counterparts in other Slavic cultures. These terms descend from Proto-Slavic *zmьjь, and most are masculine forms of the Slavic word for \"snake\". In shape it blends the classical dragon with a snake, as a winged serpent, and is sometimes shown with two legs or several heads.",
   "claim_ids": [

    "slavic-dragon-c02",
    "slavic-dragon-c06",
    "slavic-dragon-c11"

  {
   "id": "Zmei bisa berwujud binatang atau mirip manusia: berwujud naga di udara dan manusia di darat, kadang merayu perempuan, tetapi dalam sastra Rusia sering menjadi musuh utama. Motif umum tentang naga Slavia antara lain perannya sebagai penguasa cuaca atau sumber air, asal-usulnya sebagai ular, dan kisah cinta naga jantan maupun betina dengan manusia. Dalam kepercayaan Rusia, ular, kadal, ayam jantan, atau ikan mas yang mencapai umur tertentu dapat berubah menjadi zmei terbang.",
   "en": "A zmei may be beast-like or human-like, taking dragon form in the air and human form on the ground; it sometimes woos women but in Russian literature often plays the chief villain. Common motifs include its role as master of weather or water sources, its beginning as a snake, and love affairs between humans and male or female dragons.    "claim_ids": [

    "slavic-dragon-c07",

   "id": "Di Balkan, zmei pada umumnya dianggap baik, sebagai naga roh penjaga yang melawan naga jahat seperti lamia.    "en": "In the Balkans the zmei is generally seen as benevolent, a guardian-spirit dragon set against evil dragons such as the lamia.    "id": "Bentuk-bentuk khusus naga Slavia antara lain Zmey Gorynych dari legenda Rusia dan Ukraina, yang berkepala tiga sampai dua belas (satu sumber menyebutnya berkepala tiga, hijau, dan dapat menyemburkan api), serta smok dari Polandia, Ukraina, dan Belarus, misalnya Naga Wawel dari Kraków.    "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s2",

   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagian besar nama naga Slavia adalah bentuk maskulin dari kata Slavia untuk \"ular\".",
    "en": "Most of these dragon names are masculine forms of the Slavic word for \"snake\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c12",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Drachen sind in Slowenien normalerweise im böswilligen Bereich angesiedelt, und sie werden meist gemeinsam mit St. Georg dargestellt.",
   "locator": "Die Kreatur im südslawischen Bereich",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Slovenia (Slowenien), naga biasanya digolongkan jahat dan sering digambarkan bersama Santo Georg (St. Georg).",
    "en": "In Slovenia dragons are usually placed on the malevolent side and mostly depicted together with St. Georg."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c13",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Drachen sind jedoch nicht immer dem Menschen feindlich gesinnt. So beschützt etwa der Ljubljanski Zmaj (Laibacher Drache) die Stadt Ljubljana und wird dafür auch auf dem Stadtwappen abgebildet.",
   "locator": "Die Kreatur im südslawischen Bereich",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namun naga tidak selalu memusuhi manusia; Ljubljanski Zmaj (naga Ljubljana) melindungi kota Ljubljana dan karena itu tampil di lambang kotanya.",
    "en": "Dragons are not always hostile to humans; the Ljubljanski Zmaj protects the city of Ljubljana and so appears on its coat of arms."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c14",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Der Slawische Drache ist die Entsprechung des klassischen europäischen Drachen in der slawischen Mythologie.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Naga Slavia adalah padanan naga Eropa klasik dalam mitologi Slavia.",
    "en": "The Slavic dragon is the counterpart of the classical European dragon in Slavic mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c15",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s2",
   "quote": "Er ist dreiköpfig und grün, geht auf seinen zwei Hinterbeinen, besitzt eher kleine Vorderbeine und kann Feuer spucken.",
   "locator": "Die Kreatur im ostslawischen Bereich",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zmei Gorynytsch berkepala tiga dan berwarna hijau, berjalan dengan dua kaki belakang, berkaki depan kecil, dan dapat menyemburkan api.",
    "en": "Zmei Gorynych is three-headed and green, walks on two hind legs, has rather small forelegs and can breathe fire."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c16",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "The Polish smok (e.g. Wawel Dragon of Kraków) or the Ukrainian or Belarusian smok (смок), tsmok (цмок), can also be included.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Smok dari Polandia (misalnya Naga Wawel dari Kraków) serta smok atau tsmok dari Ukraina dan Belarus juga dapat dimasukkan.",
    "en": "The Polish smok (e.g. the Wawel Dragon of Kraków) and the Ukrainian or Belarusian smok or tsmok can also be included."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c17",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "The Romanian zmeu could also be deemed a \"Slavic\" dragon, but a non-cognate etymology has been proposed.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zmeu dari Romania juga bisa dianggap naga \"Slavia\", tetapi ada usulan etimologi yang tidak seasal.",
    "en": "The Romanian zmeu could also be considered a \"Slavic\" dragon, but a non-cognate etymology has been proposed."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c18",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "There is a pan-Balkan notion that the zmei (known by various cognates) is a sort of \"guardian-spirit dragon\" against the \"evil\" types of dragon",
   "locator": "Benevolent zmei of the Balkans",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada gagasan di seluruh Balkan bahwa zmei adalah semacam \"naga roh penjaga\" yang melawan jenis-jenis naga \"jahat\".",
    "en": "There is a pan-Balkan notion that the zmei is a kind of \"guardian-spirit dragon\" against the \"evil\" kinds of dragon."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "slavic-dragon-c19",
   "source_id": "slavic-dragon-s1",
   "quote": "A favorite topic of folk songs was the male zmey-lover who may marry a woman and carry her to the underworld, or a female zmeitsa (zmeitza) who falls in love with a shepherd.",
   "locator": "Balkan Slavic dragons",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tema kesukaan lagu rakyat adalah zmey jantan yang menikahi perempuan dan membawanya ke dunia bawah, atau zmeitsa betina yang jatuh cinta kepada penggembala.",
    "en": "A favourite theme of folk songs was the male zmey lover who may marry a woman and carry her to the underworld, or a female zmeitsa who falls for a shepherd."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

 "traits": [],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Ajatar adalah roh perempuan jahat dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, dikaitkan dengan ular dan dipercaya menyebarkan penyakit; sebagian sumber menyebutnya roh hutan berwujud naga.",
  "en": "Ajatar is an evil female spirit of Finnish folklore, linked with serpents and said to spread disease; some sources call her a forest spirit in dragon form.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "ajatar-c01",
   "ajatar-c02",
   "ajatar-c10"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, Ajatar adalah roh perempuan yang jahat. Ia digambarkan berkepang rambut sampai ke tumit dan berpayudara menjuntai sampai ke lutut. Lewat hubungannya dengan Hiisi dan Lempo, ia dikatakan menyebarkan penyakit dan wabah. Ia erat dikaitkan dengan ular, dan dalam seni modern sering digambarkan sebagai naga atau sosok setengah manusia setengah ular.",
   "en": "Finnish folk belief knows Ajatar as a malicious female spirit, pictured with a braid reaching her heels and breasts hanging to her knees. Through her ties to Hiisi and Lempo she is said to spread disease and plague. She is closely linked with serpents, and modern art often shows her as a dragon or a half-human, half-serpent figure.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ajatar-c01",
    "ajatar-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sumber lain menyebut Ajatar roh hutan jahat berwujud naga, ibu iblis, yang menyusui ular; siapa pun yang memandangnya akan jatuh sakit. Para penulis lama menyebutnya penyihir jahat dan troll hutan, atau roh hutan perempuan yang menakutkan dan cepat, yang menyesatkan orang. Namun menurut tradisi lain, ia justru roh pelindung perempuan yang memesona, dihormati para dewa laki-laki.",
   "en": "Another source calls Ajatar an evil forest spirit in dragon form, the mother of the devil, who suckles serpents; anyone who looks at her falls ill. Older writers called her an evil witch and forest troll, or a frightening, swift female forest spirit who leads people astray. Yet another tradition makes her a bewitchingly beautiful female guardian spirit at whose feet the male gods lie.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ajatar-c03",
    "ajatar-c10",
    "ajatar-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Nama Ajatar mungkin berasal dari kata kerja Finlandia ajattaa, \"mengejar\", sehingga dapat diartikan \"pengejar perempuan\" dengan akhiran feminin -tar. Bentuk \"Ajatar\" sendiri dibakukan oleh Matthias Castrén. Ada pula yang menghubungkan namanya dengan kata Lituania aitvaras, dan namanya mungkin muncul sebagai Aattara dalam lagu rune Finlandia Barat tentang asal-usul ular. Ajatar tidak muncul dengan nama itu dalam lagu rakyat Finlandia yang terdokumentasi, tetapi muncul dalam karya fiksi yang terilhami Kalevala, misalnya drama Kullervo karya Aleksis Kivi (1860).",
   "en": "The name Ajatar may come from the Finnish verb ajattaa, \"to pursue\", giving \"female pursuer\" with the feminine suffix -tar. The form \"Ajatar\" itself was standardized by Matthias Castrén. Some have linked the name to the Lithuanian word aitvaras, and it may appear as Aattara in Western Finnish runic songs on the origin of snakes. Ajatar does not appear by that name in documented Finnish folk songs, but she does in fiction inspired by the Kalevala, such as Aleksis Kivi's play Kullervo (1860).",

   "quote": "In Finnish folklore Ajatar is an evil female spirit. She is described as having \"hair-plait reached to her heels and whose breasts hung down to her knees\"",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, Ajatar adalah roh perempuan jahat yang digambarkan berkepang rambut sampai ke tumit dan berpayudara menjuntai sampai ke lutut.",
    "en": "In Finnish folklore Ajatar is an evil female spirit described with a braid reaching her heels and breasts hanging to her knees."

  {
   "id": "ajatar-c02",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "Through her connections with Hiisi and Lempo, she is said to spread disease and pestilence. She is closely associated with serpents, and is often depicted in modern art as a dragon or half-humanoid and serpentine figure.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Lewat hubungannya dengan Hiisi dan Lempo, ia dikatakan menyebarkan penyakit dan wabah; ia erat dikaitkan dengan ular dan dalam seni modern sering digambarkan sebagai naga atau sosok setengah manusia setengah ular.",
    "en": "Through her ties to Hiisi and Lempo she is said to spread disease and pestilence; she is closely linked with serpents and modern art often shows her as a dragon or half-human, serpentine figure."

  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c03",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "Daniel Juslenius called Ajattara an evil witch and a forest troll. According to Christian Erici Lencqvist, Ajattara is a female evil forest haltija, who is scary and fast and drives people astray.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Daniel Juslenius menyebut Ajattara penyihir jahat dan troll hutan; menurut Christian Erici Lencqvist, Ajattara adalah haltija (roh) hutan perempuan yang jahat, menakutkan, cepat, dan menyesatkan orang.",
    "en": "Daniel Juslenius called Ajattara an evil witch and forest troll; according to Christian Erici Lencqvist she is an evil female forest haltija (spirit), frightening and fast, who leads people astray."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c04",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "Eemil Nestor Setälä demonstrated that Ajattara might appear in Western Finnish runic songs as Aattara in the origin of snakes",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Eemil Nestor Setälä menunjukkan bahwa Ajattara mungkin muncul dalam lagu rune Finlandia Barat sebagai Aattara dalam kisah asal-usul ular.",
    "en": "Eemil Nestor Setälä showed that Ajattara may appear in Western Finnish runic songs as Aattara in the origin of snakes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c05",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "The word “ajatar” is possibly derived from the Finnish word ajattaa, “to pursue\" (also, \"to drive\").",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata \"ajatar\" mungkin berasal dari kata Finlandia ajattaa, \"mengejar\" (juga \"menggiring\").",
    "en": "The word \"ajatar\" possibly derives from the Finnish ajattaa, \"to pursue\" (also \"to drive\")."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c06",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "Applying this to Ajatar, the verb ajaa is suffixed by the feminine \"-tar,\" translating as “female pursuer.”",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada nama Ajatar, kata kerja ajaa diberi akhiran feminin -tar sehingga berarti \"pengejar perempuan\".",
    "en": "For Ajatar, the verb ajaa takes the feminine suffix -tar, meaning \"female pursuer\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c07",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "The form \"Ajatar\" was invented by Matthias Castrén to standarize the name.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bentuk \"Ajatar\" diciptakan Matthias Castrén untuk membakukan nama itu.",
    "en": "The form \"Ajatar\" was coined by Matthias Castrén to standardize the name."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c08",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "Although Ajatar does not appear by name in documented Finnish folk songs, she appears in fiction inspired by the Kalevala and in modern fantasy interpretations.",
   "locator": "Derivative works",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Walaupun Ajatar tidak muncul dengan nama itu dalam lagu rakyat Finlandia yang terdokumentasi, ia muncul dalam fiksi yang terilhami Kalevala dan dalam tafsiran fantasi modern.",
    "en": "Although Ajatar does not appear by name in documented Finnish folk songs, she appears in fiction inspired by the Kalevala and in modern fantasy."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c09",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "In the second act of Aleksis Kivi's play, Kullervo (1860), Ajatar is described as ferocious and shameless, encouraging the protagonist to kill his master’s family.",
   "locator": "Derivative works",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam babak kedua drama Kullervo karya Aleksis Kivi (1860), Ajatar digambarkan buas dan tak tahu malu, mendorong tokoh utama membunuh keluarga majikannya.",
    "en": "In the second act of Aleksis Kivi's play Kullervo (1860), Ajatar is ferocious and shameless, urging the hero to kill his master's family."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c10",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s2",
   "quote": "Ajatar (auch Ajattara oder Aijo) ist ein böser Waldgeist in der Gestalt eines Drachen in der finnischen Mythologie. Es wird gesagt, dass Ajatar die Mutter des Teufels ist, Krankheiten und Seuchen verbreitet und Schlangen stillt. Jeder, der sie anschaut, wird dabei selbst krank.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ajatar (juga Ajattara atau Aijo) adalah roh hutan jahat berwujud naga dalam mitologi Finlandia; konon ia ibu iblis, menyebarkan penyakit dan wabah, menyusui ular, dan siapa pun yang memandangnya jatuh sakit.",
    "en": "Ajatar (also Ajattara or Aijo) is an evil forest spirit in dragon form in Finnish mythology; she is said to be the devil's mother, spread disease and plague and suckle serpents, and anyone who looks at her falls ill."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c11",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s2",
   "quote": "Anderen Überlieferungen zufolge ist Ajatara ein betörend schöner, weiblicher Schutzgeist, dem die männlichen Götter zu Füßen liegen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut tradisi lain, Ajatara adalah roh pelindung perempuan yang sangat memesona, yang dihormati para dewa laki-laki.",
    "en": "According to other traditions, Ajatara is a bewitchingly beautiful female guardian spirit at whose feet the male gods lie."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c12",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s3",
   "quote": "Dans le folklore finlandais, l'Ajattar, également écrit Aiatar, Ajattaro ou Ajattara, est un esprit connu sous le nom de",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Finlandia, Ajattar, juga ditulis Aiatar, Ajattaro, atau Ajattara, adalah sebuah roh.",
    "en": "In Finnish folklore the Ajattar, also spelled Aiatar, Ajattaro or Ajattara, is a spirit."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c13",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s3",
   "quote": "Ajatar est liée à l'Aitvaras lituanien et l'Äi, Äijo ou Äijattar estonien.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ajatar dikaitkan dengan Aitvaras dari Lituania serta Äi, Äijo, atau Äijattar dari Estonia.",
    "en": "Ajatar is linked to the Lithuanian Aitvaras and the Estonian Äi, Äijo or Äijattar."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ajatar-c14",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",
   "quote": "J. Mikkola connected the name to the Lithuanian word aitvaras 'nightmare, kratt, will o' wisp'.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {

    "en": "J. Mikkola linked the name to the Lithuanian aitvaras, \"nightmare, kratt, will-o'-the-wisp\"."
   }
  },

   "id": "ajatar-c15",
   "source_id": "ajatar-s1",

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Poludnitsa atau \"Nyonya Tengah Hari\" adalah iblis siang hari dalam mitologi Slavia yang menghampiri orang yang bekerja di ladang pada tengah hari dan membawa sengatan panas atau penyakit.",
  "en": "Poludnitsa, or \"Lady Midday\", is a noon demon of Slavic mythology who accosts people working in the fields at midday, bringing heatstroke or illness.",
  "claim_ids": [

  ]

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Poludnitsa, yang namanya berarti \"tengah hari\", adalah tokoh mitos yang dikenal di berbagai negeri Slavia di Eropa Timur. Ia iblis siang hari dalam mitologi Slavia dan dalam bahasa Inggris dijuluki \"Lady Midday\" atau \"Noonwraith\". Asal-usul sosoknya dihubungkan dengan personifikasi sengatan matahari, suasana khas siang yang terik, dan sisa kepercayaan pada dewa serta iblis kuno. Di antara bangsa Slavia Timur, hanya orang Rusia di barat laut Rusia yang mempercayainya.",
   "en": "Poludnitsa, whose name means \"midday\", is a mythical figure known across the Slavic lands of Eastern Europe. She is a noon demon of Slavic mythology, called \"Lady Midday\" or \"Noonwraith\" in English. Her figure is traced to a personification of sunstroke, the peculiar air of a scorching noon and remnants of belief in ancient gods and demons. Among the East Slavs, only Russians in north-western Russia believed in her.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lady-midday-c01",
    "lady-midday-c02",
    "lady-midday-c11",
    "lady-midday-c15"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ia biasanya digambarkan sebagai perempuan muda berbaju putih yang berkeliaran di pematang ladang, tetapi bisa juga tampil sebagai nenek tua, perempuan cantik, atau gadis berusia 12 tahun. Ia muncul di tengah hari musim panas yang terik dalam bentuk pusaran debu, membawa sabit, arit, atau gunting. Ia menyerang orang yang bekerja di ladang pada tengah hari, menyebabkan sengatan panas, sakit leher, bahkan kegilaan.",
   "en": "She is usually pictured as a young woman in white roaming the edges of fields, though she may equally take the shape of a beautiful woman, a 12-year-old girl or an old crone. She shows herself in the heat of summer noons as a whirling cloud of dust, carrying a scythe, sickle or shears. She attacks those working the fields at midday, bringing heatstroke, neck pain and even madness.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lady-midday-c03",
    "lady-midday-c04",
    "lady-midday-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Poludnitsa menghentikan orang di ladang untuk mengajukan pertanyaan sulit; siapa yang gagal menjawab atau mengalihkan pembicaraan akan dipenggal atau dibuat sakit. Menurut satu sumber, orang yang diganggunya hanya selamat bila terus bercerita tentang pekerjaan petani, terutama mengolah rami, sampai pukul satu, karena setelah jam istirahat itu ia kehilangan kekuatan. Ia juga suka menari dan memaksa gadis yang beristirahat di ladang menari sampai senja, tetapi gadis yang mampu mengalahkannya diberi mas kawin yang kaya.",
   "en": "Poludnitsa stops people in the field to put hard questions to them; whoever fails to answer or changes the subject is beheaded or struck with illness. One source says her victims can only save themselves by talking about farm work, especially flax processing, until one o'clock, for after that rest hour she loses her power. She also loves to dance, making a girl resting in the field dance until dusk, yet a girl able to outdance her receives a rich dowry.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lady-midday-c05",
    "lady-midday-c07",
    "lady-midday-c08",
    "lady-midday-c16",
    "lady-midday-c17"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut kebanyakan kepercayaan, ia bermusuhan dan sangat berbahaya bagi manusia, terutama bagi perempuan hamil, ibu yang baru melahirkan, dan anak kecil; ia bahkan mencuri dan menukar bayi. Namun ada pula kepercayaan bahwa ia menjaga tanaman biji-bijian dan sayuran dari manusia dan dari terik matahari, dan ia dipakai untuk menakut-nakuti anak agar menjauhi tanaman berharga. Untuk melawannya, orang membuat tanda salib, berdoa, memercikkan air suci, atau melarikan diri.",
   "en": "Most beliefs hold her hostile and very dangerous to people, especially to pregnant women, new mothers and small children; she even steals and swaps infants.    "statement": {
    "id": "Ia menghentikan orang di ladang untuk mengajukan pertanyaan sulit atau mengajak bercakap; siapa yang gagal menjawab atau mengalihkan pembicaraan akan dipenggal atau dibuat sakit.",
    "en": "She stops people in the field to ask hard questions or talk; anyone who fails to answer or changes the subject is beheaded or struck with illness."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c06",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s1",
   "quote": "She may appear as an old hag, a beautiful woman, or a 12-year-old girl, and she was useful in scaring children away from valuable crops. She is only seen on the hottest part of the day and is a personification of a sun-stroke.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia bisa tampil sebagai nenek tua, perempuan cantik, atau gadis berusia 12 tahun, dan berguna untuk menakuti anak agar menjauhi tanaman berharga; ia hanya terlihat pada saat paling panas dan merupakan personifikasi sengatan matahari.",
    "en": "She may appear as an old hag, a beautiful woman or a 12-year-old girl, and served to scare children from valuable crops; she is seen only in the hottest part of the day and personifies sunstroke."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c07",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s1",
   "quote": "Poludnitsa, according to beliefs, loves to dance. If she sees a girl lying down to rest in the field, she will wake her up and begin to persuade her to dance.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kepercayaan, Poludnitsa suka menari; bila melihat gadis berbaring beristirahat di ladang, ia membangunkannya dan membujuknya menari.",
    "en": "According to belief, Poludnitsa loves to dance; if she sees a girl resting in the field, she wakes her and urges her to dance."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c08",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s1",
   "quote": "Poludnitsa cannot be beaten in dancing; however, if such a girl is found, the noon spirit will present her with a rich dowry.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Poludnitsa tak terkalahkan dalam menari, tetapi bila ada gadis yang sanggup mengalahkannya, roh tengah hari itu memberinya mas kawin yang kaya.",
    "en": "Poludnitsa cannot be beaten at dancing, but if such a girl is found, the noon spirit gives her a rich dowry."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c09",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s1",
   "quote": "In some accounts, she symbolizes the midday star, thereby being the sister of Zarya-Zarenitsa",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam beberapa kisah, ia melambangkan bintang tengah hari sehingga bersaudara dengan Zarya-Zarenitsa.",
    "en": "In some accounts she symbolizes the midday star and is thus the sister of Zarya-Zarenitsa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c10",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s1",
   "quote": "In Wendish mythology, Přezpołdnica (in Lower Sorbian, Připołdnica in Upper Sorbian) is known as Mittagsfrau (\"Lady Midday\") among German speakers of Eastern Germany's Lusatia",
   "locator": "Other mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Wendia, Přezpołdnica (Sorbia Hilir; Připołdnica dalam Sorbia Hulu) dikenal sebagai Mittagsfrau (\"Nyonya Tengah Hari\") di kalangan penutur bahasa Jerman di Lusatia.",
    "en": "In Wendish mythology, Přezpołdnica (Lower Sorbian; Připołdnica in Upper Sorbian) is known as Mittagsfrau (\"Lady Midday\") among German speakers of Lusatia."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c11",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "Происхождение образа связывают с олицетворением солнечного удара, особой атмосферой жаркого полдня и остатками верований в древних божеств и демонов",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Asal-usul sosok Poludnitsa (Полудница) dihubungkan dengan personifikasi sengatan matahari, suasana khas siang yang terik, dan sisa kepercayaan pada dewa dan iblis kuno.",
    "en": "The figure's origin is linked to a personification of sunstroke, the special atmosphere of a hot noon and remnants of belief in ancient gods and demons."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c12",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "По некоторым представлениям, полудница охраняет злаки и овощи от людей и палящих лучей солнца",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut sebagian kepercayaan, poludnitsa menjaga tanaman biji-bijian dan sayuran dari manusia dan dari terik matahari.",
    "en": "According to some beliefs, the poludnitsa guards grain and vegetables from people and from the scorching sun."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c13",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "Против полудниц использовались такие общие меры, как перекреститься, прочитать молитву, окропить её святой водой или попросту убежать от неё.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Untuk melawan poludnitsa, orang membuat tanda salib, berdoa, memercikkan air suci kepadanya, atau melarikan diri.",
    "en": "Against poludnitsy people crossed themselves, said a prayer, sprinkled holy water on her or simply ran away."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c14",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "Считалось, что полудница — наиболее опасна для беременных, недавно родивших и маленьких детей, она наказывает нарушивших изоляцию рожениц, ворует и подменяет младенцев, нападает на детей постарше.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Poludnitsa dianggap paling berbahaya bagi perempuan hamil, ibu yang baru melahirkan, dan anak kecil; ia menghukum ibu nifas yang melanggar pengasingan, mencuri dan menukar bayi, serta menyerang anak yang lebih besar.",
    "en": "The poludnitsa was thought most dangerous to pregnant women, new mothers and small children; she punished new mothers who broke seclusion, stole and swapped infants and attacked older children."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c15",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "Среди восточных славян в полудницу верили только русские на северо-западе России",
   "locator": "Распространение мифа и наименования",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di antara bangsa Slavia Timur, hanya orang Rusia di barat laut Rusia yang mempercayai poludnitsa.",
    "en": "Among the East Slavs, only Russians in north-western Russia believed in the poludnitsa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c16",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s3",
   "quote": "Nach Ablauf der Ruhestunde zwischen zwölf und eins verliert die Mittagsfrau ihre Macht.",
   "locator": "Erscheinung",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah jam istirahat antara pukul dua belas dan satu berlalu, Mittagsfrau kehilangan kekuatannya.",
    "en": "After the rest hour between twelve and one has passed, the Mittagsfrau loses her power."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c17",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s3",
   "quote": "Die von der Mittagsfrau Heimgesuchten können sich nur retten, indem sie ihr bis ein Uhr von der bäuerlichen Arbeit, insbesondere von der Flachsverarbeitung, erzählen.",
   "locator": "Erscheinung",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang yang didatangi Mittagsfrau hanya bisa selamat dengan bercerita kepadanya tentang pekerjaan petani, terutama mengolah rami, sampai pukul satu.",
    "en": "Those visited by the Mittagsfrau can only save themselves by telling her about farm work, especially flax processing, until one o'clock."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c18",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "Согласно большинству представлений, полудница — враждебна и очень опасна для людей.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut kebanyakan kepercayaan, poludnitsa bermusuhan dan sangat berbahaya bagi manusia.",
    "en": "According to most beliefs, the poludnitsa is hostile and very dangerous to people."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c19",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s3",
   "quote": "Sie besitzt gemeinsame Merkmale mit den Vilen. Beide stehlen gerne Kinder und vertauschen sie durch Wechselbälge.",
   "locator": "Erscheinung",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mittagsfrau memiliki ciri yang sama dengan vila; keduanya suka mencuri anak dan menggantinya dengan anak tukaran.",
    "en": "The Mittagsfrau shares traits with the vile; both like to steal children and replace them with changelings."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lady-midday-c20",
   "source_id": "lady-midday-s2",
   "quote": "Полуднице близки полевик, удельница, русалка и другие полевые персонажи",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tokoh yang dekat dengan poludnitsa antara lain polevik, udelnitsa, rusalka, dan tokoh ladang lainnya.",
    "en": "Figures close to the poludnitsa include the polevik, the udelnitsa, the rusalka and other field figures."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Bannik adalah roh rumah mandi uap (banya) dalam mitologi Slavia, biasanya digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua kecil berjanggut panjang yang bisa meramalkan masa depan.",
  "en": "The bannik is the spirit of the bathhouse (banya) in Slavic mythology, usually pictured as a small old man with a long beard who can foretell the future.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "bannik-c01",
   "bannik-c08"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Bannik adalah roh rumah mandi uap atau banya dalam mitologi Slavia, terutama di Rusia dan Ukraina. Ia biasanya digambarkan sebagai lelaki tua kecil tanpa pakaian, berjanggut panjang, dengan tubuh tertutup daun birch dari sapu mandi. Menurut sumber lain, ia tinggal di belakang atau di bawah tungku, biasanya tak kasatmata, dan kadang tampil sebagai lelaki tua yang kotor berambut panjang dengan tangan besar.",
   "en": "The bannik is the spirit of the steam bathhouse, or banya, in Slavic mythology, especially in Russia and Ukraine. He is usually pictured as a small, unclothed old man with a long beard, his body covered in birch leaves from bath brooms. Another source says he lives behind or under the stove, is normally invisible and sometimes looks like a dirty old man with long hair and big hands.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bannik-c01",
    "bannik-c10",
    "bannik-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Banyak kisah menyebut bannik pandai berubah wujud: ia bisa tampil sebagai warga setempat, sebagai batu atau arang di tungku, atau sebagai kucing, anjing, kelinci, dan katak. Karena banya dianggap tempat gelap yang berpotensi berbahaya, bannik dipandang sebagai makhluk usil yang kadang mencelakai. Bila diganggu saat mandi, ia bisa menyiram penyusup dengan air mendidih atau bahkan mencekiknya.",
   "en": "Many accounts say the bannik is a shapeshifter, appearing as a local person, as a stone or coal in the stove, or as a cat, dog, hare or frog. Because the banya was seen as a dark, potentially dangerous place, the bannik was viewed as a mischievous and sometimes harmful being. If disturbed while bathing, he might scald an intruder with boiling water or even strangle him.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bannik-c02",
    "bannik-c04",
    "bannik-c10",
    "bannik-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Giliran pemanasan ketiga atau keempat disediakan bagi bannik, yang suka mengundang setan dan roh hutan untuk mandi bersamanya, sehingga gambar Kristen tidak boleh dipasang. Orang meninggalkan sesaji berupa ranting cemara, air, dan sabun sambil mengucapkan terima kasih. Saat banya dibangun kembali, seekor ayam hitam dikubur di bawah ambangnya untuk menenangkan bannik. Ibu dan bayi yang baru lahir dijaga ketat karena bannik dipercaya mencoba menculik anak yang belum dibaptis, padahal ia baru benar-benar puas bila ada anak lahir di wilayahnya.",
   "en": "The third or fourth firing was set aside for the bannik, who liked to invite demons and forest spirits to bathe with him, so no Christian images were allowed. People left offerings of fir branches, water and soap along with spoken thanks. When a banya was rebuilt, a black hen was buried beneath its threshold to calm him. Mothers and newborns were closely guarded, since he was believed to try to carry off unbaptized children, yet he was only truly content once a child had been born in his domain.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bannik-c03",
    "bannik-c05",
    "bannik-c06",
    "bannik-c07",
    "bannik-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Bannik juga dipercaya mampu meramalkan masa depan. Orang yang ingin bertanya berdiri dengan punggung terbuka di pintu banya yang setengah terbuka: belaian lembut berarti pertanda baik, sedangkan cakaran berarti kemalangan akan datang.",
   "en": "The bannik was also believed able to foretell the future. A person seeking an answer stood with bare back in the half-open bathhouse door: a gentle stroke meant good fortune, while a scratch of his claws meant trouble ahead.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bannik-c08"
   ]
  }
 ],
