   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c04",
   "source_id": "bannik-s1",
   "quote": "If disturbed by an intruder while washing, the bannik might pour boiling water over him, or even strangle him.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bila diganggu penyusup saat mandi, bannik bisa menyiramnya dengan air mendidih atau bahkan mencekiknya.",
    "en": "If disturbed while washing, the bannik might pour boiling water on the intruder or even strangle him."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c05",
   "source_id": "bannik-s1",
   "quote": "offerings of fir branches, water and soap were left, capped by a formal thank you uttered aloud.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sesaji berupa ranting cemara, air, dan sabun ditinggalkan, ditutup dengan ucapan terima kasih yang diucapkan keras.",
    "en": "Offerings of fir branches, water and soap were left, ending with a formal thank-you spoken aloud."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c06",
   "source_id": "bannik-s1",
   "quote": "In order to appease the bannik, upon the rebuilding of a banya, a black hen would be suffocated, left unplucked and buried beneath the building's threshold.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Untuk menenangkan bannik, saat banya dibangun kembali, seekor ayam hitam dicekik, dibiarkan berbulu, dan dikubur di bawah ambang bangunan.",
    "en": "To appease the bannik when a banya was rebuilt, a black hen was suffocated, left unplucked and buried under the threshold."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c07",
   "source_id": "bannik-s1",
   "quote": "Despite this, most births occurred inside the banya and it was believed that the bannik was not truly happy or settled until a child was born within his domain.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kebanyakan kelahiran terjadi di banya, dan bannik dipercaya belum benar-benar senang atau tenang sampai ada anak lahir di wilayahnya.",
    "en": "Most births took place in the banya, and the bannik was believed not truly happy until a child was born in his domain."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c08",
   "source_id": "bannik-s1",
   "quote": "The bannik had the ability to predict the future. One consulted him by standing with one's back exposed in the half-open door of the bath. The bannik would gently stroke one's back if all boded well; but if trouble lay ahead, he would strike with his claws.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bannik dapat meramalkan masa depan; orang bertanya dengan berdiri berpunggung terbuka di pintu banya yang setengah terbuka, lalu bannik membelai lembut bila pertanda baik atau mencakar bila ada kesulitan.",
    "en": "The bannik could predict the future; one stood with bare back in the half-open bath door, and he stroked it gently if all boded well or struck with his claws if trouble lay ahead."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c09",
   "source_id": "bannik-s2",
   "quote": "Bannik (russisch Банник) ist ein Hausgeist aus der slawischen Mythologie.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bannik (bahasa Rusia Банник) adalah roh rumah dalam mitologi Slavia.",
    "en": "Bannik (Russian Банник) is a household spirit of Slavic mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c10",
   "source_id": "bannik-s2",
   "quote": "Er soll hinter oder unter dem Ofen leben. Normalerweise ist er unsichtbar und manchmal hat er die Form eines kleinen, schmutzigen alten Mannes mit langen Haaren und einem Bart und großen Händen. Es kann sich in Tiere wie Katzen, Hunde, Hasen und Frösche verwandeln.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia konon tinggal di belakang atau di bawah tungku; biasanya tak kasatmata, kadang berwujud lelaki tua kecil yang kotor berambut panjang, berjanggut, dan bertangan besar, serta bisa berubah menjadi kucing, anjing, kelinci, dan katak.",
    "en": "He is said to live behind or under the stove; he is usually invisible, sometimes a small dirty old man with long hair, a beard and big hands, and can turn into cats, dogs, hares and frogs."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c11",
   "source_id": "bannik-s2",
   "quote": "Da die Banja ein dunkler, potenziell gefährlicher Ort ist, wird der Bannik als schelmisches, manchmal schädliches Wesen angesehen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena banya tempat yang gelap dan berpotensi berbahaya, bannik dipandang sebagai makhluk usil yang kadang mencelakai.",
    "en": "Because the banya is a dark, potentially dangerous place, the bannik is seen as a mischievous, sometimes harmful being."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c12",
   "source_id": "bannik-s2",
   "quote": "Mütter mit Neugeborenen wurden in der Banja streng bewacht, da man glaubte, der Bannik würde versuchen, noch nicht getaufte Kinder zu entführen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ibu dengan bayi yang baru lahir dijaga ketat di banya karena bannik dipercaya mencoba menculik anak yang belum dibaptis.",
    "en": "Mothers with newborns were closely guarded in the banya, as the bannik was believed to try to abduct unbaptized children."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bannik-c13",
   "source_id": "bannik-s2",
   "quote": "In slawischen Überlieferungen, insbesondere in Russland und der Ukraine, war der Bannik der Geist der Banja, des Badehauses.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam tradisi Slavia, terutama di Rusia (Russland) dan Ukraina (Ukraine), bannik adalah roh banya, rumah mandi uap.",
    "en": "In Slavic tradition, especially in Russia and Ukraine, the bannik was the spirit of the banya, the bathhouse."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
  {
   "topic": {
    "id": "Penampilan bannik",
    "en": "The bannik's appearance"
   },
   "positions": [
    {
     "summary": {
      "id": "Satu sumber: lelaki tua kecil tanpa pakaian, berjanggut panjang, tertutup daun birch.",
      "en": "One source: a small naked old man with a long beard, covered in birch leaves."
     },

      "en": "Another source: normally invisible, sometimes a dirty old man with long hair and big hands."
     },

 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Gamayun adalah burung mitos bernubuat dari budaya Rusia, lambang kebijaksanaan dan pengetahuan yang menyampaikan ramalan lewat nyanyiannya.",
  "en": "Gamayun is a prophetic mythical bird of Russian culture, a symbol of wisdom and knowledge that delivers prophecies through its song.",
  "claim_ids": [

   "gamayun-c02"

 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Gamayun dikenal sebagai burung pembawa nubuat dan lambang kebijaksanaan serta pengetahuan. Ia dikatakan tinggal di sebuah pulau di timur yang mitis, tidak jauh dari surga, dan menurut satu sumber dianggap sebagai pembawa kabar para dewa Slavia. Biasanya ia digambarkan sebagai burung hitam besar berkepala perempuan yang menyebarkan pesan ilahi dan ramalan melalui lagu-lagunya.",
   "en": "Gamayun is known as a bird of prophecy and an emblem of wisdom and knowledge. It is said to live on an island in the mythical east, near paradise, and one source calls it a herald of the Slavic gods. It is usually shown as a large black bird with a woman's head that spreads divine messages and prophecies through its songs.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "gamayun-c01",
    "gamayun-c02",
    "gamayun-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kesusastraan Rusia abad pertengahan membedakan Gamayun, burung surga yang seumur hidup berada di udara, dari burung mitos lain seperti Alkonost dan Sirin. Dalam buku-buku abad ke-17 sampai ke-19 ia digambarkan tanpa kaki dan tanpa sayap, terus terbang dengan bantuan ekornya, dan jatuhnya menandakan kematian para negarawan. Pada masa itu sosoknya juga dihias pada benda seni terapan, meriam, dan panji.",
   "en": "Medieval Russian writing set Gamayun, a bird of paradise that spends its whole life aloft, apart from other mythical birds such as the Alkonost and the Sirin. Books of the 17th to 19th centuries describe it as having neither legs nor wings, flying endlessly with the help of its tail, its fall foretelling the death of statesmen. In that period its figure also decorated applied-art objects, cannons and banners.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "gamayun-c04",
    "gamayun-c05",
    "gamayun-c14"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya kemungkinan berasal dari Persia, karena burung Huma dikenal dalam mitologi Persia, dan citranya ditelusuri ke burung humay dari cerita rakyat Iran. Wujud visualnya berubah seiring waktu: mula-mula ia mendapat sayap dan kaki, kemudian wajah perempuan. Satu sumber menyebutnya bagian dari cerita rakyat Rusia, tetapi sumber lain menegaskan tidak ada bukti keberadaannya dalam folklor tradisional Rusia.",
   "en": "Its name may come from Persia, since the Huma bird appears in Persian mythology, and its image is traced to the humay bird of Iranian folklore. Its visual form changed over time: first it gained wings and legs, and later a woman's face. One source places it in Russian folklore, while another stresses that there is no evidence of it in traditional Russian folklore.",
   "claim_ids": [

    "gamayun-c01",
    "gamayun-c11"

  {
   "id": "Dalam satu sumber, kepakan sayapnya memicu badai mematikan dari timur, dan kehadirannya ditakuti karena penglihatannya hanya meramalkan malapetaka. Popularitasnya meningkat pada akhir abad ke-19 dan awal abad ke-20 berkat sebuah lukisan, dan pada masa Zaman Perak puisi Rusia ia sering tampil sebagai sumber nubuat tragis, misalnya dalam sajak Alexander Blok.    "en": "In one source, its flight unleashes a deadly storm from the east, and its presence is feared because its visions foretell only disaster.    "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Gamajun (bahasa Rusia: Гамаюн) adalah burung bernubuat dari mitologi Slavia.",
    "en": "Gamajun (Russian: Гамаюн) is a prophetic bird from Slavic mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "gamayun-c09",
   "source_id": "gamayun-s2",
   "quote": "Wenn er fliegt, wird ein tödlicher Sturm aus dem Osten ausgelöst. Die Anwesenheit des Gamajun wird von allen gefürchtet, da seine Visionen nur Katastrophen ohne Trost und Hoffnung vorhersagen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ketika ia terbang, badai mematikan dari timur terpicu; kehadirannya ditakuti semua orang karena penglihatannya hanya meramalkan malapetaka tanpa penghiburan dan harapan.",
    "en": "When it flies, a deadly storm from the east is unleashed; everyone fears its presence because its visions foretell only catastrophe without comfort or hope."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "gamayun-c10",
   "source_id": "gamayun-s2",
   "quote": "Die Beliebtheit des Gamajun stieg in der russischen Kultur Ende des 19. und Anfang des 20. Jahrhunderts an, als Wiktor Wasnezow das Gemälde „Gamajun, der prophetische Vogel“ malte.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Popularitas Gamayun dalam budaya Rusia meningkat pada akhir abad ke-19 dan awal abad ke-20, ketika Wiktor Wasnezow melukis \"Gamajun, der prophetische Vogel\".",
    "en": "Gamayun's popularity in Russian culture rose in the late 19th and early 20th centuries, when Wiktor Wasnezow painted \"Gamajun, the Prophetic Bird\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "gamayun-c11",
   "source_id": "gamayun-s3",
   "quote": "Несмотря на распространённое заблуждение, сведений о существовании птицы гамаюн в русском традиционном фольклоре нет",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Meskipun ada anggapan keliru yang tersebar luas, tidak ada data tentang keberadaan burung gamayun dalam folklor tradisional Rusia.",
    "en": "Despite a widespread misconception, there is no evidence of the gamayun bird in traditional Russian folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "gamayun-c12",
   "source_id": "gamayun-s3",
   "quote": "Истоки образа восходят к мифической птице хумай из иранского фольклора",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Asal-usul citra Gamayun ditelusuri ke burung mitos humay dari folklor Iran.",
    "en": "The origins of the image go back to the mythical humay bird of Iranian folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "gamayun-c13",
   "source_id": "gamayun-s3",
   "quote": "Визуальный образ птицы гамаюн претерпел изменение с течением времени: сначала у неё появились крылья и ноги ... а затем и женское лицо",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Wujud visual burung gamayun berubah seiring waktu: mula-mula ia mendapat sayap dan kaki, kemudian wajah perempuan.",
    "en": "The visual image of the gamayun changed over time: first it acquired wings and legs, and later a female face."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "gamayun-c14",
   "source_id": "gamayun-s3",
   "quote": "В XVII—XVIII веках эта птица изображалась на произведениях декоративно-прикладного искусства, пушках и знамёнах.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada abad ke-17 dan ke-18 burung ini digambarkan pada karya seni dekoratif terapan, meriam, dan panji.",
    "en": "In the 17th–18th centuries this bird was depicted on decorative applied art, cannons and banners."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Strigoi adalah arwah jahat dalam mitologi Romania yang dipercaya bangkit dari kubur, dapat berubah menjadi hewan, dan menghisap tenaga dari darah korbannya.",
  "en": "Strigoi are evil spirits of Romanian mythology believed to rise from the grave, able to turn into animals and to draw vitality from their victims' blood.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "strigoi-c01",
   "strigoi-c11"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Romania, strigoi dipahami sebagai arwah orang mati yang gelisah dan jahat, yang dipercaya keluar dari kubur pada malam hari. Mereka dapat menjelma menjadi binatang atau sosok seperti hantu, bisa menjadi tak kasatmata, dan memperoleh daya hidup dari darah korban. Tujuannya adalah mencelakai orang yang masih hidup.",
   "en": "In Romanian mythology, strigoi are understood as restless, evil souls of the dead believed to leave the grave at night. They can take the shape of an animal or a ghostly apparition, can become invisible, and gain vitality from the blood of their victims. Their aim is to bring harm to the living.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "strigoi-c01",
    "strigoi-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ada strigoi hidup dan strigoi mati. Strigoi viu adalah strigoi hidup atau tukang sihir, sedangkan strigoi mort, strigoi mati, dianggap paling berbahaya karena keluar dari kubur untuk menyiksa keluarganya sampai mereka meninggal. Menurut kepercayaan, strigoi botak di ubun-ubun, tidak memakan bawang putih dan bawang merah, serta menghindari dupa. Malam mereka adalah malam Santo Andreas (30 November), ketika mereka keluar dari kubur atau meninggalkan tubuhnya.",
   "en": "There are living and dead strigoi. A strigoi viu is a living strigoi or sorcerer, while the strigoi mort, the dead strigoi, is held to be the most dangerous, leaving the grave to torment its family until they die. By belief, a strigoi is bald on the crown, does not eat garlic or onions, and avoids incense. Their night is the night of Saint Andrew (30 November), when they leave their graves or their bodies.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "strigoi-c08",
    "strigoi-c09",
    "strigoi-c13",
    "strigoi-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Strigoi dipercaya mengambil susu sapi, sari gandum, dan kekuatan orang, menahan hujan, mendatangkan hujan es, serta membawa kematian bagi manusia dan ternak. Kekeringan di sebuah desa dianggap tanda adanya strigoaică, strigoi perempuan, yang menghalangi hujan. Untuk membunuhnya, kubur orang yang dicurigai dicari, imam membacakan tata doa, lalu jantungnya ditancapi pasak atau ditusuk paku atau pisau supaya ia terikat pada peti matinya.",
   "en": "Strigoi are believed to take milk from cows, the goodness of the wheat and the strength of people, to stop the rain, bring hail and bring death to people and cattle. Drought in a village was read as a sign that a strigoaică, a female strigoi, was holding back the rain. To kill one, the suspect's grave is found, priests read the rite, and a stake is driven into the heart or it is pierced with a nail or knife so that it stays bound to its coffin.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "strigoi-c07",
    "strigoi-c14",
    "strigoi-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Nama strigoi berakar pada istilah Latin strix atau striga dengan akhiran pembesar -oi, dan berkaitan dengan kata kerja Romania a striga, \"menjerit\". Salah satu catatan awal tentang strigoi historis adalah kisah Jure Grando Alilović (1579–1656) dari Istria. Dimitrie Cantemir menyebut striga dalam Descriptio Moldaviae, tetapi mengaitkannya dengan penyihir, bukan dengan mayat hidup peminum darah. Kepercayaan ini bertahan lama: pada 2004 keluarga di Marotinu de Sus menggali jenazah kerabat yang dicurigai menjadi strigoi, dan polisi kemudian menangkap enam anggota keluarga itu.",
   "en": "The name goes back to the Latin terms strix or striga with the augmentative suffix -oi, and is related to the Romanian verb a striga, \"to scream\". The tale of Jure Grando Alilović (1579–1656) of Istria is among the earliest accounts of a real strigoi. Dimitrie Cantemir mentions the striga in Descriptio Moldaviae but links them with witches rather than blood-drinking undead. The belief has persisted: in 2004 a family in Marotinu de Sus dug up a relative suspected of becoming a strigoi, and police later arrested six of the family members.",

   "quote": "One of the earliest mentions of a historical strigoi is the story of Jure Grando Alilović (1579–1656) from the region of Istria.",
   "locator": "Early reports",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Salah satu penyebutan paling awal tentang strigoi historis adalah kisah Jure Grando Alilović (1579–1656) dari wilayah Istria.",
    "en": "One of the earliest mentions of a historical strigoi is the story of Jure Grando Alilović (1579–1656) from Istria."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c06",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "Striga are mentioned by the Moldavian statesman and soldier Dimitrie Cantemir in his work Descriptio Moldaviae (1714–1716). He thought that the striga were mostly Moldavian and Transylvanian beliefs. However, he associated them with witches or warlocks rather than blood-drinking undead vampires.",
   "locator": "Early reports",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dimitrie Cantemir menyebut striga dalam Descriptio Moldaviae (1714–1716), menganggapnya terutama kepercayaan Moldavia dan Transilvania, tetapi mengaitkannya dengan penyihir, bukan vampir mayat hidup peminum darah.",
    "en": "Dimitrie Cantemir mentions striga in Descriptio Moldaviae (1714–1716), thinking them mostly Moldavian and Transylvanian beliefs, but associating them with witches or warlocks rather than blood-drinking undead."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c07",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "The strigoi take the milk from the cows, take the manna of the wheat, the strength of the people, stop the rains, bring hail and bring death among men and cattle.",
   "locator": "Creation",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Strigoi mengambil susu sapi, sari gandum, dan kekuatan orang, menahan hujan, mendatangkan hujan es, serta membawa kematian bagi manusia dan ternak.",
    "en": "Strigoi take milk from cows, the manna of the wheat and people's strength, stop the rains, bring hail and bring death among people and cattle."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c08",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "Strigoi viu: a living strigoi or sorcerer.",
   "locator": "Types",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Strigoi viu adalah strigoi hidup atau tukang sihir.",
    "en": "Strigoi viu is a living strigoi or sorcerer."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c09",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "Strigoi mort: a dead strigoi, the most dangerous. They emerge from their graves in order to torment their families until their relatives die.",
   "locator": "Types",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Strigoi mort adalah strigoi mati, yang paling berbahaya; mereka keluar dari kubur untuk menyiksa keluarganya sampai kerabatnya meninggal.",
    "en": "Strigoi mort is a dead strigoi, the most dangerous, emerging from the grave to torment its family until its relatives die."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c10",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "To kill them, the grave of the supposed strigoi is searched and the order is read to him by the priests and an oak, yew or branch is struck in his heart, it is pierced with a nail or a knife, to remain bound of the coffin",
   "locator": "Creation",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Untuk membunuhnya, kubur orang yang diduga strigoi dicari, imam membacakan tata doa, lalu kayu ek, yew, atau dahan ditancapkan ke jantungnya, atau ditusuk paku atau pisau agar ia tetap terikat pada peti mati.",
    "en": "To kill one, the grave of the suspected strigoi is found, priests read the order, and an oak, yew or branch is struck into the heart or it is pierced with a nail or knife so it stays bound to the coffin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c11",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s2",
   "quote": "Strigoii sunt, în mitologia românească, sufletele rele ale morților, despre care se crede că ar ieși noaptea din mormânt și s-ar transforma într-un animal sau într-o apariție fantomatică, pentru a pricinui relele celor vii.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Romania, strigoi adalah jiwa jahat orang mati yang dipercaya keluar dari kubur pada malam hari dan berubah menjadi binatang atau sosok seperti hantu untuk mencelakai orang hidup.",
    "en": "In Romanian mythology, strigoi are the evil souls of the dead, believed to leave the grave at night and turn into an animal or ghostly apparition to harm the living."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c12",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s2",
   "quote": "Exista strigoi vii și strigoi morți. Când este lună nouă, strigoii ies din morminte sau își părăsesc trupul și încep să danseze sau să facă tot felul de răutăți. Noaptea lor este aceea de Sfântul Andrei (30 noiembrie).",
   "locator": "Cine se poate transforma în strigoi",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada strigoi hidup dan strigoi mati; saat bulan baru mereka keluar dari kubur atau meninggalkan tubuhnya untuk menari atau berbuat jahat, dan malam mereka adalah malam Santo Andreas (30 November).",
    "en": "There are living and dead strigoi; at the new moon they leave their graves or their bodies to dance or make mischief, and their night is Saint Andrew's Night (30 November)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c13",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s2",
   "quote": "Se zice că strigoiul este chel în creștetul capului. El nu mănâncă usturoi și ceapă, se ferește de tămâie",
   "locator": "Identificare",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Konon strigoi botak di ubun-ubun, tidak memakan bawang putih dan bawang merah, serta menghindari dupa.",
    "en": "A strigoi is said to be bald on the crown of the head, not to eat garlic or onions, and to avoid incense."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c14",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s2",
   "quote": "Dacă într-un sat este secetă, înseamnă că acolo este o strigoaică (împiedică ploile)",
   "locator": "Strigoii și fenomenele meteorologice",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Jika sebuah desa dilanda kekeringan, artinya di sana ada strigoaică (strigoi perempuan) yang menghalangi hujan.",
    "en": "If a village suffers drought, it means a strigoaică (female strigoi) is there, preventing the rains."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c15",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "In February 2004, a woman from the village of Marotinu de Sus in Dolj County claimed that she had been visited by her late uncle",
   "locator": "Post-communist era",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada Februari 2004, seorang perempuan dari desa Marotinu de Sus di Distrik Dolj mengaku didatangi mendiang pamannya.",
    "en": "In February 2004, a woman from Marotinu de Sus in Dolj County claimed she had been visited by her late uncle."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c16",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "Dolj County police later arrested six of the family members who participated in the ritual, charging them with \"disturbing the peace of the dead\".",
   "locator": "Post-communist era",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Polisi Distrik Dolj kemudian menangkap enam anggota keluarga yang ikut dalam ritual itu dengan dakwaan \"mengganggu ketenangan orang mati\".",
    "en": "Dolj County police later arrested six family members who took part in the ritual, charging them with \"disturbing the peace of the dead\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c17",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "carpeted his own apartment with braids of garlic, allegedly as a traditional remedy against the strigoi.",
   "locator": "Communist era",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Seorang aktivis revolusi melapisi apartemennya dengan jalinan bawang putih, konon sebagai penangkal tradisional terhadap strigoi.",
    "en": "A revolutionary activist carpeted his apartment with braids of garlic, allegedly as a traditional remedy against strigoi."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "strigoi-c18",
   "source_id": "strigoi-s1",
   "quote": "Otila Hedeşan notes that the same augmentative suffix appears in the related terms moroi and bosorcoi (borrowed from Hungarian boszorka) and considers this parallel derivation to indicate membership in the same \"mythological micro-system.\"",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Otila Hedeşan mencatat bahwa akhiran pembesar yang sama muncul pada istilah terkait moroi dan bosorcoi, yang dianggap bagian dari \"sistem mikro mitologis\" yang sama.",
    "en": "Otila Hedeşan notes the same augmentative suffix in the related terms moroi and bosorcoi, seen as part of the same \"mythological micro-system\"."
   }
  }
 ],

  "id": "Astghik adalah dewi Armenia kuno yang dipuja sebagai perwujudan kecantikan, cinta, kesuburan, dan air, sekaligus pasangan dewa Vahagn.",
  "en": "Astghik is an ancient Armenian goddess revered as the embodiment of beauty, love, fertility and water, and the consort of the god Vahagn.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Astghik adalah dewi penting dalam mitologi Armenia kuno, dihormati sebagai perwujudan kecantikan, cinta, kesuburan, dan air. Ia dikenal sebagai pengantin dewa perang Vahagn, yang melambangkan api dan guntur, dan sering disamakan dengan Aphrodite serta planet Venus. Di antara dewa-dewi Armenia, hanya namanya yang jelas berakar pada bahasa Armenia.",
   "en": "Astghik is a leading goddess of ancient Armenian mythology, honoured as the embodiment of beauty, love, fertility and water. She is known as the bride of the war god Vahagn, who stands for fire and thunder, and was often equated with Aphrodite and the planet Venus. Among the Armenian deities, hers is the only name with a clearly Armenian linguistic origin.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "astghik-c01",
    "astghik-c02",
    "astghik-c03",
    "astghik-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut satu sumber, pada mulanya ia dewi penciptaan langit dan bumi, lalu berubah menjadi dewi cinta, kecantikan, dan kesuburan; kedudukannya surut ketika Aramazd menjadi dewa pencipta utama dan Anahit dewi bulan. Sumber lain menyebut bahwa pada akhir masa pra-Kristen ia menjadi dewi cinta, kecantikan perawan, serta mata air. Ia juga dianggap personifikasi planet Venus serta dewi cinta dan keibuan.",
   "en": "According to one source she was first a goddess of the creation of heaven and earth, later becoming a goddess of love, beauty and fertility; her standing declined as Aramazd rose as chief creator god and Anahit as moon goddess. Another source says that in the late pre-Christian period she became goddess of love, virginal beauty and springs. She is also seen as the personification of the planet Venus and a goddess of love and motherhood.",
   "claim_ids": [

   "id": "Sebuah kisah menceritakan bahwa Astghik biasa mandi di sungai kecil, dan kecantikannya membuat para pemuda desa sekitar berkumpul untuk melihatnya.    "en": "A tale relates that Astghik used to bathe in a stream, and her beauty drew young men from nearby villages to watch her.    "claim_ids": [

    "astghik-c10",
    "astghik-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Namanya diturunkan dari kata Armenia Kuno astgh, \"bintang\", sehingga dapat dimaknai \"bintang kecil\". Perayaan untuknya disebut Vardavar, ketika orang saling menyiram air sebagai ritus penyucian. Kuil utamanya berada di Ashtishat, dan ia berbagi kompleks kuil dengan Vahagn di Taron.",
   "en": "Her name comes from the Old Armenian word astgh, \"star\", so it can be read as \"little star\". Her festival is called Vardavar, when people drench one another with water as a rite of purification. Her main temple was at Ashtishat, and she shared a temple complex with Vahagn in Taron.",

    "id": "Ia sering disamakan dengan Aphrodite dan planet Venus.",
    "en": "She was often identified with Aphrodite and the planet Venus."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c04",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "The celebration honoring Astghik is called Vardavar, during which people drench each other with water as a ritual of 'purification.'",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Perayaan untuk menghormati Astghik disebut Vardavar, ketika orang saling menyiram air sebagai ritual penyucian.",
    "en": "The celebration honouring Astghik is called Vardavar, when people drench each other with water as a purification ritual."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c05",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "One tradition says she was Noah's daughter, born after his flood.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Satu tradisi menyebutnya putri Nuh, yang lahir setelah air bah.",
    "en": "One tradition says she was Noah's daughter, born after his flood."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c06",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "The name \"Astghik\" (Աստղիկ) is derived from the Old Armenian word \"astgh\" (Աստղ), meaning \"star.\" ... Thus, \"Astghik\" can be interpreted as \"little star,\"",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama Astghik (Աստղիկ) berasal dari kata Armenia Kuno astgh (Աստղ), \"bintang\", sehingga dapat dimaknai \"bintang kecil\".",
    "en": "The name Astghik (Աստղիկ) derives from Old Armenian astgh (Աստղ), \"star\", and can be read as \"little star\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c07",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "Originally, Astghik was the goddess of creation of heaven and earth, however, later she was transformed into a deity of love, beauty, and fertility.",
   "locator": "Mythology",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada mulanya Astghik adalah dewi penciptaan langit dan bumi, tetapi kemudian berubah menjadi dewi cinta, kecantikan, dan kesuburan.",
    "en": "Originally Astghik was the goddess of the creation of heaven and earth, but she later became a deity of love, beauty and fertility."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c08",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "With the rise of Aramazd as the chief creator god and Anahit as the moon goddess, Astghik's position diminished.",
   "locator": "Mythology",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dengan bangkitnya Aramazd sebagai dewa pencipta utama dan Anahit sebagai dewi bulan, kedudukan Astghik menurun.",
    "en": "With the rise of Aramazd as chief creator god and Anahit as moon goddess, Astghik's position diminished."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c09",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "Astghik had a habit of bathing in a stream, often in the nude. Her beauty was so captivating that young men from nearby villages would gather to watch her.",
   "locator": "The myth of Astghik",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Astghik biasa mandi di sungai kecil, sering tanpa busana, dan kecantikannya begitu memikat sehingga para pemuda dari desa sekitar berkumpul untuk melihatnya.",
    "en": "Astghik used to bathe in a stream, often nude, and her beauty was so captivating that young men from nearby villages gathered to watch her."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c10",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "To prevent the young men from getting a better view, she created a thick fog that covered the area. This fog became a permanent feature of the place, and the location came to be known as \"Msho Dasht\".",
   "locator": "The myth of Astghik",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Untuk menghalangi pandangan para pemuda, ia menciptakan kabut tebal yang menutupi daerah itu; kabut itu menetap, dan tempat itu dikenal sebagai Msho Dasht.",
    "en": "To stop the young men getting a better view, she created a thick fog over the area; it became permanent, and the place came to be known as Msho Dasht."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c11",
   "source_id": "astghik-s3",
   "quote": "她的主要神庙在阿什季沙特，位于土耳其穆什城以北，这座寺庙是瓦哈格恩，太阳神、她的情人或丈夫专用的",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kuil utama Astghik berada di Ashtishat (阿什季沙特), di utara kota Muş di Turki; kuil itu juga diperuntukkan bagi Vahagn, kekasih atau suaminya.",
    "en": "Astghik's main temple was at Ashtishat, north of Muş in Turkey; it was also dedicated to Vahagn, her lover or husband."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c12",
   "source_id": "astghik-s2",
   "quote": "Dentro del panteón armenio, Astghik es la única diosa cuyo nombre tiene un claro origen lingüístico armenio.",
   "locator": "Historia",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di antara dewa-dewi Armenia, Astghik adalah satu-satunya dewi yang namanya jelas berasal dari bahasa Armenia.",
    "en": "Within the Armenian pantheon, Astghik is the only goddess whose name has a clear Armenian linguistic origin."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c13",
   "source_id": "astghik-s2",
   "quote": "Se la considera la personificación del planeta Venus, y las fuentes la identifican como la diosa del amor y la maternidad.",
   "locator": "Historia",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dianggap personifikasi planet Venus, dan sumber-sumber menyebutnya dewi cinta dan keibuan.",
    "en": "She is considered the personification of the planet Venus, and sources identify her as goddess of love and motherhood."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c14",
   "source_id": "astghik-s2",
   "quote": "Su esposo era Vahagn, y ambos compartían un complejo de templos en Taron",
   "locator": "Historia",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Suaminya adalah Vahagn, dan keduanya berbagi kompleks kuil di Taron.",
    "en": "Her husband was Vahagn, and the two shared a temple complex in Taron."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c15",
   "source_id": "astghik-s2",
   "quote": "En el período precristiano tardío, se convirtió en la diosa del amor, la belleza virginal y los manantiales y fuentes de agua.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada akhir masa pra-Kristen, ia menjadi dewi cinta, kecantikan perawan, serta mata air dan sumber air.",
    "en": "In the late pre-Christian period she became the goddess of love, virginal beauty, and springs and sources of water."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c16",
   "source_id": "astghik-s2",
   "quote": "era la diosa armenia de la fertilidad y el amor, y consorte de Vahagn.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Astghik adalah dewi kesuburan dan cinta Armenia, dan pasangan Vahagn.",
    "en": "Astghik was the Armenian goddess of fertility and love, and consort of Vahagn."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "astghik-c17",
   "source_id": "astghik-s1",
   "quote": "Astłik or Astghik (Armenian: Աստղիկ ) a prominent goddess",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Namanya ditulis Astłik atau Astghik.",
    "en": "Her name is written Astłik or Astghik."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Hiisi adalah istilah mitologi Finnik yang semula menunjuk tempat-tempat keramat, lalu berbagai makhluk mitos yang kemudian dipandang sebagai roh jahat.",
  "en": "Hiisi is a Finnic mythological term that first referred to sacred places and later to various mythical beings, eventually seen as evil spirits.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "hiisi-c01",
   "hiisi-c12"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi bangsa-bangsa Finnik, hiisi mula-mula berarti tempat keramat, lalu dipakai untuk bermacam makhluk mitos. Folklor yang lebih kemudian, yang dipengaruhi agama Kristen, menggambarkan mereka sebagai makhluk bersifat iblis atau penipu, sering dianggap penghuni asli tanah itu yang masih kafir, mirip raksasa dalam mitologi. Mereka dikaitkan dengan tanjung yang menonjol, celah yang menyeramkan, batu besar, lubang batu, hutan, bukit, dan medan kasar lainnya.",
   "en": "In the mythologies of the Finnic peoples, hiisi first meant a sacred place and was later used for many kinds of mythical beings. Later, Christian-influenced folklore pictures them as demonic or trickster-like beings, often seen as the original pagan dwellers of the land, much like the giants of myth. They are linked with jutting promontories, forbidding crevasses, big boulders, potholes, woods, hills and other rough ground.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "hiisi-c01",
    "hiisi-c02",
    "hiisi-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Asal-usul istilah ini ditafsirkan berbeda. Satu pandangan menyebut Hiisi semula roh hutan di perbukitan, dan dalam bahasa Estonia hiis berarti hutan kecil keramat di tanah tinggi; Mauno Koski dan peneliti lain mengaitkannya terutama dengan tempat pemakaman. Dalam mantra orang Finlandia, Hiisi sering dihubungkan dengan bukit atau gunung, sebagai pemilik atau penguasanya. Daftar dewa pagan Finlandia susunan Uskup Mikael Agricola menyebut Hiisi dewa hewan buruan hutan atau bulu, serupa dengan Tapio.",
   "en": "The origin of the term is read in different ways. One view holds that Hiisi was first a spirit of hill forests, and in Estonian hiis means a sacred grove on high ground; Mauno Koski and others link it mainly with burial sites. In Finnish spells Hiisi is often tied to a hill or mountain, as its owner or ruler. Bishop Mikael Agricola's list of Finnish pagan gods calls Hiisi a god of forest game or fur, like Tapio.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "hiisi-c04",
    "hiisi-c05",
    "hiisi-c17",
    "hiisi-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sifat jahat Hiisi diduga makin diperbesar sejak Kristenisasi Finlandia pada abad ke-12 dan ke-13, sampai akhirnya hampir sama dengan iblis Kristen. Cerita rakyat lisan terutama mengisahkan makhluk penghuni situs hiisi, biasanya troll atau raksasa, yang membentuk batu-batu aneh dan bentang alam; dalam satu kisah, para raksasa melempar batu ke laut agar orang tidak bisa berperahu ke gereja. Ada hiidet yang kecil dan ada pula yang raksasa.",
   "en": "Hiisi's evil nature is thought to have grown from the Christianization of Finland in the 12th and 13th centuries until it came close to the Christian devil. Oral folklore mainly tells of beings living at hiisi sites, usually trolls or giants, who shaped odd rocks and landscape features; in one tale the giants throw rocks into the sea to stop people rowing to church. Some hiidet were small, others gigantic.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "hiisi-c06",
    "hiisi-c08",
    "hiisi-c09",
    "hiisi-c12"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Hiidet dapat bepergian dalam arak-arakan yang riuh dan menyerang orang yang tidak memberi jalan, dan hiisi bisa masuk mencuri jika pintu dibiarkan terbuka. Orang yang dikejar hiisi dianjurkan berlindung di lahan garapan, yang dalam folklor Finlandia dianggap tanah yang diberkati. Satu sumber menyebut Hiisi putra raksasa Kaleva, penjinak binatang liar, dan perwujudan Lempo; folklor Finlandia juga membedakan hiisi hutan, air, dan gunung.",
   "en": "Hiidet were said to roam in loud processions, assaulting anyone who failed to make way, and a hiisi might slip indoors to steal through an open door. A person pursued by one was told to flee to farmland, which Finnish folklore regarded as blessed ground. One source calls Hiisi a son of the giant Kaleva, a tamer of wild beasts and a form of Lempo; Finnish folk myth also told apart forest, water and mountain hiisis.",

    "hiisi-c10",

   "quote": "They are found near salient promontories, ominous crevasses, large boulders, potholes, woods, hills, and other outstanding geographical features or rough terrain.",

    "id": "Mereka berada di dekat tanjung yang menonjol, celah yang menyeramkan, batu besar, lubang batu, hutan, bukit, dan bentuk alam mencolok atau medan kasar lainnya.",
    "en": "They are found near jutting promontories, ominous crevasses, large boulders, potholes, woods, hills and other striking features or rough terrain."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c04",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "Hiisi was originally a spirit of hill forests (Abercromby 1898). In Estonian hiis (or his) means a sacred grove of trees, usually on elevated ground.",
   "locator": "Origin and etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hiisi semula roh hutan perbukitan; dalam bahasa Estonia hiis berarti hutan kecil keramat, biasanya di tanah tinggi.",
    "en": "Hiisi was originally a spirit of hill forests; in Estonian hiis means a sacred grove, usually on high ground."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c05",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "More recent speculation, by Mauno Koski and others, associates the Finnish \"Hiisi\" and the Estonian \"Hiis\" primarily with burial sites, or sacred areas associated with burial sites",
   "locator": "Origin and etymology",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dugaan yang lebih baru oleh Mauno Koski dan lainnya mengaitkan Hiisi (Finlandia) dan Hiis (Estonia) terutama dengan tempat pemakaman atau kawasan keramat yang terkait dengannya.",
    "en": "More recent speculation by Mauno Koski and others links Finnish Hiisi and Estonian Hiis mainly with burial sites or sacred areas tied to them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c06",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "It has been supposed that Hiisi's evil nature has been magnified over time, starting with the Christianization of Finland in the 12th and 13th centuries. In more recent times his nature has been nearly identical with that of a Christian devil, or even the Devil.",
   "locator": "Origin and etymology",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sifat jahat Hiisi diduga makin diperbesar seiring waktu, sejak Kristenisasi Finlandia pada abad ke-12 dan ke-13; belakangan sifatnya hampir sama dengan iblis Kristen, bahkan Iblis sendiri.",
    "en": "Hiisi's evil nature is supposed to have been magnified over time from the Christianization of Finland in the 12th and 13th centuries; more recently it has been nearly identical to a Christian devil, or the Devil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c07",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "In Bishop Mikael Agricola's list of Finnish pagan gods Hiisi is said to have been a god of forest game or fur, sharing this attribute with a similar god, Tapio.",
   "locator": "Origin and etymology",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam daftar dewa pagan Finlandia susunan Uskup Mikael Agricola, Hiisi disebut dewa hewan buruan hutan atau bulu, sifat yang juga dimiliki dewa serupa, Tapio.",
    "en": "In Bishop Mikael Agricola's list of Finnish pagan gods, Hiisi is said to be a god of forest game or fur, an attribute shared with a similar god, Tapio."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c08",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "Oral folklore concerning hiisi mostly describes creatures that dwelt in hiisi sites, typically trolls or giants. Many of the stories describe how odd rock formations or other features of the landscape were created by the actions of these creatures.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Folklor lisan tentang hiisi kebanyakan menggambarkan makhluk penghuni situs hiisi, biasanya troll atau raksasa, dan banyak cerita mengisahkan bagaimana formasi batu aneh dan bentang alam tercipta oleh ulah mereka.",
    "en": "Oral folklore about hiisi mostly describes beings dwelling at hiisi sites, typically trolls or giants, and many stories tell how odd rock formations and landscape features were made by them."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c09",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "as well as tales of how the cape of Hiidennokka was created by giants throwing rocks. One of these tales takes on a Christian element, as the giants throw rocks into the sea to prevent people going to church by boat.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada kisah tentang tanjung Hiidennokka yang terbentuk karena raksasa melempar batu; satu kisah bernuansa Kristen, ketika raksasa melempar batu ke laut agar orang tidak dapat ke gereja dengan perahu.",
    "en": "There are tales of the cape of Hiidennokka being made by giants throwing rocks; one takes a Christian turn, the giants throwing rocks into the sea to stop people going to church by boat."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c10",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "Hiidet could travel in a noisy procession and attack people who did not give way to them. If somebody left their door open, a hiisi could come inside and steal something. If you were chased by a hiisi, you should seek safety in a cultivated area.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hiidet dapat bepergian dalam arak-arakan riuh dan menyerang orang yang tidak memberi jalan; jika pintu dibiarkan terbuka, hiisi bisa masuk dan mencuri; orang yang dikejar hiisi sebaiknya berlindung di lahan garapan.",
    "en": "Hiidet could travel in a noisy procession and attack people who did not give way; a hiisi could come in and steal if a door was left open; anyone chased by a hiisi should seek safety on cultivated land."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c11",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s3",
   "quote": "Toute personne poursuivie par un hiisi peut trouver refuge dans une zone cultivée. Ainsi dans le folklore finnois, ce sont les zones cultivées qui sont bénites",
   "locator": "Habitudes",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Siapa pun yang dikejar hiisi dapat berlindung di lahan garapan; dalam folklor Finlandia, lahan garapanlah yang dianggap diberkati.",
    "en": "Anyone chased by a hiisi can find refuge in a cultivated area; in Finnish folklore it is the cultivated areas that are blessed."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c12",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "they passed into folklore as purely evil spirits vaguely analogous to trolls. According to this later view, hiidet were often small in size, but others were gigantic.",
   "locator": "Folklore",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mereka masuk ke folklor sebagai roh yang sepenuhnya jahat, agak mirip troll; menurut pandangan kemudian ini, hiidet sering berukuran kecil, tetapi ada yang raksasa.",
    "en": "They passed into folklore as purely evil spirits vaguely like trolls; in this later view hiidet were often small, though others were gigantic."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c13",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s2",
   "quote": "Hiisi, Sohn des Riesen Kaleva, wohnt im Wald an versteckten Anbetungsstätten und ist Zähmer und Bezwinger der wilden Tiere. Er ist eine Erscheinungsform von Lempo, dem Gott der Bosheit.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hiisi, putra raksasa Kaleva, tinggal di hutan di tempat-tempat pemujaan tersembunyi dan menjadi penjinak serta penakluk binatang liar; ia perwujudan Lempo, dewa kejahatan.",
    "en": "Hiisi, son of the giant Kaleva, lives in the forest at hidden places of worship and is tamer and conqueror of wild beasts; he is a form of Lempo, god of malice."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c14",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s2",
   "quote": "Im 13. und 14. Gesang des Kalevala verfolgt Lemminkäinen Hiisis Elch (oder Hirsch).",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam nyanyian ke-13 dan ke-14 Kalevala, Lemminkäinen memburu rusa elk (atau rusa) milik Hiisi.",
    "en": "In the 13th and 14th cantos of the Kalevala, Lemminkäinen hunts Hiisi's elk (or deer)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c15",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s2",
   "quote": "Die finnische Volksmythologie unterschied Wald-, Wasser- und Berghiisis.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mitologi rakyat Finlandia membedakan hiisi hutan, hiisi air, dan hiisi gunung.",
    "en": "Finnish folk mythology distinguished forest, water and mountain hiisis."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c16",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s2",
   "quote": "Der Ausdruck hiidenkirnu (Gletschertopf, „Butterfass des Hiisi“) bezeichnet eine schmale und tiefe Kluft in einem Felsen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Istilah hiidenkirnu (\"tong mentega Hiisi\") menunjuk celah sempit dan dalam di batu.",
    "en": "The term hiidenkirnu (\"Hiisi's butter churn\") denotes a narrow, deep cleft in rock."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c17",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s1",
   "quote": "In the spells (\"magic songs\") of the Finns the term Hiisi is often used in association with a hill or mountain, as a personage he also associated with the hills and mountains, such as the owner or ruler of the same.",
   "locator": "Origin and etymology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mantra (lagu sihir) orang Finlandia, Hiisi sering dikaitkan dengan bukit atau gunung, dan sebagai tokoh ia juga dihubungkan dengan bukit dan gunung sebagai pemilik atau penguasanya.",
    "en": "In Finnish spells (\"magic songs\") Hiisi is often linked with a hill or mountain, and as a personage with hills and mountains as their owner or ruler."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "hiisi-c18",
   "source_id": "hiisi-s3",
   "quote": "Hiisi, au pluriel hiidet, est un terme du folklore balto-finnois",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Hiisi, jamaknya hiidet, adalah istilah folklor Balto-Finnik.",
    "en": "Hiisi, plural hiidet, is a term of Balto-Finnic folklore."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Suur Tõll atau Tõll yang Agung adalah pahlawan raksasa dalam mitologi Estonia yang tinggal di Pulau Saaremaa dan melempar batu-batu besar ke arah musuhnya.",
  "en": "Suur Tõll, Toell the Great, is a giant hero of Estonian mythology who lived on the island of Saaremaa and hurled huge rocks at his enemies.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "toell-the-great-c01",

 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Suur Tõll, yang berarti Tõll yang Agung, adalah raksasa dalam mitologi Estonia dari Pulau Saaremaa. Karena kesaksian yang andal tentang mitologi Estonia kurang, kisahnya bersandar pada tradisi lisan. Ia hidup bersama istrinya, Piret, di desa pesisir Tõlluste, dan melemparkan batu raksasa ke segala arah, terutama ke arah musuh bebuyutannya Vanatühi atau Vanapagan, sosok yang mewakili kejahatan.",
   "en": "Suur Tõll, meaning Toell the Great, is a giant of Estonian mythology from the island of Saaremaa. Since reliable evidence on Estonian mythology is lacking, his story rests on oral tradition. He lived with his wife Piret in the coastal village of Tõlluste and flung huge rocks in all directions, chiefly at his archenemy Vanatühi or Vanapagan, a figure standing for the diabolical.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "toell-the-great-c01",
    "toell-the-great-c11",
    "toell-the-great-c12",
    "toell-the-great-c02",
    "toell-the-great-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Walaupun menjadi raja Saaremaa, Tõll hidup seperti petani biasa. Ia sering mengunjungi saudaranya, Leiger, di pulau tetangga Hiiumaa, dan tubuhnya begitu tinggi sehingga ia nyaris dapat berjalan kaki ke sana; tongkatnya sebatang pohon cemara. Ia selalu baik hati dan suka menolong, tetapi mudah marah, dan gemar makan kubis, minum bir, serta mandi sauna.",
   "en": "Though he was king of Saaremaa, Tõll lived like an ordinary farmer. He often visited his brother Leiger on neighbouring Hiiumaa, and was so tall that he could nearly walk there; his walking stick was a spruce trunk. He was always kind and helpful but quick to anger, and he loved cabbage, beer and the sauna.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "toell-the-great-c03",
    "toell-the-great-c04",
    "toell-the-great-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ketika seorang musuh memenggal kepalanya, Tõll meletakkan kepala itu di atas pedangnya dan berjalan sendiri ke kuburnya, yang konon berada di Tõlluste. Ia berjanji bangkit untuk menolong rakyat bila terjadi perang, tetapi anak-anak nakal mempermainkannya dengan seruan palsu tentang perang; Tõll bangkit, marah, lalu kembali ke kubur dan bersumpah tak akan kembali lagi.",
   "en": "When an enemy cut off his head, Tõll set it on his sword and walked to his own grave, said to lie somewhere in Tõlluste. He promised to rise and help the people in time of war, but naughty children tricked him with a false call to war; Tõll rose, grew angry, went back to his grave and swore never to return.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "toell-the-great-c06",
    "toell-the-great-c07"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ada dugaan bahwa kisah Tõll berakar pada tokoh sejarah, mungkin penguasa kuno Saaremaa yang juga sangat tinggi, dan asal namanya belum jelas; mungkin pelesetan Estonia dari nama keluarga bangsawan Jerman-Baltik von Toll. Tõll tetap hidup dalam budaya modern: dua kincir angin tua di desa Ninase dibentuk menyerupai Tõll dan Piret dan dikunjungi pengantin baru, dan kisahnya mengilhami film kartun Suur Tõll (1980). Pada 2002 pematung Tauno Kangro juga membuat monumen untuk sang raksasa dan istrinya di Kuressaare.",
   "en": "Some suppose that the Tõll stories rest on a historical figure, perhaps an ancient and very tall ruler of Saaremaa, and the origin of the name is unclear; it may be an Estonian corruption of the name of the Baltic German noble family von Toll. Tõll lives on in modern culture: two old windmills in Ninase village are shaped like Tõll and Piret and visited by newlyweds, and his tale inspired the cartoon film Suur Tõll (1980). In 2002 the sculptor Tauno Kangro also made a monument to the giant and his wife in Kuressaare.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c01",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "is a mythological giant hero who lived on the Estonian island of Saaremaa.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tõll yang Agung adalah pahlawan raksasa mitologis yang tinggal di Pulau Saaremaa, Estonia.",
    "en": "Toell the Great is a mythological giant hero who lived on the Estonian island of Saaremaa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c02",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "Suur Tõll lived with his wife Piret in the coastal village of Tõlluste (Tölluste) by the Baltic Sea. He tossed huge rocks everywhere, mostly aiming for his archenemy Vanatühi (The Devil) or other enemies of the local Saaremaa islanders.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Suur Tõll tinggal bersama istrinya, Piret, di desa pesisir Tõlluste di tepi Laut Baltik, dan melemparkan batu besar ke mana-mana, terutama ke arah musuh bebuyutannya Vanatühi (Iblis) atau musuh lain penduduk Saaremaa.",
    "en": "Suur Tõll lived with his wife Piret in the coastal village of Tõlluste by the Baltic Sea and tossed huge rocks everywhere, mostly at his archenemy Vanatühi (the Devil) or other enemies of the Saaremaa islanders."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c03",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "Although Tõll was the king of Saaremaa, he lived as a common farmer. He often visited his brother Leiger on the neighboring Hiiumaa (Dagö) island.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Walaupun raja Saaremaa, Tõll hidup sebagai petani biasa; ia sering mengunjungi saudaranya Leiger di pulau tetangga Hiiumaa (Dagö).",
    "en": "Though king of Saaremaa, Tõll lived as a common farmer; he often visited his brother Leiger on neighbouring Hiiumaa (Dagö)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c04",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "He was so tall that he could almost walk there (the Soela Strait has passageways only 2-3m deep, and is about 6km across). His walking stick was a 5 fathom spruce tree trunk.",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia begitu tinggi sehingga hampir bisa berjalan ke sana (Selat Soela hanya sedalam 2–3 m di beberapa jalur dan lebarnya sekitar 6 km); tongkatnya batang cemara sepanjang 5 depa.",
    "en": "He was so tall he could almost walk there (the Soela Strait has passages only 2–3 m deep and is about 6 km across); his walking stick was a 5-fathom spruce trunk."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c05",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "Tõll was always kind and ready to help, but very hot-tempered. He loved to eat cabbage, drink beer, and go to the sauna",
   "locator": "Legend",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tõll selalu baik hati dan siap menolong, tetapi sangat mudah marah; ia gemar makan kubis, minum bir, dan mandi sauna.",
    "en": "Tõll was always kind and ready to help but very hot-tempered; he loved cabbage, beer and the sauna."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c06",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "When an enemy decapitated him, Suur Tõll put his head on his sword and walked to his grave, which is supposed to be somewhere in Tõlluste.",
   "locator": "Death",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ketika musuh memenggal kepalanya, Suur Tõll meletakkan kepalanya di atas pedang dan berjalan ke kuburnya, yang konon berada di Tõlluste.",
    "en": "When an enemy beheaded him, Suur Tõll put his head on his sword and walked to his grave, said to be somewhere in Tõlluste."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c07",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "When Suur Tõll died, he promised to rise from the grave and help people in case of war. However, naughty children made fun of him by yelling \"Tõll, Tõll, wake up, there is a war in the yard\". Suur Tõll rose, grew angry and went back to his grave, swearing never to come back.",
   "locator": "Death",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Saat meninggal, Tõll berjanji bangkit dari kubur untuk menolong rakyat bila perang; anak-anak nakal mengolok-oloknya dengan berteriak bahwa ada perang di halaman, sehingga ia bangkit, marah, kembali ke kubur, dan bersumpah tak akan kembali.",
    "en": "On dying, Tõll promised to rise from the grave to help people in war; naughty children mocked him by shouting that there was a war in the yard, so he rose, grew angry, returned to his grave and swore never to come back."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c08",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "It is supposed that stories about Suur Tõll are based on a historical person, possibly an ancient ruler of Saaremaa, who was also very tall.",
   "locator": "Theories",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Diduga kisah-kisah Suur Tõll berdasar pada tokoh sejarah, mungkin penguasa kuno Saaremaa yang juga sangat tinggi.",
    "en": "It is supposed that the Suur Tõll stories are based on a historical person, possibly an ancient, very tall ruler of Saaremaa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c09",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "In the village of Ninase, there are two old windmills reshaped into the figures of Tõll and Piret. Newlyweds of Saaremaa go there to pay tribute to the mythical heroes of their island.",
   "locator": "Modern day",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di desa Ninase ada dua kincir angin tua yang dibentuk menjadi sosok Tõll dan Piret, dan pengantin baru Saaremaa datang ke sana untuk menghormati pahlawan mitos pulau mereka.",
    "en": "In Ninase village two old windmills have been reshaped into Tõll and Piret, and Saaremaa newlyweds go there to honour their island's mythical heroes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c10",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s1",
   "quote": "Toell the Great has inspired the cartoon movie Suur Tõll (Tallinnfilm 1980).",
   "locator": "Modern day",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tõll yang Agung mengilhami film kartun Suur Tõll (Tallinnfilm, 1980).",
    "en": "Toell the Great inspired the cartoon film Suur Tõll (Tallinnfilm, 1980)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c11",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s2",
   "quote": "Der Suur Tõll („Der große Tõll“) ist in der estnischen Mythologie ein Riese von der estnischen Insel Saaremaa.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Suur Tõll (\"Tõll yang Agung\") adalah raksasa dalam mitologi Estonia dari Pulau Saaremaa.",
    "en": "Suur Tõll (\"the great Tõll\") is a giant of Estonian mythology from the Estonian island of Saaremaa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c12",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s2",
   "quote": "Da verlässliche Zeugnisse über die estnische Mythologie fehlen, beruht die Geschichte vom Riesen Suur Tõll auf der Volksüberlieferung.",
   "locator": "Legende",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Karena kesaksian andal tentang mitologi Estonia kurang, kisah raksasa Suur Tõll bersandar pada tradisi rakyat.",
    "en": "Because reliable evidence on Estonian mythology is lacking, the story of the giant Suur Tõll rests on folk tradition."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c13",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s2",
   "quote": "Mit großen Steinen warf er nach seinem Gegner, dem Vanatühi oder Vanapagan, der das Diabolische repräsentierte.",
   "locator": "Legende",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia melempar batu besar ke arah lawannya, Vanatühi atau Vanapagan, yang mewakili kejahatan iblis.",
    "en": "He threw great stones at his opponent, Vanatühi or Vanapagan, who represented the diabolical."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c14",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s2",
   "quote": "Die Herkunft des Namens Tõll ist ungeklärt. Manches deutet auf eine estnische Verballhornung des Namens der alteingesessenen adligen deutschbaltischen Familie von Toll hin.",
   "locator": "Etymologie",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Asal nama Tõll tidak jelas; sebagian petunjuk mengarah pada pelesetan Estonia dari nama keluarga bangsawan Jerman-Baltik lama, von Toll.",
    "en": "The origin of the name Tõll is unclear; some evidence points to an Estonian corruption of the name of the old Baltic German noble family von Toll."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c15",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s2",
   "quote": "2002 schuf der estnische Bildhauer Tauno Kangro ein übermannsgroßes, schelmisches Denkmal für den Riesen und seine Frau an der Uferpromenade der Inselhauptstadt Kuressaare.",
   "locator": "Nachwirken",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada 2002 pematung Estonia Tauno Kangro membuat monumen jenaka lebih besar dari manusia untuk sang raksasa dan istrinya di promenade pantai Kuressaare, ibu kota pulau itu.",
    "en": "In 2002 Estonian sculptor Tauno Kangro made a larger-than-life, playful monument to the giant and his wife on the waterfront of Kuressaare, the island capital."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "toell-the-great-c16",
   "source_id": "toell-the-great-s2",
   "quote": "Der Suur Tõll ist eine positive Gestalt der Mythologie.",
   "locator": "Legende",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Suur Tõll adalah tokoh mitologi yang berwatak positif.",
    "en": "Suur Tõll is a positive figure of the mythology."
   }
  }
 ],

 ],
 "short_description": {
  "id": "Ala adalah iblis perempuan cuaca buruk dalam folklor Bulgaria, Makedonia, dan Serbia, yang menggiring awan badai berisi hujan es ke ladang untuk merusak panen.",
  "en": "The ala is a female demon of bad weather in Bulgarian, Macedonian and Serbian folklore who drives hail-bearing storm clouds over fields to ruin the crops.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "ala-demon-c01",
   "ala-demon-c15",
   "ala-demon-c16"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Ala (jamak ale) adalah makhluk mitologis perempuan dalam cerita rakyat orang Bulgaria, Makedonia, dan Serbia. Ia dianggap iblis cuaca buruk yang tugas utamanya menggiring awan guntur pembawa hujan es ke ladang, kebun anggur, atau kebun buah untuk menghancurkan atau menjarah hasil panen. Ale juga sangat rakus: mereka gemar memangsa anak-anak, bahkan dipercaya mencoba menelan Matahari atau Bulan sehingga terjadi gerhana, dan dunia akan kiamat jika mereka berhasil.",
   "en": "The ala (plural ale) is a female mythical being in the folk tradition of Bulgarians, Macedonians and Serbs. She is held to be a demon of bad weather whose chief task is to steer hail-bearing thunderclouds over fields, vineyards or orchards to ruin or plunder the harvest. Ale are also ravenous: they like to devour children and are even believed to try swallowing the Sun or Moon, causing eclipses, and the world would end should they succeed.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ala-demon-c01",
    "ala-demon-c02",
    "ala-demon-c15",
    "ala-demon-c16"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Wujud ala digambarkan beragam dan sering samar: angin hitam, makhluk raksasa tak berbentuk jelas, monster bermulut besar yang mirip manusia atau ular, naga betina, atau burung gagak. Ia juga bisa mengambil berbagai rupa manusia atau hewan dan merasuki tubuh seseorang. Keragaman ini diduga karena ala merupakan perpaduan iblis cuaca buruk Slavia dengan iblis serupa dari penduduk pra-Slavia di Balkan tengah. Ale dikatakan tinggal di awan, danau, mata air, tempat terpencil, hutan, gunung yang tak ramah, gua, atau pohon raksasa.",
   "en": "The ala's form is described in many, often vague ways: a black wind, a huge creature of unclear shape, a big-mouthed humanlike or snakelike monster, a she-dragon, or a raven. She may also take various human or animal shapes and possess a person's body. This variety is thought to come from the ala being a blend of a Slavic bad-weather demon with a similar demon of the pre-Slavic people of the central Balkans. Ale are said to live in clouds, lakes, springs, remote hidden spots, forests, harsh mountains, caves or giant trees.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ala-demon-c04",
    "ala-demon-c05",
    "ala-demon-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Bertemu ala membahayakan kesehatan jiwa dan raga, bahkan nyawa, tetapi kebaikannya bisa diperoleh bila ia didekati dengan hormat dan percaya; ia membuat orang kesayangannya kaya dan menyelamatkan mereka saat kesulitan. Musuh-musuhnya yang kuat adalah naga; dalam kisah yang sudah dikristenkan, Santo Elia mengambil peran naga, dan elang juga dipandang sebagai pelindung yang mengusir ala dari ladang. Dalam tradisi Bulgaria, badai dan awan hujan es ditafsirkan sebagai pertempuran antara naga atau elang yang baik melawan ala yang jahat.",
   "en": "Meeting an ala endangers mind, body and even life, yet her favour can be won by approaching her with respect and trust; she makes her favourites rich and saves them in hard times. Her mighty foes are dragons; in Christianized tales Saint Elijah takes the dragons' place, and eagles too are seen as defenders that drive ale from the fields. In Bulgarian tradition, every thunderstorm or hail cloud was understood as a fight in which a benevolent dragon or eagle confronts the wicked ala.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ala-demon-c03",
    "ala-demon-c07",
    "ala-demon-c08",
    "ala-demon-c14"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Asal nama ala diperdebatkan. Beberapa kamus menurunkannya dari kata Turki ala, \"ular\", sedangkan pendapat lain mengaitkannya dengan kata Yunani untuk hujan es, χάλαζα; menurut Marta Bjeletić, ala dan hala berasal dari kata Proto-Slavia Selatan *xala, dari akar yang menandakan amukan unsur-unsur alam. Banyak orang juga menganggap ala saudara perempuan naga, dan orang Serbia di Kosovo percaya bahwa siapa pun yang melihat kepalanya langsung menjadi gila.",
   "en": "The origin of the name ala is disputed. Some dictionaries derive it from the Turkish ala, \"snake\", while another view ties it to the Greek word for hail, χάλαζα; according to Marta Bjeletić, ala and hala come from Proto-South-Slavic *xala, from a root meaning the fury of the elements. Many also hold that the ala is the dragon's sister, and Serbs in Kosovo believed that anyone who saw her head went instantly mad.",

   ]
  }

 "images": [],
 "sources": [
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-s1",

  {
   "id": "ala-demon-s2",

   "title": "Ala (démon)",
   "author": null,

   "published": null,
   "language": "fr",
