  }
 ],
 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c01",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "is a female mythological creature recorded in the folklore of Bulgarians, Macedonians, and Serbs. Ale are considered demons of bad weather whose main purpose is to lead hail-producing thunderclouds in the direction of fields, vineyards, or orchards to destroy the crops, or loot and take them away.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ala adalah makhluk mitologis perempuan dalam folklor orang Bulgaria, Makedonia, dan Serbia; ale dianggap iblis cuaca buruk yang tujuan utamanya menggiring awan guntur pembawa hujan es ke ladang, kebun anggur, atau kebun buah untuk menghancurkan atau menjarah panen.",
    "en": "The ala is a female mythological being in Bulgarian, Macedonian and Serbian folklore; ale are demons of bad weather whose main aim is to lead hail-producing thunderclouds over fields, vineyards or orchards to destroy or loot the crops."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c02",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "Extremely voracious, ale particularly like to eat children, though their gluttony is not limited to Earth. It is believed they sometimes try devouring the Sun or the Moon, causing eclipses, and that it would mean the end of the world should they succeed.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ale sangat rakus dan terutama suka memakan anak-anak; mereka dipercaya kadang mencoba melahap Matahari atau Bulan sehingga terjadi gerhana, dan dunia akan berakhir jika mereka berhasil.",
    "en": "Ale are extremely voracious and especially like to eat children; they are believed to sometimes try devouring the Sun or Moon, causing eclipses, and the world would end if they succeeded."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c03",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "When people encounter an ala, their mental or physical health, or even life, are in peril; however, her favor can be gained by approaching her with respect and trust. Being in a good relationship with an ala is very beneficial, because she makes her favorites rich and saves their lives in times of trouble.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bertemu ala membahayakan kesehatan jiwa atau raga, bahkan nyawa; namun kebaikannya dapat diraih dengan mendekatinya secara hormat dan percaya, dan ia membuat orang kesayangannya kaya serta menyelamatkan nyawa mereka saat kesulitan.",
    "en": "Meeting an ala endangers mental or physical health or even life, but her favour can be won with respect and trust, and she makes her favourites rich and saves their lives in times of trouble."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c04",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "A given ala may look like a black wind, a gigantic creature of indistinct form, a huge-mouthed, humanlike, or snakelike monster, a dragoness, or a raven. An ala may also assume various human or animal shapes, and can even possess a person's body.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ala bisa berwujud angin hitam, makhluk raksasa yang bentuknya tak jelas, monster bermulut besar yang mirip manusia atau ular, naga betina, atau gagak; ia juga bisa mengambil berbagai rupa manusia atau hewan dan merasuki tubuh seseorang.",
    "en": "An ala may look like a black wind, a gigantic creature of unclear form, a huge-mouthed humanlike or snakelike monster, a dragoness or a raven; she may also take human or animal shapes and possess a person's body."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c05",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "It is believed that the diversity of appearances described is due to the ala's being a synthesis of a Slavic demon of bad weather and a similar demon of the central Balkans pre-Slavic population.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Keragaman wujud ala diduga karena ia perpaduan iblis cuaca buruk Slavia dengan iblis serupa dari penduduk pra-Slavia di Balkan tengah.",
    "en": "The variety of the ala's appearances is believed to stem from her being a synthesis of a Slavic bad-weather demon and a similar demon of the central Balkans' pre-Slavic population."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c06",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "Ale are said to live in the clouds, or in a lake, spring, hidden remote place, forest, inhospitable mountain, cave, or gigantic tree.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ale dikatakan tinggal di awan, atau di danau, mata air, tempat terpencil tersembunyi, hutan, gunung yang tak ramah, gua, atau pohon raksasa.",
    "en": "Ale are said to live in the clouds or in a lake, spring, hidden remote place, forest, inhospitable mountain, cave or gigantic tree."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c07",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "While ale are usually hostile towards humans, they do have other powerful enemies that can defeat them, like dragons. In Christianized tales, St. Elijah takes the dragons' role, but in some cases the saint and the dragons fight ale together.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ale biasanya memusuhi manusia, tetapi mereka punya musuh kuat yang dapat mengalahkan mereka, seperti naga; dalam kisah yang dikristenkan, Santo Elia mengambil peran naga, dan kadang keduanya melawan ale bersama.",
    "en": "Ale are usually hostile to humans, but they have powerful foes able to defeat them, like dragons; in Christianized tales Saint Elijah takes the dragons' role, and sometimes both fight ale together."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c08",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "Eagles are also regarded as defenders against ale, chasing them away from fields and thus preventing them from bringing hail clouds overhead.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Elang juga dianggap pelindung terhadap ale, mengusir mereka dari ladang sehingga awan hujan es tidak datang.",
    "en": "Eagles are also regarded as defenders against ale, chasing them from fields and so keeping hail clouds away."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c09",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "In folk tales with a humanlike ala, her personality is similar to that of the Russian Baba Yaga.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam dongeng dengan ala berwujud manusia, wataknya mirip Baba Yaga dari Rusia.",
    "en": "In folk tales with a humanlike ala, her personality resembles the Russian Baba Yaga."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c10",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "The name may instead stem from the Greek word for hail, χάλαζα",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Nama ala mungkin berasal dari kata Yunani untuk hujan es, χάλαζα.",
    "en": "The name may instead come from the Greek word for hail, χάλαζα."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c11",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "According to Serbian scholar Marta Bjeletić, ala and hala stem from the noun *xala in Proto-South-Slavic ... That noun was derived from the Proto-Slavic root *xal-, denoting the fury of the elements.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut ahli Serbia Marta Bjeletić, ala dan hala berasal dari kata benda Proto-Slavia Selatan *xala, yang diturunkan dari akar Proto-Slavia *xal-, yang menandakan amukan unsur-unsur alam.",
    "en": "According to Serbian scholar Marta Bjeletić, ala and hala come from Proto-South-Slavic *xala, from the Proto-Slavic root *xal-, denoting the fury of the elements."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c12",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "the demon’s name comes from the Turkish word ‘ala’ (snake) as that word lacks the h-sound",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut beberapa kamus, nama iblis ini berasal dari kata Turki ala (ular), meskipun pendapat itu ditolak karena kata tersebut tidak memiliki bunyi h.",
    "en": "Per several dictionaries the demon's name comes from Turkish ala (snake), a view rejected because that word lacks the h-sound."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c13",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "A very common opinion is that the ala is the sister of the dragon, and looks more or less like him.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pendapat yang sangat umum adalah bahwa ala saudara perempuan naga dan rupanya kurang lebih mirip dengannya.",
    "en": "A very common opinion is that the ala is the dragon's sister and looks more or less like him."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c14",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "In Bulgarian tradition, thunderstorms and hail clouds were interpreted as a battle between the good dragon or eagle and the evil ala.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam tradisi Bulgaria, badai guntur dan awan hujan es ditafsirkan sebagai pertempuran antara naga atau elang yang baik dan ala yang jahat.",
    "en": "In Bulgarian tradition, thunderstorms and hail clouds were read as a battle between the good dragon or eagle and the evil ala."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c15",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s2",
   "quote": "Une ala, ou hala (pluriel : ale ou hali), est une créature mythologique féminine présente dans le folklore bulgare, macédonien et serbe.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ala, atau hala (jamak ale atau hali), adalah makhluk mitologis perempuan dalam folklor Bulgaria, Makedonia, dan Serbia.",
    "en": "An ala, or hala (plural ale or hali), is a female mythological creature of Bulgarian, Macedonian and Serbian folklore."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c16",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s2",
   "quote": "Les ale sont considérés comme les démons du mauvais temps dont le principal but est de conduire des nuages de grêle vers des champs, des vignobles ou des vergers afin de détruire les récoltes.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ale dianggap iblis cuaca buruk yang tujuan utamanya menggiring awan hujan es ke ladang, kebun anggur, atau kebun buah untuk menghancurkan panen.",
    "en": "Ale are considered demons of bad weather whose chief aim is to drive hail clouds over fields, vineyards or orchards to destroy the crops."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ala-demon-c17",
   "source_id": "ala-demon-s1",
   "quote": "Serbs in Kosovo believed that the ala lowers her tail to the ground and hides her head in the clouds. Anyone who saw her head became instantly insane.",
   "locator": "Appearance",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang Serbia di Kosovo percaya bahwa ala menjulurkan ekornya ke tanah dan menyembunyikan kepalanya di awan; siapa pun yang melihat kepalanya langsung menjadi gila.",
    "en": "Serbs in Kosovo believed the ala lowers her tail to the ground and hides her head in the clouds; anyone who saw her head went instantly insane."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Goldhorn atau Zlatorog adalah chamois jantan putih bertanduk emas dalam folklor Slovenia yang menguasai ketinggian Gunung Triglav dan menjaga harta tersembunyi.",
  "en": "Goldhorn, or Zlatorog, is a white chamois buck with golden horns in Slovene folklore, ruling the heights of Mount Triglav and guarding a hidden treasure.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "goldhorn-c01",
   "goldhorn-c13",
   "goldhorn-c16"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam folklor Slovenia, Goldhorn atau Zlatorog adalah chamois jantan putih yang legendaris, atau menurut versi lain seekor ibex Alpen, yang wilayahnya berada di ketinggian Gunung Triglav, gunung tertinggi di Slovenia dan puncak tertinggi Pegunungan Alpen Julian. Ia bertanduk emas, memiliki taman di puncak Triglav, dan menjaga sebuah harta tersembunyi. Legenda ini terkenal di seluruh Slovenia, juga di Carinthia Austria dan Friuli-Venezia Giulia Italia.",
   "en": "In Slovene folklore, Goldhorn or Zlatorog is a legendary white chamois buck, or in another version an Alpine ibex, whose realm lay on the heights of Mount Triglav, the tallest mountain in Slovenia and highest peak of the Julian Alps. He had golden horns, kept a garden high on Triglav and guarded a hidden treasure. The legend is well known across Slovenia and also in Austrian Carinthia and Italian Friuli-Venezia Giulia.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "goldhorn-c01",
    "goldhorn-c02",
    "goldhorn-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam versi yang ditulis Karl Deschmann, tanduk emas Goldhorn adalah kunci harta di pegunungan sekitar Triglav. Seorang pemburu muda dari Lembah Trenta, yang ditolak gadis pujaannya karena seorang saudagar kaya dari Venesia, dibujuk \"pemburu hijau\" untuk memburu Goldhorn. Hewan itu tertembak, tetapi memakan bunga ajaib Triglav yang tumbuh dari darahnya dan mendapat daya hidup yang luar biasa; ketika ia menyerbu, sang pemburu silau oleh kilau tanduk emasnya, kehilangan pijakan, dan jatuh dari gunung, lalu jasadnya dihanyutkan Sungai Soča.",
   "en": "In the version written by Karl Deschmann, Goldhorn's golden horns were the key to a treasure in the mountains around Triglav. A young hunter from the Trenta Valley, spurned by his sweetheart for a rich merchant from Venice, was talked by the \"green hunter\" into hunting Goldhorn. The animal was shot but ate a magic Triglav flower sprung from its blood and gained tremendous life power; as it charged, the hunter, dazzled by its golden horns, lost his footing and fell from the mountain, and the river Soča carried his body down.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "goldhorn-c03",
    "goldhorn-c04",
    "goldhorn-c05",
    "goldhorn-c06",
    "goldhorn-c07",
    "goldhorn-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Versi lain menuturkan bahwa dari darah chamois yang terbunuh tumbuh bunga ajaib yang menghidupkannya kembali; dengan murka Zlatorog membunuh si pemburu, lalu menghancurkan taman gunungnya dan tidak pernah terlihat lagi. Namanya dalam bahasa Slovenia berarti \"tanduk emas\".",
   "en": "Another version tells that a wonder flower sprang from the slain chamois's blood and brought it back to life; in fury Zlatorog killed the hunter, then destroyed his mountain garden and was never seen again. In Slovene the name means \"golden horn\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "goldhorn-c14",
    "goldhorn-c15"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kisah Zlatorog pertama kali ditulis, disesuaikan dengan gaya Romantik akhir, dan diterbitkan oleh Karl Deschmann di Laibacher Zeitung pada 21 Februari 1868. Rudolf Baumbach menggubahnya menjadi puisi yang terbit pada 1877, dan Camilla serta Eduard Lucerna menjadikannya opera. Film panjang pertama berbahasa Slovenia, rekaman Janko Ravnik tahun 1931, berjudul In the Kingdom of the Goldhorn, dan Zlatorog juga menjadi merek bir dari pabrik bir Laško.",
   "en": "The Zlatorog story was first written down, recast in a late Romantic style and published by Karl Deschmann in the Laibacher Zeitung on 21 February 1868. Rudolf Baumbach turned it into verse published in 1877, and Camilla and Eduard Lucerna made it an opera. The first full-length Slovene-language film, shot by Janko Ravnik in 1931, was called In the Kingdom of the Goldhorn, and Zlatorog is also a beer brand of the Laško Brewery.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "goldhorn-c03",
    "goldhorn-c09",
    "goldhorn-c10",
    "goldhorn-c11"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c01",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "is a legendary white chamois buck, or alternatively, an Alpine ibex, that had his realm in the heights of Mount Triglav, the highest mountain in Slovenia and the highest peak of the Julian Alps.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Goldhorn adalah chamois jantan putih legendaris, atau menurut versi lain ibex Alpen, yang wilayahnya di ketinggian Gunung Triglav, gunung tertinggi di Slovenia dan puncak tertinggi Alpen Julian.",
    "en": "Goldhorn is a legendary white chamois buck, or alternatively an Alpine ibex, whose realm was the heights of Mount Triglav, Slovenia's highest mountain and the highest peak of the Julian Alps."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c02",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "The legend is well known throughout Slovenia (specifically Carinthia), as well as in Austrian Carinthia, and Italian Friuli-Venezia Giulia.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Legenda ini dikenal luas di seluruh Slovenia (khususnya Carinthia), juga di Carinthia Austria dan Friuli-Venezia Giulia Italia.",
    "en": "The legend is well known throughout Slovenia (especially Carinthia), and in Austrian Carinthia and Italian Friuli-Venezia Giulia."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c03",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "The story about Zlatorog was first written down, adapted to the late Romantic style and published by Karl Deschmann (Karel Dežman) in the Laibacher Zeitung, no. 43, on 21 February 1868.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah Zlatorog pertama kali ditulis, disesuaikan dengan gaya Romantik akhir, dan diterbitkan oleh Karl Deschmann (Karel Dežman) di Laibacher Zeitung no. 43 pada 21 Februari 1868.",
    "en": "The Zlatorog story was first written down, adapted to late Romantic style and published by Karl Deschmann (Karel Dežman) in the Laibacher Zeitung no. 43 on 21 February 1868."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c04",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "Goldhorn's golden horns were the key to a treasure hidden in the mountains around Triglav.",
   "locator": "Summary of the story as written by Karl Deschmann",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Tanduk emas Goldhorn adalah kunci menuju harta yang tersembunyi di pegunungan sekitar Triglav.",
    "en": "Goldhorn's golden horns were the key to a treasure hidden in the mountains around Triglav."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c05",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "A young and brave hunter from the Trenta Valley fell in love with a beautiful girl and managed to win her heart by bringing her beautiful flowers. However, one day, a rich merchant from Venice came by and tried to gain her attention by giving her golden jewelry and dancing with her.",
   "locator": "Summary of the story as written by Karl Deschmann",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Seorang pemburu muda pemberani dari Lembah Trenta memenangkan hati gadis cantik dengan bunga, tetapi suatu hari saudagar kaya dari Venesia merebut perhatiannya dengan perhiasan emas dan tarian.",
    "en": "A brave young hunter from the Trenta Valley won a beautiful girl's heart with flowers, but one day a rich merchant from Venice courted her with golden jewellery and dancing."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c06",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "Persuaded by another hunter, called the green hunter, who was said to have brought about the fate of several honest boys, he decided to go that very night to find Goldhorn and claim his treasure.",
   "locator": "Summary of the story as written by Karl Deschmann",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dibujuk pemburu lain, si \"pemburu hijau\" yang konon telah mencelakai beberapa pemuda jujur, ia memutuskan malam itu juga mencari Goldhorn dan merebut hartanya.",
    "en": "Persuaded by another hunter, the \"green hunter\", said to have doomed several honest lads, he decided that very night to seek Goldhorn and claim its treasure."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c07",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "The green hunter forced him on to catch the Goldhorn before it ate the magic Triglav flowers that grew from its blood, but it was too late. The Goldhorn had already eaten one and the flower gave it tremendous life power.",
   "locator": "Summary of the story as written by Karl Deschmann",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pemburu hijau mendesaknya menangkap Goldhorn sebelum hewan itu memakan bunga ajaib Triglav yang tumbuh dari darahnya, tetapi terlambat: Goldhorn sudah memakan satu dan bunga itu memberinya daya hidup yang luar biasa.",
    "en": "The green hunter pushed him to catch Goldhorn before it ate the magic Triglav flowers growing from its blood, but too late: Goldhorn had eaten one, gaining tremendous life power."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c08",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "It ran towards the hunter, who being blinded by the bliss of its golden horns, lost balance and fell from the mountain. The river Soča brought his corpse to the vale.",
   "locator": "Summary of the story as written by Karl Deschmann",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Goldhorn berlari ke arah si pemburu, yang silau oleh kilau tanduk emasnya, kehilangan keseimbangan, dan jatuh dari gunung; Sungai Soča membawa jasadnya ke lembah.",
    "en": "It ran at the hunter, who, dazzled by its golden horns, lost his balance and fell from the mountain; the river Soča carried his body to the vale."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c09",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "The story of Goldhorn was put into verses by Rudolf Baumbach and published in 1877, becoming his most popular work. It was turned into an opera by Camilla and Eduard Lucerna.",
   "locator": "Adaptations",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kisah Goldhorn digubah menjadi puisi oleh Rudolf Baumbach dan terbit pada 1877, menjadi karyanya yang paling populer; Camilla dan Eduard Lucerna menjadikannya opera.",
    "en": "The Goldhorn story was put into verse by Rudolf Baumbach, published in 1877 and his most popular work; Camilla and Eduard Lucerna turned it into an opera."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c10",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "The first Slovene-language, full-length film, recorded in 1931 by Janko Ravnik, was titled In the Kingdom of the Goldhorn.",
   "locator": "Cultural references",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Film panjang pertama berbahasa Slovenia, direkam Janko Ravnik pada 1931, berjudul In the Kingdom of the Goldhorn.",
    "en": "The first full-length Slovene-language film, recorded by Janko Ravnik in 1931, was titled In the Kingdom of the Goldhorn."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c11",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "Zlatorog is also a brand of beer produced by the Laško Brewery.",
   "locator": "Cultural references",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zlatorog juga merupakan merek bir produksi pabrik bir Laško.",
    "en": "Zlatorog is also a beer brand produced by the Laško Brewery."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c12",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s2",
   "quote": "Zlatorog (deutsch: Goldhorn) ist eine Sagengestalt aus den slowenischen Alpen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zlatorog (bahasa Jerman: Goldhorn) adalah tokoh legenda dari Pegunungan Alpen Slovenia.",
    "en": "Zlatorog (German: Goldhorn) is a legendary figure from the Slovenian Alps."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c13",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s2",
   "quote": "Er hatte goldene Hörner, besaß hoch oben am Triglav einen Garten und war Hüter eines verborgenen Schatzes.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia bertanduk emas, memiliki taman tinggi di Triglav, dan menjadi penjaga harta tersembunyi.",
    "en": "He had golden horns, owned a garden high on Triglav and was guardian of a hidden treasure."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c14",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s2",
   "quote": "Aus dem Blut des getöteten Gamsbocks wuchs auf der Stelle eine Wunderblume, die Zlatorog das Leben zurückgab. In rasender Wut tötete Zlatorog den Übeltäter. Hernach zerstörte er seinen Gebirgsgarten und ward nie mehr gesehen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dari darah chamois yang terbunuh seketika tumbuh bunga ajaib yang menghidupkan Zlatorog kembali; dengan murka ia membunuh si penjahat, lalu menghancurkan taman gunungnya dan tak pernah terlihat lagi.",
    "en": "From the slain chamois's blood a wonder flower sprang up at once and gave Zlatorog back his life; in a rage he killed the wrongdoer, then destroyed his mountain garden and was never seen again."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c15",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s3",
   "quote": "ズラトロクとは、スロベニア語で「金の角」を意味する。この伝説は、スロベニア、特にコロシュカ地方で有名であり",
   "locator": "概要",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Zlatorog (ズラトロク) dalam bahasa Slovenia berarti \"tanduk emas\"; legendanya terkenal di Slovenia, terutama wilayah Koroška.",
    "en": "Zlatorog means \"golden horn\" in Slovene; the legend is famous in Slovenia, especially the Koroška region."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "goldhorn-c16",
   "source_id": "goldhorn-s1",
   "quote": "In Slovene folklore, Goldhorn or Goldenhorn",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam folklor Slovenia ia disebut Goldhorn atau Goldenhorn.",
    "en": "In Slovene folklore he is called Goldhorn or Goldenhorn."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Drekavac, \"si penjerit\", adalah makhluk mitos Slavia Selatan, sering dianggap arwah anak yang mati sebelum dibaptis, yang keluar dari kubur pada malam hari.",
  "en": "The drekavac, \"the screamer\", is a South Slavic mythical creature, often held to be the soul of a child who died unbaptised, rising from the grave at night.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "drekavac-c01",
   "drekavac-c03",
   "drekavac-c13"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Drekavac, yang namanya berarti \"si penjerit\" dan diturunkan dari kata kerja drečati, adalah makhluk dalam mitologi Slavia Selatan dengan nama lain seperti drekalo, krekavac, zdrekavac, dan zrikavac. Legendanya terutama dikenal di Serbia, Bosnia-Herzegovina, Kroasia, dan Montenegro. Menurut anggapan yang tersebar luas, ia adalah arwah bayi yang mati tanpa dibaptis.",
   "en": "The drekavac, whose name means \"the screamer\" and comes from the verb drečati, is a creature of South Slavic mythology also called drekalo, krekavac, zdrekavac and zrikavac. Its legend is found mainly in Serbia, Bosnia and Herzegovina, Croatia and Montenegro. In the common view it is the soul of an infant who died unbaptised.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "drekavac-c01",
    "drekavac-c12",
    "drekavac-c13"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Wujudnya digambarkan sangat beragam. Dalam sebagian cerita ia lelaki mayat hidup yang keluar dari kubur pada malam hari untuk menghantui orang hidup; dalam cerita lain ia anak yang belum dibaptis yang menghantui orang tuanya dan memohon kepada orang yang lewat di dekat kuburan agar membaptisnya. Di Serbia timur ia berupa makhluk anjing mirip manusia yang berjalan dengan kaki belakang, di sekitar Arilje makhluk berleher dan berkaki panjang dengan kepala seperti kucing, dan di sekitar Gruža makhluk bertubuh belang, panjang, dan kurus dengan kepala terlalu besar yang tidak bisa terbang.",
   "en": "Its form is described in many ways. In some tales it is an undead man who leaves his grave at night to haunt the living; in others it is an unbaptised child that haunts its parents and begs passers-by near cemeteries to baptise it. In eastern Serbia it is a humanlike canine walking on its hind legs, around Arilje a long-necked, long-legged creature with a cat-like head, and around Gruža a dappled, long, spindly creature with an oversized head that cannot fly.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "drekavac-c02",
    "drekavac-c03",
    "drekavac-c04",
    "drekavac-c05",
    "drekavac-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Pada mulanya drekavac dipercaya berasal dari jiwa orang berdosa atau anak yang mati tanpa dibaptis. Ia diyakini hanya tampak pada malam hari, terutama pada dua belas hari Natal dan awal musim semi, ketika setan dan makhluk mitos lain dianggap lebih aktif. Dalam rupa anak ia meramalkan kematian seseorang, dalam rupa hewan ia meramalkan penyakit ternak; ia menghindari anjing dan cahaya terang, dan siapa pun yang terkena bayangannya akan sakit lalu meninggal.",
   "en": "The drekavac was first thought to come from the souls of sinful men or of children who died unbaptised. It was believed to be visible only at night, above all during the twelve days of Christmas and in early spring, when demons and other mythical beings were held to be more active. In the shape of a child it foretells a death, in animal shape a cattle disease; it shuns dogs and bright light, and anyone its shadow falls on will sicken and die.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "drekavac-c07",
    "drekavac-c08",
    "drekavac-c09"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kini drekavac terutama dipakai dalam cerita peringatan untuk anak-anak, walau ada orang dewasa yang masih memercayainya. Pada 1992 penduduk Krvavica menemukan sisa bangkai hewan yang tidak dikenal dan menyebutnya drekavac, tetapi kemudian ternyata itu hanya bangkai rubah yang membusuk.",
   "en": "Today the drekavac is mostly used in cautionary tales for children, though some adults still believe in it. In 1992 villagers in Krvavica found the remains of an unfamiliar animal and called it a drekavac, but it later proved to be only a rotting fox carcass.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "drekavac-c10",
    "drekavac-c11"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c01",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "Drekavac (literally \"the screamer\" or \"the screecher\"), also called drekalo, krekavac, zdrekavac or zrikavac, is a mythical creature in South Slavic mythology. The name is derived from the verb \"drečati\" (\"to screech\").",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Drekavac (harfiah \"si penjerit\"), juga disebut drekalo, krekavac, zdrekavac, atau zrikavac, adalah makhluk mitos Slavia Selatan; namanya diturunkan dari kata kerja drečati (\"menjerit\").",
    "en": "Drekavac (literally \"the screamer\"), also called drekalo, krekavac, zdrekavac or zrikavac, is a South Slavic mythical creature; the name comes from the verb drečati (\"to screech\")."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c02",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In some folk tales it is depicted as an undead man who emerges from his grave by night to haunt the living.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam sebagian cerita rakyat ia digambarkan sebagai lelaki mayat hidup yang keluar dari kubur pada malam hari untuk menghantui orang hidup.",
    "en": "In some folk tales it is an undead man who leaves his grave by night to haunt the living."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c03",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In others the revenant is portrayed as unbaptised child which rises from its grave at night to haunt its parents and can also sometimes be heard entreating people passing cemeteries to baptise it and thus end its misery.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam cerita lain ia anak yang belum dibaptis yang bangkit dari kubur pada malam hari untuk menghantui orang tuanya, dan kadang terdengar memohon kepada orang yang lewat di kuburan agar membaptisnya.",
    "en": "In others it is an unbaptised child rising from its grave at night to haunt its parents, sometimes heard begging people passing cemeteries to baptise it and end its misery."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c04",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In Eastern Serbia it has been depicted in the form of a humanoid canine creature that walks on its back legs.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di Serbia timur ia digambarkan sebagai makhluk anjing mirip manusia yang berjalan dengan kaki belakang.",
    "en": "In eastern Serbia it is depicted as a humanoid canine walking on its back legs."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c05",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In the vicinity of Arilje it has been depicted in form of a long-necked long-legged creature with a cat-like head.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di sekitar Arilje ia digambarkan sebagai makhluk berleher dan berkaki panjang dengan kepala seperti kucing.",
    "en": "Around Arilje it is depicted as a long-necked, long-legged creature with a cat-like head."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c06",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In the vicinity of Gruža it has been depicted in the form of a creature having a dappled, elongated and spindle-thin body with a disproportionately large head. This creature can't fly and it is believed to be the soul of a dead child.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di sekitar Gruža ia berupa makhluk bertubuh belang, panjang, dan kurus dengan kepala terlalu besar; makhluk ini tidak bisa terbang dan dipercaya arwah anak yang mati.",
    "en": "Around Gruža it is a dappled, elongated, spindle-thin creature with an oversized head; it cannot fly and is believed to be a dead child's soul."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c07",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "The drekavac was originally thought to have come from the souls of sinful men, or from children who died unbaptised.",
   "locator": "Original beliefs",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Drekavac semula dipercaya berasal dari jiwa orang berdosa atau anak yang mati tanpa dibaptis.",
    "en": "The drekavac was originally thought to come from the souls of sinful men or children who died unbaptised."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c08",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "It was popularly believed to be visible only at night, especially during the twelve days of Christmas (called unbaptised days in Serbo-Croatian) and in early spring, when other demons and mythical creatures were believed to be more active.",
   "locator": "Original beliefs",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ia dipercaya hanya tampak pada malam hari, terutama pada dua belas hari Natal (\"hari tanpa baptis\") dan awal musim semi, ketika setan dan makhluk mitos lain dianggap lebih aktif.",
    "en": "It was believed to be visible only at night, especially in the twelve days of Christmas (\"unbaptised days\") and early spring, when other demons and mythical beings were thought more active."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c09",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "When assuming the form of a child, it predicts someone's death, while in its animal form, it predicts cattle disease. The drekavac is believed to avoid dogs and bright light. Also, it is believed that if the shadow of drekavac falls upon some person then that person will turn sick and die.",
   "locator": "Original beliefs",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam rupa anak ia meramalkan kematian seseorang, dalam rupa hewan ia meramalkan penyakit ternak; ia menghindari anjing dan cahaya terang, dan orang yang terkena bayangannya akan sakit lalu meninggal.",
    "en": "In child form it predicts a death, in animal form cattle disease; it avoids dogs and bright light, and a person its shadow falls on will sicken and die."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c10",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "Although the creature is mostly used in cautionary tales for children, there are also some adults who still believe in its existence.",
   "locator": "Modern sightings",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Walaupun makhluk ini terutama dipakai dalam cerita peringatan untuk anak-anak, ada orang dewasa yang masih memercayai keberadaannya.",
    "en": "Though mostly used in cautionary tales for children, some adults still believe it exists."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c11",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In 1992, it was reported that in the Krvavica, the villagers found remains of an animal unlike any known from the area, and claimed it was a drekavac. ... Later, it was revealed to be just a rotten carcass of a fox.",
   "locator": "Modern sightings",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada 1992 dilaporkan bahwa penduduk Krvavica menemukan sisa hewan yang tak dikenal di daerah itu dan menyebutnya drekavac; belakangan terungkap itu hanya bangkai rubah yang membusuk.",
    "en": "In 1992 villagers in Krvavica reportedly found remains of an animal unknown in the area and claimed it was a drekavac; it later proved to be a rotten fox carcass."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c12",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s2",
   "quote": "Sa légende se retrouve essentiellement parmi les habitants de la Serbie (de la Choumadie jusqu'au Kosovo), de la Bosnie-Herzégovine, de la Croatie et du Monténégro.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Legendanya terutama dikenal di kalangan penduduk Serbia (dari Choumadie atau Šumadija sampai Kosovo), Bosnia-Herzegovina, Kroasia, dan Montenegro.",
    "en": "Its legend is found mainly among the people of Serbia (from Šumadija to Kosovo), Bosnia and Herzegovina, Croatia and Montenegro."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c13",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s3",
   "quote": "мифическое существо из фольклора южных славян, по распространённому представлению — душа мёртвого некрещёного младенца.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Drekavac adalah makhluk mitos dari folklor Slavia Selatan; menurut anggapan yang tersebar luas, ia arwah bayi yang mati tanpa dibaptis.",
    "en": "The drekavac is a mythical creature of South Slavic folklore; in the common view it is the soul of a dead unbaptised infant."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "drekavac-c14",
   "source_id": "drekavac-s1",
   "quote": "In the vicinity of Prijepolje, Lešak and Dragačevo it has been depicted as an apparition that can be seen in form of a dappled foal, dog, cat, or bird.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di sekitar Prijepolje, Lešak, dan Dragačevo ia digambarkan sebagai penampakan berupa anak kuda belang, anjing, kucing, atau burung.",
    "en": "Around Prijepolje, Lešak and Dragačevo it is an apparition in the form of a dappled foal, dog, cat or bird."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Putri Duyung Warsawa (Syrenka) adalah lambang kota Warsawa yang tampil pada lambang kota dan banyak patung, dikisahkan siap melindungi kota dengan pedang dan perisai.",
  "en": "The Mermaid of Warsaw (Syrenka) is the symbol of Warsaw, shown on its coat of arms and many statues, and said to stand ready to defend the city with sword and shield.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "mermaid-of-warsaw-c01",
   "mermaid-of-warsaw-c07"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Putri Duyung Warsawa adalah lambang kota Warsawa dan tampil pada lambang kota serta pada banyak patung dan gambar lain. Kata Polandia syrenka seakar dengan \"siren\", tetapi sosok ini lebih tepat disebut melusina, putri duyung air tawar. Melusina sendiri adalah roh air tawar bersayap dan berekor ikan yang hidup di sungai atau sumur.",
   "en": "The Mermaid of Warsaw is the symbol of the city of Warsaw, appearing on its coat of arms and in many statues and other images. The Polish word syrenka is related to \"siren\", but the figure is more properly a freshwater mermaid called a melusina. A melusina is a winged, fish-tailed freshwater spirit living in rivers or wells.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c01",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c02",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Wujudnya pada lambang kota berubah dari masa ke masa. Pada 1390 lambang Warsawa memuat makhluk berkaki burung dengan tubuh bersisik naga; segel tahun 1459 menampilkan ciri perempuan, tubuh burung, tangan manusia, ekor ikan, serta kaki dan cakar burung; dan wujud putri duyung pertama berasal dari 1622 dan bertahan sampai sekarang. Ia memegang perisai di tangan kiri dan pedang di tangan kanan. Ilham lambang ini kemungkinan berasal dari buku Physiologus abad ke-2.",
   "en": "Its form on the coat of arms changed over time. In 1390 the Warsaw arms showed a creature with bird legs and a dragon-scaled body; the seal of 1459 had female features, a bird body, human hands, a fish tail and bird legs and claws; and the first mermaid form dates from 1622 and remains in use. She holds a shield in her left hand and a sword in her right. The arms were probably inspired by the 2nd-century book Physiologus.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c04",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c14",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c12",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ada beberapa legenda tentangnya. Menurut kisah yang dituturkan pemandu wisata, putri duyung itu singgah di tepi sungai dekat Kota Tua dan memutuskan menetap; para nelayan yang kesal karena jaring mereka kusut dan ikan-ikan dilepaskan hendak menangkapnya, tetapi jatuh cinta setelah mendengar nyanyiannya. Ketika seorang saudagar kaya menangkap dan mengurungnya, para nelayan membebaskannya, dan sejak itu ia siap melindungi kota dengan pedang dan perisai. Versi lain menyebut ia menolong seorang pangeran yang tersesat saat berburu, lalu pangeran itu mendirikan kota untuk menghormatinya.",
   "en": "There are several legends about her. In the tale told by city guides, the mermaid paused on a riverbank near the Old Town and chose to stay; fishermen annoyed by tangled nets and freed fish planned to trap her but fell in love on hearing her sing. When a rich merchant caught and caged her, the fishermen set her free, and ever since she has stood ready to guard the city with sword and shield. Another version says she helped a prince lost while hunting, and he founded the city in her honour.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c06",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c07",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c09"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Kadang legenda ini diperluas dengan menyebut Putri Duyung Kecil di Kopenhagen sebagai saudarinya, yang berpisah jalan dengannya dari Laut Baltik. Di Warsawa terdapat banyak patungnya, antara lain karya Konstanty Hegel di alun-alun Kota Tua dan patung karya Ludwika Nitschowa yang didirikan pada April 1939 di Powiśle dekat Sungai Vistula.",
   "en": "The legend is sometimes extended to make the Little Mermaid of Copenhagen her sister, the two parting ways from the Baltic Sea. Warsaw has many statues of her, among them Konstanty Hegel's in the Old Town Square and Ludwika Nitschowa's, set up in April 1939 in Powiśle by the Vistula river.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c08",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c10",
    "mermaid-of-warsaw-c11"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c01",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "is a symbol of Warsaw, represented on the city's coat of arms as well as in a number of statues and other imagery.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Putri Duyung Warsawa adalah lambang Warsawa, ditampilkan pada lambang kota serta dalam sejumlah patung dan gambar lain.",
    "en": "The Mermaid of Warsaw is a symbol of Warsaw, shown on the city's coat of arms and in many statues and other images."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c02",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "Polish syrenka is a cognate of siren, but she is more properly a fresh-water mermaid called melusina.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kata Polandia syrenka seakar dengan \"siren\", tetapi sosok ini lebih tepat disebut melusina, putri duyung air tawar.",
    "en": "Polish syrenka is a cognate of siren, but she is more properly a freshwater mermaid called a melusina."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c03",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "A melusina is a mythical freshwater winged and fish-tailed spirit who lives in rivers or wells.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Melusina adalah roh air tawar mitis yang bersayap dan berekor ikan, hidup di sungai atau sumur.",
    "en": "A melusina is a mythical winged, fish-tailed freshwater spirit living in rivers or wells."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c04",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "A creature was on Warsaw's coat of arms in 1390. It showed an animal with a bird's legs and a torso covered with dragon scales. The seal of 1459 had feminine characteristics, a bird torso, human hands, a fishtail, and bird legs and claws. The first presentation of a mermaid dates from 1622.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Pada 1390 lambang Warsawa memuat makhluk berkaki burung dengan tubuh bersisik naga; segel 1459 berciri perempuan, bertubuh burung, bertangan manusia, berekor ikan, berkaki dan bercakar burung; penggambaran putri duyung pertama berasal dari 1622.",
    "en": "In 1390 the Warsaw arms showed a creature with bird legs and a dragon-scaled torso; the 1459 seal had female features, a bird torso, human hands, a fishtail and bird legs and claws; the first mermaid dates from 1622."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c05",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "The inspiration for the coat of arms was probably derived from the 2nd-century book Physiologus.",
   "locator": "Origin",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ilham lambang kota ini kemungkinan berasal dari buku Physiologus abad ke-2.",
    "en": "The coat of arms was probably inspired by the 2nd-century book Physiologus."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c06",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "The City's literature and tour guides say the mermaid decided to stay after stopping on a riverbank near the Old Town. Fishermen noticed something was creating waves, tangling nets, and releasing their fish. They planned to trap the animal, then heard her singing and fell in love.",
   "locator": "The legend of the Warsaw mermaid",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut literatur dan pemandu wisata kota, putri duyung itu memutuskan menetap setelah singgah di tepi sungai dekat Kota Tua; para nelayan melihat sesuatu membuat gelombang, mengusutkan jaring, dan melepaskan ikan, lalu berniat menangkapnya, tetapi jatuh cinta setelah mendengar nyanyiannya.",
    "en": "City literature and guides say the mermaid chose to stay after stopping on a riverbank near the Old Town; fishermen saw something making waves, tangling nets and freeing their fish, planned to trap it, then heard her sing and fell in love."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c07",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "A rich merchant trapped and imprisoned the mermaid. Hearing her cries, the fishermen rescued her. Ever since, the mermaid, armed with a sword and a shield, has been ready to help protect the city and its residents.",
   "locator": "The legend of the Warsaw mermaid",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Seorang saudagar kaya menangkap dan mengurung putri duyung itu; mendengar tangisnya, para nelayan menyelamatkannya, dan sejak itu ia, bersenjata pedang dan perisai, siap membantu melindungi kota dan warganya.",
    "en": "A rich merchant caught and imprisoned the mermaid; hearing her cries, the fishermen rescued her, and ever since, armed with sword and shield, she has stood ready to protect the city and its people."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c08",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "Sometimes this legend is expanded to say the Little Mermaid in Copenhagen is the Warsaw mermaid's sister and they went separate ways from the Baltic Sea.",
   "locator": "The legend of the Warsaw mermaid",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kadang legenda ini diperluas dengan menyebut Putri Duyung Kecil di Kopenhagen sebagai saudari putri duyung Warsawa, dan keduanya berpisah jalan dari Laut Baltik.",
    "en": "The legend is sometimes extended to say the Little Mermaid in Copenhagen is the Warsaw mermaid's sister, the two parting ways from the Baltic Sea."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c09",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "Another states she helped a prince lost hunting and he founded the city in her honour.",
   "locator": "The legend of the Warsaw mermaid",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Legenda lain menyebut ia menolong seorang pangeran yang tersesat saat berburu, dan pangeran itu mendirikan kota untuk menghormatinya.",
    "en": "Another says she helped a prince lost while hunting, and he founded the city in her honour."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c10",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "The sculpture in Warsaw's Old Town Square was designed by Varsovian sculptor Konstanty Hegel.",
   "locator": "The Old Town Market Place",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Patung di alun-alun Kota Tua Warsawa dirancang oleh pematung Warsawa Konstanty Hegel.",
    "en": "The sculpture in Warsaw's Old Town Square was designed by Warsaw sculptor Konstanty Hegel."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c11",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s1",
   "quote": "This statue, made of gunmetal, was erected in April 1939 in Powiśle near the Vistula river. The sculpture is by Ludwika Nitschowa and posed by poet Krystyna Krahelska.",
   "locator": "Powiśle",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Patung dari perunggu senapan ini didirikan pada April 1939 di Powiśle dekat Sungai Vistula; karya Ludwika Nitschowa dengan model penyair Krystyna Krahelska.",
    "en": "This gunmetal statue was erected in April 1939 in Powiśle near the Vistula; it is by Ludwika Nitschowa, modelled by the poet Krystyna Krahelska."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c12",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s2",
   "quote": "In der linken Hand hält sie einen Schild, in der rechten ein Schwert.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di tangan kiri (linken Hand) ia memegang perisai (Schild), di tangan kanan sebilah pedang (Schwert).",
    "en": "In her left hand (linken Hand) she holds a shield (Schild), in her right a sword (Schwert)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c13",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s2",
   "quote": "Die Warschauer Seejungfer (polnisch: Warszawska Syrenka) ist eine Symbolgestalt von Warschau und in Form einer Meerfrau Gestalt des Warschauer Stadtwappens.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Putri Duyung Warsawa (bahasa Polandia: Warszawska Syrenka) adalah tokoh lambang Warsawa dan, dalam wujud perempuan laut, menjadi sosok pada lambang kota Warsawa.",
    "en": "The Warsaw mermaid (Polish: Warszawska Syrenka) is a symbolic figure of Warsaw and, as a sea-woman, the figure on the Warsaw coat of arms."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-c14",
   "source_id": "mermaid-of-warsaw-s2",
   "quote": "Die aktuelle Gestalt einer Frau mit einem Fischschwanz stammt aus dem Jahre 1622. Diese Anordnung gilt bis heute.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Wujud saat ini, perempuan berekor ikan, berasal dari tahun 1622 dan berlaku sampai sekarang.",
    "en": "The current form, a woman with a fish tail, dates from 1622 and is still in use today."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Polevik adalah roh penguasa ladang dalam mitologi Slavia, digambarkan berambut rumput dan dipercaya menyesatkan atau mencelakai orang di ladang.",
  "en": "The polevik is the spirit-master of the fields in Slavic mythology, pictured with grass for hair and believed to lead people astray or harm them in the fields.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "polevik-c01",
   "polevik-c04",
   "polevik-c06"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, polevik adalah roh ladang. Satu sumber menggambarkannya sebagai makhluk cacat dengan mata berlainan warna dan rumput sebagai pengganti rambut, muncul pada tengah hari atau saat matahari terbenam, berpakaian serba hitam atau serba putih. Cerita rakyat Rusia selatan menyebutnya berambut hijau. Menurut kepercayaan setempat, ia menyesatkan pengembara di ladang, menularkan penyakit, atau menggilas orang yang tertidur dengan kudanya.",
   "en": "In Slavic mythology the polevik is a field spirit. One source describes it as a deformed creature with eyes of different colours and grass in place of hair, appearing at noon or at sunset and dressed all in black or all in white. South Russian folklore gives it green hair. Local belief holds that it leads wanderers astray in the fields, brings disease, or rides over sleepers with its horses.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "polevik-c01",
    "polevik-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Pada bangsa Slavia Timur, polevik dianggap roh penguasa ladang, tetapi gambarannya kabur karena kepercayaan tentangnya jarang ditemukan. Ia umumnya digambarkan mirip manusia dengan ciri hewan, tumbuhan, dan iblis, kadang disertai angin kencang dan percikan api. Ada keyakinan bahwa ia menjaga ladang serta memengaruhi kesuburan dan kesejahteraan ternak yang merumput di sana, dan di beberapa tempat orang memberinya persembahan agar panen baik.",
   "en": "In East Slavic tradition the polevik was held to be the spirit-master of the field, though its image is vague because beliefs about it were rare. It is mostly described as human-like with animal, plant and demonic traits, sometimes accompanied by strong wind and sparks. Some believed it guarded the field and influenced its fertility and the well-being of grazing livestock, and in places people brought it gifts for a good harvest.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "polevik-c04",
    "polevik-c05",
    "polevik-c07",
    "polevik-c09",
    "polevik-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sikapnya terhadap manusia umumnya bermusuhan: ia bisa menakut-nakuti, menyesatkan, bahkan membunuh, serta merusak ternak dan tanaman. Orang yang tertidur di ladang setelah minum minuman keras dapat dibunuh olehnya. Untuk menenangkannya, disebutkan perlunya dua butir telur, seekor ayam jantan, seekor kodok, dan seekor gagak yang diletakkan di parit tanpa terlihat orang.",
   "en": "Its attitude to people is mostly hostile: it may frighten them, lead them off the road or even kill them, and harm livestock and crops. A person who falls asleep at work after drinking may be killed by it. To appease it, two eggs, a rooster, a toad and a crow are said to be placed in a ditch when no one is looking.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "polevik-c03",
    "polevik-c06"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "etymology": null,

  "id": "Untuk menenangkan polevik, konon perlu dua telur, seekor ayam jantan, seekor kodok, dan seekor gagak yang ditaruh di parit diam-diam.",

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "polevik-c01",
   "source_id": "polevik-s1",
   "quote": "Polevik or Polewik in Slavic mythology are field spirits that appear as a deformed creatures with different coloured eyes and grass instead of hair. They appear either at noon or sunset and wear either all black or all white suits.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, polevik atau polewik adalah roh ladang yang tampak sebagai makhluk cacat dengan mata berlainan warna dan rumput sebagai pengganti rambut; mereka muncul pada tengah hari atau matahari terbenam dan berpakaian serba hitam atau serba putih.",
    "en": "In Slavic mythology the polevik or polewik are field spirits appearing as deformed creatures with differently coloured eyes and grass for hair; they appear at noon or sunset, dressed all in black or all in white."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c02",
   "source_id": "polevik-s1",
   "quote": "They are also described in south Russian folklore as field spirits with green hair. According to local beliefs they lead wandering people in a field astray, give them diseases or ride them over with their horses if they are found asleep.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Cerita rakyat Rusia selatan menggambarkan mereka sebagai roh ladang berambut hijau; menurut kepercayaan setempat mereka menyesatkan pengembara di ladang, menularkan penyakit, atau menggilas orang yang tertidur dengan kuda mereka.",
    "en": "South Russian folklore describes them as green-haired field spirits; local belief says they lead wanderers astray, give them diseases, or ride over sleepers with their horses."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c03",
   "source_id": "polevik-s1",
   "quote": "If a person falls asleep on the job after drinking, the Polewiki might murder them. Appeasing the Polewiki requires two eggs, a rooster, a toad, and a crow placed in a ditch when no one is looking.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Orang yang tertidur saat bekerja setelah minum bisa dibunuh polevik; untuk menenangkannya diperlukan dua telur, seekor ayam jantan, seekor kodok, dan seekor gagak yang ditaruh di parit tanpa dilihat orang.",
    "en": "A person who falls asleep at work after drinking may be murdered by the polevik; appeasing it needs two eggs, a rooster, a toad and a crow placed in a ditch unseen."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c04",
   "source_id": "polevik-s2",
   "quote": "Полеви́к, полево́й (бел. паляві́к, укр. польови́к)  — дух-хозяин поля в мифологии восточных славян.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Polevik atau polevoy (bahasa Belarus: paljavik, bahasa Ukraina: polovyk) adalah roh penguasa ladang dalam mitologi Slavia Timur.",
    "en": "The polevik or polevoy (Belarusian palyavik, Ukrainian polovyk) is the spirit-master of the field in East Slavic mythology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c05",
   "source_id": "polevik-s2",
   "quote": "Есть отрывочные представления о том, что полевик охранял поле, влиял на его плодородие и благополучие пасущегося на нём скота",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ada keyakinan yang terpisah-pisah bahwa polevik menjaga ladang, memengaruhi kesuburannya dan kesejahteraan ternak yang merumput di sana.",
    "en": "There are fragmentary beliefs that the polevik guarded the field and influenced its fertility and the well-being of livestock grazing on it."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c06",
   "source_id": "polevik-s2",
   "quote": "К человеку полевой относился в основном враждебно: мог напугать, сбить с дороги и даже убить, а также навредить скотине и посевам",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Terhadap manusia polevik umumnya bermusuhan: ia bisa menakut-nakuti, menyesatkan dari jalan, bahkan membunuh, serta merusak ternak dan tanaman.",
    "en": "Towards people the polevik was mostly hostile: it could frighten, lead off the road and even kill, and harm livestock and crops."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c07",
   "source_id": "polevik-s2",
   "quote": "Он описывается в основном как человекоподобное существо с отдельными животными, растительными и демоничными чертами, которого иногда сопровождает сильный ветер и искры или огоньки",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Polevik digambarkan terutama sebagai makhluk mirip manusia dengan sejumlah ciri hewan, tumbuhan, dan iblis, kadang disertai angin kencang dan percikan api atau cahaya.",
    "en": "It is described mainly as a human-like being with some animal, plant and demonic traits, sometimes accompanied by strong wind and sparks or lights."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c08",
   "source_id": "polevik-s1",
   "quote": "Polevik stands in contrast to the demoness of fields appearing at noon: Poludnisa",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Polevik dibandingkan secara berlawanan dengan Poludnitsa (Poludnisa), iblis perempuan ladang yang muncul pada tengah hari.",
    "en": "The polevik stands in contrast to the noon demoness of the fields, Poludnisa."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c09",
   "source_id": "polevik-s2",
   "quote": "Представления о полевике встречались редко, и, соответственно, дошедший до нас образ довольно смутен",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Kepercayaan tentang polevik jarang ditemukan, sehingga gambaran yang sampai kepada kita cukup kabur.",
    "en": "Beliefs about the polevik were rare, so the image that has reached us is rather vague."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "polevik-c10",
   "source_id": "polevik-s2",
   "quote": "В некоторых местах к полевому обращались с просьбой приглядеть за пасущейся скотиной, а кое-где ему приносили дары, чтобы он поблагоприятствовал хорошему урожаю.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di beberapa tempat orang meminta polevik menjaga ternak yang merumput, dan di tempat lain ia diberi persembahan agar memberi panen yang baik.",
    "en": "In some places people asked the polevik to watch grazing livestock, and elsewhere brought it gifts so it would favour a good harvest."
   }
  }
 ],

  "id": "Bies atau bes adalah roh jahat atau iblis dalam mitologi Slavia yang, setelah masuknya agama Kristen, disamakan dengan setan.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Bies (dalam bahasa Rusia bes, dalam bahasa Kroasia bijes) adalah roh jahat atau iblis dalam mitologi Slavia. Setelah agama Kristen diterima, bies disamakan dengan iblis dan kata ini sering dipakai bersinonim dengan chort. Kata ini sepadan dengan istilah Yunani Kuno daimon, dan dalam bahasa Slovenia, Kroasia, dan Serbia kata ini berarti \"amarah\".",
   "en": "Bies (Russian bes, Croatian bijes) is an evil spirit or demon of Slavic mythology. After Christianity was accepted, the bies was identified with the devil, and the word often became a synonym of chort. It corresponds to the Ancient Greek daimon, and in Slovenian, Croatian and Serbian the word means \"rage\".",
   "claim_ids": [
    "bies-c01",
    "bies-c02",
    "bies-c03"

  {
   "id": "Dalam gambaran mitologis Slavia kuno, para biesy adalah roh jahat, tokoh iblis tingkat rendah, dan perwujudan kekuatan yang memusuhi manusia, dengan berbagai kemampuan adikodrati. Mereka tidak punya rupa tetap; dalam cerita rakyat mereka memadukan ciri manusia dan hewan. Dalam demonologi rakyat, gambaran bes berkembang di bawah pengaruh tradisi gereja dan berbaur dengan kepercayaan tentang chort dan iblis.",
   "en": "In old Slavic mythological imagery the biesy are evil spirits, lesser demonic figures and personifications of forces hostile to humans, possessing all kinds of superhuman abilities.    "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Setelah agama Kristen diterima, bies (sama dengan chort atau czort) disamakan dengan iblis, sepadan dengan makhluk yang dalam bahasa Yunani Kuno disebut daimon.",
    "en": "After Christianity was accepted, the bies (like chort or czort) was identified with the devil, corresponding to the Ancient Greek daimon."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c03",
   "source_id": "bies-s1",
   "quote": "In Slovenian (bes), Croatian (bijes) and Serbian (bes) the word means \"rage\", \"fury\". It comes from the proto-Slavic běsъ.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam bahasa Slovenia (bes), Kroasia (bijes), dan Serbia (bes), kata ini berarti \"amarah\" atau \"kemurkaan\"; asalnya dari kata Slavia Purba (proto-Slavic) běsъ.",
    "en": "In Slovenian (bes), Croatian (bijes) and Serbian (bes) the word means \"rage\", \"fury\"; it comes from Proto-Slavic běsъ."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c04",
   "source_id": "bies-s2",
   "quote": "В народной демонологии славян представления о бесе развивались под влиянием книжной (церковной) традиции, сближаясь и смешиваясь с поверьями о чёрте, дьяволе.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam demonologi rakyat Slavia, gambaran tentang bes berkembang di bawah pengaruh tradisi tulis (gereja), mendekat dan berbaur dengan kepercayaan tentang chort dan iblis.",
    "en": "In Slavic folk demonology the idea of the bes developed under the influence of the written (church) tradition, converging and merging with beliefs about the chort and the devil."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c05",
   "source_id": "bies-s2",
   "quote": "Согласно христианскому учению, бесы могут «вселяться» в человека, это называется «одержимость бесами» или «беснованием»",
   "locator": "Одержимость бесом",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut ajaran Kristen, bes dapat \"masuk\" ke dalam diri manusia, yang disebut \"kerasukan bes\".",
    "en": "According to Christian teaching, demons can \"enter\" a person, which is called \"possession by demons\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c06",
   "source_id": "bies-s3",
   "quote": "In altslawischen mythologischen Darstellungen sind Biesy böse Geister, niedere dämonische Charaktere und Personifizierungen menschenfeindlicher Kräfte, die alle Arten von übermenschlichen Fähigkeiten haben.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam gambaran mitologis Slavia kuno, biesy adalah roh jahat, tokoh iblis tingkat rendah, dan perwujudan kekuatan yang memusuhi manusia, dengan segala macam kemampuan adikodrati.",
    "en": "In old Slavic mythological depictions the biesy are evil spirits, lesser demonic characters and personifications of forces hostile to humans, with all kinds of superhuman abilities."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c07",
   "source_id": "bies-s3",
   "quote": "Biesy hatten kein festes Bild. In der Folklore kombinierten sie menschliche und tierische Merkmale.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Biesy tidak memiliki rupa yang tetap; dalam cerita rakyat mereka memadukan ciri manusia dan hewan.",
    "en": "The biesy had no fixed image; in folklore they combined human and animal features."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c08",
   "source_id": "bies-s1",
   "quote": "In the 2015 videogame The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt, fiends (called bies in the original version) are massive horned predators who possess hypnotic and self-healing powers.",
   "locator": "Examples in culture",
   "context": "modern-popular-culture",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam gim video The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt (2015), makhluk \"fiend\" (dalam versi asli disebut bies) adalah pemangsa bertanduk raksasa dengan kekuatan hipnotis dan penyembuhan diri.",
    "en": "In the 2015 video game The Witcher 3: Wild Hunt, fiends (called bies in the original) are massive horned predators with hypnotic and self-healing powers."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "bies-c09",
   "source_id": "bies-s2",
   "quote": "Вселившиеся бесы влекут человека к вредному излишеству в поведении и различным психическим болезням.",
   "locator": "Одержимость бесом",
   "context": "religious-tradition",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Bes yang merasuk mendorong manusia pada perilaku berlebihan yang merugikan dan berbagai penyakit jiwa.",
    "en": "Possessing demons drive a person to harmful excess in behaviour and various mental illnesses."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [],

 ]

  "id": "Iku-Turso adalah makhluk mitologi Finlandia yang merujuk pada satu atau beberapa raksasa; dalam Kalevala ia monster laut yang jahat.",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Iku-Turso, juga dikenal sebagai Iku-Tursas, Meritursas, Tursas, atau Turisas, merujuk pada satu atau beberapa raksasa dalam mitologi Finlandia, dan dalam Kalevala ia tampil sebagai monster laut yang jahat. Nama Turisas kemungkinan kata pinjaman awal dari bahasa Proto-Jermanik yang berarti \"raksasa\". Para ahli belum sepakat apakah berbagai penyebutan Tursas dalam lagu-lagu runik merujuk pada satu makhluk atau beberapa.",
   "en": "Iku-Turso, also called Iku-Tursas, Meritursas, Tursas or Turisas, refers to one or several giants of Finnish mythology, and in the Kalevala appears as a malevolent sea monster. The name Turisas is probably an early loan from Proto-Germanic meaning \"giant\". Scholars do not agree whether the various mentions of Tursas in runic songs mean one being or several.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "iku-turso-creature-c01",
    "iku-turso-creature-c02",
    "iku-turso-creature-c03",
    "iku-turso-creature-c10"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Wujudnya tidak pasti, tetapi ia dikaitkan dengan sejumlah julukan: partalainen (\"yang tinggal di tepi\" atau \"yang berjanggut\"), Tuonen härkä (\"lembu Tuoni\"), tuhatpää (\"berkepala seribu\"), dan tuhatsarvi (\"bertanduk seribu\"). Satu lagu menyebut Meri-Tursas (\"Tursas Laut\") menghamili Loviatar, Gadis dari Utara, melalui ombak ketika ia mencari kelegaan di air, sehingga Loviatar melahirkan penyakit-penyakit dunia.",
   "en": "Its looks are uncertain, but epithets attached to it include partalainen (a dweller on the brink, or else a bearded one), Tuonen härkä (Tuoni's ox), tuhatpää (having a thousand heads) and tuhatsarvi (having a thousand horns). One song tells how Meri-Tursas, \"Sea Tursas\", impregnated Loviatar, the Maiden of North, through the waves as she sought relief in the water, so that she gave birth to the diseases of the world.",
   "claim_ids": [

   "en": "In the list of Tavastian gods compiled by Mikael Agricola in 1551, Turisas is named as the god of war who brought victory.   "id": "Sava Savanović adalah salah satu vampir paling terkenal dalam cerita rakyat Balkan dan Serbia, yang konon tinggal di sebuah kincir air tua dan meminum darah para penggiling gandum.",
  "en": "Sava Savanović is one of the most famous vampires of Balkan and Serbian folklore, said to have lived in an old watermill and drunk the blood of millers.",
  "claim_ids": [

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Sava Savanović adalah salah satu vampir paling terkenal dalam cerita rakyat Balkan dan Serbia, dan hingga kini tetap vampir yang paling dikenal di Serbia. Menurut legenda, ia tinggal di sebuah kincir air tua di Sungai Rogačica, di desa Zarožje, wilayah Bajina Bašta, dan membunuh serta meminum darah para penggiling yang datang menggiling biji-bijian mereka.",
   "en": "Sava Savanović is one of the most famous vampires of Balkan and Serbian folklore and remains the best-known vampire in Serbia today. Legend places his home in an old watermill beside the Rogačica river near the village of Zarožje, in Bajina Bašta, where he killed millers who brought their grain and drank their blood.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "sava-savanovic-c01",
    "sava-savanovic-c02",
    "sava-savanovic-c03"

Setelah para penggiling di kincir air Sungai Rogačica berulang kali ditemukan tewas dengan bekas gigitan di leher, penduduk yakin Sava telah menjadi vampir. Ketika kuburnya dibuka, jasadnya ditemukan tidak membusuk, dan untuk mematahkan kutukan sebuah pancang runcing ditancapkan ke jantungnya.",
   "en": "His story was passed down only orally and has many versions, but they share a core: a man of Zarožje village who died tragically after a killing and was buried near the scene. After millers at the Rogačica watermills were repeatedly found dead with bite marks on their necks, villagers concluded that Sava had become a vampire. When his grave was opened his body was found undecayed, and to break the curse a sharpened stake was driven through his heart.",

   "quote": "Sie wurden ausschließlich mündlich überliefert und unterscheiden sich bezüglich Ort und den beteiligten Akteuren, haben aber alle eine gemeinsame Kerngeschichte: in allen Geschichten geht es um einen Mann aus dem Dorf Zarožje",

   "source_id": "sava-savanovic-s2",
   "quote": "Nachdem jedoch wiederholt Müller in den Wassermühlen am Fluss Rogačica tot aufgefunden wurden, welche alle Bissspuren am Hals aufwiesen, war für die Bewohner schnell klar: Sava war ein Vampir geworden.",

   "context": "traditional-belief",

  "id": "Ahti atau Ahto adalah dewa air, laut, dan perikanan dalam mitologi Finlandia, yang dalam lagu-lagu runik dimintai ikan oleh para nelayan.",
  "en": "Ahti or Ahto is the god of water, the sea and fishing in Finnish mythology, asked in runic songs to give fish.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "ahti-c01",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Ahti adalah dewa air dalam mitologi Finlandia; dalam Kalevala ia disebut Ahto agar tidak tertukar dengan tokoh lain, Ahti Saarelainen. Satu sumber menyebutnya dewa laut dan perikanan yang digambarkan sebagai laki-laki berjanggut lumut, suami Vellamo, dan tinggal bersamanya di istana bawah laut Ahtola.",
   "en": "Ahti is a water god of Finnish mythology; in the Kalevala he is called Ahto so as not to be confused with the separate character Ahti Saarelainen. One source calls him the god of the sea and fishing, depicted as a man with a beard of moss, husband of Vellamo, living with her in the undersea palace of Ahtola.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ahti-c01",
    "ahti-c06"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ahti pertama kali disebut secara tertulis pada 1551 oleh Mikael Agricola sebagai pemberi ikan dari air. Ganander menyebutnya roh laut (haltija) yang memberi ikan dan dimintai tolong oleh nelayan yang kapalnya karam. Dalam lagu-lagu runik dari Kainuu ia disapa sebagai \"Raja Emas Laut, Ahti Laut yang pengasih\" dan dimintai ikan perch.",
   "en": "The earliest written mention of Ahti comes from Mikael Agricola in 1551, who named him as the one who brings fish out of the water. Ganander called him a sea haltija (spirit) who gave fish and to whom shipwrecked fishermen cried for help. Runic songs from Kainuu address him as \"Golden King of the Sea, merciful Ahti of the Sea\" and ask him for perch.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ahti-c02",
    "ahti-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Ahti juga muncul dalam mantra penyembuhan dalam berbagai wujud: Ahti Laut, Air, Hutan, Tanah, dan Angin. Asal-usul namanya diperdebatkan; satu teori menghubungkannya dengan kata kerja ahtaa, \"menggantung jaring ikan untuk dikeringkan\", sehingga Ahti menjadi dewa yang menjamin keberuntungan saat menjaring ikan.",
   "en": "Healing spells invoke him under several guises, such as Ahti of the Sea, of Water, of the Forest, of the Land and of the Wind. The origin of his name is debated; one theory links it to the verb ahtaa, \"to hang fishnets to dry\", which would make Ahti a god who ensures luck in seine fishing.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "ahti-c04",
    "ahti-c05"
   ]
  }
 ],
