 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "ahti-c01",
   "source_id": "ahti-s1",
   "quote": "Ahti is a water god in Finnish mythology. In the Kalevala, he is referred to with the name Ahto in order to not confuse him with the separate character Ahti Saarelainen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ahti adalah dewa air dalam mitologi Finlandia; dalam Kalevala ia disebut Ahto agar tidak tertukar dengan tokoh lain, Ahti Saarelainen.",
    "en": "Ahti is a water god in Finnish mythology; in the Kalevala he is called Ahto so as not to confuse him with the separate character Ahti Saarelainen."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c02",
   "source_id": "ahti-s1",
   "quote": "Ahti was first mentioned in writing in 1551 by Mikael Agricola as the one who gave fish from water (Achti wedhest caloia toi). Ganander called Ahti a sea haltija who gave fish and who shipwrecked fishermen called for help.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "historical-record",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ahti pertama kali disebut secara tertulis pada 1551 oleh Mikael Agricola sebagai pemberi ikan dari air; Ganander menyebutnya haltija (roh) laut yang memberi ikan dan dimintai tolong oleh nelayan yang karam.",
    "en": "Ahti was first mentioned in writing in 1551 by Mikael Agricola as the one who gave fish from water; Ganander called him a sea haltija who gave fish and whom shipwrecked fishermen called on for help."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c03",
   "source_id": "ahti-s1",
   "quote": "In this function, as a giver of fish, he only appears in runic songs from Kainuu, addressed as Meren kultanen kuninkas, / Meren Ahti armollinen 'Golden King of the Sea, merciful Ahti of the Sea' and asked to give perches.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Sebagai pemberi ikan, ia hanya muncul dalam lagu-lagu runik dari Kainuu, disapa \"Raja Emas Laut, Ahti Laut yang pengasih\" dan dimintai ikan perch.",
    "en": "As a giver of fish he appears only in runic songs from Kainuu, addressed as \"Golden King of the Sea, merciful Ahti of the Sea\" and asked to give perches."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c04",
   "source_id": "ahti-s1",
   "quote": "Ahti also appears in healing spells in various forms: Ahti of the Sea, Water, Forest, Land, and Wind.",
   "locator": "Description",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ahti juga muncul dalam mantra penyembuhan dalam berbagai wujud: Ahti Laut, Air, Hutan, Tanah, dan Angin.",
    "en": "Healing spells invoke him under several guises, such as Ahti of the Sea, of Water, of the Forest, of the Land and of the Wind."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c05",
   "source_id": "ahti-s1",
   "quote": "According to a later theory, Ahti's name could come from the verb ahtaa 'to hang fishnets to dry'. A net drying rack is ahde. Similar words are found across Uralic languages. This would make Ahti a god who guarantees luck in seining.",
   "locator": "Name",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut teori yang lebih kemudian, nama Ahti bisa berasal dari kata kerja ahtaa, \"menggantung jaring ikan untuk dikeringkan\", sehingga Ahti menjadi dewa yang menjamin keberuntungan dalam menjaring ikan.",
    "en": "A later theory derives Ahti's name from the verb ahtaa, \"to hang fishnets to dry\", which would make Ahti a god guaranteeing luck in seining."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c06",
   "source_id": "ahti-s2",
   "quote": "Dans la mythologie finlandaise, Ahti ou Ahto est le dieu de la mer et de la pêche. Il est représenté comme un homme avec une barbe de mousse. Il est l'époux de Vellamo et ils demeurent tous deux dans le palais sous-marin de Ahtola.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Finlandia, Ahti atau Ahto adalah dewa laut dan perikanan; ia digambarkan sebagai laki-laki berjanggut lumut, suami Vellamo, dan keduanya tinggal di istana bawah laut Ahtola.",
    "en": "In Finnish mythology Ahti or Ahto is the god of the sea and fishing, depicted as a man with a beard of moss; he is Vellamo's husband and both live in the undersea palace of Ahtola."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c07",
   "source_id": "ahti-s2",
   "quote": "Ahti est une des figures héroïques compilées par Elias Lönnrot dans le Kalevala pour créer le personnage de Lemminkaïnen.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ahti adalah salah satu tokoh kepahlawanan yang dihimpun Elias Lönnrot dalam Kalevala untuk membentuk tokoh Lemminkäinen (Lemminkaïnen).",
    "en": "Ahti is one of the heroic figures compiled by Elias Lönnrot in the Kalevala to create the character Lemminkäinen."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "ahti-c08",
   "source_id": "ahti-s1",
   "quote": "Initial theory for the origin of the name Ahti was in the North Germanic word ahva 'water', or in the same family of words (ægir, 'sea').",
   "locator": "Name",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Teori awal mencari asal nama Ahti pada kata Jermanik Utara ahva, \"air\", atau rumpun kata yang sama (ægir, \"laut\").",
    "en": "The initial theory derived Ahti from the North Germanic word ahva, \"water\", or the same word family (ægir, \"sea\")."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Aitvaras adalah roh alam dalam mitologi Lituania, berwujud ayam jantan berekor api, yang menetap di rumah dan membawa emas serta gandum curian.",
  "en": "Aitvaras is a nature spirit of Lithuanian mythology, shaped like a rooster with a fiery tail, which settles in a house and brings stolen gold and grain.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "aitvaras-c01",
   "aitvaras-c02",
   "aitvaras-c03"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Aitvaras adalah roh alam dalam mitologi Lituania, dikenal juga dengan nama lain seperti Pūkis, Damavykas, dan Spirukas, dan identik dengan Pūķis dari Latvia. Ia tampak seperti ayam jantan putih atau hitam dengan ekor menyala seperti meteor; di luar rumah ia sering digambarkan sebagai burung berpenampilan naga. Jika mati, ia berubah menjadi percikan api.",
   "en": "Aitvaras is a nature spirit of Lithuanian mythology, also known by names such as Pūkis, Damavykas and Spirukas, and identical to the Latvian Pūķis. It looks like a white or black rooster with a meteor-like fiery tail; outdoors it is often described as a bird with a dragon's appearance. When it dies it becomes a spark.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "aitvaras-c01",
    "aitvaras-c02",
    "aitvaras-c03"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Aitvaras bergerak dengan terbang, umumnya berapi, tetapi dapat berubah rupa. Ia menetap di sebuah rumah dan biasanya menolak pergi, membawa keberuntungan sekaligus kesialan bagi penghuninya: ia memasok emas dan gandum curian, yang sering menyeret keluarga itu ke dalam masalah. Jika dibuat marah, ia membakar rumah.",
   "en": "The aitvaras moves by flying, is usually fiery, but can change its appearance. It lodges in a house and mostly refuses to leave, bringing both good and bad luck to the household: it supplies stolen gold and grain, often landing the family in trouble. If angered, it burns the house down.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "aitvaras-c03",
    "aitvaras-c05"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Makhluk ini pertama kali disebut pada 1547 oleh Martynas Mažvydas. Sumber berbeda soal asal-usulnya: satu menyebutnya menetas dari telur ayam jantan berumur 9 hingga 15 tahun, sementara sumber lain menyebut telur ayam jantan berumur tujuh tahun atau buah zakar kuda jantan hitam. Menurut banyak sumber, aitvaras dapat dibeli dari iblis dengan menukar jiwa pembelinya.",
   "en": "The being was first mentioned in 1547 by Martynas Mažvydas. Sources differ on its origin: one says it hatches from the egg of a rooster aged 9 to 15 years, another from the egg of a seven-year-old rooster or the testicle of a black stallion.    "quote": "Aitvaras is a nature spirit in Lithuanian mythology. It is also known by other names, such as Atvaras, Damavykas, Pūkis, Sparyžius, Koklikas, Gausinėlis, Žaltvikšas, and Spirukas, and is identical to the Latvian Pūķis.",

   "quote": "An Aitvaras looks like a white or black rooster with a fiery tail like a meteor. An Aitvaras is said to hatch from an egg of a 9- to 15-year-old rooster. If an Aitvaras dies, it becomes a spark.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Aitvaras tampak seperti ayam jantan putih atau hitam dengan ekor menyala seperti meteor; konon ia menetas dari telur ayam jantan berumur 9 hingga 15 tahun, dan jika mati ia menjadi percikan api.",

   "quote": "In many cases, this Lithuanian creature is described as a bird with the appearance of a dragon outdoors. An Aitvaras will lodge itself in a house and most often refuse to leave.    "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Makhluk Lituania ini sering digambarkan sebagai burung yang di luar rumah berpenampilan naga; ia menetap di rumah dan biasanya menolak pergi, membawa keberuntungan dan kesialan bagi penghuninya dengan memasok emas dan gandum curian yang sering menyusahkan keluarga itu.",
    "en": "This Lithuanian creature is often described as a bird with a dragon's appearance outdoors; it lodges in a house, usually refuses to leave, and brings both good and bad luck, supplying stolen gold and grain that often get the household into trouble."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "aitvaras-c04",
   "source_id": "aitvaras-s2",
   "quote": "Erstmals erwähnt wurde das Wesen 1547 von Martynas Mažvydas.",

  },
  {
   "id": "aitvaras-c05",
   "source_id": "aitvaras-s2",
   "quote": "Der Aitvaras bewegt sich fliegend und er ist zumeist feurig, kann aber sein Aussehen ändern. Er bringt Getreide und anderen Reichtum, den er aber mitunter dem Nachbarn entwendet. Wird er verzürnt, brennt er das Haus nieder.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Aitvaras bergerak dengan terbang dan umumnya berapi, tetapi dapat mengubah penampilannya; ia membawa gandum dan kekayaan lain yang kadang dicuri dari tetangga, dan jika dibuat marah ia membakar rumah.",
    "en": "The aitvaras moves by flying and is mostly fiery but can change its appearance; it brings grain and other wealth, sometimes stolen from neighbours, and if angered burns the house down."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "aitvaras-c06",
   "source_id": "aitvaras-s2",
   "quote": "Der Aitvaras wird aus dem Ei eines siebenjährigen Hahnes oder aus dem Hoden eines schwarzen Hengstes ausgebrütet oder durch Füttern mit Eierspeise angelockt.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Aitvaras ditetaskan dari telur ayam jantan berumur tujuh tahun atau dari buah zakar kuda jantan hitam, atau dipikat dengan diberi makan hidangan telur.",
    "en": "The aitvaras is hatched from the egg of a seven-year-old rooster or the testicle of a black stallion, or lured by feeding it egg dishes."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "aitvaras-c07",
   "source_id": "aitvaras-s3",
   "quote": "Selon de nombreuses sources, les Aitvaras pouvaient être achetés auprès du diable, en échange de l'âme de l'acheteur.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut banyak sumber, aitvaras dapat dibeli dari iblis dengan menukar jiwa pembelinya.",
    "en": "According to many sources, an aitvaras could be bought from the devil in exchange for the buyer's soul."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "aitvaras-c08",
   "source_id": "aitvaras-s2",
   "quote": "das Kinderspielzeug „Drachen“ heißt entsprechend im Litauischen aitvaras",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Mainan anak layang-layang (\"Drachen\") dalam bahasa Lituania disebut aitvaras.",
    "en": "The children's toy kite (\"Drachen\") is accordingly called aitvaras in Lithuanian."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "aitvaras-c09",
   "source_id": "aitvaras-s3",
   "quote": "Algirdas Julien Greimas a consacré une étude à cette figure mythologique dans Des dieux et des hommes, en la mettant en regard des kaukai (pluriel de kaukas), sortes de nains chthoniens de la tradition lituanienne",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "scholarly-interpretation",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Algirdas Julien Greimas mengkaji tokoh ini dalam Des dieux et des hommes dengan membandingkannya dengan kaukai (jamak dari kaukas), semacam makhluk kerdil dunia bawah dalam tradisi Lituania.",
    "en": "Algirdas Julien Greimas studied this figure in Des dieux et des hommes, comparing it with the kaukai (plural of kaukas), chthonic dwarfs of Lithuanian tradition."
   }
  }
 ],
 "conflicts": [
  {

    "en": "Origin of the aitvaras"
   },

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Balaur adalah naga berkepala banyak atau ular monster dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, kadang bersayap, yang dalam dongeng biasanya jahat dan dikalahkan oleh pahlawan.",
  "en": "The balaur is a many-headed dragon or monstrous serpent of Romanian folklore, sometimes winged, usually evil in folktales and defeated by a hero.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "balaur-c01",

 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Dalam cerita rakyat Rumania, balaur (jamak: balauri) adalah sejenis naga berkepala banyak atau ular monster, kadang disebut bersayap. Kepalanya biasanya tiga, tetapi beberapa legenda menyebut tujuh bahkan dua belas. Dalam bahasa Rumania, kata balaur juga dapat dipakai untuk makhluk apa pun yang mirip monster.",
   "en": "In Romanian folklore the balaur (plural balauri) is a kind of many-headed dragon or monstrous serpent, sometimes said to have wings. It usually has three heads, though some legends give it seven or even twelve. In Romanian the word balaur can also describe any monster-like creature.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "balaur-c01",
    "balaur-c02"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam dongeng, balaur biasanya jahat: ia meminta atau menculik gadis muda atau putri, lalu dikalahkan oleh pahlawan seperti Santo Georgius atau pemuda tampan Făt-Frumos. Ia juga digambarkan sebagai makhluk mirip ular yang menjaga harta dan putri. Ada legenda bahwa air liurnya dapat menghasilkan batu permata, dan siapa pun yang berhasil membunuhnya akan diampuni satu dosanya.",
   "en": "In folktales the balaur is typically evil, demanding or abducting young maidens or a princess, and is defeated by a hero such as Saint George or the fair youth Făt-Frumos. It is also pictured as a serpentine being guarding treasures and princesses. Some legends say its saliva can produce precious stones, and that whoever slays it will be forgiven a sin.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "balaur-c03",
    "balaur-c04",
    "balaur-c09"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Menurut folklorist Tudor Pamfile, ada tiga jenis balaur dalam tradisi rakyat: penghuni air, darat, dan udara. Jenis air antara lain monster berkepala tujuh yang tinggal di sumur desa dan meminta gadis sebagai korban. Balaur udara dikaitkan dengan cuaca dan disebut juga hala atau ala; bila dua balaur bertarung di udara, timbul kerusakan seperti pohon tercabut. Menurut sebagian sumber, balaur menjadi tunggangan para Solomonari, penyihir pengendali cuaca, yang mengendalikannya dengan tali kekang emas.",
   "en": "According to folklorist Tudor Pamfile there are three types of balaur in folk tradition: water-, land- and air-dwelling. The water type includes a seven-headed monster living in a village well and demanding maidens as sacrifice. The air balaur is linked to weather and also called hala or ala; when two balauri fight in the air, damage such as uprooted trees follows.  "short_description": {
  "id": "Chort adalah roh jahat atau iblis berwujud mirip manusia dalam tradisi rakyat Slavia, sering digambarkan bertanduk, berkuku belah, dan berekor kurus.",
  "en": "The chort is an anthropomorphic malign spirit or demon of Slavic folk tradition, often depicted with horns, hooves and a skinny tail.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "chort-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Chort (чёрт; juga czart atau čert) adalah roh jahat atau iblis berwujud mirip manusia dalam tradisi rakyat Slavia. Ia sering digambarkan sama dengan iblis Kristen, lengkap dengan tanduk, kuku belah, dan ekor kurus. Dalam mitologi Slavia, satu chort kadang disebut putra dewa Chernobog dan dewi Mara, sedangkan dalam Kristen rakyat para chort dianggap antek rendahan Iblis. Ia tokoh paling populer dalam demonologi Rusia.",
   "en": "The chort (чёрт; also czart or čert) is an anthropomorphic malign spirit or demon of Slavic folk tradition. It is often depicted just like the Christian devil, with horns, hooves and a skinny tail. In Slavic mythology a single chort is sometimes called a son of the god Chernobog and the goddess Mara, while folk Christianity counts chorts as lesser minions of Satan. It is the most popular figure of Russian demonology.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chort-c01",
    "chort-c11"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat, chort dibayangkan pincang. Ia tinggal di rawa, hutan, dan perairan, dan menampakkan diri dalam pusaran angin. Ia mendatangkan cuaca buruk dan penyakit, membujuk orang bunuh diri, dan dapat menjelma ular, anjing, babi, atau kucing hitam. Dalam satu mitos ia terluka di kaki saat bertarung dengan salah satu dewa atau jatuh dari langit dan patah kaki.",
   "en": "In folk belief the chort is imagined as lame. It lives in swamps, forests and bodies of water and appears in whirlwinds. It sends bad weather and disease, urges people to suicide, and can take the form of a snake, dog, pig or black cat. In one myth it was wounded in the leg fighting one of the gods, or fell from the sky and broke its leg.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chort-c07",
    "chort-c08",
    "chort-c09"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Dalam dongeng Ceko dan Slowakia, čert tidak sepenuhnya jahat: ia sering membujuk tokoh menjual jiwa mereka demi sesuatu, tetapi kadang justru diakali oleh pahlawan dan kalah taruhan. Wujud aslinya laki-laki kecil berbulu dengan ekor, tanduk, dan satu atau dua kuku belah, tetapi ia pandai berubah rupa. Asal kata Slavia Purba *čьrtъ masih diperdebatkan; ada yang mengaitkannya dengan sihir, dengan \"memotong\" karena chort dibayangkan pincang, atau dengan \"menggaris\" sebagai penentu nasib manusia.",
   "en": "In Czech and Slovak folk tales the čert is not simply evil: it often tempts characters to sell their souls for something, but is sometimes outwitted by a hero and loses a bet. In its real shape it is a small, hairy fellow with horns, a tail and a hoof or two, yet it can change its form. The origin of Proto-Slavic *čьrtъ is debated; theories link it to sorcery, to \"cutting\" because the chort was imagined lame, or to \"drawing a line\" as a determiner of human fate.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "chort-c02",
    "chort-c03",
    "chort-c04",
    "chort-c05",
    "chort-c12",
    "chort-c13"
   ]
  }
 ],

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "chort-c01",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "is an anthropomorphic malign spirit or demon in Slavic folk tradition. Chorts are often depicted identically to Christian devils, with horns, hooves, and a skinny tail. In Slavic mythology, a singular chort is sometimes identified as a son of the god Chernobog and the goddess Mara. In folk Christianity, they are considered lesser minions of Satan.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chort (bahasa Rusia чёрт, bahasa Polandia czart, bahasa Ceko čert) adalah roh jahat atau iblis berwujud manusia dalam tradisi rakyat Slavia, sering digambarkan seperti iblis Kristen dengan tanduk, kuku belah, dan ekor kurus; dalam mitologi Slavia satu chort kadang disebut putra dewa Chernobog dan dewi Mara, dan dalam Kristen rakyat mereka dianggap antek rendahan Iblis (Satan).",
    "en": "A chort is an anthropomorphic malign spirit or demon in Slavic folk tradition, often depicted like Christian devils with horns, hooves and a skinny tail; a single chort is sometimes a son of Chernobog and Mara, and in folk Christianity chorts are lesser minions of Satan."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c02",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "One is that it is the past passive participle of an unattested verb (nouns: *čarъ, *čara (\"magic, sorcery\")) that is the exact equivalent of the Lithuanian kerė́ti (\"to bewitch\").",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Satu teori: kata *čьrtъ adalah partisipel pasif dari kata kerja tak terbukti (bentuk benda *čarъ, *čara, \"sihir\") yang setara persis dengan kata Lituania kerė́ti, \"menyihir\".",
    "en": "One theory: it is the past passive participle of an unattested verb (nouns *čarъ, *čara, \"magic, sorcery\"), the exact equivalent of Lithuanian kerė́ti, \"to bewitch\"."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c03",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "Another is that it is a substantivized t-participle of *ker- (to cut, to chop), which could be derived from a chort imagined as being lame (having one leg shorter).",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Teori lain: kata ini partisipel-t yang dibendakan dari *ker- (\"memotong, mencincang\"), mungkin karena chort dibayangkan pincang (satu kakinya lebih pendek).",
    "en": "Another: it is a substantivised t-participle of *ker- (\"to cut, to chop\"), perhaps from a chort imagined lame (one leg shorter)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c04",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "In Czech and Slovak folk tales, čert is not an evil character per se. It is often trying to tease characters in selling their souls in exchange for something (money, power, completion of a task).",
   "locator": "In Czech and Slovak culture",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam dongeng Ceko dan Slowakia, čert tidak jahat semata; ia sering membujuk tokoh menjual jiwa demi sesuatu (uang, kekuasaan, penyelesaian tugas).",
    "en": "In Czech and Slovak folk tales the čert is not evil per se; it often tempts characters to sell their souls for something (money, power, completion of a task)."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c05",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "According to another hypothesis, Proto-Slavic *čьrtъ represents a derivative of *čersti / čьrtǫ ‘to draw a line, furrow’. One way to interpret this derivation is reconstructing *čьrtъ as a supernatural Draughtsman in charge of determining human fate.",
   "locator": "Etymology",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Menurut hipotesis lain, kata Slavia Purba *čьrtъ diturunkan dari *čersti / čьrtǫ, \"menggaris, membuat alur\", sehingga *čьrtъ dapat ditafsirkan sebagai \"Juru Gambar\" adikodrati penentu nasib manusia.",
    "en": "Another hypothesis derives Proto-Slavic *čьrtъ from *čersti / čьrtǫ, \"to draw a line, furrow\", reconstructing it as a supernatural Draughtsman determining human fate."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c06",
   "source_id": "chort-s2",
   "quote": "Czart, zwany także jako czort (cz. čert, rus. czort, ros. чёрт) – w wierzeniach Słowian demon zła, znany głównie u plemion ruskich, zastąpił starosłowiańskiego biesa.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Czart, juga disebut czort (Ceko čert, Rusia чёрт), dalam kepercayaan Slavia adalah iblis jahat yang dikenal terutama di kalangan suku Rus dan menggantikan bies Slavia Kuno.",
    "en": "The czart, also called czort, is in Slavic belief a demon of evil, known mainly among the Rus tribes, which replaced the Old Slavic bies."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c07",
   "source_id": "chort-s2",
   "quote": "W wierzeniach ludowych czart wyobrażany jest jako chromy. Zamieszkuje bagna, lasy i zbiorniki wodne, objawia się także w wirach powietrznych",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam kepercayaan rakyat, czart dibayangkan pincang; ia tinggal di rawa, hutan, dan perairan, dan juga menampakkan diri dalam pusaran angin.",
    "en": "In folk belief the czart is imagined as lame; it lives in swamps, forests and bodies of water, and also appears in whirlwinds."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c08",
   "source_id": "chort-s2",
   "quote": "Czart zsyłał na ludzi niepogodę i choroby, a także namawiał do samobójstwa. Mógł przyjmować postać węża, psa, świni lub czarnego kota.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Czart mendatangkan cuaca buruk dan penyakit kepada manusia serta membujuk orang bunuh diri; ia dapat menjelma ular, anjing, babi, atau kucing hitam.",
    "en": "The czart sent bad weather and disease upon people and urged them to suicide; it could take the form of a snake, dog, pig or black cat."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c09",
   "source_id": "chort-s2",
   "quote": "W mitologii słowiańskiej czart prowadził kiedyś walkę z którymś z bogów, w trakcie której został zraniony w nogę, lub też został zrzucony z nieba i spadając złamał nogę.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Slavia, czart pernah bertarung dengan salah satu dewa dan terluka di kaki, atau dilempar dari langit dan patah kaki saat jatuh.",
    "en": "In Slavic mythology the czart once fought one of the gods and was wounded in the leg, or was thrown from the sky and broke its leg in the fall."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c10",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "чёрт побери (chort poberi ) – meaning \"be taken by the demon\" (often used as an exclamation to express frustration or pain",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "etymology",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Ungkapan Rusia чёрт побери (chort poberi) berarti \"diambil iblis\" dan sering dipakai sebagai seruan kesal atau sakit.",
    "en": "The Russian чёрт побери (chort poberi) means \"be taken by the demon\", often an exclamation of frustration or pain."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c11",
   "source_id": "chort-s3",
   "quote": "Чёрт — в славянской мифологии злой дух. Под различными прозвищами и синонимами является персонажем огромного количества сказок Восточной Европы, самый популярный персонаж русской демонологии.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Chort (Чёрт) dalam mitologi Slavia adalah roh jahat; dengan berbagai julukan ia muncul dalam sangat banyak dongeng Eropa Timur dan merupakan tokoh paling populer dalam demonologi Rusia.",
    "en": "The chort in Slavic mythology is an evil spirit; under various names it features in a vast number of Eastern European tales and is the most popular figure of Russian demonology."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c12",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "The true form of Čert is often a smallish hairy man with a tail, horns and one or two hoofs. But he is a shapeshifter and he tries to trick characters in his nicer forms",
   "locator": "In Czech and Slovak culture",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Wujud asli čert sering berupa laki-laki kecil berbulu dengan ekor, tanduk, dan satu atau dua kuku belah, tetapi ia pengubah wujud yang menipu tokoh dengan rupa yang lebih menarik.",
    "en": "The true form of the čert is often a smallish hairy man with a tail, horns and one or two hooves, but he is a shapeshifter who tricks characters in nicer forms."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "chort-c13",
   "source_id": "chort-s1",
   "quote": "Other times, čert changes roles from trickster to tricked as he loses a bet against a hero, who outsmarts him, winning his soul back.",
   "locator": "In Czech and Slovak culture",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Di kisah lain čert berubah dari penipu menjadi yang ditipu ketika kalah taruhan melawan pahlawan yang mengakalinya dan merebut kembali jiwanya.",
    "en": "At other times the čert turns from trickster to tricked, losing a bet to a hero who outsmarts him and wins his soul back."
   }
  }
 ],

 "short_description": {
  "id": "Lauma (dalam bahasa Lituania Laumė) adalah roh hutan mirip peri dan roh pelindung anak yatim dalam mitologi Baltik Timur, yang semula roh langit.",
  "en": "Lauma (Lithuanian Laumė) is a forest spirit resembling a fairy in Eastern Baltic mythology, a protector of orphans who began as a spirit of the sky.",
  "claim_ids": [
   "lauma-c01"
  ]
 },
 "long_description": [
  {
   "id": "Lauma dari Latvia atau Laumė dari Lituania adalah roh hutan mirip peri dan roh pelindung anak yatim dalam mitologi Baltik Timur. Semula ia roh langit, tetapi belas kasihnya pada penderitaan manusia membawanya turun ke bumi untuk berbagi nasib manusia. Satu sumber menyebut para Laumė sebagai dewi tertua dalam mitologi Lituania.",
   "en": "In Eastern Baltic mythology, the Latvian Lauma or Lithuanian Laumė is a forest spirit resembling a fairy and a protector of orphans. Originally a sky spirit, her compassion for human suffering brought her down to earth to share the human lot. One source calls the Laumės the oldest goddesses of Lithuanian mythology.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lauma-c01",
    "lauma-c04"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Laumė dapat muncul dalam wujud hewan, seperti kuda betina, kambing betina, beruang, dan anjing. Kemudian mereka berwujud lebih mirip manusia: biasanya berkaki cakar burung dan tampak sebagai perempuan berkepala atau bertubuh bawah kambing betina. Mereka tinggal di dekat danau, di pemandian yang ditinggalkan, di pulau danau, atau di hutan lebat, dan takut pada perkakas dari besi.",
   "en": "Laumės could appear as animals such as mares, female goats, bears and dogs. Later they took a more human form: usually with birds' claws for feet, looking like women whose head or lower half was that of a she-goat. They lived near lakes, in abandoned bath-houses, on lake islands or in dense forests, and feared iron tools.",
   "claim_ids": [
    "lauma-c02",
    "lauma-c05",
    "lauma-c08"
   ]
  },
  {
   "id": "Sifat mereka mendua. Satu sumber menyebut mereka berbahaya bagi laki-laki maupun perempuan, sementara sumber lain menggambarkan mereka sebagai peri yang disukai dalam cerita rakyat, mengikuti dewi takdir Laima, memintal, menenun, dan mencuci, menolong orang baik, tetapi menghukum perempuan jahat dan malas. Dalam bahasa Lituania, pelangi disebut laumės juosta, \"selempang Laumė\".",
   "en": "Their nature is two-sided. One source calls them dangerous to both men and women, while another describes them as popular fairies of folklore who follow the fate goddess Laima, spin, weave and wash, help good people, but punish wicked and lazy women. In Lithuanian the rainbow is called laumės juosta, \"Laumė's sash\".",
   "claim_ids": [

    "lauma-c06",
    "lauma-c07"
   ]
  }

 "claims": [
  {
   "id": "lauma-c01",
   "source_id": "lauma-s1",
   "quote": "Latvian Lauma or Lithuanian Laumė, or Yotvingian Lauma is a fairy-like woodland spirit, and guardian spirit of orphans in Eastern Baltic mythology or Yotvingian mythology. Originally a sky spirit, her compassion for human suffering brought her to earth to share our fate.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Lauma dari Latvia, Laumė dari Lituania, atau Lauma dari Yotvingia adalah roh hutan mirip peri dan roh pelindung anak yatim dalam mitologi Baltik Timur atau Yotvingia; semula roh langit, belas kasihnya pada penderitaan manusia membawanya ke bumi untuk berbagi nasib manusia.",
    "en": "Latvian Lauma, Lithuanian Laumė or Yotvingian Lauma is a fairy-like woodland spirit and guardian of orphans in Eastern Baltic or Yotvingian mythology; originally a sky spirit, compassion for human suffering brought her to earth to share our fate."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lauma-c02",
   "source_id": "lauma-s1",
   "quote": "Laumės could appear in the form of animals, as mares or as female goats, bears and dogs. Later, Laumės had an anthropomorphic appearance: they usually had birds’ claws for feet and appeared as women with the head or lower body of a female goat.",
   "locator": "In Lithuanian mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Laumė dapat muncul dalam wujud hewan seperti kuda betina, kambing betina, beruang, dan anjing; kemudian mereka berwujud mirip manusia, biasanya berkaki cakar burung, tampak sebagai perempuan berkepala atau bertubuh bawah kambing betina.",
    "en": "Laumės could appear as animals such as mares, female goats, bears and dogs; later they had a human-like form, usually with birds' claws for feet, looking like women whose head or lower half was that of a she-goat."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lauma-c03",

   "quote": "Laumės are the very oldest goddesses of Lithuanian mythology.",
   "locator": "In Lithuanian mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",

    "id": "Laumė adalah dewi-dewi tertua dalam mitologi Lituania.",
    "en": "Laumės are the very oldest goddesses of Lithuanian mythology."

  },
  {
   "id": "lauma-c05",
   "source_id": "lauma-s1",
   "quote": "Laumės descended from the sky to Earth. They lived nearby lakes, abandoned bath-houses, in islands of lakes or dense forests.",
   "locator": "In Lithuanian mythology",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Laumė turun dari langit ke bumi dan tinggal di dekat danau, di pemandian yang ditinggalkan, di pulau-pulau danau, atau di hutan lebat.",
    "en": "Laumės descended from the sky to Earth and lived near lakes, in abandoned bath-houses, on lake islands or in dense forests."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lauma-c06",
   "source_id": "lauma-s2",
   "quote": "sind in der baltischen Mythologie feenhafte Wesen, die manchmal in Vogelgestalt auftreten oder zumindest Vogelfüße haben. Sie können auch Svētās meitas »Heilige Jungfrauen« genannt werden. In der späteren Folklore können sie mit den Ragana vermischt werden.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Dalam mitologi Baltik, Lauma adalah makhluk mirip peri yang kadang berwujud burung atau setidaknya berkaki burung; mereka juga disebut Svētās meitas (\"Gadis Suci\"), dan dalam cerita rakyat kemudian dapat bercampur dengan Ragana.",
    "en": "In Baltic mythology the Laumas are fairy-like beings sometimes appearing as birds or at least with bird feet; they may also be called Svētās meitas (\"Holy Maidens\"), and in later folklore may be mixed with the Ragana."
   }
  },
  {
   "id": "lauma-c07",
   "source_id": "lauma-s2",
   "quote": "Die Laumes sind in der Folklore beliebte Feen, die im Gefolge der Schicksalsgöttin Laima auftreten. Sie spinnen, weben und waschen und zeigen sich gegenüber guten Menschen hilfreich, böse und faule Frauen werden dagegen von ihnen bestraft.",
   "locator": "lead",
   "context": "traditional-belief",
   "statement": {
    "id": "Laumė adalah peri yang disukai dalam cerita rakyat, muncul sebagai pengiring dewi takdir Laima; mereka memintal, menenun, dan mencuci, menolong orang baik, tetapi menghukum perempuan jahat dan malas.",
    "en": "The Laumės are popular fairies of folklore appearing in the retinue of the fate goddess Laima; they spin, weave and wash, help good people, and punish wicked and lazy women."
   }
